# TabMail iOS Known Issues

This is the searchable register for deliberately deferred iOS behavior. Read it before planning or reviewing a matching subsystem. The release policy is: restoring sync/list state after a fail-closed edge is acceptable; mutating the wrong message or draft is not.

**How to read a row's Status (corrected 2026-08-04).** This file used to say *"Resolved items do not remain here; their history belongs in ADRs, audit plans, and Git."* **That is no longer true and the sentence is retracted**, because a register id is a stable handle cited from commit bodies, ADRs and prior audit rounds — deleting a row breaks those citations, and this repo does not delete history. Every row instead carries its own disposition in the Status cell, and there are five. **Every id carries EXACTLY ONE of them — a row with none is the failure mode the completeness audit at the foot of this file exists to eliminate** (the 2026-08-04 pass-2 audit found one, `IOS-MIGRATION-001`, which had been adjudicated in prose but never marked, and therefore read as Open on the one row whose failure mode is a brick).

- **Open** — no ✅ marker. A live deferral. This is the only kind you may cite as an outstanding finding.
- ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION** — adjudicated, not fixed. The behaviour is still there **on purpose**; the cell states the decision, its basis in this codebase, the accepted cost, and the recoverability (with the non-recovering case named, per `MIS-IOS-008`) that makes the cost acceptable. **Do not re-raise these; if you disagree, argue against the stated basis.**
- ✅ **FIXED by `<sha>`** — the defect is gone. The cell states what invariant now holds, and any **accepted residual** the fix left behind. The original statement above the closure is kept as history, not as a live finding.
- ✅ **NOT A DEFECT** — raised by a reviewer, checked, and found not to be a defect at all. The cell states the adjudication and the evidence it was re-verified against, so the row's job is to stop the same lead being re-raised every round (`IOS-MIGRATION-001`).
- ⚠️ **DECOMPOSED** — the id was a grab bag; a per-item table below the row is authoritative (`IOS-QUEUE-003`). Its per-item table carries the dispositions; the id itself is dispositioned by that table being complete.

Superseded and retracted claims are struck through in prose or wrapped in ⚠️ RETRACTED markers **in place, never deleted**, so a reader who finds the old claim in `git log` or in a commit body finds the correction beside it.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Deferred policy |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-EPOCH-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — accepted fail-closed window (T1.3), with ONE sub-claim RETRACTED as already-closed | IMAP; iCloud; UIDVALIDITY; `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`; durable admission; first sync; silent no-op; archive; delete; move; mark read; flag; user label; draft save; demo mode; orphaned header | **What the user observes:** on an IMAP or iCloud account, a gesture made against a brand-new folder *during that folder's very first sync* appears to do nothing — the message does not animate away, the flag does not stick, and no error is shown. Repeating the gesture once the folder has finished its first sync works normally. **Why:** a durable IMAP action is refused when the target folder's `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity` is nil, because `MessageHeader.stableId` falls back to a bare numeric UID for any header lacking an `rfc822MessageId` and `IMAPProvider.resolveUID` treats a numeric id as a literal UID — executing that under an unknown epoch risks mutating a DIFFERENT message (constraint C3). Failing closed is always acceptable; mutating the wrong message is not. The refusal is a **silent no-op** by owner decision §9 D6(a) — no dialog, no toast. **The bound:** T1.2b (`7c71f6c7b`) persists the epoch from the `Mailbox.Selection` of SELECTs the sync and folder-open paths already perform, and `OK [UIDVALIDITY n]` is core IMAP4rev1 rather than a UIDPLUS extension, so every server reports it on SELECT even when it never answers a UIDVALIDITY STATUS. Audit round 7 closed a hole in that bound: a **custom NON-FAVOURITE folder** is excluded from `syncableFolders`, so no *scheduled* pass (full sync, delta sync, self-heal) ever SELECTs it, while `runBackfill` crawls it and makes its mail account-wide searchable — on a non-UIDPLUS server its epoch stayed nil and every gesture on it was refused. ⚠ **Round 7 called that refusal PERMANENT and a "brick"; round 8 RETRACTS the absolute.** On-demand navigation reaches ANY folder: `InboxView.onAppear` → `InboxViewModel.startSync()` → `performSync()` → `AccountManager.syncFolders(_:)` (`AccountManagerFetch.swift`, filtering on `!folder.path.isEmpty` alone — no role, no favourite predicate) → `SyncEngine.syncFolderMessages` → `syncMessages` → `runSyncMessages`, which carries the T1.2b SELECT-sourced bootstrap; `SyncEngineFullSync.swift` states it three lines under the `syncableFolders` construction (*"Custom non-favorited folders sync on-demand when the user navigates to them"*). The refusal was therefore **indefinite until the user opens that folder**, not permanent — still a real bug for mail reachable only through account-wide search in a folder the user never opens, which is why the crawl-sourced bootstrap stays. **Round 8 also replaced the VALUE that bootstrap writes.** Round 7 took ONE post-walk sample of `IMAPProvider.lastObservedUidValidity`, and the walk's own SELECTs were raw, so the mirror held the epoch observed when the pinned connection was CREATED and tracked no turnover during the crawl — on a resumed crawl it could be an epoch the mailbox rolled to AFTER an earlier pass's headers were written, and since this guard tests only for nil it then ADMITTED bare-UID gestures that resolved against a different message (C3). The crawl's three SELECTs (`getUidNext`, `searchExistingUIDs(folder:from:to:)`, `fetchMessageHeaders(folder:uids:…)`) now route through `IMAPProvider.selectMailboxTracked`; the walk captures its epoch ONCE at walk start, refuses (never confirms) any chunk whose own SELECT disagrees, and stamps through `SyncEngine.bootstrapCrawledFolderUidValidity`, which is **bootstrap-only AND gated on the folder holding no local header yet** — evaluated inside the write transaction, so the epoch it asserts is the epoch of every UID it unlocks. A folder that already holds rows of unproven epoch is NOT stamped: v3 has not ported ADR-IOS-061's purge-and-resync reaction, so failing closed there is the only C3-safe answer (C6). A folder whose mailbox turned over is also DECLINED for the rest of that `runBackfill` call (`epochDeclinedFolderIds`) and on every later call whose walk epoch disagrees with the stored one (`SyncEngine.crawlEpochAgrees`) — without that the outer folder loop simply re-walked the still-incomplete folder under the new epoch and inserted the mixed population the per-chunk guard had just refused. **Carve-outs and inversions (audit rounds 6–7) — do not "simplify" these away:** (1) Gmail and Exchange never populate `FolderInfo.uidValidity` at all, so their epoch is nil forever; the refusal is provider-scoped to `.imap`/`.icloud` and must never be widened to "the column is nil", which would permanently brick every Gmail and Exchange account. (2) The **demo account is excluded by id** (`DemoSeed.demoAccountId`): it is stored as `provider: .imap` but served by `DemoProvider`, which has no server and can never stamp an epoch, so without the carve-out every guarded gesture in Demo Mode was refused forever. (3) A missing `Folder` row now fails **CLOSED**, inverting what this row said before commit `8f20ab2f4`. An orphaned header (folder row deleted by `fullSync` while its headers survive — no FK since migration v2) carries a bare OLD-epoch UID under no metadata at all, and admitting it is precisely C3. The two callers that once justified the fail-open no longer reach the guard with a guessed path: `UserLabelMenuModel.applyLabel(_:)`/`removeLabel(_:)` resolve the header inside their own admission write transaction and abort when it is missing (T4.V13 deleted the `resolvedFolderPath()` helper that once guessed `"INBOX"`; resolving in-transaction is strictly stronger, since the helper still read the header in an earlier transaction and a MOVE landing in between could name the folder the message had already left), and the draft sites guard only the id shapes that resolve an EXISTING UID. (4) The two **draft** guards are classified by what the provider DOES with the id, not by the op's name — `serverDraftId` nil ⇒ pure APPEND (admit), non-numeric ⇒ Message-ID SEARCH, epoch-immune (admit), numeric ⇒ literal-UID `STORE \Deleted` + `EXPUNGE` (guard). The numeric case is re-classified again at EXECUTION time in `DraftStore.pushDraftToServer`, because two saves queued while the id was still nil are both admitted as APPEND-only and the second one executes against a live row that has since become numeric. A refused draft save loses nothing of the user's: the local `Draft` row keeps the content and only the server copy stays stale. (5) A synthesized/fake epoch was proposed and REJECTED (owner, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **RETRACTED 2026-08-04 — the "Residual" sentence that follows is STALE. It is kept in place rather than deleted, so the error stays searchable; the retraction is argued in full at the end of this cell.** **Residual:** until the D5 purge-and-resync reaction (T4.S6) lands, `lastKnownUidValidity` is bootstrap-only for VALUES and never advances past a real UIDVALIDITY turnover, so this admission guard must NOT be read as "the epoch model is now sound". Do not close T4.S6's row by pointing at T1.3. ⚠️ **END OF RETRACTED TEXT.** **Round 10 corrections, all user-visible:** (1) an EMPTY folder whose mailbox turned over mid-crawl used to be refused on every later pass forever — the stored epoch could never agree with the live one again and no writer could advance it, so the folder never gained mail again and no Smart Reindex recovered it. `SyncEngine.resetEmptyFolderCrawlEpoch` now CLEARS the stamp and the cursor (writes NULLs only, so it joins no VALUE-writer set — the count of that set is corrected from "three" to **five** at the end of this cell; the property this parenthetical asserts, that this path writes NULLs only, is unchanged) when the folder holds zero headers, and the next iteration re-derives both from the live mailbox; a folder holding rows stays refused, which is still T4.S6's case. (2) A pass that could not observe the folder's epoch — a failed walk-start SELECT, or a server that stops reporting `UIDVALIDITY` — no longer walks a folder that HAS a stored epoch; **accepted user-visible bound, CORRECTED in round 12:** if a server reports an epoch for a folder once and then stops reporting it on SELECT, that folder's crawl re-declines every cycle (one SELECT per cycle) and its un-crawled mail stays un-indexed **PERMANENTLY for as long as the server keeps omitting it** — round 11 wrote "indefinitely — not permanently", and that was wrong in the way that matters: `.refuseUnobservedEpoch` mutates nothing, so the state (stored epoch present, none observed) is re-created on every later call, i.e. a per-call decline container with a DURABLE re-entry condition, which is the same "transient container + durable re-entry ⇒ permanent refusal" shape `resetEmptyFolderCrawlEpoch` exists to break. What IS true is only that the condition is a property of the server, not a local latch: one conformant SELECT clears it (`BackfillOnlyFolderEpochTests.aFolderWithAStoredEpochIsNeverWalkedUnderAnUnobservedOne` now asserts both halves — the refusal recurring across calls, and the recovery). **Round 12 also retracts the reasoning round 11 gave for this refusal.** It argued that a non-nil stored epoch *proves the server reports UIDVALIDITY*, so refusing could only ever catch a transient failure. That inference is FALSE: the stored epoch can come from `IMAPProvider.fetchFolders`' STATUS command (through `SyncEngine.fullSync`'s folder-list upsert) while the walk's comes from SELECT, so a stamp proves the server reports UIDVALIDITY *somewhere*, not that SELECT does; and a SELECT that succeeds while reporting `UIDVALIDITY 0` normalises to nil too. The refusal stands on a stronger footing instead: RFC 3501 §6.3.1 lists UIDVALIDITY among SELECT's **REQUIRED** OK untagged responses (UNSEEN, PERMANENTFLAGS, UIDNEXT, UIDVALIDITY) and states that if it is missing the server does not support unique identifiers — RFC 9051 §6.3.2 the same. A server omitting it is nonconforming and is declaring it has no UIDs, which is an account this app cannot serve at all, so no bounded recovery is built for it and the fail-closed refusal is the whole answer (C3 outranks C6's tolerance for a stalled crawl). An account whose server NEVER reports `UIDVALIDITY` is unaffected (its folders have a nil stored epoch and are crawled normally). (3) A transient failure of the crawl's epoch-bootstrap WRITE used to leave the folder durably unstampable — the failing pass inserted headers, which made its own retry precondition false — so every gesture on that folder's mail became a permanent silent no-op with no way back; the throw now declines the folder before the walk inserts anything. **Round 12 corrections.** (1) TWO of the crawl's six IMAP-branch bookkeeping writes were UNGUARDED — the `case .fresh` completion write (`backfillComplete`/`lastKnownUidNext` when UIDNEXT ≤ 1) and the initial-cursor planting write — while the commit that added the guard and `SyncEngine.crawlWalkWriteAllowed`'s own doc both claimed *every* bookkeeping transaction re-read the folder row. `v2final` guards both (`uidValidityWalkWriteAllowed`, log strings *"skipping fully-crawled write"* / *"skipping initial cursor write"*), so this was a PORT GAP, not a v3 design choice. Unguarded, a folder re-stamped by a sibling pass while this one was on the network could be declared fully crawled by the stale pass (its mail then permanently outside automatic backfill, since every cycle selects on `backfillComplete == false`), or gain a cursor and rows from an epoch its stamp does not describe (C3). (2) `SyncEngine.crawlWalkWriteAllowed` now COMPARE-AND-SETs the folder's stamp against the premise the pass's bookkeeping was accounted under, instead of comparing it to the walk's epoch: `resetEmptyFolderCrawlEpoch` made value→nil reachable, and a nil stored epoch ADMITTED — the fail-open direction. (3) `resetEmptyFolderCrawlEpoch` was a TOCTOU across two overlapping `runBackfill` calls (the persistent worker `SyncEngine.startBackfill` launches, and `SyncScheduler`'s BGProcessing pass; `SyncEngine` is an actor, so they interleave). It checked only `headerCount == 0` and then updated by folder id, so a walk acting on an already-stale mismatch could clear a stamp a sibling had just re-derived, and the sibling's already-fetched batch — held outside SQLite, invisible to the count — then landed under a nil stamp whose final guard read the nil as agreement and marked the populated folder COMPLETE. Durable end state: rows present, no epoch, complete — excluded from every later crawl cycle and un-re-stampable, because the header-count gate refuses a populated folder, so not even Smart Reindex recovers it. It now re-validates, inside its own transaction, that the folder is still incomplete and still carries the stamp the gate saw disagree. (4) The initial-cursor write was a bare `try await`: a throw fell through to the folder loop's outer `catch`, which only logs, leaving the folder incomplete, cursor-less and undeclined, so the loop's fresh re-read handed back the same folder with no inter-folder delay — an unbounded network-rate SELECT loop under a persistent write failure (GRDB suspension / ADR-IOS-046, `SQLITE_FULL`, corruption). `v2final`'s counterpart write has the same shape, so this is a DELIBERATE divergence from the reference, not a port regression. **⚠ ROW RE-BASELINED 2026-08-02 — the second half of this row is what commit `3843940cb` actually SHIPPED, and it is FAIL-CLOSED. Earlier revisions of this row described only the admission-time nil-epoch refusal and left the reader to assume the reference's post-turnover behaviour, which was fail-OPEN across retries: the reference compares the live selection against a `Selection` minted inside the same call, so its refusal SILENTLY RE-ARMS on the next attempt and the op eventually executes under numbering it never observed. v3 does not do that.** **What the user observes (the shipped behaviour):** an IMAP or iCloud gesture that was already queued when the mailbox's UIDVALIDITY turns over never completes. It does not animate back, it does not error, and repeating the gesture after the turnover works normally. **Why:** v3 compares against the op's **durable** `PendingOperation.observedUidValidity`, stamped at admission from the header/snapshot that observed that exact UID (ADR-IOS-068), so **once the folder's epoch moves, every retry of that op fails identically and forever.** The intention is DROPPED rather than executed under numbering it never observed — the deliberate departure from never-drop that ADR-IOS-069 authorises as the queue's fourth exit and that `IOS-ACTION-002` records as a user-visible cost. Failing closed is always acceptable (C3); mutating the wrong message is not. `IMAPProvider.move` enforces this at **five** boundaries — A1 wrapper selection, A2 the post-destination-probe source re-SELECT, A3 pre-COPY, A4 pre-STORE, A5 pre-`UID EXPUNGE` — and a non-UIDPLUS server fails closed at copy-plus-soft-delete with no expunge of any kind. The UIDPLUS gate is checked BEFORE A5 deliberately: asserting first would let an epoch change refuse an already-complete op, and a refusal after a successful COPY retries into a duplicate at the destination. **The one ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, stated by that commit:** a turnover landing between the COPY and A4 leaves the copy at the destination and the original at the source. Sync reconciles it, and it is strictly safer than completing the delete. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04), WITH ONE SUB-CLAIM RETRACTED.** **(a) The retracted sub-claim, named exactly.** The *"**Residual:** until the D5 purge-and-resync reaction (T4.S6) lands, `lastKnownUidValidity` … never advances past a real UIDVALIDITY turnover"* sentence marked ⚠ RETRACTED above is **ALREADY-CLOSED, not deferred**, and is retracted in place rather than deleted. **T4.S6 HAS LANDED.** `AccountManagerUidValidityReset.uidValidityResetStampFreshEpoch` runs `folder.lastKnownUidValidity = Int(fresh); folder.uidValidityResetPendingAt = nil; try folder.update(db)` inside a write transaction that first drops every op `opIsProvenInvalidatedByReset` identifies (never-drop exit 4), and it is reached from `runUidValidityResetReaction` step 5 after every abort-capable purge has succeeded; the reaction is driven by `SyncEngineDeltaSync`'s per-folder `uidValidityResetPendingAt != nil` arm, by the full-sync loop, and by `SyncEngineEpochVerify`'s quarantine leg. The VALUE-writer set is therefore **three** — `bootstrapFolderUidValidity`, `bootstrapVerifiedFolderUidValidity`, `uidValidityResetStampFreshEpoch` — plus one NULL writer (`SyncEngineBackfillWalk`'s `resetEmptyFolderCrawlEpoch`), which is exactly the count the round-10/12 text above already states; the residual sentence contradicted its own row. 📌 **COUNT CORRECTED (2026-08-05) — the CLAIM stands, the NUMBER was wrong, and the two extras were always in-tree.** The "three" above under-counts by two and is left in place so the error stays searchable. Predicate for the corrected census, stated so the next reader can re-run it instead of trusting a number: *production Swift statements (excluding `TabMailTests/`) that write a non-NULL value into `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`, whether by Swift property assignment, `Column("lastKnownUidValidity").set(to:)`, or raw `UPDATE folder SET lastKnownUidValidity = …`*. At HEAD that census is **five**: (1) `SyncEngineDeltaSync.bootstrapFolderUidValidity` — the raw `UPDATE … WHERE id = :id AND lastKnownUidValidity IS NULL AND NOT EXISTS(… messageHeader …)`; (2) `SyncEngineDeltaSync.bootstrapVerifiedFolderUidValidity` — `.filter(Column("id") == folderId && Column("lastKnownUidValidity") == nil).updateAll(db, Column("lastKnownUidValidity").set(to: epoch))`; (3) `AccountManagerUidValidityReset.uidValidityResetStampFreshEpoch` — `folder.lastKnownUidValidity = Int(fresh)`; **(4) and (5), the two that were missing — both arms of `SyncEngine.fullSync`'s folder-list upsert loop**, `existing.lastKnownUidValidity = bootstrap` on the update arm and `folder.lastKnownUidValidity = Self.uidValidityBootstrapWrite(…)` on the insert arm. Plus the one NULL clearer already named, `SyncEngineBackfillWalk.resetEmptyFolderCrawlEpoch`'s `Column("lastKnownUidValidity").set(to: nil as Int?)`. 🚨 **THE INVARIANT IS UNAFFECTED, and that is the point of correcting the count rather than the claim.** Both fullSync arms route through `SyncEngine.uidValidityBootstrapWrite`, which is the same bootstrap rule the other two bootstrap doors carry — it returns a value only for a first observation and discharges the header-existence term explicitly (`existingHoldsRows` / `recreatedHoldsRows`, both counted inside the same write transaction as the `update`/`insert`, with no suspension between). They are enumerated in-tree at those sites, including the T4.S6b note explaining why the existing-row arm cannot route through `bootstrapFolderUidValidity` (it sets the field on a record it is already updating, so the `IS NULL` predicate cannot live in its statement). So the set of paths that can ADVANCE a stamp past a proven turnover is still exactly one — `uidValidityResetStampFreshEpoch` — and every other value-writer is bootstrap-only. **This was a stale count, not a defect.** The stamp **does** advance past a proven turnover, and the instruction *"Do not close T4.S6's row by pointing at T1.3"* is spent with it: T4.S6 closed on its own evidence, not by this row's leave. **(b) Everything else here is CLOSED as the deliberate design.** The decision: an IMAP/iCloud gesture is refused when its folder has no positively-known epoch, or when the row's `observedUidValidity` disagrees with the folder's live one. That is ADR-IOS-069's fourth never-drop exit and the owner's stated ranking — C3 outranks everything and failing closed is always acceptable. All five carve-outs above are load-bearing and none may be widened or "simplified": provider-scoped to `.imap`/`.icloud` (widening to *"the column is nil"* permanently bricks every Gmail and Exchange account), the demo account excluded by `DemoSeed.demoAccountId`, a missing `Folder` row failing CLOSED, the draft guards classified by what the provider DOES with the id, and the synthesized-epoch proposal rejected by the owner (2026-07-30). **Accepted cost:** a gesture on a folder whose epoch is not yet established is a silent no-op, and a queued op whose folder's epoch PROVABLY turns over is dropped (`IOS-ACTION-002`). **Recoverability — and this belongs at the head of the row rather than buried at its end:** the ordinary case recovers with one repeated gesture once the folder finishes its first sync. **The state where recovery does NOT fire** is an RFC-less population in a folder that already holds rows: `SyncEngine.bootstrapFolderUidValidity`'s `NOT EXISTS (messageHeader …)` term is false and `verifyAndBootstrapPrePopulatedFolderEpoch` returns `.unobservable` on an all-RFC-less sample, so neither bootstrap door can run and only an unrelated future RFC-bearing message in that folder unlocks it. That is `IOS-AI-003`'s state; `403496b21` closed its billing consequence and left the GESTURE side open **by design**, which is what this row carries. **Editorial debt, recorded rather than paid here:** this cell is ~4,000 words of accreted audit history and is the single largest contributor to a register far over any reasonable index budget. Per `../CLAUDE.md` § *Hierarchy discipline* the decision belongs in `DECISIONS.md`/`Companion/` with a one-line keyword-bearing pointer here. **Nothing may be deleted — it must be ROUTED**, by the `companion-compact` skill, as its own task: this pass was scoped to `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` alone and touched neither `DECISIONS.md` nor `Companion/`. |
| IOS-ACTION-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — owner-approved one-time upgrade loss, now IMMUTABLE | Action queue; `pendingOperation`; migration `v74`; blanket purge; upgrade; legacy op | Migration `v74_purgeLegacyPendingOperations` runs `DELETE FROM pendingOperation` with no predicate. Any action op queued by a pre-v3 build and not yet drained at upgrade is gone: it removes every legacy row regardless of type, status, decodability or shape, including `.saveDraft` and `.deleteDraft`. Nothing user-authored is touched — `PendingOperation` owns no authored bytes and has no cascade into `Draft`, `Outbox`, `MessageBody` or attachment storage, so draft identity/body/recipients/attachments and Outbox sends remain byte-identical. A dropped `.saveDraft` loses only the automatic server-push intention (the local content stays; the user saves again); a dropped `.deleteDraft` may leave a stale server draft that sync shows again, which is safer than retaining an obsolete destructive address. Startup still runs Outbox reconciliation independently. See ADR-IOS-071. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — an owner-approved one-time upgrade boundary that is now IMMUTABLE and therefore no longer revisable.** Verified at the tip: `migrator.registerTimedMigration("v74_purgeLegacyPendingOperations") { db in try db.execute(sql: "DELETE FROM pendingOperation") }`, carrying the comment *"Owner-approved C6 upgrade boundary"*. **Basis, from this codebase rather than from principle:** the alternative at the time was to decode each legacy row and validate its address space against a keying scheme v3 replaced — precisely the "reconstruct an address the system was already told and discarded" pattern that `MIS-IOS-003` and `CLAUDE.md`'s § *THE ADDRESS PROBLEM* forbid. **And the decision cannot be re-opened:** the migration has run on every device that launched the branch, so Data Integrity Rule 5 freezes its name AND its body; narrowing it now would either silently not execute (append) or re-run the whole body and crash at launch (rename). Any future change must be a new `vNN`. **Accepted cost:** un-drained pre-v3 action ops are gone at the upgrade instant. **Why the cost is bounded — re-verified, not inherited:** `PendingOperation` owns no authored bytes and no FK runs from `Draft`, `Outbox`, `MessageBody` or attachment storage into `pendingOperation`, so draft identity/body/recipients/attachments and Outbox sends are byte-identical across the migration. **Recoverability:** every lost op's target is still visible after the next sync, so the user re-issues the gesture. **A state where the user cannot re-issue it was searched for and none exists** — which is the whole reason the boundary was acceptable to the owner. |
| IOS-ACTION-002 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — not a deferral at all; this is the STATEMENT of ADR-IOS-069's fourth never-drop exit | UIDVALIDITY; provider id reset; queued op; drop; rebind; quarantine; C2; C4; C5 | On a UIDVALIDITY change or a provider stable-id reset, every queued op that named an address in the affected space is **dropped** — not rebound, not re-resolved, not re-searched, not quarantined. The user repeats the gesture; sync reconciles the visible state. **This row exists precisely because the behaviour is a deliberate carve-out of "never drop user intention", not an accident:** the invariant still holds in full on the ordinary path (offline, retry, app kill, provider error, transient read failure) and only the id-reset boundary is carved out. It never extends past queue state — Outbox sends, drafts, bodies, attachments and FTS content are never dropped under it, and double-send prevention is unchanged. Owner: *"much better than a fragile and complicated codebase."* See ADR-IOS-069. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — this row is not a deferral; it is the STATEMENT of exit 4, and it kept reading as an outstanding task only because a decision was living in a deferral register.** `tabmail-ios/CLAUDE.md` § *Core Philosophy* now carries it normatively (*"Exit 4 does not widen clause 2 … Exit 4 is the only exit that is a failure, it is deliberately narrow, and nothing else may use it"*), and it is enforced at three points in code: checkpoint A's positive-mismatch delete arm, `opIsProvenInvalidatedByReset` inside `uidValidityResetStampFreshEpoch`, and `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets`' epoch equality. **The decision, restated so it cannot be mistaken for a bug:** a **proven** id reset drops every queued op that named an address in that space, because v3 compares an op against its DURABLE `PendingOperation.observedUidValidity` — once the epoch provably moves, every retry of that op fails identically and forever, so executing it would mean executing under numbering it never observed (C3). An **unknown** epoch is an absence of evidence and stays retryable forever; the two are disjoint and exit 4 must never be widened to cover the second. **Accepted cost:** the user's queued gesture is dropped and they repeat it. **Recoverability:** sync reconciles the visible state. The state where that fails would be one in which the message is no longer addressable after the reset — but the reset reaction purges and resyncs the folder, so post-reaction every surviving message carries a fresh, stamped address. **None found.** The drop never extends past queue state: Outbox sends, drafts, bodies, attachments and FTS content are untouched, and double-send prevention is unchanged. **Editorial note:** the substance belongs in `DECISIONS.md` under ADR-IOS-069 with a one-line searchable pointer here; that routing is a separate task, and this pass wrote only `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`. |
| IOS-DATE-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — deliberate last-resort fallback; byte-identical to v1.6.38; the file's *Mandatory final review guard* was AMENDED by this closure | IMAP; missing INTERNALDATE; authored Date fallback; Inbox display; sort | When an IMAP server omits `INTERNALDATE`, the existing parser may fall back to the message-authored `Date` header. Adding a LOCAL matcher on that prefix would be a fallback routine keyed on text the fork can change at any release, forbidden by global rule 4 and fragile in exactly the way this register complains about elsewhere. **The point that makes it a decision rather than a deferral: failing toward "possibly succeeded" needs no discrimination at all**, so the string dependency is never load-bearing for correctness — it would only ever buy a better log line. **Accepted cost:** a move that may or may not have landed, until sync reconciles both folders. **Recoverability:** reconciliation needs both folders sync-reachable. A custom non-favourite folder is excluded from `syncableFolders`, but `IOS-EPOCH-001`'s round-8 correction establishes that on-demand navigation reaches ANY folder (`InboxView.onAppear` → `InboxViewModel.startSync()` → `AccountManager.syncFolders(_:)`, filtering on `!folder.path.isEmpty` alone), so reconciliation is at worst deferred until the user opens the folder — one ordinary gesture. **No permanent non-recovery state found.** <br><br>🚨 **CORRECTED AND FIXED 2026-08-05. THE DECISION ABOVE IS KEPT; ITS RATIONALE IS REPLACED, because two of the premises it rested on are falsified at the resolved fork revision `078a09b8`.** Per `MIS-019`, a false justification is worse than none — it invites reversal of a correct instruction — so both are named rather than quietly overwritten. **FALSIFIED PREMISE (a): "v3 does not retry the destructive half and drops the op for sync to reconcile."** The code did the OPPOSITE. `let copyEvidence = try await server.copy(messages:to:)` had **no `catch`**, so the throw propagated out of `IMAPProvider.move` to the generic arm of `AccountManagerQueue.executeSingleOp`, which sets `status = queued`, increments `retryCount`, adds the account to `failedAccounts` and returns `.haltLane`. Nothing was dropped and nothing was left to sync: the throw happens BEFORE the `STORE \Deleted`, so the source is untouched, the next drain re-runs A1/A2/A3 and issues **another `UID COPY`** — one more duplicate at the destination per drain, forever, while the op never retires and the lane stays halted. Durable actions retry without a cap. **FALSIFIED PREMISE (b): "genuine and malformed failures share `IMAPError.commandFailed`, separated only by a message prefix."** Not at `078a09b8`. `CopyHandler` **overrides** `handleTaggedErrorResponse` to fail with `IMAPError.copyFailed`, so a genuine tagged NO/BAD is `.copyFailed`; a timeout is `.timeout`; a dropped connection is `.connectionFailed`. The malformed case is `.commandFailed`, raised by `CopyUID.init(nio:)`'s three throw sites (cardinality mismatch, invalid UID range, >1,000,000-UID expansion). **They are different enum cases, so the discrimination this row said could only be string-based is available BY TYPE** — which is what makes the fix admissible under the standing no-string-matcher rule rather than a violation of it. **THE FIX:** catch `IMAPError.commandFailed` **only** around the `server.copy(...)` call and map it to `copyEvidence = nil`, routing it into the already-designed, already-audited no-evidence path — the same path a server that withholds `COPYUID` entirely takes. That path authorizes at most the REVERSIBLE `\Deleted` STORE (tagged OK ANDed with `liveSourceUIDs`) and makes the irreversible `UID EXPUNGE` **structurally unreachable**, because `copyProvenUIDs` is empty ⇒ `purgeAuthorizedUIDs` is empty. No new durable state, no new terminal exit, no new exit from the queue, no destination `SEARCH` (banned — ADR-IOS-068/D4, `IOS-IMAP-002`). ⚠️ **THE CATCH IS DELIBERATELY NARROW AND MUST NOT BE WIDENED (`MIS-005` mirror image):** catching bare `IMAPError` or a bare `catch` would swallow `.copyFailed` / `.timeout` / `.connectionFailed` and convert a copy that provably did NOT happen into "it may have landed", soft-deleting sources for copies that never occurred. **Pinned by** `NeverDropExitClosureTests.anUnparseableCopyUidMoveIsNeverReCopied`, which asserts the count of `UID COPY` commands on the WIRE across two drains (1, not 2) via a new `FakeIMAPServer.reportCopyUIDWithCardinalityMismatch()` seam — never on a flag or an error type. ⚠️ **ONE PREMISE OF THE FIX IS ITSELF NARROWER THAN THE CODE, and it is recorded rather than assumed:** it is NOT true that `COPYUID` parsing is the only producer of `.commandFailed` inside `server.copy`. `CapabilityHandler.handleTaggedErrorResponse` also raises `.commandFailed` and is reachable through `IMAPServer.executeCommand`'s lazy re-auth and `IMAPConnection.executeCommandBody`'s reconnect, both of which call `refreshCapabilities` → `CAPABILITY`. It needs a server that answers `CAPABILITY` with tagged NO/BAD **and** omits `[CAPABILITY]` from both greeting and LOGIN OK, inside the window between two adjacent `await`s. The disposition is the same either way — no evidence, no expunge reachable, at most a reversible `\Deleted` — so the fix is correct, but **the structural fix belongs upstream in SwiftMail's `CopyHandler`**, which has the tagged response in hand and should distinguish "tagged OK + unparseable COPYUID" from every other `.commandFailed` (owner's PR, per `feedback_swiftmail_pr_upstream`). **`v1.6.38` did not have this defect** — it probed the destination by RFC 822 Message-ID first and took the cleanup-only branch on finding the landed copy — so this was a v3 REGRESSION, introduced when that banned `SEARCH` probe was removed without a replacement for the committed-but-unparseable case. |
| IOS-GRAPH-001 | Accepted recoverable edge; **also a base defect**; ✅ **FIXED by `592f7202f`** — ⚠️ and this row's word *"read-only"* was ASPIRATIONAL, not a fact, until that commit made it one | Outlook NSE; `Shared/API/GraphAPI`; opaque Graph id; notification enrichment; raw path segment; `graphPathSegmentAllowed`; `encodedGraphPathSegment`; `GraphAPI.patch`; `GraphAPI.move`; deliberately read-only | The NSE's separate read-only `GraphAPI` metadata/body helper still interpolates opaque message and attachment ids without strict path-segment encoding. A reserved-character id can make notification enrichment fail closed to a bare notification; it performs no provider mutation, and ordinary main-app sync restores the message surface. The main-app `ExchangeProvider` path class is encoded (`f07229f37`). This is **not** a post-`v1.6.38` regression — the base has it too; changing the NSE helper remains a separately bounded follow-up. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `592f7202f`, "Encode Graph path segments in the shared client; drop its mutators".** **The invariant that now holds:** a Graph id containing `/`, `?`, `#`, `+` or `=` cannot alter the route the request selects. `GraphAPI.graphPathSegmentAllowed` (the RFC 3986 unreserved set) and `GraphAPI.encodedGraphPathSegment(_:)` are the single implementation, applied at `messageMetadata`, `messageFull` and **BOTH** segments of `attachment`. ⚠️ **THE CENSUS WAS FIVE SITES, NOT THE TWO ID KINDS THIS ROW NAMED** — `messageMetadata`, `messageFull`, `attachment` (two segments), `patch` and `move`. **And this row described the helper as "read-only" when it was not:** `GraphAPI.patch` and `GraphAPI.move` were live mutator methods. A census of `GraphAPI.` across the whole tree found **both had ZERO callers on every target**, so they are **DELETED**, and **this surface is now DELIBERATELY read-only — do not "restore" them.** That removes the NSE's entire provider-mutation surface and makes this row's "read-only" description TRUE rather than merely intended: the extension enriches a delivered notification, and every provider mutation in this app goes through the durable `PendingOperation` queue in the main app, which the extension process cannot drain. The note left at the deletion site requires any future reintroduction to encode every id. **The encoder was MOVED DOWN, not duplicated** — `ExchangeProvider` (main-app only) authored it, and `Shared` is linked by the app AND the NSE, so it had to move down for the extension to reach it; the main app's private `graphPathSegmentAllowed` is deleted and its ~25 call sites are behaviourally identical. ⚠️ **THE REMAINING FOUR-LINE WRAPPER IN `ExchangeProvider` IS NOT REDUNDANT — DO NOT DELETE IT.** Lifting the whole throwing helper into `Shared` does not compile: it throws `ProviderError.invalidURL`, and `ProviderError` is declared in `TabMail/Providers/EmailProvider.swift`, which is main-app-only and not visible from `Shared`. The shared helper therefore returns `String?` and each target keeps its own throw; the allowed set and the percent-encoding call exist exactly once and only the error domain is per-target. **A1:** `git show 07a4bb703:Shared/API/GraphAPI.swift` is byte-identical at all five sites and the shipped `ExchangeProvider` contains neither helper — the shipped architecture is NONEXISTENT, not inapplicable, and its raw interpolation is a weakness treated as a floor and not inherited. **Tests pin the INVARIANT, not the mechanism:** they assert the COMPOSED URL STRING that reached the HTTP boundary for all three live helpers including both `attachment` segments — a test asserting merely that an encoder was called would stay green if it were applied to the wrong segment or its result discarded. Red proof: the three live sites reverted to raw interpolation gave 4 failures against 4 passes, with the hostile id `AA/BB?CC#DD+EE=FF` splitting the path, injecting a second query and injecting a fragment, while all three "ordinary id is not escaped" controls stayed GREEN. |
| IOS-UNDO-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — tail 1 closed as a decision; **tail 2 RETRACTED** — its premise is FALSE at the tip | Archive/delete; mark-read; Undo; rapid read-unread-archive; notification read; event ordering | Two rare tails stay deliberately simple. First, a rapid explicit read→unread→archive burst within one fold/retry window can let the older fold completion clear the newer archive ownership, so Undo may return the message read; avoiding that for Inbox, Detail, Settings bulk, and mark-all as one class requires a new per-identity event ledger. Second, warm/cold notification read actions do not participate in the UI-local Undo invalidation protocol, so a rare notification-action/read race may retain parent behavior — **tail 2 is carried forward UNVERIFIED against the v3 base and needs fresh investigation; no plan row owns it.** Neither tail can mutate a different message, lose message content, or wedge durable work; the user can correct read state manually. Do not broaden notification or durable queue identity merely to guarantee these rare tap/order cases. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04). TAIL 2 IS RETRACTED — the fresh investigation this row asked for is what falsified it.** The retracted claim — *"warm/cold notification read actions do not participate in the UI-local Undo invalidation protocol, so a rare notification-action/read race may retain parent behavior"* — is kept above rather than deleted, so the error stays searchable. **There is no such protocol and nothing for a read action to fail to participate in:** `UndoService.UndoableActionType` has exactly ONE case, `move(fromPath:toPath:)`, so read state is never undoable, and the only `UndoService.shared` call sites are ten `push` sites, every one a destructive-move gesture. A row whose owning mechanism does not exist is not a deferral. **TAIL 1 IS CLOSED AS A DECISION, not deferred.** The decision: the read→unread→archive coalescing window (`AccountManager.registerGestureIntent` / `executeIntentCycle`, ADR-IOS-057) stays simple. **The basis is this row's own stated price** — *"a new per-identity event ledger"* covering Inbox, Detail, Settings bulk and mark-all as one class — which is exactly the "new mechanism for a minor rare edge" THE MANTRA forbids, and on which audit rule A3's tell fires (a mechanism whose purpose is to re-derive ownership a sibling path already cleared). **Accepted cost:** after an undo the message can be back in the Inbox showing as read. **Recoverability:** one ordinary gesture — swipe or tap unread. **A state where that gesture is itself blocked was searched for:** the candidates are `IOS-EPOCH-001`'s admission refusals, and they block the *durable* op rather than the local read flip, and they block the archive gesture that created the situation equally — so "the archive succeeded but the unread gesture cannot even be attempted" does not arise. **None found.** **One thing a future reader must not misread as a defect:** v3 deliberately does not restore read state on undo at all — `UndoMember` captures no `isRead`, and `AccountManagerActions.undoMove`'s restore loop is a field-level `updateAll` whose own comment says *"Preserve every unrelated field (notably current read/flag state); never `save` a stale snapshot or recreate a missing row."* Shipped `07a4bb703`'s `undoDestructiveAction` did the opposite, writing the whole gesture-time snapshot back and clobbering every concurrent change made since the gesture. **Do not "restore shipped" here** — per A1 corollary 3 the release is a floor, not a ceiling, and this divergence is the improvement. |
| IOS-OUTBOX-001 | Pre-existing; was byte-identical to v1.6.38; ✅ **FIXED by `792048ebd`** — ⚠️ and it was WORSE than this row stated | Outbox; attachment reconstruction; provider failure; status-write exhaustion; `sending`; `sentAt`; relaunch | If attachment reconstruction or a provider failure is followed by exhaustion of the existing retrying status write, the exact row can remain `sending` with `sentAt == nil` until `reconcileOutbox()` resets it on the next process launch. The durable message and attachments remain present; no provider send is silently treated as complete. A generic same-process reset cannot safely distinguish the pre-I/O case from the post-I/O/two-generals boundary and could double-send, so this shipped rare edge remains relaunch-recoverable rather than adding another send-state protocol. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `792048ebd`, "Recover a stranded send in-session and stop shadowing the wake target". ⚠️ THE ROW ABOVE UNDERSTATED IT: the edge was not "relaunch-recoverable", it was unrecoverable FOR THE WHOLE SESSION.** `reconcileOutbox()` had exactly ONE trigger — `RootView`'s launch `.task`, via `reconcilePendingOperations()`. A row left `.sending` with `sentAt == nil` is invisible to `drainOutbox` (which selects `.queued` only) and `OutboxView` offers the user no gesture for it (Retry is gated on `.failed`; Outbox Rule 10 forbids discarding a `sending` row). So the user could foreground and background the app all day and the message would never send and never fail — a dropped user intention by the **wedge corollary**, escapable only by a full process relaunch. **The invariant that now holds:** a stranded `.sending`/`sentAt == nil` row becomes claimable again on every foreground, not only on process launch. `reconcileOutboxOnForeground()` is wired to the existing `scenePhase → .active` branch in `RootView`, beside the foreground sync trigger. **The reconciliation logic itself is byte-unchanged** — the new trigger calls `reconcileOutbox()` UNCHANGED — so the `sentAt`-before-delete double-send firewall (Outbox Rule 3) travels with the new trigger exactly as it does with the launch trigger, and a reader must **not** conclude the firewall was re-implemented: a row carrying `sentAt` is finalized or handed to Sent-append recovery, and only `sentAt == nil` rows are reset. The `isDrainingOutbox` guard is load-bearing rather than decoration — `drainOutbox` is serial, holds the flag for the whole drain, and a census confirmed its loop is `sendSingleOutboxMessage`'s ONLY production caller, so `isDrainingOutbox == false` proves no provider send is in flight in this process and the `.sending → .queued` reset cannot make a row re-claimable while its SMTP transaction is on the wire. ⚠️ **THAT LAST SENTENCE WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED IN PLACE, NOT DELETED — see `IOS-OUTBOX-006`.** Every clause of it about `drainOutbox` is true; what does not follow is the conclusion, because the guard READ the latch and then `await`ed. `reconcileOutbox`'s first statement is an `await dbPool.read`, `AccountManager` is an actor, and that read runs a full NSE staging merge before it reads — so the actor was released, a drain could start, claim a row and put its SMTP on the wire, and the reset then landed on that row from a snapshot taken AFTER the claim. `isDrainingOutbox == false` at the guard says nothing about `isDrainingOutbox` at the write. The latch is now ACQUIRED, in the same synchronous run as the check and held across the reset. **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, stated by that commit and recorded here as accepted rather than fixed:** `reconcileOutbox()` still writes its `.sending → .queued` reset as a **blind UPDATE keyed on id alone**, so a stale snapshot could in principle overwrite a status another path wrote in the same pass. That shape is pre-existing and identical at `v1.6.38` and on the launch trigger; it **cannot** re-queue a `sentAt`-bearing row, and it self-corrects in one attempt because `retryCount` is preserved. Narrowing it to the compare-and-swap fence `requeueClaimedOutboxMessage` already uses would be a change to reconciliation logic, which that commit deliberately did not make. **A1:** `07a4bb703` carries the identical launch-only `reconcileOutbox()`, so the shipped release does not solve this and the fix is authored against the floor. |
| IOS-OUTBOX-002 | Pre-existing; was byte-identical to v1.6.38; ✅ **FIXED by `792048ebd`**; a **blessing test** was found and re-scoped; ⚠️ **one clause of that closure was TOO STRONG and is corrected below — the sibling mechanism is `IOS-OUTBOX-005`** | Outbox; future hold; `holdUntil`; wake timer; queued row; incidental drain; post-loop wake query; `AccountManager.earliestFutureHoldWakeTarget(now:db:)` | A genuinely future-held queued row can be shadowed by an earlier past-held or legacy-NULL row when the wake query examines only the first ordered row, so no timer is armed for the future row. The durable send is not lost: a later ordinary foreground/provider-sync drain sends it. Keep the live expected-issue pin as the regression oracle, but do not widen Outbox scheduling during this release train. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `792048ebd`.** **The invariant that now holds:** the post-loop wake query returns the earliest **genuinely future** hold, so a future-held row can no longer be shadowed by a NULL or already-elapsed one and left with no timer armed. The old query ordered `holdUntil ASC` with no future-hold predicate, and SQLite sorts NULL first under ASC while a past instant sorts before a future one, so `fetchOne` returned the wrong row, the caller's `hold > Date()` re-check failed, and no timer was set even though a future-held row existed behind it. The query is now `AccountManager.earliestFutureHoldWakeTarget(now:db:)`. **It NARROWS the timer query and widens nothing:** rows with a NULL or elapsed hold are already handled by the drain loop that runs above the timer, and only `.queued` is considered, so Outbox Rule 8 (`.failed` needs an explicit user Retry) is untouched. **A THIRD BLESSING TEST WAS FOUND AND RE-SCOPED, NOT DELETED** — recorded here because this row's defect is the one it blessed. `ComposeThrottleTests.PostLoopWakeUpQueryTests.guardAgainstPastHold` asserted `earliest?.id == "past"` and `shouldSchedule == false` **with a future-held row in the same table**: it encoded this defect as the expected behaviour. It could only do so because the suite ran against a **REPLICA** of the query written out in its own helper — and a replica cannot go red on a defect in the original. The helper now calls the production function, which makes every case in that suite an assertion about the app rather than about itself; the old expectation is replaced by the invariant it violated, and the previous display name is recorded inline. This is the same class as the blessing tests re-scoped for `IOS-DRAFT-009` (round 3), `IOS-ROUND3-D6` (`6b689890d`, three of them) and `IOS-TEST-005` (`459786db1`) — and the mechanism here, *a test asserting against a copy of production rather than production*, is the one worth carrying forward: it makes a blessing test undetectable by red-proof. **A1:** `07a4bb703` carries the identical unfiltered `.order(Column("holdUntil").asc)` query, so the fix is authored against the floor rather than restored. <br><br>⚠️ **ONE CLAUSE OF THE CLOSURE ABOVE WAS TOO STRONG — corrected in place, not deleted, because a register that overstates its own closure is a recorded defect class here.** The *query* half is true and unretracted (`AccountManager.earliestFutureHoldWakeTarget(now:db:)` does return the earliest genuinely future hold, and a NULL or elapsed row can no longer shadow it). What was too strong is the outcome clause *"…and left with no timer armed"*: that end state stayed reachable by a **different mechanism** — the caller's `hold > Date()` re-check runs at a FRESH instant with an `await dbPool.read` in between, so a deadline that elapsed during that read was future at selection and past at the re-check, and the guard fell through and armed nothing. Closed by `1a1962207` and registered separately as **`IOS-OUTBOX-005`**. **The two are cross-referenced rather than merged** — same symptom class (a held row ends a drain with no autonomous wake), different mechanisms (query ORDERING versus a two-instant handoff race), and **neither fix would have closed the other** — which is the same treatment `IOS-TEST-006` and `IOS-TEST-008` were given, for the same reason. |
| IOS-TLS-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — v3 does not restrict the offered cipher list; revisit condition made concrete | TLS; cipher suites; IMAP; SMTP; NIOSSL; CBC-SHA1; 3DES; forward secrecy; legacy servers | The SwiftMail sync raises the IMAP/SMTP floor from TLS 1.0 to **TLS 1.2** (accepted by the owner 2026-07-30). That closes the broken protocol *versions*, but at 1.2 the cipher suite does the real work and NIOSSL's default list still offers CBC-SHA1 groups — which is precisely why TLS 1.0 was negotiating before. Restricting the offered ciphers (AEAD-only: AES-GCM / ChaCha20-Poly1305, ECDHE for forward secrecy) is a separate, smaller lever with its own compatibility risk. Owner's stated reservation (2026-07-30): *"Not sure if we want it though given servers may be broken configs."* TabMail connects to arbitrary user-configured servers, and a badly-configured or elderly one may offer only a weak suite, so tightening the list would silently disconnect those users exactly as a version-floor bump would. **Do NOT tighten the cipher list as part of the v3 refactor** — it is unrelated to the action-queue defect and needs its own compatibility testing against real servers. Revisit only with evidence about which suites TabMail's actual account population negotiates. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — v3 does not restrict the offered cipher list; NIOSSL's defaults stand at the TLS 1.2 floor.** **Verified at the tip:** there is no TLS configuration in `TabMail/` or `Shared/` at all — the floor lives in the pinned SwiftMail fork (`project.yml` pins `github.com/TabMail/SwiftMail` by `branch: main`). The app's only TLS-adjacent code is `IMAPProvider.useTLS` (a test-only override, nil in production, so SwiftMail infers from the port) and the `.tlsFailed` arm of `AccountManagerOutbox.isTransientSendError`. So this is a decision about a dependency's defaults, not an unfixed TabMail defect. **Basis:** the owner already answered it for this release, with the reasoning stated — *"Not sure if we want it though given servers may be broken configs."* TabMail connects to arbitrary user-configured servers, and a badly-configured or elderly one may offer only a weak suite, so tightening the offered list would silently disconnect those users exactly as a version-floor bump does — while, **unlike** the version floor, buying no clear security boundary: TLS 1.2 with a CBC-SHA1 suite is weak, not broken. **Accepted cost:** sessions that negotiate CBC-SHA1 or a non-forward-secret suite at TLS 1.2 continue to do so. **Recoverability is not the relevant test here** and saying so is the honest answer: nothing is refused or deferred by this decision, so there is no fail-closed edge to recover from — the cost is a weaker-than-ideal session, never a lost or misdirected message. **The revisit condition, made concrete because a vague one is how a row like this survives forever:** the evidence gate this row names — *which suites TabMail's actual account population negotiates* — **cannot be discharged inside this repo.** It requires telemetry from real user connections, which the zero-retention policy (ADR-004) makes a separate product decision in its own right. So: revisit only with negotiated-suite evidence from the real account population, **and if the list is ever tightened, `IOS-TLS-002`'s clear-actionable-error requirement applies to the new failure mode too** — a user disconnected by a cipher restriction must be told that, not shown a generic connection failure. |
| IOS-TLS-002 | Accepted user-visible consequence of the TLS 1.2 floor; its stated REQUIREMENT was UNMET at the tip and is ✅ **FIXED by `b150f7ee6`** | TLS 1.0; TLS 1.1; legacy IMAP server; self-hosted; connect failure; error message | A server that cannot negotiate TLS 1.2 no longer connects. This is a **minimum**, not a pin — any server supporting 1.2/1.3 is unaffected, and modern servers already negotiate 1.3. Affected servers are genuinely unsafe (a network attacker on a TLS 1.0 session can realistically reach mail credentials). Requirement: the failure must surface as a **clear, actionable error naming the TLS floor**, never a silent or generic connection failure. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `b150f7ee6`, "Name the TLS floor when a server is refused for being below it". The requirement above was a REQUIREMENT, and it was UNMET at the tip** — no TLS-floor string existed anywhere in `TabMail/`, `Shared/` or the NSE, so both halves of the promise were broken: the user got `"The operation couldn't be completed. (NIOSSL.NIOSSLError error 0.)"`, which names nothing and suggests nothing while describing a PERMANENT condition no retry can clear, and on the send path `isTransientSendError` read the connection/TLS shape as transient, so a message addressed to a server that can never accept it stayed `.queued` indefinitely with none of the Retry/Discard agency Outbox Rules 7/9 promise. **The invariant that now holds:** a below-floor server produces `IMAPTransportSecurityError.tlsFloorNotMet(host:)` at both connect sites this provider owns (`createServer`'s IMAP connect and `send(draft:)`'s SMTP connect), rendering the same human sentence on both surfaces — it is `CustomStringConvertible` *and* `LocalizedError`, because `OutboxView` prints `errorMessage` (stored as `String(describing:)`) while the account-connect views render `userFacingDescription` — and `isTransientSendError` classifies it explicitly and FIRST, so the send stops silently retrying and the actionable message reaches the Outbox row. <br><br>🔎 **THE MATCHER IS DELIBERATELY ONE TOKEN WIDE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING — not an implementation shortcut.** The failure shapes were **measured**, against local `openssl s_server` endpoints, rather than guessed: a TLS 1.0-only server and a TLS 1.1-only server both yield `handshakeFailed(…sslError…TLSV1_ALERT_PROTOCOL_VERSION…)` — **and so does a TLS 1.3 server presenting an untrusted certificate**, arriving in the byte-identical `handshakeFailed(...sslError...)` wrapper and differing ONLY in the BoringSSL reason token (`CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED`). So the outer shape is not the discriminator: a `handshakeFailed`-or-error-domain matcher would have told a user whose certificate merely expired that **their server is too old**. `IMAPProvider.mapTransportSecurityFailure` therefore matches `TLSV1_ALERT_PROTOCOL_VERSION` and nothing else, in the rendered description rather than by importing NIOSSL so a re-wrapped rethrow still carries it — the technique `SyncEngine.isConnectionError` already uses for NIO's error families — and every unrecognised shape is returned **UNCHANGED**. Fail-to-generic is the deliberate direction: recognising too little leaves a send retrying, recognising too much labels a recoverable failure permanent and stops a send that would have gone through. <br><br>⚠️ **THE DELIBERATE NON-COVERAGE, STATED because an absolute needs its negative case (MIS-019):** a below-floor server that answers a version mismatch by returning a **lower ServerHello** instead of a `protocol_version` alert produces a different BoringSSL reason (`UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL`). **That case was not reproducible here, so it is NOT matched** and keeps its existing generic, retryable treatment rather than being matched on an unverified string. A user on such a server still gets the generic failure this row's requirement forbids. That is accepted: guessing the token would risk mapping something else onto the TLS-floor sentence, and this row's whole point is that the wrong confident message is worse than a vague one. Also accepted: the version named in the sentence tracks SwiftMail's `MailTLSMinimumVersion` default (`.tlsv12`), so if that default moves the sentence must move with it — a comment at the string says so. |
| IOS-PERF-001 | ~~**OPEN — the one deliberately-open row**~~ → ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — the runtime half is MEASURED and LANDED — **measured THREE TIMES**, and all three runs are recorded below (`GATE 77185` on `33ea05b82`: **7** waits; `GATE 42401` on `9443e8a9c`: **5** waits; `GATE fix-agent-gate 1977` on the final-audit candidate `b19dd167c`: **10** waits; **none candidate-attributable in any of the three**) — so the three owed items are recorded and **the register now has ZERO rows owing work**. The OPEN statement below is kept as history, not as a live finding. ⚠️ *deliberately*: since 2026-08-04 `IOS-TEST-009` is also 🔓 OPEN, but it was **opened by a finding, not deferred by a decision**, and it is TEST-INFRASTRUCTURE with no product-invariant impact; this row remains the only one carrying **owed work**.
