THE COUNTS AND FAMILY NAMES RECORDED IN THIS ROW ARE THE DURABLE RECORD.** If the files are gone, the row still stands on its own; re-deriving them means re-running the gate, which will produce its own timing-dependent count and must be recorded as a **fourth** run rather than silently replacing these. **Run 3 is why this row does not treat a re-run as a correction:** the gate was NOT re-run to obtain a nicer number, and any future run that produces a lower count does not retroactively make 10 wrong. <br><br>⚠️ **CORRECTED AGAIN 2026-08-05 — THE CENSUS INSTRUMENT ITSELF WAS TOO NARROW, AND ITS "ONE CANDIDATE-ATTRIBUTABLE SITE" CONCLUSION IS WRONG. This is a `MIS-007` recurrence: the census inherited the shape of its instrument.** Both instruments above read the **LINE**, never the **WORK**: (a) `git blame` of the `Task.detached(priority:)` line plus `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, and (b) a corroborating **site count** of `Task.detached(priority: .utility|.low|.background)` over `TabMail/`, `Shared/` and `TabMailNotificationService/` (11 → 12, "delta exactly +1"). **A site whose `Task.detached` line is OLD but whose CALLEE WAS WIDENED ONTO A DIFFERENT POOL INSIDE THE RANGE is invisible to both by construction** — the line never moves, so `git blame` still names a pre-release commit; the site count never moves, so the delta still reads +1. **Exactly two such sites exist and the census missed both.** **(1) `SyncEngineFTS.removeHeadersFromFTS`** — at `07a4bb703` its detached body called `SearchIndex.shared.removeMessages`, which touches only `SearchIndex`'s **private FTS pool**; at the candidate it calls `MessageContentStore.releaseUnowned(…, pool: pool)`, which does reads and a `DELETE FROM messageBody` on the **MAIN** pool (`dbPool`/`syncPool`/`backgroundPool` all wrap the same `rawPool`, and `PrioritizedDatabase.priority` is the write-queue TIER, not a `TaskPriority` — trap (1) above). Six production callers, every one an ordinary primary path: each delta sync, full sync and deletion-reconcile pass. **(2) `SyncEngineMaintenance.runPruneIfOverBudget`'s chunk release** — the same widening, weaker case, since its enclosing function already touched the main pool at QoS 17 at shipped, so only the contention with its **own delete loop** is new. ✅ **BOTH FIXED 2026-08-05: `.utility` → `.medium` at the two call sites, nothing else changed** — the same shape as `955233fde` and as the `NSEDataBridge.mergeInboxRemovals` fix above. **⇒ The sentence "THE ONE CANDIDATE-ATTRIBUTABLE SITE IS `NSEDataBridge.mergeInboxRemovals`" is SUPERSEDED: there were THREE, and `NSEDataBridge.mergeInboxRemovals` was merely the only one the instrument could see.** The row stays CLOSED because all three are now fixed, **not** because no others existed. ⚠️ **The durable rule, so the next census does not repeat it:** when the question is *which sites hold one of our pools at the wrong QoS*, the unit of attribution is the **reachable work**, not the source line — the census must be taken over the transitive callee set as it exists at **both** ends of the range, and a `git blame` / site-count instrument may only CORROBORATE, never BOUND. The static half's enumeration (18 sites / 7 violations / 2 mechanism-only) is unaffected by this correction and is not re-opened. |

## Audit round 2 — commit-body corrections

**Commit bodies are immutable history and are NOT rewritten.** Where a landed commit's body claims
more than the code delivers, the correction is recorded here, naming the sha it corrects, so a
future reader who trusts that body finds the retraction next to it.

| Commit | What the body claims | The correction |
|---|---|---|
| `e8b4f44c5` | *"every producer"* admits through the provider-address predicate, and *"the gesture the user was shown is the gesture that runs"* | Both are **overstated**. Falsified by `IOS-OUTBOX-003` below — `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend` writes `isReplied`/`isForwarded` at queue time with no admission gate at all, so a gesture the user was shown can still not be the gesture that runs — and by `IOS-NSE-001`, where a positively epoch-proven staged row is projected into the main store as "unknown" and its gesture then refused. Read the claim as scoped to the producers that commit enumerated. This is the same universally-quantified-claim hazard as `ProviderNativeActionAdmissionTests`' *"Every ordinary IMAP producer …"*, which enumerates four. |
| `4e08ff720` | the `.noCopyUidEvidence` cost is bounded at *"one unproven duplicate at the destination per drain"* | The bound was **asserted, not enforced**: `drainPendingQueue` runs up to three passes and re-claims whatever is still queued, so a later pass could re-attempt the op and seat another copy — the COPY has already gone out when that error is raised. The bound is now true, and true *because* `DrainContext.evidenceRefused` makes the drain attempt each evidence-refused op at most once. The re-derived statement lives at the throw site. Note that the sibling **partial** path (`retirePartiallyCompletedOp`) is NOT covered by that set — it does not throw — and can re-copy the unproven members once per remaining pass. ⚠️ **Round 4:** both costs are now **unreachable rather than bounded** — the throw site is deleted (see the `15d97a628` row below) and `IMAPProvider.move` no longer returns a strict subset, so neither the duplicate-per-drain nor the duplicate-per-pass can be incurred by a move. The rows stay because the drain arms they describe are still live for other producers. ⚠️ **ROUND 5 NARROWS THAT ROUND-4 SENTENCE — the words *"unreachable rather than bounded"* and *"neither … can be incurred by a move"* are RETRACTED. The retracted text is kept above so the error stays searchable; see `IOS-IMAP-006` for the retraction in full.** What `ede315ed8` actually made unreachable is **those two specific producers**: `IMAPMoveEvidenceUnavailable.noCopyUidEvidence` is deleted, and `retirePartiallyCompletedOp` has no current producer because `IMAPProvider.move` now dispositions every member. **The CLASS is not unreachable.** `IMAPProvider.move` issues `server.copy(messages:to:)` and then performs further awaited work; ANY throw after that point requeues the op, and the next drain re-enters `move` from the top and re-issues the same `UID COPY` against a source copy that is still present — seating a second copy at the destination. `DrainContext.evidenceRefused` bounds that to at most one duplicate per DRAIN, which is precisely the bound this sentence claimed could no longer be incurred. Round 4 did not achieve nothing; it achieved less than the sentence claimed. |
| `15d97a628` | that the COPY's tagged OK is *"a POSITIVE statement that every message named in the command was copied"* (RFC 3501 §6.4.7), and that holding a UIDPLUS server to `COPYUID` is safe because a withheld response code *"can differ on the next attempt"* | **Both are wrong, and audit round 4 fixes the code rather than only the record.** (a) The §6.4.7 quotation is verbatim and real, but the command issued is a `UID COPY`, and §6.4.8 says *"a non-existent unique identifier is ignored without any error message generated. Thus, it is possible for a UID FETCH command to return an OK without any data or a UID COPY or UID STORE to return an OK without performing any operations."* An absent UID is therefore never one of *"the specified message(s)"* §6.4.7 restores, so the OK proves nothing about it — yet the source cleanup and the op's retirement were authorized for the whole requested set, letting an absent batch member ride out of the queue on a present sibling's OK. (b) RFC 4315 §3 does not make `COPYUID` a free choice the server can revisit: it makes the response code a SHOULD *"with limited exceptions, discussed below"* and then names two, at two different normative strengths — for a mailbox the client may COPY or APPEND to but not SELECT or EXAMINE the server *"SHOULD NOT send an APPENDUID or COPYUID response code as it would disclose information about the mailbox"*, and for a `UIDNOTSTICKY` mail store it *"MAY omit"* it *"as it is not meaningful"*. Both are properties of the MAILBOX, not of the attempt, so for those servers "next attempt" never comes — the same permanent wedge this commit had just removed from the non-UIDPLUS arm, still halting the lane and re-copying on every drain. What the commit got RIGHT and round 4 keeps: deleting the `supportsUIDPlus` capability guard, and the ranking that incomplete visible cleanup beats both a wedge and a mailbox-wide `EXPUNGE`. See `IOS-IMAP-004` for what is guaranteed now. |
| `209a55cdf` | *"They now throw."* of `purgeStagedStateForFolder(...)` and `purgeInboxRemovalMarkersForAccount(...)`, and *"each of these now satisfies C3"* | **They do not throw** — both return `Bool` — and the *"each of these now satisfies C3"* claim is **overstated** for that reason. ⚠ **THIS ROW'S OWN REASONING WAS INVERTED AND IS CORRECTED (audit round 3, PF-1).** It used to argue *"…carry no `@discardableResult`, **so** a caller that ignores the result compiles silently and learns nothing about a failed purge."* That is backwards: the ABSENCE of `@discardableResult` is precisely what makes ignoring a non-`Void` result emit *"Result of call to … is unused"*, and under this project's zero-production-warning gate a warning is a **build failure**. Ignoring the result is therefore not something that can reach `main`. Verified by census — all four call sites consume the value: `guard NSEDataBridge.purgeStagedStateForFolder(…) else` (`AccountManagerUidValidityReset.swift`), `!NSEDataBridge.purgeInboxRemovalMarkersForAccount(…)` (same file), and two `#expect`s in `UidValidityResetPurgeCompanionTests`. The row's correct parts stand; only the inverted clause is retracted. No behaviour is changed by this row — it corrects the record. |
| `94fac3e79` | that `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader(...)` Strategy 2 *"now asks whether its candidates AGREE about which message they are, instead of counting them"*, that the identity witness *"is correct regardless of which horn of the Gmail question holds, so the question never had to be settled"*, and that `IOS-DRAFT-009` is *"now pinned from both sides — agreeing copies must resolve, disagreeing candidates must refuse."* | **The whole fix was REVERTED in audit round 3 and the body's closing sentence is now inverted.** Agreeing copies do NOT resolve — they refuse, along with every other multi-row case, because the guard is `candidates.count == 1` under `.limit(2)` again and `Draft.swift`'s executable code was byte-identical to `cec7450e1` as of `3bd9f0bac`. (A round-4 cleanup has since deleted ONE function from that file — `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader`'s non-`db` convenience overload, orphaned when the reply-target send fix moved its last caller to the `db`-scoped one, and dead code under the global Code Quality rule 5. The file is therefore no longer byte-identical to `cec7450e1`; Strategy 2 and its cardinality guard are untouched and still are.) The `(fromAddress, subject, date)` witness was withdrawn as unsound in both directions: it omits the body, the attachments and `replyTo` — everything the resolver actually consumes — so two different messages colliding on a Message-ID can satisfy it and foreign content reaches the user's outgoing mail (**C3**); and its timestamp is INTERNALDATE, which RFC 3501 §6.4.7 makes a SHOULD-not-MUST across a COPY, so genuine copies can disagree on it. The Gmail multi-label question is therefore **not** settled and is open again on `IOS-DRAFT-009`. The commit's own pinning test was the blessing test that made the claim unfalsifiable — both fixtures carried the same body marker, so an arbitrary representative was undetectable; round 3 inverted it in place as `ReplyQuoteIdentityTests.severalCopiesOfOneMessageFailTheReplyTargetClosed` and added `ReplyQuoteIdentityTests.witnessAgreeingRowsWithDifferentBodiesResolveToNothing`, which fails red against the witness resolver. What survives from the body: the *diagnosis* that `count == 1` measures folders rather than messages still stands. ⚠️ **The accepted cost it names does NOT survive — round 4 REVERSED it on new information, and the words *"the send still works"* are RETRACTED wherever they appeared.** The body said a refusal drops only the quote and attribution, never the user's authored text. That was true of `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader` in isolation and **false of the system**: `ComposeView.loadDraftOrPrepopulate` only LOGGED the refusal and `ComposeView.send` then derived the outgoing parent from its own `replyTo` PARAMETER, so the reply left with **no `In-Reply-To` and no `References`** (a brand-new message in every client that threads); `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend`'s `guard let originalId = replyToHeaderId` short-circuited, so the parent got **no `isReplied`/`isForwarded` write and no Reply action-tag clear**; the send was **permitted**, compose was **dismissed**, and `AccountManagerOutbox.finalizeOutboxMessage` made the local draft deletable on delivery, taking the last local record of what it replied to with it — none of which the user was ever shown, because the quote was restored from the draft row and the compose *looked* correct. The SEND's parent is now the persisted draft row's own guarded resolution (`ComposeDraftGuards.sendReplyTarget` / `persistedReplyTargetClaim`), and a row that CLAIMS a parent which resolves to nothing **blocks the send** while leaving the draft openable, editable, savable and discardable — so the cost of a Strategy-2 refusal is now *"no quote, and no send from this draft until the user starts the reply again from the original message"*, not *"quote only, send proceeds"*. The full account — including the Undo-Send reopen path (`UndoReopenCompose.composeView(for:)`), which lost the same thing with no refusal at all — is the round-4 correction at the end of the `IOS-DRAFT-009` row. Pinned by suite `ComposeSendReplyTargetTests` (file `TabMailTests/Views/ComposeSendReplyTargetTests.swift`). |

## Audit round 2 — surfaced, deliberately NOT fixed

**Attribution here was re-derived from git, NOT inherited from the audit brief, and the brief's
classification did not survive that check.** The brief listed every item below as "pre-existing,
verbatim in the shipped release". Only three of them are. `observedUidValidity` does not appear
**anywhere** in `07a4bb703` (`git grep -c` returns nothing), so no finding that mentions an epoch can
be verbatim in the release — the mechanism postdates it entirely. Three further items were
introduced by the audit **round-1 remediation itself**. The distinction is load-bearing: "shipped and
proven adequate" is an argument for leaving something alone, and it cannot be borrowed by code that
never shipped. Each row therefore states its **introducing commit**, and the ancestry test used was
`git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> e8b4f44c5` (candidate band = `e8b4f44c5..cec7450e1`).

Nothing below is fixed here: the pre-existing and pre-candidate items are out of the round-2 scope,
and the candidate-introduced ones are reported for the coordinator to rule on rather than fixed
unilaterally. **Severity is stated honestly: two are C3.**

### A. Genuinely pre-existing — verbatim in the shipped release `v1.6.38` (`07a4bb703`)

Verified by reading the release's own source, not by inheriting the claim. These are not regressions
of the forward-port that surfaced them, and holding a forward-port hostage to every pre-existing
defect in the app is the scope creep this repo forbids.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-SEARCH-001 | Pre-existing **C3**; shipped verbatim in v1.6.38; ✅ **FIXED by `766badea2`** | Search; `SearchView.openResult(_:)`; cached local result; headerId; UIDVALIDITY reset; `markReadOnOpenIfNeeded`; wrong-message mutation | `SearchView.openResult(_:)` takes `if let headerId = result.headerId { navigationPath.append(headerId); return }` — it navigates on a cached composite address with **no re-resolution and no identity validation**; only the *remote* branch re-resolves. After a correct UIDVALIDITY reset re-seats that `(accountId, folderPath, uid)` address, tapping a stale local search result opens message **Y** while the user believes they tapped **X**, and `MessageDetailViewModel.markReadOnOpenIfNeeded()` then registers a durable read mutation on Y. Audit round 1 fixed only the remote sibling, which is why the two branches now differ. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `766badea2`, "Prove search-result identity before a local tap opens and marks read".** **The invariant that now holds:** a local search result opens a message **only when the RFC 822 Message-ID of the row now at the captured address still equals the one captured when that result was rendered**; on disagreement — or a vanished row — it navigates nowhere and raises *"Result no longer available"*. No durable read mutation can therefore land on a message the user was not shown. `SearchResult` carries `capturedRfc822MessageId`, taken from the header row the local search ALREADY reads, so the witness costs **zero** extra I/O; `resolveLocalResultHeaderId` re-reads the row at the captured address inside one `dbPool.read` and compares through `MessageIdentity.comparableRfc822Identity`, the tree's single identity-COMPARISON normalizer — no second normalizer and no new witness were minted. ⚠️ **THE FIX THIS ROW IMPLIED WAS A NULL FIX, AND WAS NOT TAKEN.** Contrasting the local branch (*"no re-resolution"*) with the remote one (*"re-resolves"*) is misleading: `resolveRemoteResultHeaderId` filters on `messageId && accountId (+ folderPath)` and returns the matched row's `.id`, **which IS that same composite address** — it establishes EXISTENCE, not IDENTITY, and against a re-seated address it returns exactly the wrong row. Routing the local branch through it would have looked like a C3 closure while closing nothing. What was missing was a CONTENT witness, which is what landed. **Not an ADR-IOS-068/D4 violation:** D4 forbids an RFC 822 Message-ID SELECTING or AUTHORIZING a mutation target; here the target is still selected by the durable composite address exactly as before, and the RFC id can only REFUSE it, never widen it or nominate a different one — the same content-witness use `AIWriteTarget.resolveCurrentHeader` arm 6, `MessageAICache`, the FTS/body stores and threading already make. **A1:** shipped `07a4bb703` carries the local branch verbatim, so the release did not solve this and the fix is authored under A1 step 3 ("nonexistent"). |
| IOS-SEARCH-003 | In-range **C3** (not shipped); REGISTRABLE-HIGH, not blocking; ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04)** in the same commit that adds this row — with one accepted residual, stated below | Search; remote result; `SearchView.tapOutcome`; `SearchView.presentableRemoteResults`; `SearchResult.capturedRfc822MessageId`; `MessageHeaderInfo.rfc822MessageId`; `ExpectedMessageIdentity`; `MessageDetailViewModel.markReadPermitted`; content witness; fail-dangerous seam; unwitnessed mark-read; wrong rationale | The REMOTE arm of the search tap travelled **unwitnessed**, so a durable mark-read on a remote result could land on a re-seated address — the same C3 misattribution `IOS-SEARCH-001` closed for the LOCAL arm, one branch over. `presentableRemoteResults` built every remote `SearchResult` without `capturedRfc822MessageId` even though `MessageHeaderInfo.rfc822MessageId` (the struct's second field, set by the one true producer `IMAPProvider.mapMessageInfo`) was already in the value being mapped, and `tapOutcome` then hard-forced `provenRfc822MessageId: isRemote ? nil : …`, discarding it a second time. **Silent in both directions:** `SearchResult.capturedRfc822MessageId` is nil-DEFAULTED so the omission compiled clean, and `MessageDetailViewModel.markReadPermitted` is `guard let openIdentity else { return true }` — FAIL OPEN. Nothing reported it. <br><br>🚨 **THE MORE DANGEROUS HALF WAS THE COMMENT, and it is the reason this row exists.** The code was accompanied by *"A remote result carries NO content witness (there was no local row to capture one from) … a false proof is worse than none."* The **second** clause is sound and the **first** is FACTUALLY FALSE — a witness needs no local row; the provider supplied one — so the sound conclusion was drawn from a false premise and protected the defect from review. A wrong rationale outlives wrong code, because nothing compiles it and no test fails on it (same failure shape as the retraction in `faf4d133f`, same file range). The comment is corrected in place at `tapOutcome`, and the test that blessed it (`aResolvableRemoteResultOpensWithoutAnUnprovenWitness`) is rewritten — note it would have stayed **GREEN** through the fix, since its fixture minted a witness-less result, so no gate could have found it. <br><br>**The invariant that now holds:** a remote search result carries the provider's own Message-ID for the record it rendered, and that witness reaches `MessageDetailViewModel`, where it can only **REFUSE** the open's durable mark-read — never select, widen or nominate a target (**not** an ADR-IOS-068 / D4 violation; the target is still chosen by the durable composite address). Rendering is deliberately NOT gated: a re-seated address still opens, because refusing to render is a regression with no C3 payoff. **Severity, adjudicated:** REGISTRABLE-HIGH rather than blocking — every pre-range path carried NO witness on ANY tap (`b1ae6e262` authored the mechanism, `afa7889ee` authored this remote path, both in range), so the range is a strict improvement, and the window is one live on-screen search session, not unbounded. <br><br>⚠️ **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL — a NEW fail-closed population, named because the fix's absolute is otherwise unfalsifiable (`MIS-019`).** A live local row whose own `rfc822MessageId` is absent or differs while the server reports one now loses **only** its mark-read-on-open. The message still opens and renders; it stays unread. **Recoverable by one ordinary gesture** (mark read by hand, or the row's own next sync-driven update) and therefore acceptable per THE MANTRA. **The non-recovering case, named per `MIS-IOS-008`:** none — nothing is deleted, refused or queued, and no durable state records the refusal, so the next tap re-evaluates from scratch. The opposite population (provider supplies no Message-ID at all) keeps today's **fail-open** behaviour with **no new branch**, because `ExpectedMessageIdentity.init?(capturedRfc822MessageId:)` already rejects an absent or unnormalizable value; the mirror-image "refuse when no witness" over-correction (`MIS-005`) is pinned red by `SearchRemoteResultIdentityTests.remoteResultWithNoProviderWitnessStillOpensAndMarksRead`. **A1:** neither shipped `07a4bb703` nor `v2final` `e28dd4edb` contains `capturedRfc822MessageId`, `OpenTarget`, `tapOutcome` or any content witness on any tap path — both push a bare address into `MessageDetailView(messageId:)`, which has no expected-identity parameter. The shipped architecture is **NONEXISTENT** (A1 step 3); there is no shipped property to restore and none to inherit. |
| IOS-QUEUE-001 | Pre-existing; shipped verbatim in v1.6.38; ✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`** — ⚠️ and it was a **NEVER-DROP violation**, not the delay this row recorded | Action queue; `buildLanes(_:)`; lane key; connected component; folder-less; UID collision; halt | `buildLanes(_:)` unions ops on `"accountId:msgId"`, **omitting the folder**. On IMAP a UID is mailbox-local, so UID 77 in INBOX and UID 77 in Archive — different physical messages — are treated as the same lane member. Two unrelated gestures then serialize against each other and, worse, can halt each other: a `.haltLane` caused by one message's unresolved op stops a queued gesture on a completely different message. The effect is delay and starvation, not a wrong-message mutation. **Do not redesign `buildLanes` opportunistically** — audit round 2 deliberately routed around it rather than through it. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`, "Key drain lanes by folder and finish moves on every retire path". ⚠️ THIS ROW'S SEVERITY WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE: it was a NEVER-DROP violation, not "delay and starvation, not a wrong-message mutation".** A lane halts on the first evidence refusal, so an op permanently wedged on `(INBOX, 77)` also starved an unrelated message at `(Archive, 77)` — **the wedge corollary with a bystander**, which no sync pass recovers. The row's own sentence *"the effect is delay and starvation, not a wrong-message mutation"* correctly ruled out C3 and then treated the remainder as benign; a starved intention that never recovers is inside the non-recoverable set on its own account. **The invariant that now holds:** two ops whose addresses differ in the FOLDER are never merged into one lane, so one message's permanent evidence wedge cannot starve a different message that merely shares a UID. The lane key is now `"accountId:folderPath:messageId"`, matching the address a `PendingOperation` actually has. **Why the change is safe in the direction it can be wrong:** every one of the 22 `PendingOperation` producers takes `folderPath` from `Folder.path` or `MessageHeader.folderPath`, so the field is always populated, and a colon inside a folder path can only OVER-merge two lanes — the conservative direction, and exactly what the code did for every op before. Ops on the same `(account, folder, UID)` still share one lane, asserted by a control test, because that serialization is the whole reason lanes exist. **A1:** `buildLanes` is BYTE-IDENTICAL at `07a4bb703` — the shipped release has the same defect — so there was no behaviour to restore and the fix is authored, treating the release as a floor rather than a ceiling. |
| IOS-OUTBOX-003 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — pre-existing **product behaviour**, shipped verbatim in v1.6.38; the queue-time write stays optimistic by design | Outbox; reply; forward; `isReplied`; `isForwarded`; `persistQueuedSend`; `deleteCompletedSendAtomic`; optimistic UI; `\Answered`; unaddressable parent | **The end-to-end gap, stated whole because each half alone is misleading.** For a parent the app cannot positively address — the `IOS-EPOCH-001` fail-closed window — the UI can still show "replied" while the server's `\Answered` is never set. There are **two producers** of that local flag and only one of them is gated. `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend` writes `UPDATE messageHeader SET isReplied = 1` (and the `isForwarded` sibling) **optimistically at queue time with no admission gate at all**; `AccountManagerOutbox.deleteCompletedSendAtomic` writes it again at completion, and audit round 2 moved *that* write inside its `if let flagAdmission` guard so the local flag and the durable op now share one fate **at the completion producer**. The queue-time write is unchanged: it is verbatim in `07a4bb703`, it is correct optimistic-UI behaviour per ADR-IOS-001, and changing when a reply shows as replied is a product decision rather than a bug fix. **Consequence to hold onto:** the completion path no longer re-asserts a server-side claim it did not queue, but the queue-time claim still outlives an unaddressable parent, so the invariant *"local reply state never claims a server-side flag that was never queued"* does **not** hold end-to-end. Sync restores truth on the next pass that can address the folder. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — the queue-time write stays optimistic and ungated, and that is a product decision the owner has already made by shipping it.** **The decision:** `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend` continues to write `isReplied`/`isForwarded` at queue time with no admission gate. Three grounds, in order of weight. **(1)** It is **verbatim in `07a4bb703`** and is correct ADR-IOS-001 optimistic-UI behaviour; changing WHEN a reply shows as replied is a product change, not a bug fix. **(2)** Gating it would mean the user replies and the "replied" indicator does not appear, on an account whose folder epoch has not settled — a visible regression traded for an invariant no user can observe. **(3)** The invariant this row wanted — *"local reply state never claims a server-side flag that was never queued"* — is the **wrong invariant for an optimistic-UI field**. The right one is *"the completion path never re-asserts a server-side claim it did not queue"*, which round 2 established and which **holds**: `deleteCompletedSendAtomic` writes both flags inside its `if let flagAdmission` guard, so the completion producer and the durable op share one fate. **Accepted cost:** the "Consequence to hold onto" sentence above, which survives this closure verbatim and is the honest statement of the residual. **Recoverability:** a sync pass — `SyncEngineDeltaSync`/`SyncEngineFullSync` assign `header.isReplied = info.isReplied` from server flags, so the next pass that can address the folder restores truth. **The state where that does not fire** is a folder whose epoch is never established (the `IOS-AI-003` population) — but note that in that state the flag itself was never queued either, so the divergence is *"UI optimistically ahead of a server the app cannot address"*, which is precisely what optimistic UI means. **There is no state in which the local flag contradicts a KNOWN server truth.** |
| IOS-QUEUE-003 | ⚠️ **DECOMPOSED 2026-08-04 into NINE per-item dispositions — 3 ALREADY-CLOSED, 3 CLOSED-DECISION, and the 3 formerly-OPEN items are now ✅ FIXED by `2c0d47ed0`. ZERO live remainder.** The id is retained as a stable handle; the per-item table below is authoritative | Action queue; F2b L4 demote lane; legacy unstamped rows; `ProviderNativeActionAdmissionTests`; `withRegisteredProvider`; 404-as-success; ungated diagnostic logs; `retainedForRetry`; `sweepStaleActionTags`; gmail-fixture retention | Carried forward unchanged from the previous round's register, none of them candidate-attributable: the F2b L4 demote lane; legacy unstamped rows parking permanently; `ProviderNativeActionAdmissionTests`' overclaiming *"Every ordinary IMAP producer …"* name; the six inline `withRegisteredProvider` sites; 404-as-success; ungated diagnostic logs; D-1 `retainedForRetry` being ownerless; D-2 `sweepStaleActionTags` starvation; and the D-5 gmail-fixture retention test. <br><br>⚠️ **THAT SENTENCE IS WHY THIS ROW SURVIVED EVERY AUDIT ROUND UNTOUCHED, AND IT IS THE FINDING.** Nine unrelated findings under one id cannot be adjudicated as a unit: any reviewer who reached it had to hold nine independent questions at once, so the row was carried "unchanged" round after round rather than decided. It is decomposed below and each item is disposed of on its own evidence. **The id is NOT retired and NOT renumbered** — it is referenced from commit bodies and prior rounds — but its live remainder is now exactly the three items marked OPEN, and nothing else. <br><br>✅ **THOSE LAST THREE ARE NOW CLOSED by `2c0d47ed0`, "Close the three live IOS-QUEUE-003 items: name, helper, log gating". THIS ID HAS NO LIVE REMAINDER.** ⚠️ **WHICH CENSUS WAS TAKEN, stated because two sources disagreed:** a triage document's summary table said *"4 already-closed / 3 fix-now / 2 closed-decision"* while its own per-item table said 3/3/3. **The per-item table is authoritative and is the one used**, and the implementing agent's independent re-census reproduced it exactly — items 1–3 ALREADY-CLOSED, 4–6 CLOSED-DECISION, 7–9 the live remainder. The summary line was simply wrong arithmetic over its own table; no item moved class. **Where the implementing agent's numbers differed from the REGISTER (item 8), the agent's committed census wins and this row is corrected below rather than left standing with a false count.** No production behaviour changed in that commit; its only production edits are logging gates. |

#### `IOS-QUEUE-003` decomposed — the nine items, adjudicated one at a time (2026-08-04)

**Read this table, not the row above.** Each item was verified against the tree at decomposition
time. ~~✅ **As of `2c0d47ed0` the row has NO live surface at all** — items 7, 8 and 9 landed, so all
nine are dispositioned and `IOS-QUEUE-003` may not be cited as an open finding for anything.~~
⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-04: eight of nine are dispositioned. Item 2's ALREADY-CLOSED was false and is
retracted in place; its live statement is `IOS-QUEUE-006`.** The "no live surface" sentence is struck
rather than deleted because it is exactly the kind of completeness claim this register has a recorded
habit of carrying past the code — an item was marked closed on a basis nobody re-greped.

| # | Item | Disposition | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Legacy unstamped rows parking permanently | **ALREADY-CLOSED** | Migration `v74_purgeLegacyPendingOperations` (`DELETE FROM pendingOperation`, verified in `AppDatabase.swift`) removed every pre-v3 row, and a full **producer census** shows no new one can be created: every current producer of a `nonDraftTypes` op stamps the epoch — `AccountManagerActions` (via `admission.observedUidValidity`), `AppDelegate`'s two notification-action sites (via `AccountManager.admissionEpochForNewGesture`, read in the SAME write transaction as the insert), and `AccountManagerOutbox`'s `markReplied`/`markForwarded` (via `flagAdmission.observedUidValidity`). The only unstamped producer is `.setTag`, which is deliberately excluded from checkpoint A because action tags are **local-only** (ADR-IOS-036). **No producer of a permanently-parked row remains** — the closure is the census, not the migration alone. |
| 2 | D-1 `retainedForRetry` is ownerless | ⚠️ **RETRACTED 2026-08-04 — the "ALREADY-CLOSED" below was FALSE. Reopened and superseded by `IOS-QUEUE-006`** | ⚠️ **The retracted basis is kept verbatim so the error stays searchable:** *"It has an owner at the tip: `AccountManagerActions.RoleMoveDisposition.retainedForRetry` is produced by `roleMoveRejectDispositions` at five guards and consumed by `pendingIds`, and `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`'s G2 note names it as the disposition a nil epoch stamp yields (see `IOS-IMAP-007` arm 3). The 'ownerless' observation was true when written and is not true now."* **Being CONSUMED by `pendingIds` is not being OWNED by a retry.** `RoleMoveAdmission.pendingIds` is a computed filter (`ids(with: .retainedForRetry)`), and its five production reads at HEAD are two `log(...)` strings in `NotificationActionRouter.execute` and two `result["pending_ids"]` serialisations in `EmailArchiveTool` / `EmailDeleteTool`. Not one stores, schedules or redrives the work. `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`'s G2 note NAMES the disposition; naming is not owning. Recorded as `MIS-024` instance 2 — a closure discharged by citing a mechanism without greping what that mechanism does. **The live statement is `IOS-QUEUE-006`;** it is REGISTRABLE, not blocking, but this item was never closed. |
| 3 | D-2 `sweepStaleActionTags` starvation | **ALREADY-CLOSED as stated; RESTATED here as a decision naming its bound** | `SyncEngineMaintenance.sweepStaleActionTags` is bounded by `maxPerSweep = SyncConfig.pruneChunkSize` per sweep and iterates non-inbox folders against a TTL cutoff computed once. The concern was that a folder late in the iteration order is never reached when the per-sweep cap is exhausted early. **That is a FAIRNESS property of a maintenance sweep, not a dropped intention:** action tags are local-only (ADR-IOS-036), so nothing on the wire depends on the sweep, and the TTL only ever *clears* a stale tag — a folder that is skipped keeps a tag slightly longer, and the next sweep reaches it. The bound is deliberate (an unbounded sweep is the hot-path scan audit rule A6 exists to catch); **do not remove the cap to close this.** |
| 4 | F2b L4 demote lane | **CLOSED-DECISION** | Already argued in code, at `AccountManagerQueue`'s `actionIdentityResolutionFailed` arm: *"`v2final` demotes this case to its queue tail instead of dropping it, via `ProviderError.persistentActionFailure` — machinery this tree does not have (F2b L4). Terminal drop is the disposition v3 already shipped and keeps; the intention loss is bounded and visible, and adding a demote path is a separate change."* **The register points at that decision; it does not re-open it.** The accepted cost is a bounded, visible terminal drop, and the alternative is a demote lane this tree has no machinery for. |
| 5 | 404-as-success | **CLOSED-DECISION** | `executeSingleOp`'s `isConfirmedGoneError` branch is gated to structural **404/410** and `ProviderError.messageNotFound` only, and it explicitly does **NOT** delete the local header on the string-matching branch — the code says why: *"too risky for false positives"* — while the delete it does perform is scoped to the exact `(account, folder, messageId)` row, because *"broader matches risk deleting unrelated messages that happen to share a UID in a different IMAP folder"*. A verified-permanent 404 is never-drop **exit 2** (provider-authoritative), which is the one thing the never-drop rule allows here. The gate is already as narrow as the evidence permits; widening it to the string branch would be the clause-2 conflation this repo records as its most repeated defect. |
| 6 | D-5 gmail-fixture retention test | **CLOSED-DECISION** | Test-fixture retention only; no production behaviour depends on it and none is changed by keeping it. |
| 7 | `ProviderNativeActionAdmissionTests`' overclaiming name | ✅ **FIXED by `2c0d47ed0`** | *"Every ordinary IMAP producer persists the native UID and its source UIDVALIDITY"* was universally quantified over a set the test body enumerates as **four** — `markRead`, `markUnread`, `markFlagged`, `move`, all entered through `AccountManagerActions`. **The producer set is strictly larger:** archive and delete are covered by the sibling `archiveDeleteUseMove`; `AppDelegate`'s two `NotificationActionRouter` cold-queue sites (stamped via `AccountManager.admissionEpochForNewGesture`) and `AccountManagerOutbox`'s `markReplied` / `markForwarded` (stamped via `flagAdmission.observedUidValidity`) are not covered there at all. Renamed to *"markRead, markUnread, markFlagged and move each persist the native UID and its source UIDVALIDITY"*, following `b87804055`'s convention exactly — the retired display name is recorded verbatim in the test's doc block, so the two existing citations of it (in `NeverDropExitClosureTests` and `ActiveAIQueueTests`, both quoting it as the canonical example of this hazard) still grep. **No assertion was added, removed or weakened**, and the suite's case count is unchanged at 10. The renamed test was confirmed passing BY DISPLAY NAME, and the retired name appears zero times as an executed test. |
| 8 | ~~Six~~ **seventeen** inline `withRegisteredProvider` call sites across **three** duplicated helpers | ✅ **FIXED by `2c0d47ed0`** — ⚠️ **THIS ITEM'S CENSUS WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED IN PLACE** | ⚠️ **"Six inline `withRegisteredProvider` sites" was WRONG in both halves, and the retracted figure is kept here rather than deleted because this register has a recorded habit of carrying a count past the code it describes.** The censused tree held **THREE byte-identical private static definitions and SEVENTEEN call sites** — `PostSendServerDraftCleanupTests` (1 definition, 3 calls), `UidValidityResetPurgeCompanionTests` (1, 8), `UidValidityResetReactionTests` (1, 6). **"Six" is exactly the call-site count that `d49811e92` — the commit this register itself cites for the item — added to `UidValidityResetReactionTests` alone.** The number was taken at that commit and never re-verified; the tree has since grown two more copies of the helper and eleven more call sites. **And there was no pre-existing SHARED helper to consolidate "onto"** — the item's premise that one existed is also false; every call site already used its own file-local copy. The three bodies were verified byte-identical before removal (only the doc blocks' example phrasing differed) and are now one definition, `TestProviderRegistry.withRegisteredProvider`, in `TabMailTests/Infrastructure/ProcessGlobalTestState.swift` — the file whose own type doc already names the provider registry as one of the process-global surfaces its trait exists to serialize. No new file, so no XcodeGen regeneration. The consolidated doc block preserves the **UNION** of all three retired ones, including the never-defer-the-unregister contract and the instance that actually suffered it. **The implementing agent's committed census is authoritative over both this register's original text and the pass-2 brief.** |
| 9 | Ungated diagnostic logs | ✅ **FIXED by `2c0d47ed0`** — a census with a per-line disposition, as this item required, NOT a blanket wrap | Censused every `print(` / `NSLog(` / `os_log(` in `AccountManagerQueue.swift`, `ActiveAIQueue.swift` and `AppDelegate.swift`. `NSLog(` and `os_log(`: **zero** occurrences in all three. `print(`: **83 lines** censused — 7 were not ungated diagnostics (6 already inside `DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()` blocks, 1 being `NotificationActionRouter.log`'s own emission, which IS the gate), leaving **76 ungated diagnostics: 73 GATED here, 3 KEPT UNGATED BY DECISION.** 🚨 **The three exemptions are the finding, and each names the exemption it claims** (global rule 12 admits only *(a)* an explicit owner request — **none claims (a)** — or *(b)* structured error/alert that production observability genuinely needs): (1) `AccountManagerQueue.executeSingleOp`'s `CRITICAL: Failed to delete completed PendingOperation … will re-execute on next drain` — the op's wire effect has ALREADY landed and the retiring row could not be deleted, so the next drain applies it a SECOND time, and this is the only witness that a specific duplicate is about to be created; (2) `retirePartiallyCompletedOp`'s `CRITICAL: could not narrow partially-completed …` — same class at the partial-completion boundary, naming a duplicate that will be seated at the destination; (3) `executeSingleOp`'s identity-refusal terminal-drop line — **this is item 4's F2b L4 TERMINAL DROP of a durable user intention, which this table accepts expressly on the ground that the loss is "bounded and VISIBLE", and this print IS that visibility.** Gating it would silently convert an accepted, observable drop into an unobservable one and retroactively weaken a decision recorded in this register — not a change a logging cleanup is entitled to make as a side effect. **Why the exemption is genuine and not a convenience:** the test is THE MANTRA's — a duplicate already created on the server and an intention already dropped from the queue are NOT recovered by a later sync, a retry, or one ordinary gesture; and `DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()` is false for every ordinary user (it needs the ten-tap unlock AND a `tabmail.ai`-domain account), so gating these would make the only witness to a non-recoverable outcome conditional on an unlock the affected user cannot obtain. Everything else censused fails that test in the other direction and is gated: queue tracing, per-pass/per-op counters, the `[QueueDiag]` dump, provider-authoritative retirements, local-header cleanup a later sync re-adds, and AI-derived-content failures the next `repopulateFromDatabase` recomputes. **NO LOG WAS DELETED, no log text was altered, and no control flow was restructured.** |

### B. Pre-candidate, but NOT in the shipped release — v3 forward-port line

These postdate `v1.6.38` and predate the round-1 remediation, so the "shipped and proven
adequate" argument does **not** apply to them — but they are equally not the round-2 candidate's
doing. The epoch mechanism itself is post-release: `observedUidValidity` does not exist anywhere
in `07a4bb703`. They are out of round-2 scope and recorded for the v3 line's own reckoning.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-NSE-001 | **NOT in the shipped release**; introduced PRE-candidate by `6e9840ed4`; ✅ **FIXED by `ce6fb384c`** | NSE; staging; `StagedMessage.toInboxRow()`; `StagedInboxRow.toMessageHeader()`; `insertNewHeaderFromStaging`; `observedUidValidity`; dropped epoch; refused gesture | The NSE stages a message together with the UIDVALIDITY its fetch observed, and **two projections then discard it**: `StagedMessage.toInboxRow()` and `StagedInboxRow.toMessageHeader()` do not carry `observedUidValidity`, and `insertNewHeaderFromStaging(...)` contains zero occurrences of it. A row whose epoch was **positively proven** is therefore admitted into the main store as "unknown", and the next gesture on it is refused by the very guard that epoch exists to satisfy — the `IOS-EPOCH-001` window, entered on evidence we had and threw away. **Attribution, independently derived and contradicting the brief:** this is *not* verbatim in the shipped release — `git grep -c observedUidValidity 07a4bb703` returns nothing, so the epoch does not exist there at all and cannot be dropped there. `6e9840ed4` ("Refuse NSE staged content whose identity is not positively proven") added the staging column, `NSEMessageMetadata.observedUidValidity` and `StagedMessage.observedUidValidity`, but did not carry the field through the two projections or the insert, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor 6e9840ed4 e8b4f44c5` confirms it predates the candidate band. So: real, not candidate-attributable, and **not** covered by the "shipped and proven adequate" argument. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `ce6fb384c`, "Carry the NSE's proven UIDVALIDITY through the staging projections".** **The invariant that now holds:** a staged row whose epoch the NSE POSITIVELY OBSERVED with its own live SELECT enters the action path carrying that epoch, so the very next gesture on a just-pushed message is admitted on evidence the system already had — instead of being refused by the guard that evidence exists to satisfy. **All THREE projections carry it now**, not two: `NSEDataBridge.StagedMessage.toInboxRow()` (the field did not exist on `StagedInboxRow` at all, so the in-memory ADR-IOS-049 read model could not carry it), `StagedInboxRow.toMessageHeader()` (the header `AccountManager.resolveHeadersForAction` synthesises for a staged-but-not-yet-durable row, which every gesture feeds to the admission predicate), and `NSEDataBridge.insertNewHeaderFromStaging()` (the merge's durable `MessageHeader` write, which set fifteen fields and never this one). **NO NEW SOURCE OF EPOCH TRUTH AND NO MIGRATION** — no probe, no re-derivation, no column. The value is read from the staging row the NSE **already wrote** and normalized through the tree's existing `SyncEngine.knownUidValidity`, which rejects nil **and** the `0` sentinel (RFC 3501 §2.3.1.1 types UIDVALIDITY as `nz-number`, so `0` means "not reported"), so an unknown epoch stays unknown and is never upgraded into a false trust claim. This **finishes** `6e9840ed4`, which added the column and simply did not carry it through; it does not author a new mechanism. **Fail-safe by construction, both halves verified before the change was written:** the refusing comparison already exists and is the same one guarding every other durable path (`admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` admits only on epoch EQUALITY, plus positivity and `messageId == String(uid)`), so a carried epoch can only ever match or disagree — **it can never widen admission, and C3 is untouched**; and the disposal path for a stale epoch already exists (ARM 1 deletes a staged row positively disagreeing with a settled folder epoch, ARM 2 keeps a quarantined row for retry rather than merging it), so no positively-stale value can reach the insert. ⚠️ **ACCEPTED LIMITATION, stated by that commit: this narrows the row on the PUSH side ONLY.** A folder that has never earned an epoch still refuses the gesture — that is the pre-existing `IOS-EPOCH-001` window (T1.3) and it is **unchanged**. The carry supplies the **row's** half of the comparison, not the folder's. Do not read this flip as `IOS-EPOCH-001` having moved. **A BLESSING TEST was re-scoped, not deleted:** `MessageHeaderObservationEpochTests`' *"NSE staged-only headers remain epochless until staged-row epoch validation lands"* asserted precisely the dropped behaviour as expected; it is now *"An unstamped NSE staged row still projects an epochless header"*, keeping the half that is still true — an absence of evidence is never upgraded to proof — with the previous display name and the reason recorded inline. |
| IOS-NSE-002 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — the epoch comparison IS the fence; **NOT in the shipped release**; introduced PRE-candidate by `6e9840ed4` | NSE; purge; cross-process; App Group; committed DELETE; fence; stale epoch | The staged-state purge is **not a cross-process fence**. A committed `DELETE` proves only that the rows visible *inside that transaction* are gone; the notification-service extension may still be running and can commit a row afterwards, carrying an old or unknown epoch, into a store the main app believes it has just cleaned. Nothing coordinates the two processes at that boundary. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — the claim above stays literally true, but the FENCE is not what protects the invariant; the epoch comparison is, and it does.** **The decision:** no cross-process fence is built — no advisory lock, no App Group generation counter, no two-phase handshake. **Why the invariant holds without one, read from the guards rather than assumed:** `NSEDataBridge.uidValidityStagingRowStatus` reports `isOldEpoch` only on a **positive** disagreement with a **settled** folder epoch, and such rows are skipped **and deleted**; while the folder is quarantined the keep arm wins and rows are **kept, never deleted**, because a mismatch cannot yet distinguish "permanently stale" from "correctly observed the new epoch ahead of our stamp"; a row landing on an existing durable row must pass `nseMergeIdentityConfirmed`, which demands positive evidence (RFC equality unconditionally — with a positive RFC *disagreement* winning even when epochs agree — or epoch equality with a non-quarantined folder); and a folder read FAILURE is treated as QUARANTINED, so *"we could not look"* keeps the row rather than merging it. A late-committing NSE row carrying an old epoch is therefore **caught** once the reaction stamps E2 and **held** — not merged, not deleted — in the interval. **Answering the hazard with a fact about the DATA rather than with a lock is strictly more robust across a process boundary the OS may kill at any point**, which is the substantive reason this is a decision and not a deferral. **The residual is NAMED rather than claimed absent:** `NSEIMAPConnection` maps a reported `UIDVALIDITY` of `0` to nil; a nil epoch is fail-open in `uidValidityStagingRowStatus` (both signals false), and `insertNewHeaderFromStaging` does not consult `nseMergeIdentityConfirmed` (that guard serves the existing-durable-row arm). So a stale, RFC-less, epoch-less staged row inserted as a **new** header at an E2-recycled UID would seat wrong content — which **would be C3**. It requires ALL of: a server that omits `UIDVALIDITY` on SELECT (RFC 3501 §6.3.1 makes it REQUIRED, and `IOS-EPOCH-001` round 12 establishes that such a server *"is declaring it has no UIDs, which is an account this app cannot serve at all"*), **and** a UIDVALIDITY turnover on that same server, **and** an RFC-less message, **and** a concurrently-running NSE. Every component is already outside what this app supports. **The trade is stated so the chosen side is visible:** the narrow alternative — refuse to insert a NEW header from a staged IMAP row with a nil epoch — is tempting and should be *considered* by anyone who reopens this, but it would silently drop push-delivered mail on any server with a momentary SELECT hiccup, i.e. trade a C3 edge on an **unsupported** server for a data-visibility loss on **supported** ones. **v3 takes the current side.** Reopen only on the owner's call, and only with that trade restated. |
| IOS-QUEUE-002 | **NOT in the shipped release**; introduced PRE-candidate by `7cffc8309`; was unreachable; ✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`** | Action queue; checkpoint A; draft/reset arm; bare inequality; non-zero guard | Checkpoint A's draft/reset arm compares epochs on **bare inequality**, without the non-zero guards its rewritten sibling now requires — so a zero on either side would read as a disagreement rather than as an absence of evidence. **Unreachable today** (no path supplies a zero to that arm), which is why it is recorded rather than fixed; it is one refactor away from being reachable. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`, "Key drain lanes by folder and finish moves on every retire path".** **The invariant that now holds:** checkpoint A's draft/reset arm routes through `SyncEngine.knownUidValidity` — the same normalizer its IMAP sibling already required — so a **zero** on either side is read as an ABSENCE of evidence, never as a positive epoch disagreement. That matters because exit 4 (the delete arm) is the only arm at that checkpoint permitted to delete an op, and **unknown is not proof**: a bare inequality would have let "we could not determine the epoch" take the one exit reserved for a *proven* turnover. The reachability that made it latent was real but thin — SwiftMail defaults `Mailbox.Selection.uidValidity` to `UIDValidity(0)` — and it was "one refactor away", which is why the fix pins the **guard** rather than the reachability. **A1:** `knownUidValidity` has zero occurrences in the shipped file, so the shipped architecture is NONEXISTENT here and the fix is authored, not restored. |
