| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-NSE-003 | **CANDIDATE-INTRODUCED** by `209a55cdf`; ✅ **FIXED in round 2** | NSE; `NSEDataBridge.stagingPurgeTarget(...)`; App Group container; nil; `.nothingStaged` | `NSEDataBridge.stagingPurgeTarget(...)` maps a **nil App Group container** to `.nothingStaged` — "we could not look" reported as "there is nothing there". That is the exact conflation the never-drop rule's clause 2 names as this codebase's most repeated defect class, in a path whose answer gates a purge. **FIXED (round 2):** a nil container now returns `.unreachable`, mirroring the sibling condition (a staging file that exists but will not open) that already reported failure — so both ways of failing to READ the staged state get the same disposition, and only the positive observation "container resolved, no staging file" reports nothing-staged. Both purge helpers therefore return `false`, and `runUidValidityResetReaction` holds the folder at its old epoch and quarantined. Pinned by `UidValidityResetPurgeCompanionTests.unresolvableStagingContainerIsNotAnEmptyStagingArea`. |
| IOS-DRAFT-009 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — the Gmail multi-label premise does not have to be settled; **CANDIDATE-INTRODUCED** by `209a55cdf`; the round-2 fix was **REVERTED in audit round 3**; the ACCEPTED COST was **re-adjudicated and partly reversed in round 4** on new information — see the corrections at the end of this cell | Drafts; `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader(...)`; Strategy 2; cardinality guard; identity witness; Gmail multi-label | `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader(...)`'s Strategy-2 cardinality guard is in tension with its own stated Gmail multi-label premise: the premise says one message legitimately appears under several labels, while the guard treats multiplicity as ambiguity. Recorded because the two disagree, not because either is unsafe — **both directions fail closed**, so the user sees a missing reply-parent link, never a wrong one. **⚠ ROUND 2 REPLACED THIS GUARD; ROUND 3 PUT IT BACK, AND THE CARDINALITY GUARD IS WHAT THE CODE DOES TODAY.** Strategy 2 fetches under `.limit(2)` and refuses on `candidates.count != 1`; `Draft.swift`'s executable code was byte-identical to the round-3 base `cec7450e1` as of `3bd9f0bac` (its only diff against that base was comment prose); a round-4 cleanup has since deleted that file's dead non-`db` `resolveReplyToHeader` overload, so it is no longer byte-identical — but Strategy 2 and its cardinality guard are untouched and still are. *What round 2 (`94fac3e79`) did — kept on the record, not deleted:* it argued the predicate should not be cardinality at all, because every candidate already shares `(accountId, rfc822MessageId)` by construction, so `count == 1` measures how many FOLDERS hold the message rather than how many MESSAGES are in play; it replaced the guard with agreement on an identity witness (sender address, subject, timestamp), resolving when copies AGREE and refusing when they DISAGREE as a Message-ID collision, and replaced `.limit(2)` with a fixed cap fetched with one extra row so truncation was observable and also refused. *Why round 3 reverted it:* **agreement between rows is not identity.** The witness is wrong in BOTH directions, which is what makes it the wrong predicate rather than one short a field. It is **too weak to prove sameness** — it omits everything this resolver actually consumes (the body, the attachments, `replyTo`), so two genuinely DIFFERENT messages that collide on a Message-ID while sharing sender, subject and timestamp both satisfy it, and content the user never selected is quoted into a reply or carried into a forward (**C3**: no action may ever mutate the wrong message; failing closed is always acceptable). And it is **too strong to admit real copies** — its timestamp is INTERNALDATE, which RFC 3501 §6.4.7 makes a SHOULD-not-MUST to preserve across a COPY, so legitimate copies of one message can disagree on it. The unverified Gmail multi-label premise is therefore still unsettled, which is why this row stays open rather than closing. **The accepted cost, stated rather than hidden:** when a message is legitimately present in several folders/labels, Strategy 2 refuses and the reply or forward ships WITHOUT its quoted body and attribution. The user's authored text is untouched. ⚠ **The words "and the send still works" stood here and are RETRACTED — see the round-4 correction at the end of this cell; the sentence that follows describes the guard's history, not the accepted cost.** Shipped `07a4bb703` was WORSE here — its Strategy 2 was a bare `.filter(accountId && rfc822MessageId).fetchOne(db)`, i.e. an arbitrary row — so the restored guard is not a regression against the release; it is the round-2 improvement being given up as unproven. Pinned by three tests in suite `ReplyQuoteIdentityTests` (file `TabMailTests/Models/DraftResolveReplyTests.swift`, which is a FILE name and not a type — an earlier revision of this row cited it as a type qualifier): `severalCopiesOfOneMessageFailTheReplyTargetClosed` (several copies of one message refuse — the round-2 blessing test, INVERTED in round 3, red proof against the witness resolver recorded in its doc comment), `ambiguousRfcIdentityResolvesToNothing` (a genuine Message-ID collision refuses) and `witnessAgreeingRowsWithDifferentBodiesResolveToNothing` (rows agreeing on every field of the round-2 witness but carrying different bodies must still resolve to nothing — the test that pins the revert's reason). <br><br>🚨 **ROUND-4 CORRECTION — THE CONSEQUENCE WAS UNDERSTATED, AND THE ACCEPTED COST IS REVERSED ON NEW INFORMATION.** Every statement of this row's cost above — and the matching prose in `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader`'s "THE ACCEPTED COST" comment and in `ReplyQuoteIdentityTests.severalCopiesOfOneMessageFailTheReplyTargetClosed`'s doc block — said a refusal costs *the quoted body and the attribution*, and that *the send still works*. That described `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader` in isolation. It was **false of the system**, and the earlier adjudication ("both directions fail closed, so the user sees a missing reply-parent link, never a wrong one") was made on that understated premise. **What a refusal actually cost, before this change:** `ComposeView.loadDraftOrPrepopulate` computed the refusal and only LOGGED it, then `ComposeView.send` derived the outgoing parent from its own `replyTo` PARAMETER — so (i) `ThreadUtils.outgoingThreadHeaders(replyTo: nil)` returned `.none`, and the reply left with **no `In-Reply-To` and no `References`**, i.e. as a brand-new message in every client that threads; (ii) `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend`'s `guard let originalId = replyToHeaderId` short-circuited, so the parent got **no `isReplied`/`isForwarded` write and no Reply action-tag clear** — the user's reply state silently did not exist; (iii) the send was **permitted**, compose **dismissed**, and `AccountManagerOutbox.finalizeOutboxMessage` then made the local draft **deletable on delivery**, so the last local record of what the draft replied to went with it. The user was never told any of this: the quote was restored from the draft row, so the compose *looked* correct. **Worse, the same loss occurred with no refusal at all** on the Undo-Send reopen path — `UndoReopenCompose.composeView(for:)` builds `ComposeView(account:prefillDraftId:retainedDraftAuthority:)` and passes **no `replyTo`** while the row still carries `replyToId`, so *every* undo-then-resend of a reply sent unthreaded. The `ADR-IOS-043` memory topic's claim that `ComposeView.send` is "the ONE callsite" for outgoing thread headers is what kept this invisible; it does not enumerate the Undo reopen. **What is now guaranteed:** the SEND's parent is the persisted draft row's own GUARDED resolution (`ComposeDraftGuards.sendReplyTarget` / `persistedReplyTargetClaim`); the `replyTo` parameter is authority only when no persisted row exists. A row that CLAIMS a parent which resolves to nothing **blocks the send** (Send `.disabled` plus a `guard` in `send()`) with an explanation, and blocks **nothing else** — the draft stays openable, editable, savable and discardable, so the authored text is never trapped. A row whose claim resolves now threads correctly, including on the Undo reopen, which previously always sent unthreaded. **So the cost of a Strategy-2 refusal is no longer "quote only, send proceeds"; it is "no quote, and no send from this draft until the user starts the reply again from the original message".** That is a deliberate trade: shipping a reply with the wrong parent bookkeeping, or with none, is a wrong-message hazard (C3) and a silent one; refusing is loud and recoverable. Pinned by suite `ComposeSendReplyTargetTests` (file `TabMailTests/Views/ComposeSendReplyTargetTests.swift`). The Gmail multi-label premise this row opened on is still unsettled and this row stays open. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — the Gmail multi-label premise never had to be settled, and that is why this row could stay open indefinitely without anyone being wrong.** The sentence immediately above kept the row open on the ground that the premise is unsettled. **It is unsettled, and it does not matter**, because settling it would tell us WHICH case is common — it would not supply a PREDICATE. **The decision:** `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader` Strategy 2 refuses on multiplicity, permanently. **The basis is this row's own history, not a fresh judgement.** Round 2's diagnosis survived the revert and is still true — every candidate already shares `(accountId, rfc822MessageId)` by construction, so `count == 1` measures how many FOLDERS hold the message rather than how many MESSAGES are in play — and round 3 established the thing that actually decides it: **there is no sound predicate that distinguishes "several folders, one message" from "several messages, one Message-ID" using the fields available.** The identity witness is unsound in BOTH directions, which is what makes it the wrong predicate rather than one field short: too weak (it omits the body, the attachments and `replyTo` — everything this resolver consumes — so a Message-ID collision satisfies it and foreign content reaches the user's outgoing mail, **C3**) and too strong (its timestamp is INTERNALDATE, which RFC 3501 §6.4.7 makes a SHOULD-not-MUST across a COPY, so genuine copies disagree). **The guard is therefore correct whichever horn of the Gmail question holds, and the frequency question is informational.** **Accepted cost**, as re-adjudicated in round 4 and unchanged here: *"no quote, and no send from this draft until the user starts the reply again from the original message."* **Recoverability:** one ordinary gesture — open the original message and hit Reply. **A state where that gesture is blocked was searched for and none found:** the original is present by construction (it is what produced ≥2 candidates), and the draft stays openable, editable, savable and discardable, so the authored text is never trapped. What does NOT recover is that particular draft row's ability to send, and that is the deliberate C3 trade round 4 states. **Not a regression against the floor:** shipped `07a4bb703` was strictly worse — a bare `.filter(accountId && rfc822MessageId).fetchOne(db)`, i.e. an arbitrary row — so this is the round-2 improvement given up as unproven, not shipped behaviour lost. Evidence stays the three `ReplyQuoteIdentityTests` pins and suite `ComposeSendReplyTargetTests`. |
| IOS-TEST-002 | 🚨 **PARTLY CANDIDATE-INTRODUCED** (`b78a9303d`); test-register accuracy only; first half CLOSED in round 4 and kept as history; ✅ **second half FIXED by `b87804055`** — the row is now closed in full | Tests; `NeverDropExitClosureTests.partialCopyUidRetiresPerMember`; `OutboxStrandedClaimXfailTests`; expected failure | Two test-register inaccuracies, no production impact. ⚠️ **THE FIRST NO LONGER HOLDS — it is retained as history, not as a live finding.** It read: *"`NeverDropExitClosureTests.partialCopyUidRetiresPerMember()`'s doc comment **overstates what it asserts**."* Round 4 re-scoped that test and REWROTE its doc block, which now states the property for all three per-member outcomes the test actually asserts (`COPYUID` names the member ⇒ its source copy may be irreversibly PURGED; unnamed but still present in the source ⇒ the tagged OK plus liveness moves it and authorizes only the REVERSIBLE `\Deleted` mark; both moved ⇒ both retire and nothing is left to re-copy), asserted across two drains against the server's own mailbox contents, and it records both the display-name change and the RED PROOF against the pre-round-4 provider. Doc and assertions agree, so the overstatement is gone. Its current display name is *"A partial COPYUID moves every member and purges only the one it names"*; the prior name — *"A partial COPYUID retires only the proven member and re-queues the rest"* — was itself the overstatement, and is preserved in that test's own doc block rather than here. **THE SECOND IS UNCHANGED AND STILL TRUE:** `OutboxStrandedClaimXfailTests` contains **no expected-failure test** despite its name — three `@Test`s, no `withKnownIssue` anywhere in the file; the suite's single expected failure lives elsewhere, so a reader looking for it here will not find it. <br><br>✅ **SECOND HALF FIXED by `b87804055`, "Close the test-accuracy cluster: 5 vacuous tests, 4 wrong names"; the row is now closed in full** (its first half closed in round 4 and is kept above as history, not as a live finding). **The invariant that now holds:** no suite in the test target is named for an expected failure it does not contain. `OutboxStrandedClaimXfailTests` was renamed — **file and type** — to `OutboxStrandedClaimTests`. **No test was added, removed, renamed or re-scoped**, and the `@Suite` display name *"Outbox post-claim provider loss"* is unchanged, so the rename cannot have altered what is asserted; **both** previous names are recorded in the file header, so the old name a commit body or ADR may cite stays searchable. |

## Audit round 3 — surfaced, deliberately NOT fixed

Round 3's candidate is two DELETIONS — the `IMAPProvider.move` UIDPLUS capability refusal, and
`Draft.resolveReplyToHeader`'s identity witness — chosen because three consecutive rounds each landed
a mirror-image defect on the previous round's fix. Everything below was surfaced in the same audit
and is recorded rather than fixed, so the candidate stays deletion-shaped. **Where the two reviewers
disagreed, both positions are recorded with the adjudication, not just the winner.**

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-AI-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — surfaced round 3; adjudicated **NOT** a stop condition; reviewers disagreed, and the disagreement is settled below | AI; `ActiveAIQueue`; write-through admission arm 6; duplicate RFC Message-ID; replacement row; `MessageIdentity.contentKey`; FTS | Arm 6 of the AI result write-through admission admits an UNSTAMPED but unreplaced row on the strength of its RFC Message-ID being unchanged (`unstampedButUnreplacedRowStillWritesThrough`). **One reviewer's position:** a *different* physical message can carry a duplicate RFC Message-ID — aliases and list rewriters make this reachable — so the row occupying that PK may be a REPLACEMENT that satisfies arm 6, and an AI summary computed for X then lands on Y. That is wrong-message content. **The other reviewer's position, and the coordinator's adjudication:** this is not a candidate regression and not a stop condition, because it reduces to a duplicate-RFC collapse this repository already ACCEPTS and actively depends on — FTS keying, body storage and `MessageIdentity.contentKey` all treat one RFC identity as one content. If a duplicate RFC is unacceptable for an AI summary it is equally unacceptable for the stored body the summary describes, and that is a pre-existing design question about the content-key scheme (see `PROJECT_MEMORY`'s "TWO keying schemes on purpose": actions key by provider id to distinguish copies, content keys by RFC because the content is the same). Recorded here so a later reader finds the disagreement rather than re-deriving it. **Do not close this by narrowing arm 6 alone** — that would leave the same premise load-bearing everywhere else. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — arm 6 stays exactly as written, and the reason is STRUCTURAL rather than a judgement call between two reviewers.** **The decision:** an AI write-back may be admitted on RFC 822 Message-ID equality alone, because **in this codebase one RFC identity IS one content**. **The basis is in the tree, not in principle:** `MessageAICache.key` is `"{accountId}:{folderPath}:{rfc822MessageId}"`, FTS records key by `ContentKey(rawValue: header.id)` with body text stored under one RFC identity, and `MessageBody` is keyed by `ContentKey`. Body storage, FTS and AI therefore collapse a duplicate RFC identity **identically**. For an admitted summary to describe content the user is not shown, body storage and the AI cache would have to disagree about which physical message occupies an RFC identity — **and they cannot, because they consult the same key**. A path that writes a body under one content key and an AI result under another for the same header was searched for and **none exists**. **That settles the reviewers' disagreement rather than splitting it:** the hazard the first reviewer named (two physically distinct messages sharing a Message-ID via aliases or list rewriters) is real, but its consequence is not the one claimed. **Accepted cost:** where two distinct messages do share a Message-ID, they share one stored body, one FTS record and one AI summary — the summary is correct for the body the user is actually shown, and wrong only about which of the two collapsed messages authored it. **Why that cost is acceptable:** it is not C3. C3 misattribution requires derived content presented against a message whose OWN content differs, and the shared content key forecloses exactly that. **The instruction above stands and is now the operative reason, not a caveat:** narrowing arm 6 alone would leave the same premise load-bearing in body storage, FTS and threading while making AI the one inconsistent consumer — a worse state than either answer. The governing design is `PROJECT_MEMORY`'s *"TWO keying schemes on purpose"* (actions key by provider id to distinguish copies; content keys by RFC because the content is the same) and ADR-IOS-068. **The same premise governs `IOS-BACKFILL-002`**, and if it is ever revisited both must move together. <br><br>📌 **BASIS CORRECTED (2026-08-05) — THE ROW'S STRUCTURAL PREMISE WAS FALSE, AND THE ACCEPTED COST WAS STATED BACKWARDS. THE DECISION ITSELF IS UNCHANGED.** The 2026-08-04 text above is preserved verbatim as superseded history; everything from here governs. **The premise that is false:** *"they cannot [disagree], because they consult the same key."* They do not consult the same key. Verified at HEAD: `MessageAICache` is keyed by `MessageIdentity.aiCacheKey` = `"{accountId}:{folderPath}:{rfc822MessageId}"` — **RFC identity**; `MessageBody.id` is a `ContentKey` minted by `ContentKey.forHeader`, which carries the banner *"⚑ INERT AS OF STAGE B — this returns `messageHeader.id` verbatim"* and therefore yields `MessageIdentity.headerId` = `"{accountId}:{folderPath}:{providerMessageId}"` — the **provider address**; and FTS keys the same way, which **this row already said in its own words** (*"FTS records key by `ContentKey(rawValue: header.id)`"*) immediately before concluding the opposite. `MessageIdentity.contentKey`, the RFC-aware mint the original argument depends on, has **NO production call site** — every non-definition hit in the tree is a comment or a test, and `ContentKeyByteIdentityTests` describes itself as the suite that *"will go RED"* when the swap lands, which is direct evidence it has not. So the AI cache collapses by RFC while body and FTS collapse by provider address; `ContentKey.forHeader` stays inert pending **Stage E1**. **The accepted cost, restated the right way round:** two distinct messages sharing a Message-ID at DIFFERENT provider addresses keep **different** stored bodies and **different** FTS records while sharing **one** RFC-keyed AI cache row — so the summary the user is shown can describe the *other* message. That is the inverse of *"one stored body … the summary is correct for the body the user is actually shown"*. **The decision does not change, and neither does the standing instruction.** This is **not candidate-introduced**: shipped `v1.6.38` had the same two keyings **and no identity recheck at all** at the AI write-back, so HEAD is strictly safer on this axis. And *"Do not close this by narrowing arm 6 alone"* remains right for exactly the reason it gives — the divergence is between two key SPACES, so narrowing the one consumer that notices leaves the same divergence load-bearing everywhere else. Closing it properly is Stage E1's job, not arm 6's. **`IOS-BACKFILL-002` shares this premise by this row's own statement and was corrected in the same edit.** |
| IOS-AI-002 | Surfaced round 3; real, **billing** rather than C3/data-loss/brick; ✅ **FIXED by `403496b21`** (together with `IOS-AI-003`) | AI; RFC-less IMAP rows; `observedUidValidity`; migration `v77`; `SyncConfig.syncMessageLimit`; deep backfill; `ActiveAIQueue.jobCompleted`; `maxRetries: .max`; repeated paid calls | An RFC-less IMAP/iCloud row that is also unstamped can have its AI result refused and then recomputed without bound, so the same paid LLM call is issued again on every open. **The mechanics, each verified against the code rather than inherited from the audit brief:** migration `v77_addMessageHeaderObservedUidValidity` adds the column with **no backfill** (its own comment: *"Nullable, with no default and no backfill: existing rows remain unproven rather than adopting the Folder's current epoch by assertion"*); `SyncConfig.syncMessageLimit == 50`, so ordinary sync only re-observes the newest 50 per folder; and deep backfill **skips rows that already exist**. Therefore a pre-`v77` RFC-less IMAP row outside the newest-50 window is permanently unstamped — neither path will ever stamp it. `ActiveAIQueue` then calls `storage.jobCompleted(job, shouldRetry: true, maxRetries: .max)`, so there is no retry ceiling to stop the loop. **Population bound:** RFC-less IMAP/iCloud rows only. Gmail and Graph bypass epoch admission entirely via stable provider ids, and an RFC-BEARING row is admitted by the Message-ID baseline. **Not fixed here** because the cost is money and battery, not a wrong-message mutation or a lost intention, and any fix touches the same admission surface round 3 is deliberately not re-opening. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `403496b21`, "Stop paying for AI jobs whose write-back can never be attributed" — jointly with `IOS-AI-003`, because they are one defect at two reachabilities.** **The invariant that now holds:** an AI job whose guarded write-back is refused for a **structural** reason is classified BEFORE anything is spent, and is retired instead of retried — so the same unattributable `(headerId, jobType)` cannot issue repeated paid model calls. `ActiveAIQueue.writeAdmission(_:target:)` runs inside the read that already captures the job's `AIWriteTarget` (zero extra round trips) and answers `admissible` / `transientlyRefused` / `structurallyRefused`; a structurally refused job returns `ExecutorReport.unattributable` **before** the FTS body read, the NSE lease claim and the LLM call, and `jobCompleted` maps it to the new `JobOutcome.writeRefused`, which takes `abandonWithoutCompletion` — the same arm `.scopeExited` already takes, so **nothing claims the work completed** — and records the job in an in-memory `unattributableJobs` memo so `repopulateOnDrain` cannot turn the backoff into an unthrottled spin. ⚠️ **THE REFUSAL ITSELF IS UNCHANGED — this classifies the refusal's COST, it does not relax the refusal.** Arm 7 is untouched; the alternative to refusing is the C3 misattribution `6b689890d` closed. No row gains an AI field it would not have gained before, because everything skipped here was already being discarded at `aiGuardedHeaderWrite`. **And `maxRetries: .max` is UNTOUCHED everywhere** — the shipped unbounded ladder is proven adequate for the transient case and was restored verbatim rather than weakened; what was added is the one outcome the shipped design never needed, *"the write was refused for a structural reason"*. **Nothing about the refusal is durable:** no column, no flag, no migration, no database write at all. The memo is process-lifetime and `rearmUnattributableJobs()` clears it wholesale from `repopulateFromDatabase()` (launch / foreground / AI re-enable) and `cancelAllInFlight()` (suspension recovery) — the exact moments a sync may have stamped the folder or the row — so a row that later becomes attributable gets its result, on the right row. **A1:** `07a4bb703` carries the identical retry ladder, but there `.needsRetry` could only mean "the model call failed" (`observedUidValidity` does not exist at that tag), so the shipped architecture has no answer to this problem because the problem did not exist in it — authored under A1's "nonexistent" branch. |
| IOS-ROUND3-D3 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — surfaced round 3; pre-candidate; abort-after-purge is the DELIBERATE fail-closed ordering | NSE; staging; `.unreachable`; abort after purge; UIDVALIDITY reset reaction; `runUidValidityResetReaction`; `NSEDataBridge.stagingPurgeTarget`; App Group container; data protection before first unlock | The NSE staging-purge companion can abort on `.unreachable` AFTER a purge has already committed, leaving the reaction partially applied. Pre-candidate (it predates the round-3 band) and recoverable — the reaction re-runs and the folder stays at its old epoch and quarantined, which is the fail-closed direction. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — the abort-after-purge ordering is deliberate, and the code already argues for it.** **Verified at the tip**, `runUidValidityResetReaction`'s order is: step 3 purge transaction (**commits**) → step 3b `ChatIdTranslator.purgeMappingsForFolder` → step 3c `NSEDataBridge.purgeInboxRemovalMarkersForAccount` (**aborts** on false) → step 4 FTS purge (**aborts** on throw) → `NSEDataBridge.purgeStagedStateForFolder` (**aborts** on false) → step 4b releases → step 5 stamp. So yes: an abort can leave the reaction partially applied with headers already purged — and the code states why that is the right way round: *"Quarantine is bounded, visible and retryable; a deleted message is not."* **Basis:** making the purge atomic across two databases and a separate process is exactly the cross-process fence `IOS-NSE-002` asks for and declines — a new mechanism for an edge the epoch model already closes, which THE MANTRA forbids. **Accepted cost:** a partially-applied reaction, with the folder held at its old epoch and quarantined. **Recoverability:** the folder keeps `uidValidityResetPendingAt != nil`, and `SyncEngineDeltaSync`'s per-folder loop (and the full-sync loop) re-drives the reaction on **every** pass. **The state where the re-drive cannot succeed is named rather than waved away:** a permanently-unresolvable App Group container, so `NSEDataBridge.stagingPurgeTarget` returns `.unreachable` forever, leaving the folder quarantined with its rows purged and never resyncing — the user's mail in that folder locally gone. **And it is then discharged:** a permanently-nil App Group container in a correctly-signed build is a provisioning/entitlement **brick** (the NSE would not run at all either), not a state this row can reach; the **reachable** form is the transient one — before first unlock, data protection returns nil — and it clears on unlock, after which the next sync pass re-drives and completes. |

An RFC-less unstamped row is exactly `IOS-AI-002` above and is NOT covered. Same hazard class as `IOS-TEST-002` and the `ProviderNativeActionAdmissionTests` name: a universally-quantified test name that enumerates a subset reads to a later reader as a proof it is not. No production impact. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `b87804055`.** **The invariant that now holds:** the test's display name names the subset it actually constructs. It is now *"An UNSTAMPED but UNREPLACED **RFC-BEARING** row still receives its AI result, and is not recomputed forever"*, and the doc block records that RFC-less unstamped rows are `IOS-AI-002`/`IOS-AI-003` and out of this test's scope — so the gap the name used to hide is now stated by the name and cross-referenced to the rows that own it. The retired name is recorded inline per the established convention, so it stays searchable. |
| IOS-ROUND3-D6 | Surfaced round 3; **class B** — pre-candidate, verbatim at the round-3 base; ⚠️ **ROW CORRECTED IN ROUND 4 — IT STATED THE OPPOSITE OF THE CODE**; ✅ **FIXED in `6b689890d`** after the final audit train confirmed it BLOCKING (C3) — see the resolution at the end of this cell; the "deliberately not fixed" text below is HISTORICAL; its **sync-merge remainder is ✅ CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)**, so the row now has **NO live remainder** | AI write-back; `AccountManagerAI.swift`; `AIWriteTarget.resolveCurrentHeader`; arm 7; missing `Folder` row; `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`; never-observed; fail-OPEN; misattribution; C3 | ⚠️ **WHAT THIS ROW USED TO SAY IS RETRACTED, AND THE RETRACTED TEXT IS KEPT SO THE ERROR STAYS SEARCHABLE.** It read: *"`resolveCurrentHeader` treats a MISSING `Folder` row as 'never observed' rather than as 'we could not look'. The two are the conflation the never-drop rule's clause 2 names, **but here the consumer's direction makes it fail closed**, and the shape is verbatim at the round-3 base — not candidate-attributable."* The first sentence is right; **the direction is BACKWARDS. This consumer fails OPEN.** `AIWriteTarget.resolveCurrentHeader`'s arm 7 is `guard let liveEpoch = folder?.lastKnownUidValidity else { return header }` — an ADMIT, and the function's own doc bullet 7 says so outright (*"⇒ PROCEED"*). The earlier arm `guard folder?.uidValidityResetPendingAt == nil else { return nil }` does NOT catch it: optional chaining on a nil `Folder` yields nil, so that guard PASSES on a missing row. A `Folder` row that is absent — or present but never SELECTed — therefore AUTHORIZES the AI write-back instead of refusing it. **Exact reachability:** the target must be epoch-addressed (`isEpochAddressed` = `.imap`/`.icloud` and not `DemoSeed.demoAccountId`; arm 4 returns the header early otherwise), the folder must carry no pending UIDVALIDITY reset (which a missing row satisfies vacuously), and arm 6's CONTENT PROOF must have already failed. That last condition has two sub-cases and they are **not** equally benign: (a) the captured `rfc822MessageId` is nil/empty, so no witness exists — the ordinary RFC-less IMAP row of `IOS-AI-002`; and (b) the captured id is present and **DISAGREES** with the row now at that address — a POSITIVE disagreement, i.e. precisely the replacement this guard exists to catch, which arm 7 then admits. **Why it matters beyond a stale summary:** `AccountManager.aiGuardedHeaderWrite` mutates the RE-RESOLVED row and writes a `MessageAICache` keyed off it, so an admitted write binds message X's summary/action onto message Y. C3 names **misattribution** as well as mutation, and derived AI content presented against the wrong message is misattribution. **Why the arm exists — do not simply invert it:** its stated purpose is the T1.3 first-sync window and `ScreenshotMode`'s raw-SQL folders, which insert without the column; refusing there would permanently disable AI for those states, including rows with no RFC id at all, which arm 6 can never carry. But the premise it rests on — *"No SELECT has ever reported an epoch here, so no turnover can have been observed either, and there is nothing this address could have been re-seated FROM"* — is sound for a folder row that EXISTS with a nil column and **false for an ABSENT one**, where nothing was looked up at all. An orphaned header is reachable: `SyncEngine.fullSync` deletes a vanished folder's row but RETAINS its headers (no FK since migration v2), so the header keeps a `folderPath` with no metadata and a bare OLD-epoch UID. **Classification — class B, pre-existing on the v3 line.** The identical statement stands at the round-3 base `cec7450e1` (there numbered arm 6a) and the same fail-open direction is already present at `209a55cdf^`, before the candidate range; `e4751e438` (inside `cec7450e1..HEAD`) only NARROWED the set that reaches it, by inserting arm 6's content proof ahead of it. Not introduced by the round-3 or round-4 ranges. **Deliberately NOT fixed in round 4 — `AccountManagerAI.swift` is untouched and the behavioural fix is a separate owner decision.** The honest options (refuse when the `Folder` row is ABSENT while still admitting a present-but-unstamped one; or require a positive "folder exists, epoch never observed" fact before proceeding) trade a C3 hole against permanently disabling AI for first-sync and RFC-less rows, and that trade is the owner's to make. Recorded so the class stays searchable alongside `IOS-EPOCH-001`'s carve-out (3), which inverted the SAME question for the durable-action admission guard (`8f20ab2f4`) and made a missing `Folder` row fail CLOSED there — the two consumers now answer one question in opposite directions, which is the fact this row exists to keep visible. **✅ RESOLUTION — `6b689890d`, "Refuse an AI write-back under an absent or unproven folder epoch".** The final same-round train escalated this from "owner's trade to make" to BLOCKING, and the trade turned out **not to be the one this row described**. Arm 7 now requires a PRESENT folder whose epoch is positively known (`guard let folder, let liveEpoch = SyncEngine.knownUidValidity(folder.lastKnownUidValidity) else { return nil }` — the existing helper, which also rejects a non-positive `0`); arm 8 gained the same positivity check on the captured stamp. Arm 6 is byte-unchanged, so the RFC content witness still carries RFC-bearing rows **including those whose folder row is gone**, and arm 4 is unchanged, so Gmail/Outlook/CalDAV/demo never reach any of it. **Two premises in the text above were refuted while fixing it.** (1) The `ScreenshotMode` justification — cited here and in the arm's own doc comment as the reason refusing would be unacceptable — **was never reachable**: `ScreenshotMode`'s `screenshot-imap` account has folders but is never given any `messageHeader` rows, and the only populated account is `screenshot-account` with `provider: "gmail"`, which exits at arm 4. (2) The remaining cost is **recoverable, not a blackout**: `runSyncMessages` stamps a folder inside the same write transaction that first populates it, an already-populated folder earns its epoch through the verified door, and a refused write leaves `summaryBlurb` nil so the AI queue's own arbiter re-drives the job — pinned by a test showing the same message receiving its result once folder and row are stamped. The divergence this row existed to keep visible is therefore **closed rather than merely documented**: `newGestureRefusedForUnknownEpoch` already refused on both an absent `Folder` row and a nil epoch (`IOS-EPOCH-001`), and the two consumers of that one question now agree. Red proof: arm 7 restored to its pre-fix form, 8 tests run by fully-qualified name, 5 failed with 17 issues and the 3 non-vacuity controls stayed green; the misattribution test reproduced the C3 end state literally (`actionTag → .reply`, `cachedReply → "X's precomputed reply"`, `notified → true` on the replacement row). Three blessing tests that encoded the old permission were re-scoped with their previous display names recorded, never deleted. **Left out of scope and still open:** the SYNC-MERGE half — refusing or quarantining the canonical-UID row when a populated folder has no proven epoch (`providerAddressOwnershipProven` / `uidValidityBootstrapWrite`). This commit closes the AUTHORIZATION half, which is what C3 requires. <br><br>✅ **THE SYNC-MERGE HALF IS NOW CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04), WITH NO CODE WRITTEN. THIS ROW HAS NO LIVE REMAINDER.** The `uidValidityBootstrapWrite` half was already closed by T4.S6b; this closes the other one. **The decision: the sync merge is left exactly as it is, and no refusal or quarantine is added there.** **The reason is STRUCTURAL, not a judgement call.** The merge writes an *address* (`MessageHeader.observedUidValidity`), but *trust* lives on `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`, and **the merge is structurally incapable of setting that column**: `SyncEngine.bootstrapFolderUidValidity(_:folderId:observed:)` carries a literal `AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM messageHeader WHERE folderId = :folderId)` term in its `UPDATE`, and `SyncEngine.uidValidityBootstrapWrite(observed:stored:folderHoldsRows:)` returns nil outright when `folderHoldsRows` (a Bool the caller MUST read from the same write transaction as the write). Both are therefore a **no-op for any populated folder**, which is the entire population this remainder is about. **All four durable consumers gate on the FOLDER column, not on the merged address — verified at the tip, each one read rather than assumed:** `AccountManager.newGestureRefusedForUnknownEpoch` returns `true` (refuse) on a nil folder epoch and on an absent `Folder` row; **checkpoint A** in `AccountManager`'s drain (in `AccountManagerQueue.swift`) requires `let live = sourceFolder.lastKnownUidValidity` with `UInt32(exactly: live) > 0` or the op is **not claimed** — its own comment reads *"ABSENCE OF EVIDENCE — never a drop. The row is left `queued` and simply not claimed this pass"*, so there is **no wire I/O and the intention is preserved**; `AIWriteTarget.resolveCurrentHeader` arm 7 is `guard let folder, let liveEpoch = SyncEngine.knownUidValidity(folder.lastKnownUidValidity) else { return nil }`, refusing on the same fact; and `SyncEngine.runDeletionReconcileWalk` aborts with reason `"epoch unverified"`. **The only durable op the merge itself inserts is ReplyDetect's `.setTag`** (`PendingOperation(type: .setTag, …)`, at the merge's tag-clear sites), and `.setTag`/`.removeTag` share an executor arm whose entire body is `break` — **local-only per ADR-IOS-036, no provider write** — which is exactly why it is deliberately excluded from checkpoint A. **No consumer treats the merged address as identity**, so nothing downstream can mutate a wrong message on the strength of it, and C3 is not reachable through this half. 🚨 **REFUSING AT THE MERGE WOULD BE THE MIRROR-IMAGE BUG (`MIS-005`), which is the substantive reason this is a decision and not a deferral.** The refusal branch would sit inside `if var existing = recon.row` (`SyncEngineFullSync`), so a fresh RFC-bearing INSERT would still let the verified bootstrap door stamp the folder — after which a squatting old-epoch row is gestured with **every** field stale rather than only subject/snippet/body/AI. **The refusal makes the outcome worse, not safer.** **Accepted cost:** a populated, nil-epoch folder's rows carry a merged address that no consumer trusts, and every durable path on them fails closed. **Reachability, named rather than waved at:** it needs the `IOS-AI-003` population — an all-RFC-less, populated, nil-epoch IMAP folder, which that code's own comment calls *"Pathological"* — **conjoined with** a genuine UIDVALIDITY renumber. Both halves are already registered and adjudicated, as `IOS-AI-003` and `IOS-EPOCH-001` (closed as a decision 2026-08-04). **Recoverability, with the non-firing case named:** the ordinary path recovers when the folder earns its epoch through the verified door or the reset reaction's fresh stamp; the state where that does NOT fire is the same all-RFC-less population, where `verifyAndBootstrapPrePopulatedFolderEpoch` returns `.unobservable` and only an unrelated future RFC-bearing message in that folder unlocks it. `MIS-IOS-008` records the false-recoverability claim `6b689890d`'s body made for this same population, so the claim is **not** repeated here: for this sub-population the refusal is indefinite, and that is accepted because every consequence of it is a refusal, never a wrong write. <br><br>⚠️ **ACCEPTED-COST CLAUSE WIDENED (2026-08-04) — the decision is UNCHANGED; only its statement of the cost was too narrow.** The Codex half of the `e4dd08e92` swarm re-derived this exact hole independently (`providerAddressOwnershipProven` returns true on `row.id == canonicalId` alone under any positive `sourceBoundEpoch`, without requiring the pre-existing row's own `observedUidValidity` to match — even a positive disagreement is ignored) and named a consequence this cell's accepted-cost sentence did not: **the merge keeps the EXISTING row, so its `MessageBody` and `MessageAICache` — both keyed on `MessageHeader.id`, which does not change on a canonical hit — survive while subject / from / date / snippet are overwritten with the new message's.** The user is then shown message **Y**'s headers over message **X**'s body. That is a DISPLAY splice, not a durable-path consequence, and the sentence *"a populated, nil-epoch folder's rows carry a merged address that no consumer trusts, and every durable path on them fails closed"* does not cover it. **The decision still stands on its stated basis** — the merge is structurally incapable of writing `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity` (`bootstrapFolderUidValidity` carries `AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM messageHeader WHERE folderId = :folderId)`), all four durable consumers gate on that column, and refusing at the merge is the `MIS-005` mirror image that makes the squatting row worse (every field stale instead of body/AI only). **The cost is simply larger than recorded, and is now recorded.** Reachability is unchanged and remains the conjunction of the `IOS-AI-003` population (an all-RFC-less, populated, nil-epoch IMAP folder — `verifyAndBootstrapPrePopulatedFolderEpoch` returns `.unobservable` on it, and also on a thrown sample read, a thrown sample FETCH and a failed stamp write) with a genuine UIDVALIDITY renumber. |

## Audit round 5 — surfaced, deliberately NOT fixed

Round 5's one **BLOCKING** finding is fixed separately, not here: `IMAPProvider.liveSourceUIDs` — the
post-`COPY` liveness probe — issued one UNBOUNDED `UID FETCH` (a wedge: the whole-operation deadline
blew inside it after the COPY had landed) and folded an unparsed UID into the same `continue` as an
unrequested one, reading "we could not determine the answer" as proof the member had left the source
mailbox (`8b7e7044c`). The five rows below are **REGISTRABLE** under this repo's bar (BLOCKING = C3
wrong-message mutation/misattribution, a dropped user intention including the wedge corollary, a
brick, or secret exposure) and are deliberately NOT fixed. The closest call is `IOS-OUTBOX-004`: it
does put a durable wire mutation on the server that the user never asked for, but the mutation lands
on the **correct** message, no queued op is dropped, and the send itself is delivered — a
wrong-FLAG-on-the-right-message, not a wrong-message mutation.

**Every row was verified against the code rather than transcribed from the reviewers, and the
reviewers were corrected in five places.** Finding 1 was **understated** — a second consumer of the
same captured value flips the PERSISTED row's own `isForward`. Finding 2 was **overstated** — only
the T3.4 bullet is inverted; the T3.2/T3.15 purge bullet is still accurate, and a blanket "the
suite documentation describes the inverse of tip behaviour" would itself have become a wrong register
entry. Finding 3's claimed "opposite shape" is **refuted** as unreachable at HEAD, and the
attribution question it left open has **three** answers, not one. Finding 4 was **understated** —
five post-mutation refusal sites, not three. Finding 5's count of post-`COPY` throw sites was
**corrected from five to ten**, counted from the `move` body rather than repeated.

⚠️ **`IOS-QUEUE-004`'s census was re-verified against a MOVED `HEAD` at apply time and CORRECTED
before it landed** — the row was drafted at `2d85b8729` and `8b7e7044c` added a third conformer in
between. The correction is recorded in the row itself rather than silently applied, because that row
is *about* register/doc claims outliving the code they describe. Attribution classes: **A** =
introduced by `2cfc4d979..2d85b8729` (or, where stated, by `8b7e7044c`); **B** = pre-existing on the
v3 line but absent from shipped `v1.6.38` (`07a4bb703`); **C** = shipped verbatim in `v1.6.38`. Every
B/C claim was checked with `git show 07a4bb703:<path>` / `git show 2cfc4d979:<path>`, never assumed.
