| IOS-OUTBOX-004 | Surfaced audit round 5; **class A** consequence (`a1f17499a`) riding a **class B** precondition; **REGISTRABLE**; ✅ **FIXED by `f2e918a4e`** | Undo-Send; reopen; forward; `UndoReopenCompose.composeView(for:)`; `ComposeView.isForward`; `AuthoredSendSnapshot.isForward`; `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend`; `OutboxMessage.isForward`; `deleteCompletedSendAtomic`; `OperationType.markReplied`; `\Answered`; `$Forwarded`; Reply action tag | **A forward that is undone and re-sent is recorded, locally and on the server, as a REPLY.** `UndoReopenCompose.composeView(for:)` builds `ComposeView(account:prefillDraftId:retainedDraftAuthority:)` and supplies **no** `isForward`, so the view's `isForward` property takes its `= false` default. The compose still LOOKS and QUOTES like a forward, because `loadDraftOrPrepopulate` reads the persisted row (`quoteIsForward = draft.isForward`, and `resolvedForward = replyTo != nil ? isForward : draft.isForward` — `replyTo` is nil here, so the ROW wins) — but `ComposeView.send` captures the **view** property into `AuthoredSendSnapshot.isForward`, and that snapshot is what both durable consumers read. **Concrete reachability:** IMAP/iCloud account; user opens a message and taps Forward (`MessageDetailView` → `ComposeView(replyTo: msg, isForward: true)`, and `MessageDetailView+Actions` for the swipe entry); sends; taps Undo on the send toast inside the hold window. `PendingSendService.undo()` RETAINS the `Draft` row (it returns a `RetainedDraftAuthority`; it does not delete) and `RootView` presents `UndoReopenCompose`. The user taps Send again. **Wrong outcome, four parts:** (1) `AccountManagerOutbox.persistQueuedSend` takes the `else` arm and runs `UPDATE messageHeader SET isReplied = 1` on the parent instead of `isForwarded`; (2) the same arm CLEARS a `reply` action tag the parent legitimately carried (the user forwarded, they did not reply); (3) `OutboxMessage.isForward` is stored false, so on delivery `AccountManagerOutbox.deleteCompletedSendAtomic` queues `PendingOperation(type: .markReplied)`; (4) `IMAPProvider.markReplied` STOREs `[.answered]`, so the server gets **`\Answered`** and never `$Forwarded` (`IMAPProvider.markForwarded`'s `.custom("$Forwarded")` is never reached). Sync does not repair it — `SyncEngineDeltaSync`/`SyncEngineFullSync` assign `header.isForwarded = info.isForwarded` from server flags, so the next pass CLEARS the locally-correct forwarded badge (set optimistically by the FIRST, discarded send) and leaves the wrong `isReplied`. **Attribution.** The missing argument is **class B** — `UndoReopenCompose.swift` is byte-identical at `2cfc4d979` and HEAD. The HARM is **class A**: before `a1f17499a`, `ComposeView.send` passed `replyTo?.id` (nil on this path) as `replyToHeaderId`, so `persistQueuedSend`'s `guard let originalId = replyToHeaderId` short-circuited and NO flag, tag or op was written at all; `a1f17499a` correctly restored the parent (`snapshot.resolvedReplyTarget?.id`) and thereby armed the wrong-flag branch. **Shipped `v1.6.38` does NOT have this defect** — `git show 07a4bb703:TabMail/Views/Compose/UndoReopenCompose.swift` passes `isForward: snapshot.isForward` and `PendingSendService.ReopenSnapshot` there carries an `isForward` field, populated from `draft.isForward`. The v3 reopen was re-shaped around `RetainedDraftAuthority` and the mode was not carried across. **A SECOND, wider consequence of the same capture, not previously reported:** `ComposeView.send`'s save-before-send writes `ownedDraft = Draft(… isForward: snapshot.isForward …)`, so the resend also FLIPS the persisted row's `isForward` to false while its primary key stays `forward:<accountId>:<stableId>`. A later reopen of that row then renders reply attribution for a forward, and `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader`'s Strategy 2 dead-ends on `guard draftKey.hasPrefix(prefix)` (prefix `"reply:"` against a `"forward:"` key), which can escalate a later send from mis-flagged to BLOCKED. That write is class B (present at `2cfc4d979`), but it is only reachable through the class-A activation above. **Why REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING:** the mutation lands on the correct message (`resolveOriginalMessage` resolves the real parent), no queued op is dropped or wedged, the forward itself is delivered with its own body and attachments, and nothing is misattributed to a different message — so it is outside the non-recoverable set. It is registered rather than shrugged off because sync converges on the WRONG answer rather than repairing it, and the user has no in-app way to clear a server-side `\Answered`. **Test coverage does not reach it:** `ComposeSendReplyTargetTests.undoSendReopenSendsAndThreads` (suite type `ComposeSendReplyTargetTests`, file `TabMailTests/Views/ComposeSendReplyTargetTests.swift`) constructs `draftId = "reply:acc1:orig@example.com"` with `insertDraft`'s default `isForward: false` and drives its send through `driveSend`, whose own `isForward` is an independent parameter defaulting to false — so the suite never models a forward, and never models the view-property-versus-row divergence at all. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `f2e918a4e`, "Restore the forward mode on Undo reopen; guard the reply-target read".** **The invariant that now holds:** the mode a resend is recorded under is the mode of the **persisted draft row**, on every compose entry point including the Undo-Send reopen — so a forward that is undone and re-sent is recorded as a **forward**, locally and on the server, and the parent gets `isForwarded` + `$Forwarded` rather than `isReplied` + `\Answered`, with no legitimate Reply action tag cleared. **A1 — this is RESTORATION, not authoring:** shipped `07a4bb703:UndoReopenCompose.swift` builds the reopened compose with `isForward: snapshot.isForward`, its `ReopenSnapshot.isForward` populated from `draft.isForward`; v3's `ReopenSnapshot` is `{ id, authority }`, so the SAME source of truth — the retained row's own `isForward` column — is read in the one `dbPool.read` that already ran for `Account`. **A7 census re-run independently:** production sites that bind a persisted `Draft` row are exactly two — `DraftComposePresenter` (which already passed the mode) and `UndoReopenCompose` (which passed none); `ReopenSnapshot` has exactly one consumer; every other `ComposeView(` site supplies `replyTo` and, for forwards, `isForward: true`, and a fresh reply/forward derives its draft key FROM `isForward`, so property and row agree by construction. **The instance IS the class: one site.** **ONE addition on top of the restoration** (A1 corollary 3 — the release is a floor, not a ceiling): shipped read the mode through a `try?`, and a thrown read there would silently supply `false` and re-create this exact defect, so the retained-authority branch of `loadDraftOrPrepopulate` now **fails closed** when the row's `isForward` disagrees with the mode the view was constructed with — a disagreement that can only mean *"we could not read the mode"*, never a fact about it. Scoped to the retained-authority path deliberately, so a row already flipped by the superseded bug still opens normally everywhere else. |
| IOS-TEST-003 | Surfaced audit round 5; **class A** (`ede315ed8`); test-register accuracy only, no production effect; ✅ **FIXED by `b87804055`** | Tests; `IMAPMoveWireContractTests`; suite doc; T3.4; `COPYUID`; UIDPLUS; `IMAPProvider.liveSourceUIDs`; `\Deleted` STORE; stale documentation | **`IMAPMoveWireContractTests`' suite-level doc comment states the INVERSE of tip behaviour for its T3.4 bullet.** The header says *"A server that advertises UIDPLUS is held to per-member `COPYUID`, so a member it did not name survives while its named sibling is cleaned, and a server that names none of them cleans nothing."* At the tip that is false for the SOFT-DELETE leg on both halves. `IMAPProvider.move`'s authorization gate takes the `else` arm whenever `copyProvenUIDs.count != sourceUIDs.count`, calls `IMAPProvider.liveSourceUIDs` (a `UID FETCH .uidFlagsOnly` of the op's own source UIDs), and sets `authorizedUIDs = live` — so every requested member the source still holds gets the `\Deleted` STORE, named or not, and the provider's own comment says so outright (*"WHY FACT 2 IS NOW ALLOWED ON A UIDPLUS SERVER TOO, which round 3 deliberately did not do"*). A UIDPLUS server naming NONE of the members therefore does not "clean nothing": it soft-deletes every live member. **What the header still gets right, and must not be over-corrected:** the PURGE leg is unchanged — `purgeAuthorizedUIDs = copyProvenUIDs ∩ live`, gated additionally on `serverSupportsUIDPlus` before `server.expunge(messages:)` — so the T3.2/T3.15 bullet (*"UIDPLUS purges exactly the named UID; no-UIDPLUS purges nothing"*) is accurate. Only the T3.4 bullet, which speaks of soft-delete and purge together as one authorization, is inverted. **Concrete reachability:** a reviewer or future implementer reads the suite header to learn the move's cleanup contract before touching `IMAPProvider.move`, concludes an unnamed member is left untouched in the source, and reasons about a fix (or a "simplification") from a premise the code contradicts — the same hazard class as `IOS-TEST-002` and `IOS-ROUND3-D5`, where a doc/name promised more than the assertions delivered. **Attribution — class A, mechanically checked:** the header text at `2cfc4d979` is byte-identical to HEAD (`git show 2cfc4d979:TabMailTests/Providers/IMAPMoveWireContractTests.swift`, lines 8–40), and `ede315ed8` is the ONLY commit in `2cfc4d979..2d85b8729` touching that file — it changed the production authorization gate and updated the tests without updating the suite-level header, so the doc was accurate at the range base and became wrong inside the range. **Not fixed here** because it is documentation-only with no production effect: it is registered and carried to the final audit train rather than fixed mid-round, and the correction is a header rewrite of the T3.4 bullet only. ⚠ **It will not fix itself incidentally.** `8b7e7044c` added 309 lines of new tests to `TabMailTests/Providers/IMAPMoveWireContractTests.swift` after this row was drafted and left the suite header untouched — a large edit to that very file has already passed over the inverted bullet once. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `b87804055`, "Close the test-accuracy cluster: 5 vacuous tests, 4 wrong names"** (verified: that commit touches `TabMailTests/Providers/IMAPMoveWireContractTests.swift`, so the closure is backed by an edit to the file this row is about, not only by the commit's prose). **The invariant that now holds:** `IMAPMoveWireContractTests`' suite-level doc describes the move's cleanup contract as the code implements it — the **soft-delete** leg authorizes every requested member the source still holds, `COPYUID`-named or not, while the **purge** leg remains `copyProvenUIDs ∩ live` gated additionally on `serverSupportsUIDPlus`. The T3.2/T3.15 purge bullet, which was always accurate, was NOT over-corrected. The hazard this row named — a reviewer reading the suite header to learn the contract before touching `IMAPProvider.move`, and reasoning about a "simplification" from a premise the code contradicts — is closed at the artefact a reviewer actually reads. |
| IOS-DRAFT-010 | Surfaced audit round 5; consequence **class A** (`a1f17499a`), the swallow itself **class C** in substance / **class B** in its present form; **REGISTRABLE**; ✅ **FIXED by `f2e918a4e`** | Drafts; compose; `ComposeView.loadDraftOrPrepopulate`; `try?`; `Draft.resolveReplyQuote`; `ComposeDraftGuards.persistedReplyTargetClaim`; `.unresolved`; blocked send; `DraftComposePresenter`; GRDB suspension; `SQLITE_BUSY`; clause-2 conflation | **`ComposeView.loadDraftOrPrepopulate` still `try?`-swallows the reply-target read that now GATES the send, so "we could not look" is presented to the user as "this parent can no longer be identified" — the clause-2 conflation `a1f17499a` removed one layer up, re-created one layer down.** The read is `let quote: Draft.ReplyQuote? = try? await AppDatabase.dbPool.read { try Draft.resolveReplyQuote(…) }`; its result feeds `ComposeDraftGuards.persistedReplyTargetClaim(rowClaimsTarget: draft.isReplyOrForward, resolved: quote?.header)`, and for a row that CLAIMS a parent a nil answer maps to `.unresolved`, which sets `ComposeView.unresolvedReplyTargetMessage` (*"The message this draft replies to can no longer be identified, so it can't be sent."*), disables Send and hard-`guard`s in `send()`. A thrown read and a genuine refusal arrive as the same nil. **The asymmetry inside the same function is the tell:** the DRAFT ROW read three statements earlier is `do`/`catch` into `ComposeDraftGuards.readState`, i.e. it distinguishes a throw; the reply-target read does not. `DraftComposePresenter.resolveReplyTo` — the sibling that answers the same question one layer up — was deliberately moved to the `db`-scoped overload precisely so it could PROPAGATE the throw into `.loadFailed`, and its doc says so. **Concrete reachability:** open a persisted reply/forward draft while the database is momentarily unreadable — a GRDB suspension (`0xdead10cc` class, ADR-IOS-041/046) on a background-to-foreground boundary, or `SQLITE_BUSY` behind a contended writer. The row is read successfully, the quote read throws, `.unresolved` is produced, and a draft whose parent is perfectly resolvable becomes UNSENDABLE with a message that asserts an identity failure that did not happen. The Undo-Send reopen path is the worst case because `UndoReopenCompose` presents `ComposeView` directly with no presenter above it, so this `try?` is the ONLY read of the reply target on that path. **Recovery, which is why this is REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING:** the draft stays openable, editable, savable and discardable (`SendReplyTarget.blocked` blocks the send and nothing else), and one ordinary user gesture — close and reopen the draft — re-runs the read. Nothing is dropped, nothing mutates the wrong message. **Attribution, three separate answers because the reviewer's single-class framing does not survive the check:** the CONSEQUENCE (a swallowed throw now blocks the send) is **class A** — `ComposeDraftGuards.persistedReplyTargetClaim`, `sendReplyTarget` and the `.unresolved` arm are all introduced by `a1f17499a`, and at `2cfc4d979` the same `try?` fed only `if let quote` (quote/attribution) with no claim recorded. The `try?` in its PRESENT form is **class B** — the `resolveReplyQuote` call site exists at `2cfc4d979` and does not exist at `07a4bb703`. The SWALLOW itself is **class C** in substance: `git show 07a4bb703:TabMail/Views/Compose/ComposeView.swift` reaches the reply target through `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader(draftKey:replyToId:isForward:)`'s non-`db` convenience overload, whose whole body was `try? AppDatabase.dbPool.read { … }` (that overload was deleted as dead code by `c63b7a3cb`, and its body is preserved verbatim in `ReplyTargetThrownReadTests`' `swallowingResolveReplyToHeader`). **Test coverage does not reach it:** `ComposeSendReplyTargetTests`' harness reproduces this step through the `loadClaim` helper, which reads with a plain `try await pool.read` and therefore cannot observe the swallow; `ReplyTargetThrownReadTests` proves the distinction only for the PRESENTER's overload, not for this call site. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `f2e918a4e`, "Restore the forward mode on Undo reopen; guard the reply-target read".** **The invariant that now holds:** a THROWN reply-target read and a genuine identity refusal are no longer the same nil. The read is carried as a `Result` and classified by `ComposeDraftGuards.readState` — **the SAME guard the draft-row read three statements earlier already used, so no new helper was minted** — and a `.error` takes the SAME arm that read takes. The user is therefore never told *"The message this draft replies to can no longer be identified, so it can't be sent"* on the strength of an assertion about identity the database never made; that was the never-drop clause-2 conflation (`MIS-IOS-004`) re-created one layer below the one `a1f17499a` removed. **Failure direction, stated because getting it wrong drops a send or double-sends:** it FAILS CLOSED — block the send, keep the compose open, surface the retryable message. Outbox Rule 4 (*"prefer double-send over drop"*) governs an intention already PERSISTED to the outbox, and nothing is queued at this point; the durable artefact here is the `Draft` row, which this arm leaves **byte-identical**. Outbox Rule 1 is the rule that governs — the compose view is the user's last chance, so show the error and do NOT dismiss — and failing open would ship the reply against an unproven parent, which is precisely the *"reply leaves as a brand-new message, parent bookkeeping skipped"* defect the surrounding guard family exists to prevent. **Recovery is one ordinary gesture:** close and reopen the draft re-runs the read. **A1:** shipped `07a4bb703` reaches the reply target through `Draft.resolveReplyToHeader`'s non-`db` convenience overload, whose entire body was `try? AppDatabase.dbPool.read { … }` — so shipped has the SAME swallow and does not solve this; shipped's swallow was harmless only because nothing gated on it, and the gate is class A (`a1f17499a`). Restoring shipped was therefore not available and this arm is authored. |
| IOS-QUEUE-004 | Surfaced audit round 5; **class A** (`ede315ed8`), with a further **class A** conformer added by `8b7e7044c`; comment-only, no behaviour change; ✅ **FIXED by `8bf28f3fe`**; ⚠️ **census CORRECTED TWICE — at apply time against a moved `HEAD`, and again by the fix, which found the ASSIGNING TRIAGE had undercounted the pre-mutation sites** | Action queue; `AccountManagerQueue`; `DrainContext.evidenceRefused`; `ProviderEvidenceUnavailable`; `IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal.movedAcrossCopy`; `IMAPEpochEvidenceMissing`; `IMAPLivenessProbeInconclusive`; A3/A4/A5; post-mutation refusal; stale doc comment | **`DrainContext.evidenceRefused`'s doc comment claims the surviving refusals *"are raised BEFORE any wire mutation, so the bound is now about round trips rather than duplicates"*. Both halves are false at the tip.** `ProviderEvidenceUnavailable` has exactly **three** conformers (`IMAPProvider.IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal`, `IMAPProvider.IMAPEpochEvidenceMissing`, `IMAPProvider.IMAPLivenessProbeInconclusive`) and **five** reachable refusal sites in `IMAPProvider.move`; only ONE of the five is pre-mutation. (a) `IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal.unknownAtProbe` is raised at the destination probe, before the COPY — the doc is right about this one. (b) `IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal.movedAcrossCopy` is raised from the `catch` around the destination-epoch comparison, whose whole input is the server's `COPYUID` response — it cannot exist before the `UID COPY` has been executed and committed. (c) `IMAPLivenessProbeInconclusive.unparsedUid(folder:requested:)` is thrown from `IMAPProvider.liveSourceUIDs`, which `move` calls ONLY on the `copyProvenUIDs.count != sourceUIDs.count` arm — i.e. after the `UID COPY`, by construction. (d) `IMAPEpochEvidenceMissing` (from `requireUidValidity`, either `unknownLiveEpoch` on a zero SELECT epoch or `unknownAdmittedEpoch` on a zero admitted epoch) at assertion **A4**, which sits after the COPY and after the liveness probe. (e) the same type at assertion **A5**, which sits after the COPY *and* after the `\Deleted` STORE. A minor fifth counterexample: at **A3** the same type is reachable after the INBOX-only legacy `tm_*` flag-strip STORE, though that strip is idempotent and reversible. **The strongest evidence that the comment is stale is inside the tree it describes:** `IMAPLivenessProbeInconclusive`'s own doc comment states that the refusal *"is raised AFTER the `UID COPY`, so bounding the op to one attempt per drain bounds the destination duplicates a re-attempt would seat"* — the exact opposite of the `evidenceRefused` paragraph, in the same repository, landed in the same session. **Concrete reachability:** a UIDPLUS-advertising IMAP server whose SELECT stops reporting `UIDVALIDITY` (SwiftMail defaults `Mailbox.Selection.uidValidity` to `UIDValidity(0)`, which `requireUidValidity`'s `live > 0` guard turns into `unknownLiveEpoch`) between the COPY and A4. The op has already copied to the destination, is requeued by the `error is ProviderEvidenceUnavailable` arm, is skipped for the rest of THIS drain by `evidenceRefused` — and re-attempted on the next drain, where the COPY runs again and seats another destination copy. So `evidenceRefused` is still doing exactly the duplicate-bounding job the comment says it no longer needs to do, which is `IOS-IMAP-006` below. **No behaviour change is proposed:** the skip is correct, the `.haltLane` disposition is correct, and the comment's own closing sentence (*"a future producer may again refuse post-mutation"*) is the right instinct — it is merely already true, twice over. **Attribution — class A, in two increments.** The paragraph was written by `ede315ed8`, whose deletion of `IMAPMoveEvidenceUnavailable.noCopyUidEvidence` it describes; the surviving producers it enumerates were not re-checked against the A4/A5 sites that stand in the same function. `8b7e7044c` then added the third conformer, so the paragraph's *"the surviving producers — the destination-epoch and source-epoch refusals"* no longer even enumerates the conformer set. ⚠️ **CENSUS CORRECTED AT APPLY TIME, and the correction is recorded rather than silently applied.** This row was drafted and verified at `2d85b8729`, where it read *"exactly two conformers … and four reachable refusal sites … only ONE of the four is pre-mutation"*. `8b7e7044c` landed between drafting and application, and the re-grep before pasting found the third conformer, so the counts above are the re-verified ones. The retracted draft figures are kept in this sentence because this row is precisely about a documentation claim outliving the code it describes; landing it stale would have been self-refuting. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `8bf28f3fe`, "Correct the evidence-refusal census in the drain's skip comment" — comment-only, no behaviour change, no test.** **The invariant that now holds:** `DrainContext.evidenceRefused`'s doc states the censused truth, and the retraction is written INTO the comment rather than silently applied, because this is exactly a case of documentation outliving the code it describes. **⚠️ AND THE CENSUS ABOVE IS CORRECTED — the pre-mutation set is larger than this row (and the triage that assigned it) counted.** Both got the conclusion right and the arithmetic wrong: **`IMAPProvider.move`'s A1 wrapper selection and A2 post-destination-probe source re-SELECT `requireUidValidity` throws are ALSO pre-mutation**, alongside `IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal.unknownAtProbe` at the destination probe — which is what made the retracted *"raised BEFORE any wire mutation"* sentence plausible in the first place. The corrected census is therefore: `ProviderEvidenceUnavailable` has **three** conformers (`IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal`, `IMAPEpochEvidenceMissing`, `IMAPLivenessProbeInconclusive`), and the refusal sites split into **three pre-mutation** (A1, A2, `unknownAtProbe`) and **four post-`UID COPY`**: `movedAcrossCopy` (whose whole input is the `COPYUID` response), `unparsedUid` from `liveSourceUIDs` (reached only on the `copyProvenUIDs.count != sourceUIDs.count` arm), A4 (before the `\Deleted` STORE) and A5 (before the `UID EXPUNGE`) — with A3 a fifth post-mutation site that costs nothing to repeat, sitting after the INBOX-only legacy `tm_*` flag strip, which is idempotent and reversible. **The conclusion survives the correction intact:** four post-COPY sites is what matters, so the once-per-drain bound is STILL about destination duplicates and not merely round trips — a UIDPLUS server that stops reporting `UIDVALIDITY` on SELECT between the COPY and A4 leaves the op copied and requeued, and every re-attempt seats another copy (`IOS-IMAP-006`). The skip stays for both reasons: it is the general contract for `ProviderEvidenceUnavailable`, not a patch for one error case, **and** four of its sites already refuse post-mutation today. **This row is now closed on its own subject matter** — a stale doc claim — having required one more census correction on the way, which is the fact worth carrying: two independent counts of the same five-branch function both came out wrong, in the same direction, and only counting from the `move` body rather than repeating a prior count found it. |
| IOS-IMAP-006 | Surfaced audit round 5; **RETRACTS** the round-4 sentence in this file's `4e08ff720` commit-body-correction row; ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — the class is inherent to owning the sequence; residual class **C/B** hazard with a **class A** increment on the non-UIDPLUS path (`ede315ed8`) | IMAP; `IMAPProvider.move`; post-`COPY` throw; duplicate at destination; drain retry; `DrainContext.evidenceRefused`; A4; A5; `liveSourceUIDs`; non-UIDPLUS | ⚠️ **THE `4e08ff720` CORRECTION ROW'S ROUND-4 CLAIM IS OVERSTATED AND IS RETRACTED HERE.** It reads: *"both costs are now **unreachable rather than bounded** — the throw site is deleted … so neither the duplicate-per-drain nor the duplicate-per-pass can be incurred by a move."* What `ede315ed8` actually made unreachable is **those two specific producers**: `IMAPMoveEvidenceUnavailable.noCopyUidEvidence` is deleted, and `retirePartiallyCompletedOp` has no current producer because `IMAPProvider.move` now dispositions every member. The CLASS is not unreachable. **The residual, stated as the class rather than as an instance:** `IMAPProvider.move` issues `server.copy(messages: sourceUIDs, to: destination)` and then performs further awaited work; ANY throw after that point leaves the op requeued (`.haltLane` in both the `ProviderEvidenceUnavailable` arm and the generic connection arm), and the next drain re-enters `move` from the top and re-issues the same `UID COPY` against a source copy that is still present — seating a second copy at the destination. `evidenceRefused` bounds this to at most one duplicate per DRAIN, which is exactly the bound the retracted sentence says can no longer be incurred. **The real count is TEN post-`COPY` throw sites, not five** (enumerated by step, in order): (1) the `Task.checkCancellation()` immediately after the COPY; (2) `throw IMAPDestinationEpochRefusal.movedAcrossCopy`; (3) the liveness probe `liveSourceUIDs` (a `UID FETCH`, so any network or parse failure); (4) A4's source re-SELECT (`selectMailboxTracked`); (5) A4's `requireUidValidity`; (6) the `\Deleted` `server.store`; (7) the second `Task.checkCancellation()`; (8) A5's source re-SELECT; (9) A5's `requireUidValidity`; (10) `server.expunge(messages: purgeAuthorizedUIDs)`. Sites 4–10 are byte-for-byte the same sites that stood at `2cfc4d979`. **Concrete reachability:** an IMAP move to Archive on a server without UIDPLUS; the COPY succeeds; the connection drops during the `liveSourceUIDs` FETCH or during A4's re-SELECT; the generic drain arm requeues the op and marks the account failed for this drain; the next drain re-runs the COPY and the user now has two copies in Archive, with the original still in INBOX. The source copy is never wrongly destroyed (the purge stays gated on `serverSupportsUIDPlus` AND per-member `COPYUID`), which is why this is a duplicate, not a loss. **Attribution.** The class is **C/B** — sites 4–10 predate the range and the shape (COPY, then more awaited work, then requeue-on-throw) is inherent to owning the sequence, which `v1.6.38` did not do (it delegated to SwiftMail's `move`). The range's **class A** increment is site 3: on the NON-UIDPLUS hot path the probe is always reached (`copyProvenUIDs` is empty, so the equality branch is never taken), and at `2cfc4d979` that class had NINE post-COPY throw sites, because the deleted `noCopyUidEvidence` guard was unreachable for it (`authorizedUIDs = serverSupportsUIDPlus ? copyProven… : sourceUIDs` was never empty there). Across all server classes the range is net-neutral (one site deleted, one added); on the non-UIDPLUS path it is +1. For a UIDPLUS server naming every member both the base and the tip have nine, because the tip skips the probe entirely on the `copyProvenUIDs.count == sourceUIDs.count` branch. **Scope of this row:** the RESIDUAL class only — "a post-COPY throw for network reasons still costs a duplicate on the next drain". The liveness probe's own oversized-FETCH and unparsed-UID defects were fixed separately (`8b7e7044c`) and are NOT this row; that fix does not narrow the class — site 3 remains a throw site (its new `IMAPLivenessProbeInconclusive` refusal joins the class rather than widening it, since `evidenceRefused` bounds it to one attempt per drain) and sites 1–2 and 4–10 are untouched. **Why REGISTRABLE:** duplicated mail is recoverable by the user and this repo's stated ranking (`IOS-IMAP-001`, `retirePartiallyCompletedOp`'s doc, the never-drop rule) prefers it to both a dropped intention and a wedge; no message is destroyed and no mutation lands on a different message. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — the class is INHERENT to owning the sequence, and removing it would trade a C3 guard for a UX nicety.** **The decision:** a post-`COPY` throw is allowed to cost at most one duplicate at the destination per drain. **Why it cannot be closed as written:** any COPY followed by further awaited work, with requeue-on-throw, can re-COPY — the ten enumerated throw sites ARE that shape, and sites 4–10 are byte-for-byte the ones that stood at `2cfc4d979`. The only construction that removes the class is atomic `UID MOVE`, which `IOS-IMAP-003` deliberately gave up in exchange for the five epoch assertions on **C3** grounds; reversing that trade to remove a *recoverable duplicate* inverts this repo's ranking outright. Everything short of it — a durable "COPY already issued" receipt — is exactly the receipt/alias machinery `MIS-IOS-003` and `CLAUDE.md` § *THE ADDRESS PROBLEM* forbid. **Accepted cost:** a duplicate at the destination with the original still in the source. `DrainContext.evidenceRefused` bounds the evidence-refused subset to one attempt per drain, and the generic connection arm additionally inserts the account into `failedAccounts`, stopping it for the rest of that drain. **Recoverability:** one ordinary gesture — delete the duplicate. **The state where the duplicate cannot be deleted was searched for:** it would require the destination folder's epoch to be unknown (the `IOS-EPOCH-001` window) — **and in that window the original move would not have been admitted either**, so the state does not arise from this path. **None found.** The source copy is never wrongly destroyed, because the purge stays gated on `serverSupportsUIDPlus` AND per-member `COPYUID`, so the worst case is duplication and never loss. **The round-4 retraction this row carries stands unchanged and must not be re-flattened**: `ede315ed8` made two specific PRODUCERS unreachable, not the class. |

## Drain-time move re-key — accepted residual

`59423bb7d` and `f7c3354c5` close THE ADDRESS PROBLEM for the case the wire already answered: the
drain re-keys each moved row to the destination address `COPYUID` named, and undo of an
already-drained move is then an ordinary reverse move admitted through the same predicate as any
forward gesture. The row below is what remains when the wire does NOT answer, or answers in a shape
the pairing guard refuses. It is registered rather than fixed.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-IMAP-007 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — there is no third option; introduced-adjacent by `59423bb7d` + `f7c3354c5` (the limitation is inherent to `COPYUID`, not to the change); ⚠️ **scope CORRECTED at apply time — the draft named ONE arm, the code has THREE** | IMAP; `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`; `IMAPProvider.copyProvenDestinations`; `COPYUID`; RFC 4315 §3; `UIDNOTSTICKY`; per-member; G1 pairing; `IMAPProvider.isStrictlyAscending`; G2 epoch stamp; `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`; undo after drain; `AccountManager.undoMove`; `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` | **STATE THE CLOSURE, NOT ONE INSTANCE: undo of an already-drained move is a whole-command closed no-op unless EVERY member's row ends the drain carrying BOTH the destination address `COPYUID` proved AND an epoch stamp equal to the destination folder's `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`.** The drain finishes a move locally by adopting the destination address the server itself reported (`MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`), and `AccountManager.undoMove` then reverses it as an ordinary forward move admitted through `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets`, which requires `message.observedUidValidity == liveEpoch` and a parseable positive UID. The refusal is deliberately **WHOLE-COMMAND** — `undoMove` guards on `admission.messages.count == currentRows.count` and returns an empty result otherwise, so a partly-admissible bundle is refused entirely with zero local and zero durable mutation (*"a partly-reversed move is a worse outcome than an unreversed one, because the user cannot see which half moved"*). **THREE ARMS reach that state, all verified at the tip:** **(1) G4 — no `COPYUID` for that member, PER MEMBER.** State the condition correctly: it is *"no `COPYUID` for that member"*, **NOT** *"no UIDPLUS"*. RFC 4315 §3 makes the response code a SHOULD *"with limited exceptions"*, and those exceptions are properties of the MAILBOX, not of the server or of the attempt: a mailbox the client may COPY to but not SELECT/EXAMINE (*"SHOULD NOT send"*), and a `UIDNOTSTICKY` mail store (*"MAY omit"*). A fully UIDPLUS-advertising server therefore returns no `COPYUID` for such a mailbox. `finishMove` decides per member and never per operation, so a partly-named batch is partly re-keyed — while undo of that batch is still refused whole. **(2) G1 — the whole `COPYUID` response refused for pairing, BATCH-WIDE, which is strictly wider than (1).** `IMAPProvider.copyProvenDestinations` returns an EMPTY array — no member re-keyed at all — unless BOTH expanded lists are strictly ascending (`IMAPProvider.isStrictlyAscending`, equal neighbours rejected). `CopyUID.init(nio:)` in the pinned SwiftMail fork builds its mapping as a POSITIONAL `zip` and validates cardinality but not ordering, so promoting the destination half to a mutation address makes a mis-pairing C3-critical; refusing the response whole is the fail-closed answer. The same function also yields nothing for a wholly absent `CopyUID` or a zero destination UIDVALIDITY (batch-wide), and skips a member with a zero destination UID or a source UID this call never requested (per member). **(3) G2 — re-keyed, but the epoch stamp withheld.** `finishMove` writes `observedUidValidity` only when the server-reported destination epoch EQUALS the destination folder's `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`, and otherwise re-keys the row with a NIL stamp — deliberately, because `AccountManagerActions.roleMoveRejectDispositions` treats a POSITIVE disagreement as its only terminal arm, so stamping optimistically would TERMINALLY DROP the user's next gesture (the mirror image of the bug the re-key fixes). A nil stamp is refused by `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` too, so such a member has a correct address and is still un-undoable. **Reachability:** any move into a `UIDNOTSTICKY` or COPY-but-not-SELECT mailbox, plus any server that simply omits the code for some or all of a set (arm 1); a nonconforming server whose `COPYUID` lists are not both ascending (arm 2); a destination folder whose stored epoch has not caught up with the epoch the server stamped on this copy (arm 3). Modelled in tests by `FakeIMAPServer.withholdCopyUID(forSourceUIDs:)` and `FakeIMAPServer.reportCopyUIDDestinationsDescending()`. **Why REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING:** nothing is lost and nothing is misattributed. The copy is in the destination, the source copy is left `\Deleted`-but-present (the purge stays gated on per-member `COPYUID` — `IOS-IMAP-004`), no queued operation is dropped or wedged, and the row is repaired by a later sync of the destination folder — `finishMove`'s G2 note states the nil-stamp case is *"recoverable by the next sync of that folder"* — after which an ordinary gesture on the message works normally again. The user-visible cost is that one undo does nothing until then. ⚠ **The refused undo entry is not itself revived by that repair:** `undoMove` authenticates each member by the `originalHeaderId` it captured, and the only production caller of `UndoService.applyRekeys` is the drain (`AccountManagerQueue.executeSingleOp`), so a re-key applied by any other path leaves the stacked member naming an id that no longer exists — another whole-command refusal. What recovers is the message's actionability, not that particular undo. **Deliberately not "fixed":** the only ways to name the message without `COPYUID` are an RFC 822 Message-ID `SEARCH` (banned by ADR-IOS-068/D4 and registered as `IOS-IMAP-002` — it returned every copy sharing the Message-ID and mutated all of them) or a durable receipt/alias object, which this change exists to avoid. Failing closed is the correct outcome. **Pinned by a red-first invariant test:** `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests.undoDoesNothingForAMemberCopyUidNeverNamed()` (suite type `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests`, display name *"Finish the move locally"*, file `TabMailTests/Services/FinishTheMoveLocallyTests.swift`) — it places an unrelated BYSTANDER at the stale source UID in the destination folder, so the naive alternative (queue the inverse naming the source UIDs) is a C3 wrong-message mutation, caught by the `FakeIMAPServer` wire oracle. ⚠️ **SCOPE CORRECTED AT APPLY TIME, recorded rather than silently applied.** The drafted row stated arm 1 only — *"when the server does not name a member in `COPYUID`, that member is not re-keyed"* — and would therefore have described a narrower condition than the code enforces, missing the batch-wide G1 pairing refusal and the G2 nil-stamp arm entirely. The closure above was re-derived from `copyProvenDestinations`, `finishMove` and `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` at the tip before pasting. The draft's *"an ordinary gesture and an ordinary undo both work again"* was also narrowed to what the code supports, per the ⚠ note above. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — the closure stated at the head of this row IS the decision, and there is no third option to look for.** **The decision:** undo of an already-drained move stays a whole-command closed no-op in the three arms enumerated, and the whole-command shape is deliberate — *"a partly-reversed move is a worse outcome than an unreversed one, because the user cannot see which half moved."* **The alternatives are both banned and both already enumerated in this row:** an RFC 822 Message-ID `SEARCH` (ADR-IOS-068/D4, registered as `IOS-IMAP-002` — it returned every copy sharing the Message-ID and mutated all of them), or a durable receipt/alias object, which `59423bb7d`/`f7c3354c5` exist precisely to avoid. `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests.undoDoesNothingForAMemberCopyUidNeverNamed()` already pins the C3 hazard of the naive alternative by placing a BYSTANDER at the stale source UID, so the "obvious" fix is not merely unavailable — it is proven wrong by a red-first test. **Accepted cost:** one undo silently does nothing. **Recoverability, stated WITH its named exception rather than as a bare "recoverable":** the message's ACTIONABILITY recovers at the next sync of the destination folder (`finishMove`'s G2 note). What does **NOT** recover is that particular undo ENTRY — `AccountManagerActions.undoMove` authenticates each member by the `originalHeaderId` it captured, and the only production caller of `UndoService.applyRekeys` is the drain, so a re-key applied by any other path leaves the stacked member naming an id that no longer exists, which is another whole-command refusal. **That is a real, named, non-recovering thing**, and it is bounded to *"one undo does nothing"* — no loss, no misattribution, no dropped or wedged op. **Editorial note for a future reader, so nobody re-measures it:** arm (3)'s reachability shrinks materially now that `IOS-NSE-001` has landed (`ce6fb384c`), because the destination folder's `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity` is current more often. |

## Final audit train — surfaced, deliberately NOT fixed

The final same-round Claude + Codex train ran on candidate `60a8751cb`. It returned **2 BLOCKING**
findings, both of which are **fixed separately and are therefore NOT in this table**. Both have since
landed:

- **A SEARCH result becoming a draft mutation target** — `459786db1`, "Refuse a draft address derived
  from a Message-ID SEARCH". Absence of `APPENDUID` now yields `.unaddressable`. Its documentation
  half is `d2cb96e4e`, which also retires the ADR exempt-list row that had sanctioned the search.
- **An AI write authorized by an absent/unproven folder epoch** — `6b689890d`, "Refuse an AI
  write-back under an absent or unproven folder epoch". Tracked as `IOS-ROUND3-D6`, whose row above
  now carries the full resolution.

⚠️ **This round is therefore INVALIDATED as a clean gate.** The standing rule is that both models must
be clean on one exact candidate in a single round, and two remediations mean a new candidate; a fresh
same-round train is required on the post-fix sha. The rows below are the REGISTRABLE remainder. Where the two reviewers disagreed, the coordinator verified
against the code and the row says which way it went.

⚠️ **The train also corrected two of this file's own claims** — see the `IOS-IMAP-002` retraction
below and the correction at the head of `IOS-TEST-004`. Both are recorded rather than silently
applied, because a register that overstates or misstates a defect is itself a recorded defect class
here.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-UNDO-002 | Surfaced by the final train; found **independently by BOTH reviewers**; **class A**; ✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`**, with an **accepted residual** | Undo; drain; `AccountManagerQueue.executeSingleOp`; `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`; `UndoService.applyRekeys`; `SearchIndex.rekeyHeaders`; `@MainActor` hop; `originalHeaderId`; whole-command refusal | **An undo landing in the window between the drain's re-key commit and its publication to the undo stack is refused whole.** `executeSingleOp` commits the GRDB write (rows re-keyed to the destination address, pending operation deleted), then `await`s `SearchIndex.rekeyHeaders`, and only then calls `UndoService.applyRekeys`, which must hop to `UndoService`'s `@MainActor`. An `Undo` gesture inside that suspension pops a stack entry still naming the **stale** `originalHeaderId`; `AccountManager.undoMove` authenticates each member by that id, `MessageHeader.fetchOne` returns nil, and the whole command is refused — and the later `applyRekeys` cannot repair an entry already popped off the stack. **Why REGISTRABLE:** it is **fail-closed**. The message is correctly moved, nothing mutates the wrong message, no queued operation is dropped, and the user recovers by moving it back with an ordinary gesture. The cost is one undo that silently does nothing. Narrowing the window (publishing re-keys inside the same transaction, or before the FTS await) is the obvious direction but touches the drain's ordering, which is exactly the surface three audit rounds have already churned. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`, "Key drain lanes by folder and finish moves on every retire path".** **The invariant that now holds:** the undo stack learns a move's new addresses **before** the drain gives up the thread to anything on a separate database. The re-keys are published to the undo stack BEFORE the cross-database FTS round trip, and both the completion path and the narrowing path now go through one shared helper, `publishRekeys`, so the two orderings cannot drift apart later. The undo stack names its members by the same primary key the re-key just changed, so ordering the publication after an `await` on a separate SQLite pool meant a user who hit Undo in that window undid nothing. ⚠️ **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, stated by that commit and NOT to be overclaimed away:** `UndoService` is `@MainActor`, so the publication still awaits **one actor hop**. The fix removes the **FTS round trip** from in front of the publication; **it does not remove every suspension**, and a sufficiently fast Undo inside that remaining hop still pops a stack entry naming the stale `originalHeaderId` and is refused whole — fail-closed, exactly as this row describes, and recovered by moving the message back with an ordinary gesture. **A1:** `finishMove`, `applyRekeys` and `rekeyHeaders` have ZERO occurrences in the shipped file — there is no drain-time re-key at all at `07a4bb703` — so the shipped architecture is NONEXISTENT here and the fix is authored. |
| IOS-SEARCH-002 | Surfaced by the final train (Claude reviewer); **class A**; ✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`** | FTS; `MessageHeaderRekey.apply`; collision leg; `SearchIndex.rekeyHeaders`; `appliedRekeys`; `ftsRekeys`; `staleIds`; indexed-but-unfindable; `messageUserLabel`; `MessageBody` | **On the drain side only, the re-key's collision leg leaves an FTS entry pointing at a deleted header.** `MessageHeaderRekey.apply` deletes the old header and its body **before** the collision guard `guard try MessageHeader.fetchOne(db, key: newId) == nil`, then returns `false`. The **sync** caller compensates — the id never enters `ftsRekeys` and the stale row rides `staleIds`. The **drain** caller does not: `executeSingleOp` calls `SearchIndex.rekeyHeaders` only for `appliedRekeys`, which excludes the collision, so the old id's FTS entry survives with no header behind it — the *indexed but unfindable* class. The carried `messageUserLabel` rows and the body are dropped rather than merged onto the surviving destination row. **Reachability is narrow:** it needs a local row already sitting at the `COPYUID`-proven destination address at drain time. **Why REGISTRABLE:** recoverable — an orphan sweep clears the ghost and bodies re-fetch; no message is destroyed and no mutation lands on the wrong message. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`.** **The invariant that now holds:** no FTS entry survives a drain-side re-key collision with no header behind it — the *indexed but unfindable* class is closed on the drain side, converging on the disposition the **sync** caller already had via `staleIds`. When `MessageHeaderRekey.apply` hits an occupied destination id it deletes the old row and inserts nothing, so the old header id names no header at all; `finishMove` now REPORTS those ids and the drain drops them with `SearchIndex.removeMessages`. **One implementation note that is deliberate rather than accidental, recorded so nobody "cleans it up" without knowing why:** the report channel is a **defaulted `onCollidedRekey` closure** rather than a changed return type, because `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests.swift` — the only other call site — was dirty with another agent's uncommitted work when this was written, and staging it would have committed their change. The closure works and is documented; it was kept rather than churned once that file landed. |
| IOS-QUEUE-005 | Surfaced by the final train (Claude reviewer); **class A**; was **LATENT — not reachable at the tip**; ✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`** | Action queue; `retirePartiallyCompletedOp`; `executed.provenDestinations`; `IMAPProvider.move`; narrowing pass; drain contract | **The partial-retirement leg discards `executed.provenDestinations`.** `retirePartiallyCompletedOp` returns before the delete/re-key write, so members retired in a narrowing pass keep their **source** address and stay dead to the next gesture until a sync repairs them. **Not live today:** `IMAPProvider.move` returns `provenIds = ids`, so the strict-subset test never fires for an IMAP move — no current provider reaches it. It is registered because it is a gap in the drain's *stated standing contract*, which any future provider returning a strict subset would fall into silently. **Why REGISTRABLE:** unreachable at the tip, and recoverable by sync if it ever became reachable. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `635cb78b1`.** **The invariant that now holds:** a member retired in a NARROWING pass ends the drain carrying the destination address `COPYUID` proved — the drain's stated standing contract is now whole rather than a trap laid for a future provider. `executed.provenDestinations` is passed into `retirePartiallyCompletedOp`, which finishes the move for the members `COPYUID` actually proved, **freezing the op to those members first so an unproven member keeps its source address**. Without it a narrowed retirement left every proven member optimistically moved but still keyed to its SOURCE UID, i.e. refused by `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` — so the user's next gesture on it would be a silent dead no-op, which is exactly the defect `59423bb7d`/`f7c3354c5` exist to remove. ⚠️ **THE SAFETY BRANCH WAS NOT DELETED, AND MUST NOT BE:** deleting it would make a strict-subset return retire the WHOLE op, re-opening a never-drop hole for any future provider — the precise thing `4e08ff720` added it to prevent. `retirePartiallyCompletedOp` became `internal` for the same reason `executeSingleOp` and `DrainContext` already are: no production provider returns a strict subset, so a test is this path's only reachability. |
| IOS-LABEL-002 | Surfaced by the final train (Codex, Scope 2 / **T6.9 feature-drop sweep**); **class B** (`a790dd61d`, ancestor of `2d85b8729`, absent from shipped `07a4bb703`); ✅ **FIXED by `e265428ff`** — ⚠️ and it was NOT only a feature drop, it was hiding a live **never-drop violation**; its last live remainder (accepted cost 3, the NSE label arm) closed by `592f7202f` | Feature drop; Outlook; Exchange; user labels; `UserLabelMenuModel.supportsRemoteUserLabels`; `loadLabels`; `applyLabel`; `removeLabel`; `createAndApply`; local-only labels | **Shipped `v1.6.38` let Outlook accounts load, create, apply and remove user labels LOCALLY; the candidate hides them entirely.** In the shipped release `UserLabelMenuView` operated for Outlook and the Exchange executor logged that remote mutation was unsupported while still completing the queue operation, so the local `UserLabel` / `MessageUserLabel` state stayed visible and usable. On v3, `UserLabelMenuModel.supportsRemoteUserLabels` and its four call sites exclude Outlook, so **existing local labels on Outlook accounts become invisible**. **This is the one genuine feature drop the whole-tree sweep found** — 0 production files deleted, 0 files with a net decrease in `guard` count, 0 user-visible UI strings removed, and every other removed function traced to a rename or a documented subtraction with a live replacement. **Why REGISTRABLE:** no data is lost — the rows are neither migrated away nor deleted, so restoring an explicit local-only policy would reveal them again. The user-visible cost is that an Outlook user's existing labels stop appearing until that decision is made. **Owner-facing:** whether Outlook labels should be local-only or absent is a product call, not a correctness one. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `e265428ff`, "Propagate Outlook user labels to Graph as message categories". ⚠️ THE ROW ABOVE FRAMED THIS AS A PRODUCT CALL — "local-only or absent" — AND THE ANSWER TURNED OUT TO BE NEITHER, because the framing hid a live NEVER-DROP VIOLATION.** The queue's two Exchange arms were `print("[Queue] addUserLabel not yet supported for Exchange")` followed by `return .allMembers`: **the durable op was RETIRED AS SUCCESSFUL having done nothing.** That is not a missing feature, it is **exit-2 abuse** — nothing provider-authoritative said the work was done or inapplicable, so the user's gesture left the queue on no evidence at all. The row's *"no data is lost"* framing was true of the LOCAL rows and silent about the queue. **The invariant that now holds:** Outlook user labels behave like IMAP's and Gmail's — visible, creatable, and they REACH THE SERVER; both Exchange arms now execute a real mutation, so the op leaves the queue only on provider success. An Outlook user label IS a Graph message `category` (there is no separate label resource on Graph), so add/remove is a `categories` PATCH via the new `ExchangeProvider.setUserLabel(messageId:category:add:)`, which is **READ-FILTER-WRITE and never a blind PATCH** — Graph REPLACES the whole array, so a blind write would destroy categories set by Outlook desktop, Outlook web or a server-side rule; it shares one `mutateCategories(id:transform:)` helper with `stripLegacyCategories`, and the PATCH is skipped when the array would be unchanged. `ExchangeProvider.parseGraphMessage` now maps `categories` into `userLabelIds` (the data was already on the wire and already decoded — only the mapping was missing). **A1:** shipped `07a4bb703` had NO provider gate at all and carried the same `print("… not yet supported for Exchange")` verbatim, so **the shipped release SHOWED Outlook labels and silently never sent them**; `supportsRemoteUserLabels` does not exist there — it is a `v2final` invention, and `v2final` returns `false` for `.outlook` too. Remote Outlook propagation is NONEXISTENT in the shipped architecture, so this is authored, and shipped's visible-but-inert labels are explicitly **not** restored. **THE ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION FROM IMAP:** the Outlook label id is the category display name **VERBATIM, not lowercased** — IMAP lowercases because RFC 3501 keywords are case-insensitive, whereas Graph category names are case-SENSITIVE display strings, so minting a lowercased id would give a user-created `Receipts` and the server's echoed `Receipts` two different `UserLabel.id`s and a duplicate menu row. **Wire values stay bare (`IOS-LABEL-001`/D10):** `PendingOperation.userLabelId` carries `UserLabel.providerLabelId`, never the account-prefixed `UserLabel.id`, because a prefixed value would write a wrong category name onto the user's real message. **THREE ACCEPTED COSTS, stated by that commit:** (1) a category TabMail creates is not registered in the user's Outlook master list, so Outlook may render it without a colour until the user adds it there — exactly an IMAP keyword's position (registered nowhere), fixed by one ordinary gesture; **no OAuth scope was added, deliberately**, since `/me/outlook/masterCategories` needs `MailboxSettings.ReadWrite` and adding it would force every existing Outlook user through re-consent, while message-level `categories` PATCH works within the `Mail.ReadWrite` the app already has. (2) read-modify-write loses an update racing a concurrent Outlook client between the GET and the PATCH — **pre-existing**, `stripLegacyCategories` has had this race since it shipped; recovery is one ordinary sync pass and no mechanism was built. (3) `NSEDataBridge.filterUserLabels`'s `"outlook"` arm still returns `[]`, so an **NSE-delivered Outlook message carries no label rows until the next main-app sync** (which now materializes them); that file was owned by another live agent and was deliberately not touched. Cost (3) is the only live remainder and is recoverable by sync. <br><br>✅ **THAT LAST REMAINDER IS NOW CLOSED TOO, by `592f7202f`'s second item — the row carries NO live remainder.** `NSEDataBridge.filterUserLabels`'s `"outlook"` arm returned `[]`, and after `e265428ff` made `ExchangeProvider.parseGraphMessage` map Graph `categories` into `userLabelIds` it was the only path still dropping them; its comment still cited the `userLabelIds: []` that commit had deleted. It now returns `rawLabels.filter { !ExchangeProvider.isLegacyActionTagCategory($0) }` — the **SAME** predicate the main-app read path filters with and `stripLegacyCategories` deletes the server's `tm_*` categories with, not a second copy, so a category one path erases can never be surfaced by the other as a label the user owns (ADR-IOS-036: action tags are local-only). 🚨 **THIS ARM KEEPS GRAPH CATEGORY NAMES VERBATIM AND MUST NEVER BE LOWERCASED** — the IMAP arm directly above it lowercases because RFC 3501 keywords are case-INsensitive, while Graph category names are case-SENSITIVE display strings, so copying the sibling would derive a second `UserLabel.id` for one category and grow a duplicate menu row. **A1:** `git show 07a4bb703:TabMail/Services/NSEDataBridge.swift` has the arm returning `[]` verbatim, and in the shipped release that was CORRECT because its `parseGraphMessage` also returned `userLabelIds: []` — the arm's contract (*"match what the main-app read path derives"*) is the shipped one and is restored; only the value it must produce changed underneath it. **ACCEPTED LIMITATION:** this is a LATENCY fix, not a correctness one — the NSE is best-effort and must never be the only path carrying a durable guarantee, and the main-app sync already materialized these labels, so the pre-fix gap closed on the next sync pass. No migration, no new column, no change to the staging projections. A blessing test was re-scoped rather than deleted: `NSEMergeFullHeaderTests`' *"Outlook merge inserts NO user-label rows (categories not yet surfaced)"* (`outlookNoUserLabels`) asserted `count == 0` and encoded the defect; it is now *"Outlook merge keeps Graph categories verbatim and drops only tm_*"* (`outlookCategoriesBecomeUserLabels`), with the previous display name and the reason recorded inline. <br><br>📝 **NOTE (final audit train round 3, Claude reviewer) — `userLabelIdsAreAuthoritative: true` on Exchange is INERT but UNENFORCED.** Census re-verified against the tree at documentation time: **1 declaration** (`MessageHeaderInfo.userLabelIdsAreAuthoritative`, `TabMail/Providers/EmailProvider.swift`), **2 producers** (`GmailProvider`, from `userLabelCatalog.isAuthoritative`; `ExchangeProvider.parseGraphMessage`, hardcoded `true`), and **0 production readers** — every remaining occurrence in the tree is a test assertion. So the flag asserts a `$select` property of the Graph call sites (the reviewer counted **seven**) that is **argued in prose and pinned by nothing**. **No defect today** — with no consumer, a wrong value cannot mislead anything. **Recorded because the moment a reconcile consumer lands, that prose argument becomes load-bearing**: a consumer that treats `true` as *"this header carries the message's COMPLETE label set"* would strip labels from every header produced by a `$select` that omits `categories`. Pin the property before adding the first reader, not after. |
| IOS-MIGRATION-001 | ✅ **NOT A DEFECT (adjudication RE-VERIFIED against source 2026-08-04)** — raised by the final train (Codex), adjudicated by the coordinator; recorded so it is not re-raised every round | Migrations; `registerMigration`; `registerTimedMigration`; `c1ca55a54`; v1–v67; immutability; GRDB identifier | ⚠️ **NOT A DEFECT — recorded because it LOOKS like one and has now cost one reviewer's attention.** Every shipped registration `v1_createTables` … `v67_addUidResolutionRetryCount` was changed from GRDB's `registerMigration` to the project's `registerTimedMigration` wrapper (`c1ca55a54`, before `2d85b8729`, absent from `07a4bb703`), which reads at a glance like editing applied migrations — the rule in `tabmail-ios/CLAUDE.md` § *Data Integrity Rules* 5. **It is not.** GRDB records applied state by the migration's **identifier**, and the wrapper forwards the identifier, the foreign-key mode and the closure unchanged; GRDB then skips the body of any already-applied identifier. Neither the identifier nor the executed body changed, so a fresh install and an existing database still converge. The reviewer that raised it reached the same conclusion (*"I found no concrete brick"*) and filed it as registrable. **What the immutability rule actually protects is the identifier and the body — not the name of the function used to register them.** A comment inside `v67`'s body was also reworded, which likewise cannot affect an applied database. <br><br>✅ **RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE ACTUAL SOURCE, 2026-08-04, and the adjudication HOLDS — this row now carries an explicit disposition rather than reading as Open.** It was carried without a disposition marker through the previous register pass, which under this file's own legend (*"**Open** — no ✅ marker"*) made it read as a live finding: exactly the failure mode the completeness audit exists to eliminate, on the one row where the consequence of being wrong is a **brick** (launch crash), which is in the non-recoverable set. **The evidence, read from `AppDatabase.swift` rather than inherited from the prior adjudication:** `registerTimedMigration(_:foreignKeyChecks:migrate:)` forwards **the identifier unchanged** and **the `foreignKeyChecks` mode unchanged** to GRDB's own `registerMigration`, and calls `try migrate(db)` — the caller's closure, unchanged — on **both** branches of its `guard DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()`. The only difference between the two branches is a `ContinuousClock` sample and two `BackgroundSyncLogger.log` lines around the identical `try migrate(db)`, and the `catch` **rethrows**, so a failing migration still fails. GRDB records applied state by the **identifier**, so every already-applied `v1_createTables` … `v67_addUidResolutionRetryCount` is still skipped by identifier and no already-applied body is re-executed. A fresh install and an existing database therefore still converge. **Nothing to fix, and nothing to re-raise:** the immutability rule (`tabmail-ios/CLAUDE.md` § *Data Integrity Rules* 5) protects the identifier and the executed body, and the wrapper changes neither. |
| IOS-BACKFILL-002 | Surfaced while closing out the B1 draft-SEARCH fix (lead raised by the implementing agent, **verified against the code by the coordinator**); **class C — shipped `v1.6.38` behaviour, not candidate-introduced**; ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** | Backfill; `BackfillBodyQueue.confirmGoneAtThreshold`; `currentUIDs`; `MessageExistenceProbe`; `uids.max`; UID remap; re-key; RFC duplicates; body misattribution | **The backfill's UID-remap re-key resolves an RFC-duplicate multi-hit by taking the highest UID, which is a guess, not proof.** `confirmGoneAtThreshold` (`TabMail/Services/Sync/BackfillBodyQueue.swift:864`) probes by `rfc822MessageId`; if the stored UID is absent but the search returns others it picks `uids.max`, commented *"the highest UID is the newest copy"*. That is sound for a genuine remap of one message, but when **two distinct messages share a Message-ID** in the same folder — the exact `IOS-IMAP-002` scenario D4 exists to forbid — the local header re-keys onto the **wrong copy** and then fetches that copy's body under this header's identity, carrying the original's derived AI state onto it. **Why this is NOT the D4 violation B1 fixed, stated precisely:** the address produced here is a **local** header address for a body *fetch*, never a wire mutation target — no `STORE`, no `EXPUNGE`, no queued operation is authorized by it. D4 forbids search-derived **mutation** targets; this is a search-derived read address, which is why `currentUIDs` remains on the ADR's normative exempt list. **Why REGISTRABLE:** shipped behaviour (this re-key is the 2026-06-09 FTS body-loss fix, and removing the `MessageExistenceProbe` conformance would convert every backfill miss into deleting the user's local header — strictly worse); it requires duplicate Message-IDs in one folder; and it is recoverable, since a later full sync reconciles the folder's UIDs and a body refetch corrects the content. **Not adjudicated as clean — deliberately deferred to the final train** rather than fixed mid-round. <br><br>✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — a search-derived READ address is not a mutation address, and the rejected alternative deletes the user's header.** **The decision:** `BackfillBodyQueue.confirmGoneAtThreshold` keeps resolving an RFC-duplicate multi-hit by `uids.max`. Three grounds, in the order that decides it. **(a) D4-clean by KIND, not by care.** The address produced is a **local header address for a body FETCH** — never a `STORE`, an `EXPUNGE` or a queued-operation target — and ADR-IOS-068 keeps `currentUIDs` on its normative exempt list for exactly that reason. D4 forbids search-derived *mutation* targets, and this is not one; the distinction is the whole reason this looks like C3 and is not. **(b) The rejected alternative is strictly worse.** Removing the `MessageExistenceProbe` conformance converts every backfill miss into **DELETING the user's local header** — and this re-key IS the 2026-06-09 FTS body-loss fix, shipped in `v1.6.38`, so the heuristic is load-bearing shipped behaviour rather than an unexamined guess. **(c) No available field resolves the tie.** The population is duplicate Message-IDs *within one folder*, and this is the same conclusion `IOS-DRAFT-009` reached for the reply resolver, for the same reason: the fields that would separate two messages sharing a Message-ID are exactly the fields this path does not yet have. **Accepted cost:** with two distinct messages sharing a Message-ID in one folder, the local header re-keys onto the wrong copy and fetches that copy's body under this header's identity, carrying the original's derived AI state onto it — **misattribution of DERIVED content**, which is the same duplicate-RFC content-key premise `IOS-AI-001` records this repo as already accepting everywhere (body storage, FTS, `MessageAICache`). **If that premise is ever revisited, this row and `IOS-AI-001` must move together.** **Recoverability:** a later full sync reconciles the folder's UIDs and a body refetch corrects the content. **The state where that reconciliation cannot run was searched for** — it needs the folder to be reached by a sync pass, and per `IOS-EPOCH-001`'s round-8 correction on-demand navigation reaches any folder — **and none that is permanent was found**; the worst case is deferred until the user opens the folder. <br><br>📌 **PREMISE CORRECTED (2026-08-05), MOVING WITH `IOS-AI-001` AS THIS ROW REQUIRED. THE DECISION IS UNCHANGED.** The cited premise — *"the same duplicate-RFC content-key premise `IOS-AI-001` records this repo as already accepting everywhere (body storage, FTS, `MessageAICache`)"* — is preserved above as superseded history and is **FALSE at HEAD**: body storage and FTS collapse by the **provider address** (`ContentKey.forHeader` is `⚑ INERT AS OF STAGE B` and returns `messageHeader.id` verbatim), while `MessageAICache` collapses by **RFC identity** (`MessageIdentity.aiCacheKey`). See `IOS-AI-001`'s *BASIS CORRECTED (2026-08-05)* for the full derivation. **What that changes here, and what it does not.** It does **not** change the decision, the D4-clean-by-KIND ground (a), the strictly-worse-alternative ground (b), or the no-available-field ground (c) — none of them depends on how the content stores are keyed. It changes only the **shape of the accepted cost**: when `confirmGoneAtThreshold` re-keys a header onto the wrong RFC-duplicate copy, the misattributed derived content is the **address-keyed body and FTS record** that ride along with the re-keyed header id, and separately the **RFC-keyed AI cache row**, which was already shared between the two copies before the re-key. Both remain corrected by the same later full sync + body refetch this row already names. |

## Final audit train ROUND 2 — ✅ CLEAN, and its registrable remainder

**The gate is met.** The fresh same-round Claude + Codex train ran on candidate **`427db6bba`** —
the post-remediation sha — and **both models returned 0 BLOCKING in one round**: Codex
**0 BLOCKING / 1 REGISTRABLE**, Claude **0 BLOCKING / 3 REGISTRABLE**. That is the standing
requirement (both models clean on one exact candidate in a single round), and unlike round 1 it was
satisfied without a remediation invalidating it.

**Both reviewers independently found the SAME first finding** (`IOS-AI-003` below), from different
starting points and with the same non-blocking classification. Independent convergence is the
strongest evidence this register carries; it is recorded as such.

Both also independently confirmed the two round-1 fixes are sound rather than over-refusing — the
specific mirror-image risk this round existed to hunt. Verified in code by both: `.unaddressable`
retires the `.saveDraft` op normally and wedges no lane, the local `Draft` row keeps its content,
arm 6's RFC content witness and arm 4's provider bypass are unchanged **in effect**, and the
`COPYUID`-gated source expunge was not touched and its evidence not widened. Codex additionally read
shipped `07a4bb703` and found the candidate unambiguously safer than the floor: the shipped draft
path took the **first** search hit with no exact-verify and no cardinality check, returned the literal
string `"unknown"` as a serverId on a miss, and its `deleteDraft` issued a **bare mailbox-wide
`server.expunge()`**.

Only an unrelated future RFC-bearing message in the same folder unlocks verification. **Why REGISTRABLE and not blocking:** it is a refusal, never a wrong write, so not C3; an AI summary is recomputable derived content explicitly outside the never-drop set; and it is **not** the wedge corollary — the job yields its slot each cycle and starves no other intention, least of all a durable `PendingOperation`. `readJobOutcome`'s `!message.isInInbox ⇒ .scopeExited` bounds the loop to inbox-resident rows. **It materially extends `IOS-AI-002`'s billing/battery loop** to newly-synchronized, positively-stamped headers, and the arm-8 doc comment already names this exact hazard (*"a paid API call repeated forever for a summary that could never land"*). ⚠️ **`6b689890d`'s commit body claims the refusal is recoverable. what changed is that a permanent refusal is now recognised as permanent instead of being treated as a transient failure. **The terminal condition, stated precisely by that commit and worth keeping:** because the target is re-captured at the top of every attempt, arms 1–3 cannot fire and arm 6 admits on the row's own non-empty RFC id, so the condition reduces exactly to arms 7 and 8 — an epoch-addressed row with **no** RFC 822 Message-ID whose folder's numbering is absent or unknown, or whose own `observedUidValidity` is unset or disagrees. **Proof it was not over-broadened, two-sided:** a folder mid UIDVALIDITY reset holding the same RFC-less unstamped row classifies `transientlyRefused`, is NOT retired, and lands once the reaction finishes; and an ATTRIBUTABLE row whose model call merely FAILED stays `admissible` on every cycle and still survives an unbounded number of failures at `maxRetries: .max` — pinned by a test driving 10,000 consecutive retrying completions. **`maxRetries: .max` was not touched anywhere.** **And the refusal leaves nothing durable** — no column, no flag, no migration, no database write — so `rearmUnattributableJobs()` clears the memo wholesale at launch, foreground, AI re-enable and suspension recovery, and both re-opening doors (a later sync stamping folder + row; the row gaining an RFC id) are pinned independently. <br><br>📝 **NOTE (final audit train round 3, Claude reviewer) — the classification's THIRD shape, recorded so it is not re-raised: an ABSENT `Folder` row classifies STRUCTURAL, not transient.** `ActiveAIQueue.writeAdmission(_:target:)` returns `.transientlyRefused` only when `Folder.fetchOne` **succeeds** *and* that row's `uidValidityResetPendingAt != nil`; a folder row that is **absent altogether** falls through to `.structurallyRefused`, so that message's AI jobs are retired for the process lifetime. The two-sided proof above covers a **present** folder mid-reset and is silent about an absent one, which is why this is written down rather than left to be rediscovered. **Not a defect, and no mechanism is owed.** The recovery path, by symbol: `ActiveAIQueue.rearmUnattributableJobs()`, called from `repopulateFromDatabase()` (launch / foreground / AI re-enable) and `cancelAllInFlight()` (suspension recovery), clears the memo wholesale — so the retirement lasts one process lifetime, not forever. AI output is **recomputable derived content**, nothing durable is written on this path, and **no user intention is involved**, so the never-drop contract is not engaged and nothing here is a wedge: the job yields its slot and starves no other work. |

## Final audit train ROUND 3 — ✅ CLEAN on `33ea05b82`, and every finding FIXED rather than deferred

**The gate is met on the zero-deferral candidate.** The fresh same-round train ran **READ-ONLY** on
candidate **`33ea05b82`** — the Claude reviewer plus Codex (`codex-cli 0.146.0`,
`--sandbox read-only`), same round, same exact sha — and **both returned 0 BLOCKING**:
Codex **0 BLOCKING / 1 REGISTRABLE**, Claude **0 BLOCKING / 2 REGISTRABLE + 3 NOTE**. That is the
standing requirement (both models clean on one exact candidate in a single round).

**Both reviewers independently RE-DERIVED the review range** — **22 commits / 92 files /
+8667 −1726** (`427db6bba..33ea05b82`) — **and independently CONFIRMED the register at 53 ids /
53 rows with exactly one disposition each**: **31 FIXED · 19 CLOSED-DECISION · 1 OPEN
(`IOS-PERF-001`) · 1 NOT A DEFECT · 1 DECOMPOSED**. Each named the parsing traps it ruled out; the
traps below were **re-verified mechanically at documentation time** and are recorded because every
future recount meets them: (a) the two *Fixed by D4* rows
(`IOS-IMAP-002`, `IOS-IMAP-004`) sit **below** the deferral tables and are invisible to a
`^| IOS-` grep — they are also **bolded** (`| **IOS-IMAP-002** |`), so an unbolded id pattern misses
them twice over; (b) `IOS-QUEUE-003` is **one** id whose disposition is carried by a nine-row
sub-table, not by its own status cell; and (c) several status cells carry BOTH a `FIXED` and a
`CLOSED AS A DECISION` marker (headline disposition plus a sub-remainder), so a first-match
classifier silently mis-sorts them — `IOS-ROUND3-D6` is FIXED with a sync-merge remainder closed as
a decision, and counting it the other way is exactly how a recount lands on 30/20 instead of 31/19.

⚠️ **ZERO reviewer premises were REFUTED this round — the first time in this push.** Eleven were
refuted across the earlier rounds. The supervisor still independently verified every claim against
real code before accepting it; that verification, not the reviewers' confidence, is what the three
commits below record.

**Every finding was FIXED, not deferred, per the owner's zero-deferral goal** — *"no additional task
should remain… this includes non-blocking items"*:

- **`1a1962207`** — Codex's REGISTRABLE. Registered as **`IOS-OUTBOX-005`** below.
- **`45ad66d38`** — a Claude REGISTRABLE. **Not given a new id**: it closes `IOS-IMAP-001`'s own
  **accepted residual (2)**, which that row already named as NOT fixed, so the resolution is recorded
  there. The census turned out wider than the residual stated — **four** materialisation paths.
- **`9443e8a9c`** — the suite's single `withKnownIssue` was named *"OPEN: … the unguarded merge
  pass"* with reason *"`runSyncMessages` has no UIDVALIDITY guard"*, and **both were false**: T4.S6
  landed that guard (`runSyncMessages` re-reads the folder row via `Folder.fetchOne` inside its own
  write transaction and abandons the pass on `uidValidityResetPendingAt != nil` or on a **both-known**
  epoch disagreement). What it actually pins is the **unknown-epoch arm**, which the guard
  deliberately does not refuse — both-known is required so a first sync, or a server reporting no
  bound fetch epoch, fails OPEN rather than bricking the folder — and that arm is reachable in the
  fixture only because `MockEmailProvider` binds no fetch epoch. **Test-target only, zero production
  code; the known-issue count stays at 1, now truthfully labelled.**

**Two of the three NOTEs are carried into the register by this pass**, each as a one-line amendment
on the row that owns it — `IOS-AI-003` (an absent `Folder` row classifies STRUCTURAL, not transient)
and `IOS-LABEL-002` (`userLabelIdsAreAuthoritative` is inert but unenforced). Neither is a defect
worth a mechanism; both are recorded so they are not rediscovered. The third is not registered here.

**Migration state at the candidate:** the registered identifiers differ from the base by **exactly
one addition — `v82_accountScopedUserLabelIdentity`** — with **zero renames and zero duplicates**,
so **`v83` is the next free number**. A registered migration is immutable the moment any database,
including a dev simulator, has run it.

**Register after this pass: 54 ids / 54 rows, exactly one disposition each — 32 FIXED ·
20 CLOSED-DECISION · 0 OPEN · 1 NOT A DEFECT · 1 DECOMPOSED, and ZERO rows owing work.**
