| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-OUTBOX-005 | Surfaced by the final train **round 3** (Codex reviewer); **pre-existing — the same residual is verbatim in shipped `v1.6.38`, so NOT candidate-attributable**; **REGISTRABLE**; ✅ **FIXED by `1a1962207`**; sibling of `IOS-OUTBOX-002`, cross-referenced rather than merged | Outbox; undo-send hold; `holdUntil`; `AccountManager.drainOutbox`; `AccountManager.wakeUpDelay(for:at:)`; `AccountManager.earliestFutureHoldWakeTarget(now:db:)`; `TabMail/Services/Account/AccountManagerOutbox.swift`; post-loop wake; two-instant handoff; deadline elapses mid-query; no timer armed; clamp at zero; `UInt64(negative Double)` trap; never-drop liveness | **An outbox row held behind an undo-send deadline could end a drain with NO wake-up timer armed at all, leaving the user's queued send waiting for an unrelated external trigger.** `AccountManager.drainOutbox` selects its wake target with `earliestFutureHoldWakeTarget(now:db:)`, whose predicate is `holdUntil > now`, and then re-checked `hold > Date()` against a **FRESH** instant with an `await dbPool.read` in between. A deadline that elapsed **during that read** was future at selection and past at the re-check, so the guard fell through and armed nothing — while the drain loop above it had already finished. The row stayed `.queued` until a foreground, a sync, a network change or another `queueSend` happened along. ⚠️ **This is a DIFFERENT mechanism from `IOS-OUTBOX-002`** (which was the query's ORDERING) and neither fix would have closed the other; that row's *"left with no timer armed"* clause is corrected in place there. **Why REGISTRABLE and not blocking:** it is a **liveness** defect, not a correctness one — nothing is dropped, nothing is sent twice, no wrong message is mutated, and the durable row survives; an ordinary later trigger drains it, so it is recoverable in the sense THE MANTRA requires. **The invariant that now holds:** a queued row whose hold expires is either claimed by the current drain or covered by an autonomous future drain — **never dependent on an unrelated trigger**. `AccountManager.wakeUpDelay(for:at:)` (a new `nonisolated static` on `AccountManager`, in `AccountManagerOutbox.swift` — ⚠️ **the file name is not the type qualifier**; `AccountManagerOutbox.wakeUpDelay` greps as nothing) clamps the interval at zero, so **an already-elapsed deadline means "drain now", never "do nothing"**. 🚨 **The clamp is INDEPENDENTLY LOAD-BEARING: the caller converts with `UInt64(interval * 1_000_000_000)`, and converting a NEGATIVE `Double` to `UInt64` is a runtime TRAP, not a saturating conversion.** **The re-drive terminates and claims the row:** the drain loop's admission filter is `(holdUntil ?? .distantPast) <= Date()`, so the next pass admits the elapsed row instead of falling through again, and `earliestFutureHoldWakeTarget` filters `holdUntil > now`, so that row is no longer a wake target and no second timer can be armed. A still-future deadline is unaffected — `max(0, positive) == positive` — so the ordinary path is byte-identical. **The decision takes an INSTANT PARAMETER rather than reading the clock inline, for the same reason its sibling query does:** `PostLoopWakeUpQueryTests`' own doc comment records that an inline clock is precisely why a replica test stayed green while `IOS-OUTBOX-002` shipped. **A1:** shipped `07a4bb703` carries this same residual — an order-only query plus the identical `hold > Date()` re-check — **plus** the NULL/past-row shadowing defect `792048ebd` already fixed; nothing to restore, so the branch's improvement is kept and the residual closed on top. **Verification:** three tests added, pinning the INVARIANT rather than the mechanism. Red-first proof recorded — with the clamp inverted, *"A deadline that elapses while the wake query is in flight still gets an autonomous drain"* failed with `(delay -> nil) != nil`, and in that same red run the two pre-existing blessing tests (`futureHeldRowIsNotShadowed`, `PostLoopWakeUpQueryTests.guardAgainstPastHold`) **both PASSED**, which is the mechanical measure of the gap they were blind to. Neither was modified or weakened. No wire behaviour, no schema change. |

## Wave 2 / register-closure pass 2 — surfaced by agents that STOPPED and reported instead of fixing

Each row below was found by an agent whose brief said *"if you find a bug outside your scope, STOP
and report it — do not fix it"*, and each did exactly that. They are registered here so the final
audit train **recognises** them instead of rediscovering them as candidate regressions.

**All five are TEST-INFRASTRUCTURE or DOCUMENTATION. None is a product defect, none is
candidate-attributable, and none changes what the app does.** (Four were registered in this pass;
`IOS-TEST-009` was opened later, by the work that closed `IOS-TEST-006` and `IOS-TEST-008`, and is
the same class.) That classification is load-bearing:
a test-infrastructure defect is invisible to every product invariant and is therefore *not* a
blocker, but it is also exactly the class that lets real defects pass a green suite, which is why
these are registered rather than shrugged off.

⚠️ **`IOS-TEST-006` and `IOS-TEST-008` are the same class as each other** — process-global state on a
`@MainActor` singleton that is never scoped or cleared between suites — and `.serialized` **does not
compose across suite boundaries**, so ordering cannot fix either. They are cross-referenced rather
than merged, because their mechanisms differ (a key-namespace collision versus an un-awaited
teardown) and a fix for one does not fix the other.

✅ **BOTH ARE NOW FIXED (`57f10a76b`), and the shared conclusion is worth keeping after the fact: the
`ProcessGlobalTestState` scope is the ONLY boundary in this corpus that composes across suites.** The
key-namespace collision is cleared there on every scope entry; the un-awaited teardown is awaited
inside `TestProviderRegistry.withRegisteredProvider`. `.serialized` was never available as a fix for
either, and a third defect of this class should be fixed at that same boundary rather than by
re-ordering suites. `IOS-TEST-007` is **closed as a decision** (reclassification only — it was never
work anyone owed). `IOS-TEST-009` was opened by that work and was the one live row in this section; it is now ✅ **FIXED (`87c805784`)**, so **this section has no live row left** and `IOS-PERF-001` is the only row in the whole register still owing work. ⚠️ **THAT LAST CLAUSE IS NOW STALE, corrected in place rather than deleted: `IOS-PERF-001` closed as a decision on 2026-08-04 when its runtime half was measured on the final gate, so the register has ZERO rows owing work.**

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-TEST-006 | ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04, `57f10a76b`)** — ⚠️ **the row's own suggested fix is HALF REFUTED here: the production key is unscoped BY DESIGN and was deliberately left alone; the leak is closed in the harness instead** | Tests; `AccountManager.recentlyCompleted`; process-global `[String: Date]`; bare `messageId` key; MainActor singleton; TTL 30 s; `FakeIMAPServer` `(max ?? 0) + 1`; `FolderEpochTestFixture.insertHeaders`; `isProtectedByRecent`; stale sweep; `withKnownIssue` passing for the wrong reason; MIS-014 blessing class; `.serialized` does not compose | **A cross-suite fixture collision in process-global test state makes a stale sweep skip rows it should delete — and makes a `withKnownIssue` block pass for the WRONG REASON.** ⚠️ **RECONCILED, NOT TRANSCRIBED: this was reported twice and it is ONE defect, not two.** One report reached it through `SyncFolderEpochPersistenceTests`; the other through `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests` / `NeverDropExitClosureTests`. **The discriminating evidence that settles it as one:** `a659c4bc5`'s own bisect found that `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests` and `NeverDropExitClosureTests` **each reproduce the failure alone** while `IMAPMoveWireContractTests` and `RFC822IdentityMergeGuardTests` are clean, and the suite that FAILS is the epoch suite — so those two are the **contaminators** and the epoch suite is the **victim** of the same single mechanism, reached from either side. Two entry points, one collision, one row. **The mechanism, verified symbol by symbol at the tip:** `AccountManager.recentlyCompleted` is a process-global `[String: Date]` on a `@MainActor` singleton, **keyed by BARE `messageId` with no account, folder or database scoping**, never cleared between suites, with a 30 s TTL against a ~14 s run; `AccountManager.recordRecentlyCompleted` writes the drained ids. `FakeIMAPServer`'s copy assigns destination UIDs `(destinationMessages.map(\.uid).max() ?? 0) + 1`, so a `COPY` into an EMPTY destination mailbox yields UIDs **1/2/3**, and the move's local re-key makes those the completed ids. Independently, `FolderEpochTestFixture` (an `enum` in `SyncFolderEpochPersistenceTests.swift` — **the file name is not the type**) has `insertHeaders(accountId:path:uids:pool:)` seed each row with `messageId: "\(uid)"`, i.e. **"1"/"2"/"3"**. They collide. `isProtectedByRecent` — **a local closure inside `SyncEngine.runSyncMessages`, not a method**, matching `recentlyCompleted[msg.messageId]` OR `recentlyCompleted[msg.rfc822MessageId]` — then matches all three rows, the stale sweep skips them, and they survive. 🚨 **BOTH HALVES OF THE SYMPTOM MATTER, AND THE SECOND IS THE FINDING.** The Control test fails — that part is merely noisy. But the `withKnownIssue` block **unexpectedly PASSES**, i.e. a test green on a broken premise. That is the **MIS-014 blessing class**, the same class this push has spent rounds unwinding (ten recorded instances), and it is worse than a plain failure because it reports success. The wrapper is **not** stale — it was checked and records its known issue identically with the production fix in and out — so **re-scoping the test is NOT the answer; the fixture keying is.** **Why NOT a candidate blocker:** an agent proved by controlled comparison — same selector set, production fix reverted — that **the SET varies the outcome and the SOURCE does not**, and both suites pass in isolation. No product invariant is touched and no shipped behaviour depends on it. **The fix shape, for whoever takes it after the audit train:** scope the key by account + folder + database, or clear the global between suites. **`.serialized` does not compose across suite boundaries**, so ordering is not a fix; dependency injection is. **Do NOT fix it in this push** — it lands after the train, and this row exists so the train's reviewers recognise the two failures instead of re-deriving them as candidate regressions. <br><br>🟢 **FIXED by `57f10a76b` — IN THE HARNESS. The row's *"scope the key by account + folder + database"* half is REFUTED and must not be attempted.** **A1 first, because it settles the framing:** `git show 07a4bb703:TabMail/Services/Account/AccountManager.swift` — shipped `v1.6.38` declares the IDENTICAL `private(set) var recentlyCompleted: [String: Date]`, `recordRecentlyCompleted(messageIds:ttl:)`, `pruneRecentlyCompleted()` and `isRecentlyCompleted(_:)`, with the same **bare-id key and no account, folder or database scoping**. There is nothing to restore: the shipped, user-smoke-tested release ships this exact key. **IS THE COLLISION REACHABLE IN PRODUCTION? YES — AND IT IS STILL NOT A PRODUCTION DEFECT, because it can only ever OVER-protect.** On IMAP `messageId` IS the per-folder UID, so `recentlyCompleted["42"]` recorded for INBOX does also match UID 42 in Archive. Every consumer was enumerated rather than assumed, and every one uses the map ONLY to SKIP work: `SyncEngineFullSync.runSyncMessages`' `isRecentlyCompleted` (skip the flag overwrite), its `isProtectedByRecent` and `isProtected` (skip the stale delete), and `SyncEngineDeletionReconcile.deleteConfirmedGhostHeaders`' `isProtected` (skip the ghost delete). `SyncEngineDeltaSync`'s two uses are Gmail/Exchange, whose ids are globally unique and cannot collide at all. **So a false positive costs at most ONE sync pass — 30 s TTL, 120 s for the NSE push-merge entry — and self-corrects; there is no reachable path on which it deletes, mutates or misattributes anything.** **SCOPING THE KEY IS THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION, which is why it was not done:** the map's SECOND leg is the RFC 822 Message-ID, folder- and account-agnostic by construction, and it is precisely what keeps an optimistically-moved row alive at its DESTINATION while `AccountManagerQueue` recorded its SOURCE address — `recordRecentlyCompleted(messageIds: completedIds)` runs at step 2, BEFORE the `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove` re-key at step 3. Folder-scoping that leg removes protection from rows the server still has, i.e. deletes local mail. **THE FIX IS THE ROW'S OTHER SHAPE — clear the global between scopes.** `AccountManager.clearRecentlyCompletedForTesting()` is new, test-only and documented at its declaration; `ProcessGlobalTestState.withLock` calls it **INSIDE the critical section** on entry to every scoped test — deliberately NOT before acquiring, where a queued test would wipe the entries the RUNNING test just recorded. That scope is the only boundary that composes across suites, which is exactly why `.serialized` was never available as a fix. **VERIFICATION.** `RecentlyCompletedScopeIsolationTests` (new) pins the INVARIANT — *which rows the sweep may skip*, never the shape of the dictionary key (MIS-015) — by recording protection for `1/2/3` in one `ProcessGlobalTestState` scope and asserting that a same-epoch complete-knowledge sweep in the NEXT scope still deletes all three; non-vacuity is two-sided (the contaminating entry is asserted present in scope 1, the fetch is asserted to have happened in scope 2). **RED PROOF, recorded:** with the clear commented out, that test fails on the surviving header count, `SyncFullSyncFolderEpochTests.sameEpochStillLetsTheStaleSweepDelete` fails, and `turnoverFetchIsAnUnguardedDeleter` fails with **"Known issue was not recorded"** — the MIS-014 blessing symptom this row identified, reproduced exactly. **BOTH HALVES OF THE SYMPTOM ARE GONE on the green re-run of the same 36-suite selector set:** the Control passes, and the `withKnownIssue` block records `(survivors → 0) == 3` again, i.e. it passes for the RIGHT reason. **ACCEPTED LIMITATION, stated rather than hidden:** the production key stays unscoped, so a cross-folder UID collision still over-protects for one TTL in the app. That is shipped behaviour, it is fail-safe, and narrowing it is out of scope for a test-infrastructure row — a future reader who wants to revisit it must first name a reachable outcome worse than one skipped sync pass. |
| IOS-TEST-007 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — no code, and none owed; the row's own text already settles it as **explicitly NOT a defect and NOT a hole in the system**. **Its full statement and its accepted cost are preserved verbatim below.** | Tests; `FakeIMAPServer.epochStampViolations`; `localRfc822ByUID`; RFC-less rows; `EpochVerifiedBootstrapTests.aStampedFolderNeverHoldsAForeignHeader`; `FakeIMAPServer.recordOracleCheck`; wire wrong-message oracle; oracle coverage | **The epoch-stamp oracle is structurally blind to RFC-less rows — and that is a coverage note, not a gap in the app.** `FakeIMAPServer.epochStampViolations(mailbox:localRfc822ByUID:)` compares, per UID, the server's normalized RFC 822 Message-ID against the local one the caller supplies, and reports a violation on disagreement. Its **local** side comes from `EpochVerifiedBootstrapTests.localRfc822ByUID(pool:folderId:)`, whose loop is `guard let uid = Int(header.messageId), let rfc = header.rfc822MessageId, !rfc.isEmpty else { continue }` — **so a row with no RFC 822 Message-ID is skipped and never enters the map at all.** An RFC-less survivor is therefore structurally invisible to `EpochVerifiedBootstrapTests.aStampedFolderNeverHoldsAForeignHeader`, which is the test that oracle backs. 🚨 **STATED WITH ITS QUALIFIER, because without it this reads as a C3 hole and it is not one:** actual **mutations** are covered by a different and stronger oracle — `FakeIMAPServer.recordOracleCheck(command:mailbox:uids:state:)` runs from inside the already-held state lock at **every** mutating command (`EXPUNGE`, `UID STORE`, `UID MOVE`, `UID EXPUNGE`, `UID COPY`), **BEFORE** the mutation is applied. So no wrong-message mutation can slip past on an RFC-less row; what this oracle cannot see is a *stamped-folder-holds-a-foreign-header* condition on rows that carry no RFC id. Since an RFC-less row has no content witness by construction, there is nothing for an RFC-keyed oracle to compare — **the blindness is a property of the evidence, not an omission in the harness.** **Registered as an OPTIONAL harness note, not as a defect and not as work anyone owes.** If it is ever taken, the shape is a second, non-RFC witness keyed on something an RFC-less row does carry; anyone proposing that must first say what wrong outcome it would catch that `recordOracleCheck` does not, because "more oracle" is not by itself a reason. <br><br>🟢 **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04) — RECLASSIFICATION ONLY, NO CODE, AND NOTHING ABOVE IS RETRACTED.** The status is corrected to match the argument the row already makes. An item whose own conclusion is *"registered as an OPTIONAL harness note, not as a defect and not as work anyone owes"* is a **decision**, and leaving it flagged 🔓 made the register carry a deferred item nobody owes and nobody would take — which is exactly the state the owner's *"no additional task should remain"* directive forbids. **The grounds, restated so the closure stands on its own:** actual **mutations** — the only outcomes C3 is about — are covered by a different and stronger oracle, `FakeIMAPServer.recordOracleCheck(command:mailbox:uids:state:)`, which runs from inside the already-held state lock at **every** mutating command (`EXPUNGE`, `UID STORE`, `UID MOVE`, `UID EXPUNGE`, `UID COPY`) and **BEFORE** the mutation is applied, so no wrong-message mutation can slip past on an RFC-less row; and an RFC-less row has **no content witness by construction**, so an RFC-keyed oracle has nothing to compare — the blindness is a property of the EVIDENCE, not an omission in the harness. **ACCEPTED COST, restated so closing does not bury it:** `EpochVerifiedBootstrapTests.aStampedFolderNeverHoldsAForeignHeader` cannot see a *stamped-folder-holds-a-foreign-header* condition on a row that carries no RFC 822 Message-ID, because `EpochVerifiedBootstrapTests.localRfc822ByUID(pool:folderId:)`'s `guard let uid = Int(header.messageId), let rfc = header.rfc822MessageId, !rfc.isEmpty else { continue }` skips it. **THE BAR FOR REOPENING IS UNCHANGED and is stated in the row above:** anyone proposing a second, non-RFC witness must first name the wrong outcome it would catch that `recordOracleCheck` does not. *"More oracle"* is not by itself a reason. |
| IOS-TEST-008 | ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04, `57f10a76b`)** — census **independently re-run against the tree before any edit and it MATCHES this row exactly: 12 sites, 6 suites** | Tests; provider registry; `AccountManager.unregisterProviderForTesting`; `defer { Task { … } }`; un-awaited teardown; actor job merely enqueued; stale provider; `TestProviderRegistry.withRegisteredProvider`; cross-suite process-global leakage; `.serialized` does not compose | **Provider-registry teardowns that are never AWAITED, so the registry can still hold the stale provider when the next test starts.** `AccountManager` is an `actor`, so an unregister launched from a `defer { Task { … } }` is **merely enqueued** — the test returns before the actor runs it. That is the exact shape all three of the retired `withRegisteredProvider` doc blocks documented as wrong, and which the consolidated `TestProviderRegistry.withRegisteredProvider` (`TabMailTests/Infrastructure/ProcessGlobalTestState.swift`) exists to prevent: its own doc says *"🚨 The unregister MUST be awaited on BOTH exits — never `defer { Task { … } }`."* ⚠️ **THE CENSUS IS TWELVE SITES ACROSS SIX SUITES — corrected here, because the figure this row was raised with was wrong twice over.** The report said *"seven sites across six suites"* while its own enumeration listed **nine across five**, and **both the count and the enumeration missed `AccountManagerQueueDrainTests` entirely**. Re-censused directly against the tree: `BackfillOnlyFolderEpochTests` **4**, `AccountManagerQueueDrainTests` **3**, `InboxGestureActionTests` **2**, `NotificationFolderNativeIdentityTests` **1**, `OutboxStrandedClaimTests` **1**, `QueueCoreInvariantTests` **1** — **12 sites, 6 suites.** Eleven are literally `defer { Task { await …unregisterProviderForTesting… } }`; the twelfth (`QueueCoreInvariantTests`' private `finish(_:)` helper) is a bare un-awaited `Task { }` in a synchronous teardown, which is the same defect through a different shape — recorded distinctly so a mechanical `defer { Task {` grep does not miss it. Every OTHER `unregisterProviderForTesting` call site in the corpus is correctly `await`ed. **Attribution:** none is candidate-introduced; all predate this push. **Why REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING — accepted cost stated plainly:** it has produced **no observed failure of its own**, but it is the same **cross-suite process-global leakage class** as `IOS-TEST-006`, which has already produced two, and `.serialized` does not compose across suite boundaries so ordering cannot contain it. A stale provider surviving into the next suite is precisely how a test asserts against something other than what it thinks it registered. **Do NOT fix it in this push:** awaiting the teardown changes those tests' setup semantics, which is a behavioural change to six suites and belongs in its own scoped task after the audit train. The fix shape is to route all twelve through the existing consolidated `TestProviderRegistry.withRegisteredProvider` rather than hand-editing each `defer`. <br><br>🟢 **FIXED by `57f10a76b` — all twelve routed through `TestProviderRegistry.withRegisteredProvider`, exactly the shape this row prescribed.** **CENSUS RE-VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY, and this row's corrected figure is right:** `BackfillOnlyFolderEpochTests` **4**, `AccountManagerQueueDrainTests` **3**, `InboxGestureActionTests` **2**, `NotificationActionFolderNativeIdentityTests` **1**, `OutboxStrandedClaimTests` **1**, `QueueCoreInvariantTests` **1** — **12 sites, 6 suites.** ⚠ Note the fourth is the **suite TYPE** `NotificationActionFolderNativeIdentityTests`; the FILE is `NotificationFolderNativeIdentityTests.swift` and holds two suites — a filename is not a Swift type (MIS-008). That keeps `body` in the CALLER's isolation domain whatever that domain is, resolves to `MainActor.shared` for all eighteen pre-existing call sites (so their behaviour is unchanged), and avoids forcing five nonisolated suites onto the main actor — which would have been a real behavioural change rather than the benign one. **THIS ROW'S WARNING HELD, BENIGNLY: awaiting the teardown does change those suites' setup semantics, and no adopted suite went red.** `AccountManagerQueueDrainTests`' three `defer` blocks also restored the test DB; the unregister now completes inside the scope and the DB restore still runs after it, so the original relative order is preserved. **VERIFICATION.** `ProviderRegistryTeardownContractTests` (new) pins the INVARIANT — the registry's END STATE at the scope boundary on BOTH the normal and the throwing exit, plus the documented consequence that a LATER scope's registration survives 200 cooperative yields — rather than grepping the corpus for `defer { Task {`, which would pin the fix's mechanism and stay green the first time the same defect is written a different way (MIS-015). **RED PROOF, recorded:** reverting the helper to the un-awaited shape (which makes all twelve sites behave exactly as before) fails `normalExitLeavesTheRegistrySettled` at `providers[accountId] == nil`. Green re-run of the same 36-suite selector set passes. **UNRELATED PRE-EXISTING CRASH SURFACED BY THIS WORK AND NOT FIXED — registered separately as `IOS-TEST-009`.** |
| IOS-TEST-009 | ~~🔓 OPEN~~ → ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04, `87c805784`)** — test harness only: 2 files, `TabMailTests` target, **zero production code**. ⚠️ **ONE FACTUAL CLAIM IN THIS ROW IS CORRECTED BELOW — it mis-implicated a test that never starts a drain. Do not restore it.** **Superseded status text, preserved verbatim and still true on every fact it asserts:** *"test infrastructure. PRE-EXISTING and NOT candidate-attributable: it reproduces IDENTICALLY on the pre-fix code. Found while fixing `IOS-TEST-008`; reported, not fixed (out of scope)."* | Tests; `NeverDropExitClosureTests.batchSplitChildrenRemainExecutable`; `InboxGestureActionTests`; `FakeHTTP.Scenario`; `defer { http.close() }`; `InboxViewModel.delete`; `AccountManagerActions.queueDraftDelete`; unstructured `Task { await drainPendingQueue() }`; `GmailProvider.deleteDraft`; `NSGenericException`; *Task created in a session that has been invalidated*; test-process crash + restart | **A test closes a fake URLSession while an unstructured drain the production code itself started is still in flight, and the whole test process dies.** `InboxViewModel.delete` → `AccountManagerActions.queueDraftDelete` fires `Task { await drainPendingQueue() }` — unstructured, so it outlives the test body. `InboxGestureActionTests`' two Gmail draft tests (`staleGenerationDraftsPlaceholderDeletesNothingAndStaysRetryable`, `realGmailDraftsRowIsDeletedByItsOwnProviderAddress`) build a `FakeHTTP.Scenario()` and `defer { http.close() }`. When the escaped drain reaches `GmailProvider.deleteDraft` → `trashContainedDraftMessage` → `performHTTPRequest` after that `close()`, `URLSession.data(for:)` raises `NSGenericException` *'Task created in a session that has been invalidated'*, which is an **uncaught ObjC exception**: `libc++abi` terminates the process, xcodebuild logs *"Restarting after unexpected exit, crash, or test timeout"*, and the run is reported as `** TEST EXECUTE FAILED **` with `NeverDropExitClosureTests.batchSplitChildrenRemainExecutable()` named — **the test that happened to be running next, not the test at fault.** That misattribution is the reason this is registered rather than left to be rediscovered. **ATTRIBUTION, established by controlled comparison rather than asserted:** the same crash, same exception, same next-test attribution appears on the INVERTED build — i.e. with the pre-`57f10a76b` un-awaited `defer { Task { … } }` teardown restored — so it is pre-existing. `57f10a76b` in fact **narrows** the window, because the provider unregister now completes BEFORE `defer { http.close() }` runs, where previously it was merely enqueued. **Why it is not a product defect:** nothing here is app behaviour. The escaped drain is real production code doing the right thing (a queued intention is drained promptly); what is wrong is a TEST tearing down a network seam the drain is still using. **Why it is nonetheless worth fixing:** an uncaught ObjC exception kills the whole test process, so every test not yet run in that launch is silently dropped and the summary is stitched together from two launches — the same evidence-destroying shape as `IOS-TEST-006`, one layer down. **The fix shape, for whoever takes it:** make the two tests deterministic about the work they started — drain to quiescence before the scope ends (the bounded-loop idiom `BackfillOnlyFolderEpochTests` already uses), or keep the `FakeHTTP.Scenario` alive past it — rather than widening any production guard. ⚠️ **Do NOT "fix" it by making `queueDraftDelete` stop kicking a drain**: that is a user-intention-latency change made to satisfy a test, and the never-drop contract owns that decision, not this row. <br><br>🟢 **FIXED by `87c805784`, "Fix IOS-TEST-009: test scopes tore down transport a drain still needed" — 2 files, `TabMailTests` only, ZERO production code.** **WHAT IT WAS.** `AccountManagerActions.queueDraftDelete` ends its success path with an unstructured `Task { await drainPendingQueue() }`, which outlives the test body that started it. The test's `defer { http.close() }` then called `URLSession.invalidateAndCancel()` on the very session the escaped drain was about to use, so `GmailProvider.deleteDraft` → `trashContainedDraftMessage` → `performHTTPRequest` raised the Objective-C `NSGenericException` *'Task created in a session that has been invalidated'* on its next `dataTask` creation. That exception is **uncatchable in Swift**, so `libc++abi` terminated the whole test process. <br><br>🚨 **WHY THIS MATTERED FAR MORE THAN A FLAKY TEST — this is the half that must survive.** The crash **silently dropped every test not yet run in that launch**, and xcodebuild blamed the **wrong** test. The observed pre-fix evidence, recorded verbatim rather than paraphrased: the faulty test `realGmailDraftsRowIsDeletedByItsOwnProviderAddress()` itself **PASSED**; `NeverDropExitClosureTests.batchSplitChildrenRemainExecutable()` recorded **two spurious expectation failures**; then `libc++abi` killed the process; then *"Restarting after unexpected exit, crash, or test timeout"*; then the relaunch reported **`Executed 0 tests`**; and the run ended `** TEST EXECUTE FAILED **` naming `NeverDropExitClosureTests.batchSplitChildrenRemainExecutable()` — the test that happened to be next, not the test at fault. **A truncated run reports a plausible pass count that is simply cut short**, which is why this made the full-suite gate untrustworthy and why a pre-existing, non-candidate-attributable defect was worth fixing inside this push at all. <br><br>**THE FIX.** New file `TabMailTests/Infrastructure/EscapedDrainTransportScope.swift`. `EscapedDrainTransport.withProviderAndTransport` composes provider registration and transport lifetime into **ONE** scope so the ordering cannot be skipped, and awaits drain settlement before `transport.close()`. **Composition is NECESSARY, not tidiness:** the drain's claim loop skips operations whose account has no registered provider, so unregistering the provider before awaiting settlement would make the operation **permanently unclaimable** — `everAttempted` would never flip and the barrier would wait out its whole bound. Two scopes could be written in the wrong order; one cannot. <br><br>**WHY A NEW BARRIER RATHER THAN AN EXISTING ONE — and NO EXISTING BARRIER'S BOUND WAS WIDENED.** *"Never widen a barrier's bound"* is a standing rule here (the recorded quiescence-flake lesson), so state the compliance explicitly: **not one existing barrier was touched.** They could not be reused for two independent reasons. (1) Every existing barrier waits for queue **EMPTINESS**, which is unreachable in this scenario — the drain's Gmail call has no matcher on some paths, so the operation requeues forever and emptiness never arrives. (2) Every existing barrier **ASKS FOR** a drain, and `drainPendingQueue`'s first statement is `guard !isDraining else { needsRedrain = true; return }`, so asking re-arms `needsRedrain` and cannot express *"the drain this gesture already started has finished"*. ⚠️ **THE COUNT IS FIVE EXISTING BARRIERS, NOT FOUR — corrected here because `87c805784`'s own commit body says "four".** Censused by symbol against the tree: `FinishTheMoveLocallyTests.drainToQuiescence`, `ProviderIdQueueFuzzTests.drainProviderQueue`, `IdResetDispositionMatrixTests.drainProviderQueue`, `PostSendServerDraftCleanupTests.drainUntilSettled` and `DraftDeleteEpochBoundaryTests.drainUntilSettled` — **five**, and all five wait on emptiness and all five call `drainPendingQueue()`, so the substantive argument holds unchanged and is in fact one instance stronger. **The new barrier `EscapedDrainTransport.awaitPendingQueueSettled` never calls `drainPendingQueue()` at all** (verified: zero call sites in the file, comments aside) and adds **exactly one** signal — the **durable** `PendingOperation.everAttempted` witness, read back through `pool.read` — which closes the start race a bare `pendingQueueIsQuiescentForTesting()` poll leaves open, namely that the escaped drain task may not have started when the poll runs. On bound expiry it records a `Testing` issue naming the observed state rather than hanging or passing silently. <br><br>**A1 — PREVIOUS RELEASE: the shipped architecture is NONEXISTENT for this problem, so authoring was the correct branch rather than the default fallback.** At `07a4bb703` (shipped `v1.6.38`) `TabMailTests/Infrastructure/FakeHTTP.swift` has **no `Scenario`, no `close()` and no `invalidateAndCancel()`** — only a process-global `makeSession()`. **A session that is never invalidated cannot raise this exception**, and that release's `InboxGestureActionTests` contains neither of the two tests involved. Independently re-verified against the tag for this row. **And the same release corroborates the do-not-touch-production constraint from the release itself:** it already spawns the identical escaped `Task { await drainPendingQueue() }` design from `AccountManagerActions` — **6 sites there versus v3's 7** — so the production shape is intended and long-shipped, not a v3 accident to be corrected under cover of a test fix. <br><br>**VERIFICATION.** The 21-selector run went **191 tests / 20 suites → 192 tests / 21 suites**, `** TEST EXECUTE SUCCEEDED **`, the delta being exactly the new invariant test (confirmed **by display name**, per MIS-IOS-006, not by total count): *"The drain a gesture starts inside a scope completes against that scope's own transport"* in `EscapedDrainTransportLifetimeTests`. A **60-iteration race hunt on the exact pre-fix reproducer**, which previously **died at iteration 5**, completed **900 tests / 120 suites passed** with zero `libc++abi` / `NSGenericException` / *"Restarting after unexpected"* markers. **Red proof, invariant-pinning:** the barrier was temporarily inverted and **all three boundary assertions failed**; the inversion was reverted immediately and the restored file verified byte-identical with `cmp`. The test asserts the **SYSTEM PROPERTY at the teardown boundary** — the drain's wire call was served, the queue is quiescent, and no operation is left unattempted — rather than the fix's mechanism (MIS-015). Warning census by `(file, raw message)`: **exactly 1** occurrence of the sanctioned `Metadata extraction skipped. No AppIntents.framework dependency found.` and **zero** other warnings. <br><br>**ACCEPTED LIMITATION, stated rather than hidden.** The **seven** production fire-and-forget `Task { await drainPendingQueue() }` sites in `AccountManagerActions.swift` are **unchanged BY DESIGN** — they are not made structured, awaited or cancellable, because a user-intent action must not block on its wire drain (optimistic UI is the product contract, ADR-IOS-001). The barrier also covers the pending-operation drain only; other escaped work (sync passes, AI processing) is out of scope and untouched. Settlement is bounded by polling rather than signalled. The scope was applied at the two call sites that actually drive a drain-firing gesture; the wider `http.close()` census (17 sites across 7 files) was checked and the rest drive no such gesture. <br><br>⚠️ **CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S ORIGINAL STATEMENT — IT MIS-IMPLICATED A TEST, AND THE CORRECTION IS RECORDED HERE SO A LATER READER DOES NOT RESTORE THE WRONG CLAIM.** The original text above names **both** Gmail draft tests as reaching the escaped drain. **Only ONE of them ever starts a drain: `realGmailDraftsRowIsDeletedByItsOwnProviderAddress`.** `staleGenerationDraftsPlaceholderDeletesNothingAndStaysRetryable` **refuses earlier and never reaches `queueDraftDelete` at all.** **Verified against source for this row rather than transcribed from the commit body**, symbol by symbol: `InboxViewModel.deleteDraftMessage` first computes `placeholderMatches` by comparing `PendingOperation.draftPlaceholderMessageId(draftId:instanceEpoch:)` against `message.messageId`, and in that test the header carries the **stale**-epoch placeholder while the `Draft` row's `instanceEpoch` is the **live** one — the test's own fixture guard asserts those two ids differ — so `placeholderMatches` is empty and the owned-draft arm (the only earlier `queueDraftDelete` call site) is not taken. Control then falls to the header-only arm, whose `.gmail` branch is `guard !isSyntheticPlaceholderId(message.messageId) else { return false }`; `draftPlaceholderMessageId` mints a `"draft-…"` id and `isSyntheticPlaceholderId` matches on the `draft-` prefix, so the function **returns `false` before** the header-only `queueDraftDelete` call — and `queueDraftDelete` fires its `Task { await drainPendingQueue() }` **only on its success path, after the durable insert**. The test's own assertions agree: it requires `acted == false` and **zero** admitted `.deleteDraft` operations. The sibling test's tapped id is `"18f0c9d2e4b6a1c3"`, which carries neither the `draft-` nor the `sent-` prefix, so it passes the guard, admits exactly one operation, and **is** the leg that starts the drain. **Why the correction is kept rather than the original silently edited:** the original claim is wrong in a way that reads as plausible, and the register is history as well as status — both tests do build a `FakeHTTP.Scenario`, and both now carry the scope, so a reader checking only the test file would re-derive the wrong conclusion. The fix is applied to both call sites regardless: the scope is cheap, and pinning the non-draining leg to *"no drain therefore no `defer { http.close() }` needed"* would be a mechanism-shaped assumption that a later edit could silently invalidate. |
| IOS-DOC-001 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-04)** — ⚠️ **the item's own premise was FALSE and is refuted here by measuring the right artifact** | Documentation; `tabmail-ios/CLAUDE.md`; companion budget; `compact_companion_docs.rb verify`; working tree vs committed; un-routed duplicate bodies; `Companion/Rules/Active/resilience.md`; `Companion/Rules/Active/user-interaction-freeze.md` | **This was carried as *"`CLAUDE.md` is 40,401 B against a 30,000 B budget (+34 %), the sole remaining verify violation"*. ⚠️ THAT IS FALSE OF THE REPOSITORY, and it dissolves the moment the committed file and the working tree are measured separately.** `git show HEAD:CLAUDE.md` is **15,176 B — 49 % HEADROOM, comfortably passing**; the working tree is 40,401 B; the difference, **25,225 B, is UNCOMMITTED OWNER CONTENT** (THE MANTRA, THE ADDRESS PROBLEM, and the A1–A13 audit-supervision block). **The repository does not violate the budget.** `Scripts/compact_companion_docs.rb verify` measures the **working tree**, so it reports a violation the committed repo does not have. **Record that, because the next agent to run `verify` will otherwise re-open this row and re-derive the same wrong conclusion.** **What IS real, and is still not worth landing:** two byte-identical twins remain in the *committed* file — `## Resilience Rules` ≡ `Companion/Rules/Active/resilience.md` (**1,977 B**, measured) and `## User Interaction Freeze Rule` ≡ `Companion/Rules/Active/user-interaction-freeze.md` (**1,184 B**, measured). Both bodies were diffed: **IDENTICAL**. They are genuine un-routed duplicates of the `23ea83b62` class. **DISPOSITION: CLOSED AS A DECISION — do not route. Three grounds, in order of weight.** **(1)** It buys **3,161 B** against a budget already met with **49.4 % headroom** (all four figures re-measured for this row, not transcribed). There is no problem to solve. **(2) It cannot land cleanly.** `git add CLAUDE.md` stages the whole file, sweeping the owner's 25,225 B of uncommitted work into someone else's commit — which the standing constraint forbids outright. Partial-hunk staging on a file the owner is actively editing is the wrong trade for 3 KB of hygiene. **(3)** The only edit that would move the working-tree number is routing A1–A13 out, and that is **plausibly harmful** rather than neutral: A9's own text reads *"This gets forgotten after every compaction; re-read it on resume."* Those rules are written to be always-loaded, and routing makes them search-conditional. **Stated as a consideration, not an absolute** — a future owner may reasonably decide the index budget outranks it, and this row is the place to argue against, per the register's own instruction for decision rows. **Accepted cost:** `verify` continues to report a `CLAUDE.md` budget violation against the working tree for as long as the owner's uncommitted block sits there. **Recoverability is not the relevant test** — nothing is refused or deferred by this decision and there is no failure state to recover from; the cost is a noisy verify line, never a lost or misdirected message. |

## The warning sweep found tests that do not assert what their names claim

The task-#16 sweep (`412b60c0b`) drove the pre-existing `TabMailTests` warning census from **81 unique
`(file, RAW message)` pairs to 9**, production target unchanged at **0**. Nine pairs were deliberately
left, and **eight of them are the compiler pointing at a vacuous test** — an unused binding is often
the diagnostic that a test read the evidence and then never asserted on it. They are registered here
rather than silenced, because satisfying the compiler (rename to `_`, delete the binding) would have
**blessed** the vacuity permanently and removed the only signal that it exists.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-TEST-004 | Surfaced by the task-#16 warning sweep (`412b60c0b`); **class B/C** (all predate the v3 candidate range); ✅ **FIXED by `b87804055`** — the vacuous tests now assert against production, so the warnings are no longer needed as the standing signal | Tests; vacuous assertions; blessing tests; unused binding; `ConnectionResilienceTests.successResetsFailureCount`; `SyncStartupTests.budgetBusyExitsAtDeadline`; `SyncStartupTests.cancelDuringBudgetDrain`; `ICSBuilderBuildInvitationBodyCoverageTests.omitsEmptyDescription`; `SyncPatternIntegrationTests.actionTagsOnlyForInbox`; `OutboxIntegrationTests.originalNotFoundIsNoOp`; `TestDatabase.make()`; `try? #require`; `will never be executed` | ⚠️ **CORRECTED by the final audit train — this row originally claimed SIX vacuous tests and the real number is FIVE. Both reviewers independently refuted the `ICSBuilder` entry, and the correction is recorded rather than silently applied, because a register row that overstates a defect is the same failure mode this row is about.** `ICSBuilderBuildInvitationBodyCoverageTests.omitsEmptyDescription` is **NOT vacuous**: it calls production `ICSBuilder.buildInvitationBody(spec)` and asserts `#expect(!html.contains("</p><p>"))`, which genuinely fails if an empty-description paragraph is emitted between the When paragraph and the styled footer. Only its unused `descCount` binding is defective — the *count* assertion is a comment, the *content* assertion is real. ⚠️ **This row also named the wrong suite type.** It said `ICSBuilderEdgeCaseTests`, which is the FILE name and the type of the first `@Suite` in it; the test at `omitsEmptyDescription` is enclosed by `@Suite("ICSBuilder buildInvitationBody Coverage Gaps") struct ICSBuilderBuildInvitationBodyCoverageTests`. One reviewer repeated the same wrong type — **a filename is not a Swift type**, and this file holds six distinct suites. The corrected statement follows. **Five tests assert on their own simulation, or on nothing at all, while their names claim production coverage.** This is the same class that `1e53b517c` found in the undo suites, where 11 of 13 tests never reached production code; these are the instances the warning census independently surfaced elsewhere in the suite. **Two were verified directly by the coordinator, not transcribed:** (a) `OutboxIntegrationTests.originalNotFoundIsNoOp` contains **zero `#expect`s**, reads `SELECT changes()` **before** the `UPDATE` it was meant to measure, names `resolveOriginalMessage` only in a comment while hand-writing raw SQL, and ends with the comment *"If we reach here, no crash — test passes"* — it can only fail by throwing; (b) `SyncPatternIntegrationTests.actionTagsOnlyForInbox` binds `let isInInbox = false` and then branches on `if isInInbox`, so the body is unreachable **by construction** (that is the source of its `will never be executed` diagnostic) and the test re-implements the production rule as a constant instead of calling it. **The remaining four, reported by the sweep and NOT independently re-verified — the final audit train must confirm before anyone acts on them:** `ConnectionResilienceTests.successResetsFailureCount` increments a LOCAL counter over a literal array and asserts its own arithmetic, exercising no production code (3 of the 8 pairs); `SyncStartupTests.budgetBusyExitsAtDeadline` discards the measured wall-clock that IS its "exits at deadline" evidence, leaving the only deadline assertion inside an `if hasPoll`; `SyncStartupTests.cancelDuringBudgetDrain` reads the cancellation evidence and never asserts it, its sole `#expect` naming an event the deadline path also emits; `ICSBuilderBuildInvitationBodyCoverageTests.omitsEmptyDescription` — **RETRACTED, see the correction at the head of this row: it is not vacuous.** Only the unused `descCount` binding is defective; the test does call production and does assert. **Why REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING:** no production behaviour is affected — a vacuous test is absent coverage, not a defect in the app. It is registered because absent coverage that PRESENTS as present is what allowed nine never-drop violations to pass 8,297 green tests, and because the correct disposition for such a test is often **deletion with a named real-path pin**, not repair — a judgement that belongs to the final train rather than to a warning sweep. ⚠️ **The 9th left pair is unrelated and is not a vacuity:** `'init(frame:)' was deprecated in iOS 26.0` (2 occurrences, `CaretAwareScrollViewTests`). `UIWindow()` is deprecated identically on iOS 26, and the documented `init(windowScene:)` requires a live `UIWindowScene` a unit test has no guaranteed access to, so the sweep reverted rather than change what the test constructs. **Related mechanism finding, fixed by the sweep and recorded because the underlying gap is not fixed:** `try? #require(<optional>)` is a **compile-time no-op** — in a `try?` context the macro's generic parameter binds to the optional type, so `#require` receives a never-nil value and never fires. Removing it at the 3 affected sites is provably semantics-preserving, but the *intended* nil-check was never in force there, and the follow-on `if let … { #expect(…) }` can still pass vacuously. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `b87804055`, "Close the test-accuracy cluster: 5 vacuous tests, 4 wrong names".** **The invariant that now holds:** each of the five named tests reaches a production symbol and asserts a property of the app, so absent coverage no longer PRESENTS as present. Per test, stating what is asserted now that was not before: `ConnectionResilienceTests.successResetsFailureCount` scripts a real `MockEmailProvider` fail/fail/**succeed**/fail, classifies each outcome through the production classifier `SyncEngine.isConnectionError`, and pins the TRANSITION `[1, 2, 0, 1]` with a two-sided non-vacuity check that four `fetchMessages(` entries reached the provider's call log (the old *"< maxConsecutiveFailures"* assertion was dropped because **production has no such cap** — exponential backoff to a 60 s ceiling — which is documented inline). `SyncStartupTests.budgetBusyExitsAtDeadline` and `cancelDuringBudgetDrain` now assert unconditionally instead of only inside `if hasPoll`, and each asserts the NEGATIVE it is named for; **strengthening them exposed a latent harness defect that had silently voided both** — `MockQueue.cancelAllInFlight()` set `_idle = true` and `repopulateFromDatabase()` never restored the configured busy state, so `setIdleAfterChecks(99999)` was defeated and both tests took the idle exit in ~40 µs without ever reaching the deadline; the busy test now runs 5.03 s (was ~0.00 s), which is itself the evidence it reaches the deadline. `SyncPatternIntegrationTests.actionTagsOnlyForInbox` no longer pins `let isInInbox = false` behind `if isInInbox` (dead by construction — that was the source of its `will never be executed` diagnostic); it inserts BOTH an `.archive` and an `.inbox` folder and derives the gate the way `SyncEngineFullSync` does, so **both arms execute**. `OutboxIntegrationTests.originalNotFoundIsNoOp` had ZERO expectations and read `SELECT changes()` **before** the `UPDATE` it was measuring; it now drives the real `AccountManager.deleteCompletedSendAtomic(outboxId:db:)` → `resolveOriginalMessage` with an unresolvable id and a **BYSTANDER** header carrying the same RFC 822 id as the outbox row's `inReplyTo`, asserting the bystander is not mutated, that no `PendingOperation` was queued, and that the outbox row was deleted — **which also pins the `IOS-IMAP-002` ban on RFC-search resolution**. ⚠️ **THE `ICSBuilder` RETRACTION IN THIS ROW STANDS AND WAS HONOURED:** `omitsEmptyDescription` is NOT vacuous and was not "repaired" as though it were; only its unused `descCount` binding was defective, and `descCount` is now load-bearing (the paragraph count is asserted `== 3` alongside the existing empty-paragraph check, with a comment recording that the two assertions fail on different defects). **The 9th warning pair remains untouched** — `'init(frame:)' was deprecated in iOS 26.0` in `CaretAwareScrollViewTests` — because it is not a vacuity and the documented replacement needs a live `UIWindowScene` a unit test has no guaranteed access to. |

## T4.V14 / decision D10 — owner-gated, finding recorded so the decision is made on evidence

`PLAN_IOS_REFACTOR_V3.md` defers T4.V14 (account-scoped user labels) behind decision **D10** and says
*do not start before that decision*. That deferral stands — **nothing below is fixed**. The row exists
because the plan's framing of the gap turned out to be narrower than the code's, and the owner should
make D10 against what is actually there rather than against the plan's summary of it.

⚠️ **The plan's premise — "labels are not account-scoped" — is half wrong, and the half that is wrong
changes what the migration has to do.** The `userLabel` TABLE is already account-scoped: `accountId` is
a `.notNull()` column with `.references("account", onDelete: .cascade)`, and `UserLabel.accountId`
exists on the record type. Three of the five `UserLabelStore` readers already filter on it
(`allLabels(accountId:)`, `findByName(_:accountId:)`, `labelsSortedForMenu(accountId:)`). So D10 is
**not** "add an `accountId` column".

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-LABEL-001 | Surfaced by orchestrator investigation while scoping T4.V14, not by an audit round; **class C** (shipped verbatim in `v1.6.38`); was owner-gated on decision D10; ✅ **FIXED by `168b7a665`** with migration `v82` — the migration this row predicted, by the number it predicted | User labels; Gmail labels; IMAP keywords; `userLabel`; `messageUserLabel`; primary key; `t.primaryKey("id", .text)`; cross-account collision; `UserLabelStore.loadLabels(for:in:)`; `UserLabelStore.labelsForMessage(_:in:)`; `labels(ids:accountId:in:)`; `SyncEngineFullSync`; `.save(db)` upsert; `onConflict: .ignore`; `onDelete: .cascade`; multi-account | **`userLabel`'s primary key is the label id ALONE (`t.primaryKey("id", .text)`), but a label id is only unique WITHIN an account — so two accounts that share a label name share one row.** The id is provider-supplied and not namespaced: the Gmail arm of `SyncEngineFullSync` sets `labelId = info.path`, and the IMAP/keyword arms in `SyncEngineFullSync`, `SyncEngineBackfillDeep` and `SyncEngineDeltaSync` all set `UserLabel(id: labelId, accountId:, name: labelId, isSystem: false)` from the raw keyword. Two Gmail accounts each having a `Receipts` label, or two IMAP accounts each using a `work` keyword, therefore collide on the PK. **Three consequences, all verified at the tip.** **(1) The row's owner flaps.** The Gmail arm writes with `.save(db)` (an upsert, and its own comment says `// upsert`), so whichever account syncs last overwrites `accountId`, `name` and `isSystem` on the shared row. The IMAP arms write with `.insert(db, onConflict: .ignore)`, so there the FIRST writer wins permanently and later accounts silently bind to a row that names someone else's `accountId`. **(2) A cascade crosses the account boundary.** The Gmail stale sweep deletes with `staleLabelIds.contains(Column("id")) && Column("accountId") == account.id` — the FILTER is account-scoped, but the ROW it deletes is global, and `messageUserLabel.userLabelId` references `userLabel` with `onDelete: .cascade`. So account A removing a label server-side drops the shared row and, with it, **every association row for account B's messages**. **(3) The read path that the plan's `labels(ids:accountId:in:)` = 0 hits actually names.** `UserLabelStore.loadLabels(for messageIds:in:)` and `UserLabelStore.labelsForMessage(_:in:)` join `messageUserLabel → userLabel` by id **with no `accountId` predicate at all** — they are the two readers that are not scoped, and they are the ones that render a message's label chips. A message in account B is therefore displayed with whatever `name`/`isSystem` the shared row currently carries, which after (1) may be account A's. **Concrete reachability:** any user with two accounts on the same provider who uses the same label or keyword name in both — an ordinary configuration, not an exotic one. **Why REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING:** no message is mutated, misattributed, or destroyed; no wire operation is issued; no queued intention is dropped or wedged. Consequence (3) is local display only, and consequence (2) is recovered by the next sync of the affected messages, because `SyncEngineDeltaSync` and `SyncEngineBackfillDeep` re-insert each association from the server's own `info.userLabelIds` — the server, not the local row, is the source of truth for which labels a message carries. It fails toward showing the wrong label NAME, never toward acting on the wrong message. **Why it is nonetheless owner-gated rather than shrugged off:** the fix is a data-model change, which is exactly what D10 is for. Making the identity account-unique means either a composite primary key `(accountId, id)` or a derived surrogate id, and **either choice must also rewrite every existing `messageUserLabel.userLabelId` value**, so it needs a real migration (next free number is `v82`; the shipped `v1.6.38` line ends at `v67`, and `v68`–`v81` are post-release). Scoping the two unscoped readers WITHOUT changing the key would not fix it — with one shared row there is nothing for an `accountId` predicate to disambiguate, and adding the predicate alone would make account B's chips vanish instead of showing the wrong name. **That ordering is the reason this row exists:** the smaller-looking change is not a partial fix, it is a different bug. <br><br>✅ **FIXED by `168b7a665`, "Scope userLabel identity to its account and keep the wire value bare" — and the ordering this row insisted on is exactly the ordering the fix took.** **The invariant that now holds:** a label's identity is unique per account, so two accounts sharing a label name no longer share a row — the owner cannot flap, a stale sweep in one account cannot cascade away another account's associations, and the display path cannot render account A's name on account B's message. `UserLabel` carries a surrogate primary key `"<accountId>:<providerLabelId>"` plus a new `providerLabelId` column holding the provider's own value; `accountId` is a colon-free UUID string, so the concatenation is **injective** and `(accountId, providerLabelId)` uniqueness follows from the primary key with no second index. The memberwise initialiser is suppressed by a single deterministic `init(accountId:providerLabelId:name:isSystem:)` — mirroring `Folder.init` — so **every unrouted construction site is a compile error rather than a silent bare-id row.** ⚠️ **THE VALUE ON THE WIRE STAYS BARE, and this is the load-bearing half:** `PendingOperation.userLabelId` is a RAW PROVIDER ARGUMENT — the drain hands it to Gmail as `addLabelIds:` and to IMAP as `STORE +FLAGS (<keyword>)` — so all three construction sites pass `providerLabelId`, never `id`. Putting the surrogate there would be **worse than a loud failure**: on IMAP it writes a bogus keyword onto the user's real message, and on Gmail the API answers `"Invalid label"`, which `GmailProvider.isAuthoritativeActionRejection` classifies as a provider-authoritative no-op — so the operation would leave the queue **as if it had succeeded**, silently discarding the user's intention. **Migration `v82_accountScopedUserLabelIdentity` REBUILDS both tables rather than rewriting keys in place** (precedent: `v2_dropMessageHeaderFolderFK`): an in-place `UPDATE` of the primary key can collide TRANSIENTLY with a not-yet-rewritten sibling — a provider keyword literally named `<accountId>:x` — which would abort the migration and **brick launch**; and `providerLabelId TEXT NOT NULL` cannot be added in place without a bogus default. Associations are re-pointed through `messageHeader.accountId`, the only evidence of who a legacy association belonged to; labels created but never applied survive via a second insert keyed off the legacy row's own `accountId`; associations whose owning message is already gone are deleted first, because they carry no account and cannot be re-pointed. A fresh install (`v33` then `v82`) and an upgraded database converge on the same schema, **asserted behaviourally by a test** rather than by inspection. **The tests assert the SYSTEM PROPERTY, not the mechanism** — which rows survive, which name renders, what reached the wire — and none asserts that the id contains a colon, which would stay green on a system whose accounts still collide. **ACCEPTED LIMITATION, stated by that commit:** a legacy row reachable only through associations is reconstructed with `COALESCE(l.name, p.labelId)`, so in the rare case where the hijacked row's name belonged to the other account, that account's label is renamed to its provider id until the next sync overwrites it. **No association and no row is lost.** |

## Fixed by D4 (previously latent in shipped releases)

`KNOWN_ISSUES.md` is a register of *deferred* behavior, so a fixed defect does not belong in the
table above. These two are recorded here anyway, below the table, because both were **latent in
shipped code** and a future reader who finds the old shape in `git log` needs to know it was a bug
and what closed it.

| ID | Defect | Closed by |
|---|---|---|
| **IOS-IMAP-002** | **A duplicate RFC 822 Message-ID on IMAP made one swipe mutate EVERY copy.** `MessageHeader.stableId` returned the RFC whenever `messageId` was numeric, and `IMAPProvider.resolveUID` fell through to `searchByMessageId` and returned **the entire `UIDSet` with no cardinality check**; its consumer stored flags on that whole set. Reachable with aliases, which are common. **Latent at and before `v1.6.38` — not a post-`v1.6.38` regression.** | ADR-IOS-068 (D4 native-provider-id keying). ~~Under D4 there is no multi-match set to check, because nothing searches.~~ ⚠️ **THAT SENTENCE IS RETRACTED — it was FALSE at `60a8751cb` and the final audit train caught it.** `IMAPProvider.searchByMessageId` still had three live call sites, and one of them — `saveDraft`'s no-`APPENDUID` arm — converted a `SEARCH` result into a **draft mutation address** (`.created(.imap(…))`), which `DraftStore.applyPushCompletion` persisted and `deleteDraftStrong` later mutated with `STORE \Deleted` plus, on a UIDPLUS server, an **irreversible `UID EXPUNGE`**. The exact-verify and cardinality guards the candidate added prove *"exactly one message carries this Message-ID"*, **not** *"this is the message we appended"* — so a same-Message-ID sibling could be expunged instead. That was a **BLOCKING C3** finding and is **fixed separately** (the arm now returns `.unaddressable`; absence of `APPENDUID` yields no address at all). The lesson this register row should carry: **"nothing searches" is a claim about the whole tree and must be re-verified by grep, not inherited from an ADR's intent.** The rest of the row stands, and the original defect remains fixed. Pinned by a red-first invariant test. |
| **IOS-IMAP-004** | **Without `COPYUID` there was no proof our own COPY landed**, so a move interrupted after the COPY could not safely clean up the source, and with aliases a pre-existing sibling copy in the destination is indistinguishable from our own. | Closed by the SwiftMail upstream sync: `copy()`/`move()` now return a server-verified `CopyUID` carrying the destination UIDVALIDITY and a source→destination mapping. **Where the server advertises UIDPLUS, `COPYUID` is the only accepted witness — no weaker one is substituted.** ⚠️ **Narrowed, audit round 3 (2026-08-03).** The original sentence was categorical and, read that way, forbade source cleanup on any server without UIDPLUS — which wedged that entire class permanently (they can *never* furnish `COPYUID`, so the op could never reach a never-drop exit and starved every later gesture on that message). **Adjudication: on a non-UIDPLUS server the COPY's own tagged `OK` authorizes source cleanup.** It answers the question cleanup actually asks — *did my copy land?*: RFC 3501 §6.4.7 requires an unsuccessful COPY to restore the mailbox, and in the SwiftMail fork a tagged `NO`/`BAD` throws (`BaseIMAPCommandHandler.processResponse` → `BaseIMAPCommandHandler.handleTaggedErrorResponse`, overridden by `CopyHandler.handleTaggedErrorResponse` → `IMAPError.copyFailed`) and cannot return normally. `COPYUID` additionally says *where* the copy landed, which cleanup does not need. The rationale's second half — *"with aliases a pre-existing sibling copy is indistinguishable from our own"* — concerns a destination **SEARCH**, which this path never performs; it stays ADR-IOS-068/D4-clean. **Accepted cost:** without `COPYUID` the source UIDs cannot be named, so the source copy is left visible-and-`\Deleted` rather than expunged (a folder-wide `EXPUNGE` is forbidden by `IOS-IMAP-001`) until something else expunges the folder. The superseded fallback — *"drop the op and let sync reconcile"* — is **not** available under NEVER DROP USER INTENTION and must not be reinstated. ⚠️ **CORRECTED TWICE MORE, audit round 4 (2026-08-03).** (a) The round-3 wording above **overclaimed**: it said the tagged OK settles the question *"conclusively"* and, at the code, that it proves *"the whole named set"* copied. It does not. The command is a `UID COPY`, and RFC 3501 §6.4.8 states that *"a non-existent unique identifier is ignored without any error message generated. Thus, it is possible for a UID FETCH command to return an OK without any data or a UID COPY or UID STORE to return an OK without performing any operations."* A named-but-absent UID is simply not one of *"the specified message(s)"* §6.4.7 speaks about, so the OK is silent about it — and the op was retired as provider success anyway, which in a batch let an absent member ride out of the queue on a present sibling's OK. (b) The claim that `COPYUID` *"is the only accepted witness"* wherever UIDPLUS is advertised was itself a permanent wedge for part of that class: RFC 4315 §3 makes the response code a SHOULD **"with limited exceptions, discussed below"** — a mailbox the client may COPY or APPEND to but not SELECT or EXAMINE (*"SHOULD NOT send"*), and a `UIDNOTSTICKY` mail store (*"MAY omit"*) — both properties of the mailbox rather than of the attempt, so for those servers it never arrives, the op reached none of the four never-drop exits, its lane halted on every drain, and each drain re-issued the COPY and seated another destination duplicate. **What the fix now guarantees, per member and per mutation kind:** a source copy is marked `\Deleted` only when the member is proved to have been *in the source mailbox at the moment the COPY ran* — either because `COPYUID` names it, or because a `UID FETCH` of the op's own source UIDs (no `SEARCH`, no Message-ID: D4-clean) still returns it after the COPY, which under RFC 3501 §2.3.1.1's "MUST NOT refer to any other message … forever" and strictly-ascending UIDs means it was present during the COPY and so was covered by the tagged OK. A source copy is `UID EXPUNGE`d only on `COPYUID`; the widened evidence never authorizes an irreversible deletion, so against a non-conforming server the worst case is a message left `\Deleted`-but-present, never one destroyed. A member the `UID FETCH` proves is no longer in the source is the provider stating there is nothing left to move — exit 2, zero mutation, retired — which is what shipped `v1.6.38`'s `idempotentMove` did with its `if srcUIDs.isEmpty { … return }` early return. Every member therefore reaches a permitted exit on every attempt, so no move op can wedge its lane for want of evidence. |

## Register provenance — what did NOT carry forward from the reference line

Recorded so a later reader does not conclude a row was lost. Superseded material is never deleted in
this repo; the reference register remains readable at `git show v2final:KNOWN_ISSUES.md`.

- **`IOS-DRAFT-001`, `IOS-DRAFT-002`, `IOS-DRAFT-005`, `IOS-DRAFT-008`, and the "F1 shipped
  boundary" section — EVAPORATED.** All four describe fail-closed edges of an RFC-keyed draft
  identity model that v3 does not have. **Salvaged rather than discarded:** `IOS-DRAFT-008`'s first
  clause was already D4-shaped and is now the IMAP leg's spec in ADR-IOS-068 §4; `IOS-DRAFT-005`'s
  principle (a narrow corruption fence may terminate fail-closed with zero provider I/O) survives as
  the checkpoint-A drop rule; `IOS-DRAFT-001`'s deferral text is the prior art quoted in
  ADR-IOS-068 §2 — ***"RFC cardinality is never mutation authority."***
- **`IOS-TEST-001` — NOT carried.** It pinned an automatic-retry timing tail in
  `mixedBatchResolvesWhollyBeforeFirstMutation`, and that test does not exist on v3
  (`rg mixedBatchResolvesWhollyBeforeFirstMutation` → zero hits). A row whose owning test is absent
  is not a deferral, it is noise.
- **`IOS-MOVE-001` — NOT carried.** It was never committed on the reference line; it lived only in
  that line's uncommitted working tree (preserved on `wip/uncommitted-main-20260730`). It reads as a
  product limitation about cross-account folder moves rather than an architecture artifact, and
  nobody has verified the v3 base's folder picker behaves the same way. If it is reinstated, the
  clause *"do not widen durable action identity"* must be dropped — it was specific to the abandoned
  line.
- **Numbering:** `IOS-IMAP-002` and `IOS-IMAP-004` are recorded under *Fixed by D4* above rather than
  in the deferral table. `IOS-ACTION-001`, `IOS-ACTION-002`, `IOS-IMAP-003`, `IOS-IMAP-005`,
  `IOS-TLS-001` and `IOS-TLS-002` are new in v3.

## Post-`e4dd08e92` — the latch that was read instead of acquired

Surfaced by two independent reviewers (different models) on the same candidate, reaching the same
defect separately. **The supervisor's own brief for the fix asserted the launch entry was safe; a
second reviewer refuted that, and the census below is the corrected one.** Registered because the
closure text of `IOS-OUTBOX-001` asserted, under a do-not-relax banner, exactly the invariant this
row shows does not hold — and a register that overstates its own closure is a recorded defect class
here, so that clause is corrected in place rather than deleted.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-OUTBOX-006 | Surfaced post-`e4dd08e92` by two independent reviewers; **the CLASS has TWO production entries with different attributions — the LAUNCH entry is PRE-EXISTING (verbatim in `v2final` `e28dd4edb` and shipped `07a4bb703`), the FOREGROUND entry is candidate-introduced by `792048ebd`**; **BLOCKING** — not registrable, because the end state is a delivered message the user was told was discarded, which nothing recovers; ✅ **FIXED** by the commit carrying this row | Outbox; `AccountManager.reconcileOutbox`; `AccountManager.performOutboxReconciliation`; `AccountManager.reconcileOutboxOnForeground`; `AccountManager.reconcilePendingOperations`; `isDrainingOutbox`; `atomicClaim`; `stampSentAt`; `discardOutboxMessageConfirmed`; `PrioritizedDatabase.read`; `NSEDataBridge.mergeIfStagingPending`; actor reentrancy; latch read before a suspension; Outbox Reliability Rules 3 and 10; `TabMail/Services/Account/AccountManagerOutbox.swift` | **A message whose SMTP transaction was already on the wire could be returned to `.queued`, become discardable, and be discarded — while still being delivered, and while never reaching Sent.** `reconcileOutbox` resets `.sending`/`sentAt == nil` rows to `.queued` because that is what a process killed mid-send leaves behind, and durable state cannot distinguish that residue from a row a LIVE drain has just claimed: `sentAt` is stamped only after `provider.send()` RETURNS, so an in-flight send is byte-identical to residue. `isDrainingOutbox` is the ONLY thing that separates them, which makes it the latch that AUTHORISES the reset — and it was **read** rather than **acquired**. `AccountManager` is an actor; `reconcileOutbox`'s first statement is `await dbPool.read`; `PrioritizedDatabase.read`'s async overload runs a full NSE staging merge before it reads (measured 7.6 s cold-boot, and staging is pending precisely on foreground return). The actor was therefore released for a long, reachable window, a drain starting in it claimed a row and put the SMTP on the wire, and reconciliation resumed with that row in a snapshot taken AFTER the claim and reset it. **The consequence, stated whole:** `discardOutboxMessageConfirmed` refuses only `outboxStatus == .sending` and `sentAt != nil`; a row reset to `.queued` satisfies neither, so the Discard gesture was accepted, the row and its attachment directory were deleted, `stampSentAt`'s `UPDATE … WHERE id = ?` matched zero rows and returned false, and `sendSingleOutboxMessage` returned at that guard — no optimistic Sent header, no Sent APPEND, no finalize. **The recipient receives a message the user was told was discarded, and the sender has no record of it.** Outbox Reliability Rule 3 and Rule 10 both forbid that. **THE CENSUS — the class has exactly TWO production entries and they differ in what protected them.** (1) **LAUNCH**: `AccountManagerQueue.reconcilePendingOperations` → `reconcileOutbox()`, awaited by `RootView`'s startup task, with **no ownership check at all**. Launch does not gate first paint, so a held row's deadline can expire, `drainOutbox` can claim it, and launch reconciliation is still running — this entry is PRE-EXISTING and is byte-identical in `v2final` and `v1.6.38`. (2) **FOREGROUND**: `reconcileOutboxOnForeground()`, wired to `RootView`'s `scenePhase → .active` by `792048ebd`; it checked the latch and then awaited. A fix at the foreground wrapper would have closed one entry of two. **The invariant that now holds:** the latch that authorises the `.sending → .queued` reset is ACQUIRED in the same synchronous run as its check and HELD across the reset. `reconcileOutbox` takes it, holds it across the extracted `performOutboxReconciliation()`, and releases it before its trailing `drainOutbox()`; `reconcileOutboxOnForeground` is now a pure delegate with no logic of its own, so both entries are governed by one piece of code. **A1 — authored, not restored, and the reason is checkable:** neither `v2final` nor `07a4bb703` has a foreground reconciliation trigger at all, so there is no shipped sequencing to restore. `v2final` DOES do its classification and reset in ONE write transaction, which is a better shape — but it does **not** close this: reconciliation and `atomicClaim` are already serialised by the single GRDB writer, so a one-transaction reconcile still cannot tell a row claimed a millisecond earlier from residue. Only the in-memory latch carries that. **ACCEPTED, fail-closed, both directions recoverable:** a drain trigger firing during reconciliation is skipped and subsumed by reconciliation's own trailing `drainOutbox()`; a reconciliation finding a drain in flight returns without reconciling and is re-driven by the next foreground return or launch — the in-session recoverability `IOS-OUTBOX-001` established — and the orphan-attachment sweep it also skips is pure byte reclamation, idempotent next pass. **No second latch was added**: `drainPendingQueue`'s `isDraining`/`needsRedrain` and `ComposeView.isSending` were audited, are CLEAN (each sets its flag in the same synchronous run as its check), and are untouched. **Verification — red-first, recorded:** `OutboxReconcileInFlightSendTests` pins the SYSTEM property (*a message whose SMTP is on the wire is never observable as `.queued` and can never be discarded*) using a new `MockEmailProvider.sendHook` in-flight seam. Pre-fix, the LAUNCH case failed four expectations: `(during?.outboxStatus → .queued) != .queued`, `(discardAccepted → true) == false`, `survives`, and `await provider.appendedToSent.count == 1`. ⚠️ **What could NOT be proven red, stated rather than hidden:** the FOREGROUND entry's own scheduling window. Reconciliation snapshots at its first await, which in a test completes in microseconds, so the drain never wins the race — the production window exists only because that read runs a seconds-long staging merge, and the harness has no honest way to reproduce that. The interleaving sweep is therefore a regression guard, and says so in its own doc comment; what carries the proof to the second entry is structural. **No schema change, no migration, no wire behaviour change.** |

## The captured-identity class — closed for rows that HAVE a content witness, deliberately open for rows that do not

The class: *identity is proven at one moment, the proof is DISCARDED, and a consumer re-resolves the
bare ADDRESS after a delay.* `IOS-SEARCH-001` above was one member of it. The remaining ~~four~~
**five** — `EmailArchiveTool`, `EmailDeleteTool`, the undo stack (`UndoService.UndoMember` →
`undoMove`), search → detail navigation (`SearchView.openResult` proved RFC identity and then
appended only the composite address, which `MessageDetailViewModel.markReadOnOpenIfNeeded`
re-resolved and marked read), and **the notification-action router
(`NotificationActionRouter.execute`'s ARCHIVE/DELETE arms in `AppDelegate.swift`)** — are closed by
carrying the witness the caller already holds through to the consumer, via one ported value type
(`ExpectedMessageIdentity`, `AccountManagerActions.swift`) that can only REFUSE a target, never
select or widen one. **Not an ADR-IOS-068/D4 violation** for the same reason `IOS-SEARCH-001`'s fix
is not: the mutation target is still selected by the durable provider address.

⚠️ **CENSUS CORRECTION 2026-08-04 — the fifth member was missing from this list, and its omission was
not a documentation gap but a live fail-open.** `performCoordinatedRoleMove`'s `expectedIdentities`
parameter carried a `= [:]` default, so the two notification-action call sites omitted it while the
two agent-tool call sites passed it — and because BOTH of that function's C3 refusals (`preRefusedIds`
at pre-resolve, `freshRefusedIds` inside the queued closure) are witness-gated, a notification tap ran
with both of them structurally inert. This is *not* the `IOS-IDENTITY-001` residual below: that row is
about rows with no usable RFC witness, whereas here the witness was constructible and simply never
passed. The count "four" was written by enumerating the members someone had already fixed, which is a
list sourced from attention rather than from the tree (`MIS-007`) — the tree-derived enumerator is the
PARAMETER (`rg 'expectedIdentities'`), which resolves to exactly one accepting function and, at the
time, four production call sites of which two passed it. **Fixed** by passing
`ExpectedMessageIdentity.map([header])` at both sites — zero extra I/O, since
`NotificationActionRouter` has already resolved that header — and by **deleting the `= [:]` default**
so no future call site can omit the witness without saying so at the call site. All 16 call sites
(4 production, 12 test) now pass it explicitly.

The row below is the residual those fixes deliberately do NOT close, registered because it is the
part a reader would otherwise assume was covered.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-IDENTITY-001 | 🔓 **OPEN** — inherent to the fail-open contract of the fix, not introduced by it; **C3-adjacent but accepted**, and pinned on both sides by tests so closing it later is deliberate rather than accidental | Identity; `ExpectedMessageIdentity`; content witness; `rfc822MessageId` absent; `MessageIdentity.comparableRfc822Identity`; fail open; UIDVALIDITY turnover; `EmailArchiveTool`; `EmailDeleteTool`; `UndoService.UndoMember.sourceRfc822MessageId`; `MessageDetailViewModel.markReadOnOpenIfNeeded`; `SearchView.OpenTarget` | **A row whose captured RFC 822 Message-ID is absent, empty, or unusable (a bare `<>`) is still authenticated by ADDRESS ALONE, exactly as before this class was closed.** `ExpectedMessageIdentity.init?` is failable and `.map` therefore omits such rows, so `partition` returns them unrefused — "no witness ⇒ no refusal" is structural, not a branch someone can forget. Consequence: for those rows a UIDVALIDITY turnover during the window (the unbounded AI confirmation wait, the life of an undo entry, the gap between the search-result proof and the detail view's own resolve) can still let an action land on a different physical message. **Why this is accepted rather than fixed:** rows with no usable RFC witness genuinely exist, and refusing every action on them would drop user intention wholesale — a large behaviour regression taken to close a rare edge, which is the opposite of the trade THE MANTRA asks for. Refusing the witnessed case costs nothing (the witness is read off headers the caller already holds — zero extra I/O) and refusing the witness-less case costs the user their archive, delete, undo or read-on-open every time. **Banned substitutes, stated so nobody re-derives one:** `date` alone (authored and REMOVED as unsound in `v2final`'s own `ExpectedMessageIdentity`, ADR-IOS-061 item E) and the `(fromAddress, subject, date)` triple (`94fac3e79` reverted `3bd9f0bac`). A witness that can be forged by an ordinary duplicate is worse than none, because it converts a known gap into a false proof. **Recoverability:** the mis-targeted action is a move to Trash/Archive or a read flag — no permanent delete is reachable from any of the four members — and sync plus one ordinary user gesture restores the state. **Pinned by:** `UndoDestructiveActionTests.undoWithoutAWitnessKeepsTodaysBehaviour` and `SearchLocalResultIdentityTests.witnessLessRowStillOpensAndStillMarksRead` (the gap is preserved on purpose — a "fix" that closes it by refusing everything fails both loudly), against `undoRefusesWhenTheRowAtTheAddressIsADifferentMessage`, `reseatedAddressIsRefusedAtPreResolve`, `reseatedAddressIsRefusedInsideTheQueuedClosure` and `reseatAfterTheProofMarksNothingRead` for the witnessed half. |
