| IOS-NOTIFY-001 | 🔓 **OPEN** — accepted; the SHORT interval of this gap is ✅ fixed (the witness is now passed), this row is the LONG one, which no witness available on the device can close | Notifications; `NotificationActionRouter.execute`; `AppDelegate.swift`; ARCHIVE/DELETE action buttons; push `userInfo`; `rfc822MessageId` absent from payload; UIDVALIDITY turnover; UID reuse; `clearDeliveredNotificationsForPurgedMessages`; C3 misattribution | **A notification's `messageId` is an ADDRESS, and the tap can happen days after delivery.** The push `userInfo` carries `messageId`/`provider`/`accountId`/`accountEmail` and **no** `rfc822MessageId`, so nothing in the notification records WHICH physical message it was about. Two intervals follow, and only one is closable on-device. **Interval 2 (tap → execution) is now CLOSED:** the router resolves a real `MessageHeader` before dispatching, so `ExpectedMessageIdentity.map([header])` is free, and both of `performCoordinatedRoleMove`'s refusals run strictly later than that capture — pass 1 after its own re-resolve, pass 2 after the FIFO write queue has taken an unbounded amount of other work, which is exactly the window a turnover can purge the row and re-seat a different message under the same composite id. **Interval 1 (push delivery → tap) stays OPEN:** by tap time the resolve already returns whatever now occupies the UID, so a witness captured then is captured FROM the impostor and cannot refuse it. **The epoch gate cannot substitute, and this is the whole reason the witness type exists:** checkpoint A (`AccountManagerQueue`) compares the op's stamped `observedUidValidity` against the folder's `lastKnownUidValidity`, but a post-turnover resync writes the impostor row with the NEW epoch and the folder carries the NEW epoch, so `stamped == live`, the guard AGREES and admits. `ExpectedMessageIdentity`'s own header states it outright — *"an epoch guard proves the row is addressable in the CURRENT epoch; it cannot prove the row is the message the producer pointed at, because after a turnover the impostor's epoch is fresh too."* **What bounds interval 1, with the caller named and its position established (`MIS-024`):** `AccountManager.clearDeliveredNotificationsForPurgedMessages` removes the delivered notifications for exactly the message ids the purge transaction captured. Its one production caller is `runUidValidityResetReaction`, which invokes it in step 4 — **after** the step-3 purge commits and **before** the step-5 fresh-epoch stamp, and therefore before any resync can seat an impostor at the vacated UID. It is a genuine ordering guarantee rather than an after-the-fact cleanup. **Negative case, stated because this is the half a reader would over-credit (`MIS-019`):** it clears only ids the purge CAPTURED, it is best-effort (`UNUserNotificationCenter.removeDeliveredNotifications` promises no completion), and it runs only when a turnover is actually DETECTED — though an undetected turnover also means nothing purged or re-inserted locally, so no impostor row exists under that id either. A notification whose message was never local is not in the captured set — the **staging-only** case, which is ordinary rather than exotic: the NSE stages and delivers in its own process and the main app merges later, and `uidValidityResetPurgeTxn` sources its id list solely from `MessageHeader.filter(Column("folderId") == folderId)` while `NSEDataBridge.purgeStagedStateForFolder` returns `Bool` only, so staged rows cannot feed `clearDeliveredNotificationsForPurgedMessages` at all. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-05 — the previous wording of this sentence said such a tap *"resolves `.absent` and takes the cold-op path, not this one"*, and BOTH halves are wrong.** (i) After the resync that follows the turnover, the tap does **not** resolve `.absent`: `resolveAfterMerge` returns `.found(Y)` for whatever now occupies the UID, and `NotificationActionRouter.execute`'s ARCHIVE/DELETE arms then build `ExpectedMessageIdentity.map([header])` **from Y itself**, so the witness agrees with the impostor and cannot refuse it — this tap lands squarely on interval 1, exactly as the row's *"a witness captured then is captured FROM the impostor"* sentence already says. (ii) The cold path is not a safe fallback either, so it bounds nothing: `queueColdPendingOperation` stamps `observedUidValidity: AccountManager.admissionEpochForNewGesture(…)` — the **NEW** epoch, read in the admitting transaction — onto a **bare UID**, which is precisely the shape an address-reuse can misresolve. It is the right stamp for the gesture it was written for (a genuinely cold push whose message has not synced yet) and is not a bound on this scenario. Neither correction changes the row's disposition: this is interval 1, still OPEN, still accepted, for the reason stated below. **Why the push payload is NOT being extended:** adding `rfc822MessageId` would mean changing the NSE and the push worker (a separate repo and deploy) to close a residual that a live, correctly-ordered mechanism already bounds — the widening THE MANTRA asks us not to do for a rare edge. **Attribution:** shared with the reference line in shape; `v2final:AccountManager.recordRoleMove` took `headers: [MessageHeader]` and derived the witness itself, so it had no omittable seam — v3's id-plus-optional-map signature is where the guard could be, and was, dropped (`MIS-018`, half-port drops the guard). |

## The coverage-vs-survivor-count class — closed at the two sync consumers, deliberately open at the v54 thread merge

The class: *a decision about EXHAUSTION, COMPLETENESS or "the server has no more" is taken from a
number produced AFTER a client-side narrowing.* Every `EmailProvider.fetchMessages` implementation
ends in one (`IMAPProvider.mapMessageInfo` and `ExchangeProvider.parseGraphMessage` return nil on an
unparseable date; `GmailProvider` drops the same nils in its task group — `ProviderBrokenDateTests`
pins all of it), so the returned array's `.count` means "returned AND parseable", never "returned".
The two sync members — `SyncEngine.selectStaleHeaders` / `runSyncMessages`' complete-knowledge branch
and `SyncEngine.fetchOlderMessages`' `mayHaveMore` — are closed by carrying honest server-reported
coverage out of the fetch site (`FetchCoverage`, `EmailProvider.swift`), which is where
`Mailbox.Selection.messageCount`, the raw FETCH record count, Gmail's `nextPageToken` and Graph's
`@odata.nextLink` already existed and were being discarded.

The two rows below are what those fixes deliberately do NOT close.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-SCROLL-001 | 🔓 **OPEN** — accepted, recoverable without a user gesture; the coverage half of the same decision is ✅ fixed, this is the PROGRESS half, which is a termination guard and not a coverage claim | Infinite scroll; `SyncEngine.fetchOlderMessages`; `mayHaveMore`; `FetchCoverage.serverRecordCount`; full page, zero inserts; `\Deleted` page; no UIDPLUS; soft-deleted source copy; `runBackfill` UID walk; `backfillComplete` | Continuing to page requires BOTH *coverage* (`found >= SyncConfig.infiniteScrollFetchLimit`, now the SERVER's own record count) and *progress* (this folder materialised at least one new row). **A full page in which EVERY record is skipped therefore stops paging.** The reachable instance is a page whose every record the server reports `\Deleted` — routine on a server without UIDPLUS, where each completed move leaves a soft-deleted source copy behind that `IOS-IMAP-001`/D3 deliberately refuses to materialise. **Why the progress half is not simply dropped:** this pull's cursor IS the folder's oldest local row (`oldestDate`), so a round that inserts nothing would re-ask the identical window forever. Removing the guard trades a bounded gap for an unbounded spin. **Why the fix is not a persisted page cursor:** that is a new column and a new migration to close an edge that already recovers. **Recoverability, with the non-recovering case named:** `SyncEngine.runBackfill`'s UID-range walk advances its cursor on COVERAGE (`UIDWalkCursor.confirmRange` fires when a range is ACCOUNTED FOR, not when it inserts), so the mail beyond the stalled page still arrives locally and the next reset re-arms the scroller; it is invoked by `SyncEngine.startBackfill` after every sync and by `SyncScheduler`'s BGProcessing pass. It does NOT recover a folder wrongly marked `backfillComplete` by the `.fresh` branch when the server reports no UIDNEXT — ~~that case is registered separately and is not reachable by ordinary sync~~ ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-04: it was NOT registered separately. No such row existed** — the id space held no `IOS-BACKFILL-001` and nothing else described the case, so this sentence discharged a named non-recovery by pointing at a register row that had never been written (`MIS-024`: a "covered by X" is a claim about X's existence and reachability, and both must be checked in the same sentence). The case is real and is **now** `IOS-BACKFILL-001` below, where it is ✅ **FIXED**; the "not reachable by ordinary sync" half was true and is preserved there (it needs a server that omits a REQUIRED response code). |
| IOS-BACKFILL-001 | ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04)** — the case `IOS-SCROLL-001` named as "registered separately" when no such row existed; fourth member of the same class, one layer further up | Backfill; `SyncEngineBackfillWalk`; `WalkStart.fresh`; `IMAPProvider.getUidNextWithEpoch`; `Mailbox.Selection.uidNext`; `UID(0)`; `initialCursor < 1`; `backfillComplete`; UIDNEXT absent; nonconforming server; `MIS-IOS-004`; `epochDeclinedFolderIds` | **A SELECT that never reported UIDNEXT permanently marked the folder fully crawled.** SwiftMail's `Mailbox.Selection.uidNext` is non-optional with a `UID(0)` default (`SwiftMail/IMAP/Models/Mailbox.swift`) and `SelectHandler` assigns it only when the wire carried `* OK [UIDNEXT n]`, so "the server did not say" arrived at `SyncEngineBackfillWalk`'s `.fresh` branch as the **number 0**. `initialCursor = uidNext - 1` was then `-1`, which took the `initialCursor < 1` early-out — a branch written for UIDNEXT **1**, the one value that PROVES the mailbox never held a message (RFC 3501 §2.3.1.1 assigns UIDs strictly increasing from 1) — and wrote `backfillComplete = true`. Completion removes the folder from `remaining` on every later pass, so **nothing ever revisited it and the whole mailbox stayed outside automatic backfill**. This is `MIS-IOS-004`'s class — *"could not determine"* treated as an authoritative answer — and the **tell was already in the file**: the epoch beside it WAS normalised for the zero case (`observed != 0 ? observed : nil`) and the UIDNEXT was not. **The fix:** `getUidNextWithEpoch` returns `uidNext: Int?`, normalising 0 → nil at the same site and for the same reason as the epoch, so absence of evidence is unrepresentable as a number at the decision point; the `.fresh` construction declines the folder (`epochDeclinedFolderIds`, `continue`) instead of computing a cursor from it. The row is left EXACTLY as the pass found it — `backfillComplete` still false, no cursor, no stamp — so the next call meets the same preconditions and retries. **The `initialCursor < 1` completion path is unchanged and still fires for UIDNEXT 1**, together with its NB3 epoch bootstrap in the same transaction, so a genuinely empty folder still settles and is still gestureable; both directions are pinned (`aFolderWithNoReportedUidNextIsNeverMarkedFullyCrawled`, `aGenuinelyEmptyMailboxStillSettles`). **Accepted residual, with its bound:** a server that omits UIDNEXT on *every* SELECT is now never deep-crawled at all — one SELECT per `runBackfill` call, declined, forever. That is a deliberate trade of a bounded, self-correcting refusal for a silent permanent mis-completion, and it is a nonconforming server (RFC 3501 §6.3.1 lists UIDNEXT among SELECT's REQUIRED OK untagged responses), which is why the case is not reachable by ordinary sync. **Recoverability, with the caller named:** the folder stays in `remaining`, so the first SELECT that does report a UIDNEXT crawls it with no user gesture — `runBackfill` is invoked by `SyncEngine.startBackfill` after every sync and by `SyncScheduler`'s BGProcessing pass. Meanwhile its recent window still arrives on navigation (`InboxView.onAppear` → `InboxViewModel.startSync()` → `AccountManager.syncFolders(_:)` → `SyncEngine.syncFolderMessages` → `runSyncMessages`), the same path `IOS-EPOCH-001`'s round-8 correction relies on. |
| IOS-SCROLL-002 | 🔓 **OPEN** — accepted, fails CLOSED and self-heals with no user gesture; the PERSISTENT member of this class is ✅ fixed, these are the transient ones | Infinite scroll; `InboxViewModel.hasMoreMessages`; `resetMessages`; `reloadMessages`; `loadMoreMessages`; `InboxListComposer.compose`; overlay folder-drop; `excludeIds`; triage mode; survivor count | `InboxViewModel` writes `hasMoreMessages` at **three** sites and all three still compare `loadedMessages.count` — the COMPOSED row count — against a page size (`resetMessages` and `reloadMessages`' `applyDiff` against `targetWindowSize`; `loadMoreMessages` phase 1 against `SyncConfig.inboxPageSize`). The fourth site, `loadMoreMessages` phase 2's `hasMoreMessages = pull.mayHaveMore`, is already server-coverage-sourced and is the shape the other three are measured against. **What was fixed:** the label filter, the only PERSISTENT narrowing `InboxListComposer.compose` applied after `InboxListReader.gather`'s per-folder SQL `LIMIT`, now runs INSIDE that query, so the `LIMIT` bounds MATCHING rows and both the count and the pagination cursor (`loadedMessages.last?.date`) become honest. **What remains, enumerated rather than summarised:** compose still narrows D after the `LIMIT` via (1) the overlay folder-drop in `applyOverlay` — a swiped-away row is dropped in memory while its durable row is still in the folder; (2) `excludeIds` (step 2.5), which drops already-loaded rows AFTER the SQL `LIMIT` chose them, reachable in **triage mode only**, where the sort is not date-monotonic so an already-loaded row can legitimately re-enter a later page's window; (3) the staged-row `filterUnread` re-filter and the P/S `beforeDate` cut, which touch only P and S; and (4) the `isSystem`/`shouldExcludeLabel` corner of compose's step-6 belt, which no filter reachable from `LabelFilterPickerView` can produce. **Why registered rather than fixed:** every one of them can only make the count SMALLER, so they fail closed — the scroller stops early, no mail is deleted, mis-addressed or dropped — and every one is transient. **Recoverability, with the caller named:** `reloadMessages` recomputes `hasMoreMessages` from a fresh D query, so the moment the overlay drains (or the user's triage window moves) the flag is re-armed with no user gesture; it is invoked by `InboxViewModel.runReloadCoalesced` from the `.inboxDataDidChange` observer registered in `init`, and by `listDidAppear`. **Why the fix is not a coverage signal plumbed out of the reader:** it would have to fail OPEN to be useful, and `InboxView`'s infinite-scroll sentinel is `if viewModel.hasMoreMessages { Color.clear.onAppear { viewModel.loadMoreMessages() } }` — a `hasMoreMessages` that stays true while the composed page is EMPTY re-arms that sentinel on every render while the cursor `loadedMessages.last?.date` cannot advance, which is an unattended network pull of the whole mailbox. That is the mirror image of the bug (`MIS-005`), so the ordering was fixed instead of the signal compensated (`CLAUDE.md` A3). **If it is ever revisited, fix the ordering again, not the comparison** — move the remaining narrowings ahead of the `LIMIT` rather than teaching the three sites to read a coverage flag. |
| IOS-THREAD-001 | 🔓 **OPEN** — same class, third member; **registered rather than fixed because the only production caller is an IMMUTABLE applied migration** | Threading; `ThreadUtils.runFragmentMergeToFixpoint`; `runFragmentMergeOnce`; `findAdoptableThreadId`; `LIMIT 1`; fixpoint; `merged == 0`; migration `v54`; Gmail thread fragments; split inbox thread | `runFragmentMergeToFixpoint` declares convergence the first time a pass reports `merged == 0`. That reads as candidate coverage and is not: the per-row probe `findAdoptableThreadId` returns the FIRST connected `computedThreadId` it finds (`LIMIT 1`, across three prioritised queries with no `ORDER BY`), so when a row is connected to two fragments and the probe happens to name the one it ALREADY carries, the row does not adopt — and because the probe is deterministic on unchanged state, every later pass returns the same answer. Convergence is declared with cross-fragment candidates still unmerged, and the user sees an inbox thread that stays split. **Why this is not fixed here:** the only production caller is `AppDatabase`'s `v54` migration, and **a registered migration is immutable the moment any database has run it** (global `CLAUDE.md` Data Integrity rule 5 / `MIS-IOS-001`) — including every dev simulator. A real fix therefore needs a NEW migration (v83 is the next free number) plus a re-walk of historic rows, which is disproportionate to a cosmetic threading defect in already-migrated data. **Blast radius:** display-only. `computedThreadId` groups rows for the thread list; no message is deleted, mis-addressed or dropped, and no wire operation depends on it. **Recoverability:** newly-arrived mail is threaded by `ThreadUtils.assignComputedThreadId` at insert time and is unaffected; only rows already in the DB at the v54 upgrade can carry the split. **If it is fixed later, fix the probe, not the loop** — the loop's termination is sound once the probe covers all candidates (return the full connected set and adopt a deterministic minimum, rather than the first row SQLite happens to hand back). |

## Codex-half swarm adjudication (`e4dd08e92` review, judged against HEAD `6d460aa99`, 2026-08-04)

The Claude half of the 24-dissection swarm was triaged into the sections above. The Codex half was
graded materially harder and its findings were unjudged; they are adjudicated here **against HEAD,
not against the review sha**, because five commits landed after it (`da57fde6f`, `3a4276837`,
`3c90f1bef`, `b1ae6e262`, `6d460aa99`). Two Codex findings were **REFUTED** as live findings — the
`v74` purge (already `IOS-ACTION-001`, owner-approved and immutable) and the canonical-PK ownership
hole (already `IOS-ROUND3-D6`'s sync-merge remainder, closed as a decision the same day, whose
accepted-cost clause is widened in place above). **ONE finding is BLOCKING — `IOS-NSE-005`.** The
other six rows are the registrable remainder; each names the guard or the recovery that keeps it out
of the non-recoverable set, and what would move it in.

⚠ **`IOS-NSE-005` was first graded REGISTRABLE in this same section and the verdict was changed the
same day; the row carries the retracted basis verbatim.** It was registered on four named defenses.
The coordinator read `mergeNSEStagingData`'s statement order independently and established that the
fourth — "the re-headed row ages out on its predecessor's clock" — rests on `abandonedCutoff`, which
is a **deletion** predicate applied after the merge commits, not an **admission** predicate on the
step-1 read. The other three were then re-tested against HEAD and none of them closes the path. Two
process points a later reader should take from this rather than from the row itself: **an
adjudication that says "registrable BECAUSE mechanism M recovers it" is only as good as M's position
in the statement order** — `MIS-024` is now at five recurrences (this is instance 5) and its check
has been extended: a live caller is necessary but not sufficient, and the temporal hand-offs (*ages
out*, *gets reaped*, *expires*, *is reconciled later*) always require establishing whether the cited
mechanism runs BEFORE the write it is claimed to prevent; and **the first fix that comes to
mind for this one (an age predicate on the merge's step-1 read) converts the misattribution into a
dropped message**, so the row's brief names that trap explicitly.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Deferred policy |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-QUEUE-006 | 🔓 **OPEN** — recoverable by one ordinary user gesture; ⚠️ **this row also RETRACTS `IOS-QUEUE-003` item 2's "ALREADY-CLOSED — it has an owner at the tip"**, which was false | Action queue; `RoleMoveDisposition.retainedForRetry`; `RoleMoveAdmission.pendingIds`; `performCoordinatedRoleMove`; `roleMoveRejectDispositions`; `EmailArchiveTool`; `EmailDeleteTool`; `NotificationActionRouter.execute`; receipt without an owner | **`.retainedForRetry` is a REPORT, not a retry. No production consumer stores, schedules or redrives it.** Census at HEAD, restated so it reproduces (`MIS-007`/`MIS-008`): `rg -n 'pendingIds' TabMail/ Shared/ TabMailNotificationService/` returns **17 lines in 4 files**, and the number that matters is **FOUR reads of the PROPERTY** — `AppDelegate.swift` twice (`NotificationActionRouter.execute`'s ARCHIVE and DELETE arms, `archiveOutcome.pendingIds.contains(header.id)` / `deleteOutcome.pendingIds.contains(header.id)`, each selecting an `else if` arm whose entire body is a `log(...)` string) and `admission.pendingIds` once each in `EmailArchiveTool` / `EmailDeleteTool`, bound to `pendingHeaderIds`. ⚠️ **An earlier wording of this row said "exactly five production reads", which does not reproduce:** the fifth counted hit is the DECLARATION itself (`AccountManagerActions.swift`, `var pendingIds: Set<String> { ids(with: .retainedForRetry) }`), and the remaining twelve are a **same-named LOCAL variable shadowing it** inside the two tools (`let pendingIds = zip(resolvedNumericIds, resolved)…` — the numeric ids that get serialised into `result["pending_ids"]` alongside the message *"…are still outstanding — their outcome could not be confirmed. Do not repeat the action."*). A bare-symbol grep cannot separate a property read from a shadowing local; enumerate by the STATE "reads `RoleMoveAdmission.pendingIds`", not by the name. **The substantive claim is unaffected: none of the four reads stores, schedules or redrives the work.** So when `performCoordinatedRoleMove` classifies an id `.retainedForRetry` — a swallowed or thrown `resolveHeadersForAction`, a thrown absence probe, a thrown role-folder lookup, an account with no folder for the role, or an id dropped by the fresh re-resolve inside the queued closure — **no `PendingOperation` row is inserted and nothing will ever try again**, while the agent is instructed not to repeat. <br><br>⚠️ **`IOS-QUEUE-003` item 2 said this was already closed, and its basis does not survive reading.** It reads: *"It has an owner at the tip: `AccountManagerActions.RoleMoveDisposition.retainedForRetry` is produced by `roleMoveRejectDispositions` at five guards and consumed by `pendingIds`."* **Being consumed by `pendingIds` is not having a retry owner** — `pendingIds` is a computed filter (`ids(with: .retainedForRetry)`) whose only consumers print or serialise it. That is `MIS-024`'s exact shape ("covered by X" discharged without greping what X does), and it is recorded there as instance 2. **Item 2's disposition is corrected to OPEN and superseded by this row.** <br><br>**Why this is REGISTRABLE and not BLOCKING.** The local mutation and the durable op are the *same* transaction — that is what `durablyAdmitted` asserts — so a non-admitted id gets **no local mutation either**, and every optimistic overlay entry is released on both exits (`releaseOverlayEntry` runs for `droppedByFreshResolve` and for `freshIds`). The message therefore stays visible where the user left it. Nothing is destroyed, nothing is mis-addressed, and no queued row is dropped (none was created). Recovery is **one ordinary user gesture** — the user swipes again — which is exactly the mantra's test. **The state where that does not fire is named:** the AI-tool and notification-action paths, where the *reporter* is not the user, so nobody is prompted to re-issue. The user still sees the unarchived message in their Inbox; they simply are not told the agent failed. **If this is fixed later, fix the report or add an owner — do not make `retainedForRetry` terminal.** Promoting "we could not determine" to a terminal disposition is `MIS-IOS-004`, this codebase's most repeated defect, and the rank lattice in `RoleMoveAdmission` exists specifically to make that promotion impossible. |
| IOS-QUEUE-007 | 🔓 **OPEN** — recoverable; pre-existing, byte-identical in shipped `v1.6.38`; the abandoned-Task contract is documented at the helper itself | Action queue; `withTimeout`; `AsyncTimeout.swift`; abandoned Task; `AccountManager.executeSingleOp`; `SyncConfig.pendingOperationTimeoutSeconds`; `IMAPProvider.mutateAdmittedUIDs`; `UID STORE`; ordering fence; duplicate COPY; `sendSingleOutboxMessage` | **A provider mutation that exceeds its deadline is abandoned, not joined, and the queue immediately releases ownership of it.** `withTimeout`'s own doc says so: *"The cancelled operation task is abandoned — it will clean up when it eventually finishes or when the process exits."* `executeSingleOp` wraps every `executeOperation` in it, receives `TimeoutError`, and requeues the row. `IMAPProvider.mutateAdmittedUIDs` contains **no** cancellation checkpoint around `server.store(...)` — it is `withActionConnectionSelection { requireUidValidity; selectMailboxTracked; requireUidValidity; store }` — and SwiftMail's NIO continuations are the non-cooperative case `withTimeout` was written for. **Two reachable outcomes.** (1) *Ordering:* a timed-out `UID STORE +\Seen` on M can land AFTER the retry and after the user's newer `markUnread(M)`, leaving the message read against the newer intention. (2) *Duplication:* a `move` whose `UID COPY` completed just before the deadline is retried, producing a second destination copy. **Why REGISTRABLE.** Both land on the **correct** message — the abandoned task carries the UID and epoch it already admitted, and the source expunge stays `COPYUID`-gated — so C3 is not reached; no queued intention is dropped (the op is requeued, and its retry is idempotent); and the wrong end state is visible and correctable by one ordinary gesture, with the duplicate removable by an ordinary move-to-Trash. **The mechanism the reference used and this tree does NOT have, named so nobody assumes it:** searches for `onAbandon`, `zombieActive` and zombie registration return **zero** production implementations here; the v2final line added synchronous zombie registration, an account-wide fence and a reaper for exactly this race. **Do not port that as a first move** — it is a large compensating mechanism (A3) for a rare edge, and the cheaper direction is a cancellation checkpoint immediately before the wire command plus an account-wide in-flight fence keyed on `(account, folder, uid)`. ⚠️ **Interacts with `IOS-QUEUE-003` item 4:** any fix that keeps a timed-out op claimed must not turn a permanently slow server into a permanent lane wedge. |
| IOS-QUEUE-008 | 🔓 **OPEN** — candidate-introduced by `635cb78b1` (the `IOS-QUEUE-001` fix); recoverable; **the lane key must NOT be reverted** | Action queue; `AccountManager.buildLanes`; lane key `accountId:folderPath:messageId`; provider-stable ids; Gmail; Exchange/Graph; concurrent lanes; ordering; `optimisticMoveToFolder`; `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` | **Folder-qualifying the lane key is right for IMAP and splits same-resource ordering for the stable-id providers.** `buildLanes` unions on `"\(op.accountId):\(op.folderPath):\(id)"`. On IMAP that IS the address (UID is mailbox-local) and the change closed a never-drop wedge-with-bystander. On Gmail and Graph the id is **folder-independent**: `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets`' non-IMAP arm returns `admitted.map(\.messageId)` — the provider resource id — with a `nil` epoch, and `optimisticMoveToFolder` writes the header's `folderPath` to the destination in the same transaction that inserts the op. So a second move on the same message enqueues with `folderPath = B` while the first still carries `folderPath = A`, the two land in **different connected components**, and `drainPendingQueue` launches **one Task per lane concurrently**. Offline A→B then B→C can reach Gmail as B→C first: final labels `{B, C}` — the message is in two places and both ops retire as provider successes, so sync imports the wrong state rather than repairing it. **Exchange sibling:** `stripLegacyCategories` and `setUserLabel` each GET-then-replace the whole Graph category array, so a concurrent pair is a lost update. **Why the IMAP guard does not also cover this:** the second gesture on a just-moved IMAP row is refused by `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` (the optimistic move set `observedUidValidity = nil`), so IMAP cannot produce the two-row precondition at all — the non-IMAP arm has no such gate. **Why REGISTRABLE.** Every op acts on the correct provider resource (no C3), nothing is deleted (Gmail move is a label change; Graph ids churn on move — see the severity note at the end of this cell, which CORRECTS "stale no-op"), no queued row is dropped, and the wrong end state is visible and fixed by one ordinary gesture. **If it is fixed later, fix the KEY's provenance, not its shape:** the lane key should be the op's ADDRESS SPACE — folder-qualified where the provider's ids are folder-local, account-qualified where they are stable — decided from `Account.provider` the same way `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets` and `stickyDurableMember` already decide it. 🚨 **Reverting to `accountId:messageId` re-opens `IOS-QUEUE-001`, which was a never-drop violation with a bystander.** 📌 **SEVERITY NOTE (2026-08-04) — Exchange/Graph id churn, settled from the code; it does NOT change this row's classification, and it surfaces an ADJACENT question this adjudication did not have the remit to judge.** ⚠️ **PARTLY STALE — read the dated closure at the END of this note first.** Three of this note's statements were true of the tree on 2026-08-04 and are FALSE at HEAD. They are **tense-wrapped in place rather than deleted**, because this note is the provenance for why `IOS-GRAPH-002` was opened at all, and superseded material in this repo stays routed and searchable. ⏳ **AS OF 2026-08-04 the repo-side facts were:** *"`ExchangeProvider.moveMessage` issues `POST /messages/{id}/move` and **discards the response** (`let _ = try await request(...)`) … and it never learns the new one."* ⏳ **END OF SUPERSEDED TEXT (limb 1).** **The other half of that same sentence is STILL TRUE AT HEAD and must survive this correction:** no `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` header appears anywhere in `ExchangeProvider` — the only mention of it is the comment at `ExchangeProvider.swift:587` documenting its absence — so the app uses Graph's default (mutable) ids, which Microsoft documents as changing when a message moves. This is **THE ADDRESS PROBLEM in the Graph address space** (`MIS-IOS-003` — "reconstructed an address the wire already gave us" — applies verbatim to Graph, not only to IMAP), structurally identical to the `COPYUID` the IMAP path also validates and throws away. **What that means for the second gesture, and why "404s into a stale no-op" was wrong:** the local row keeps the OLD Graph id (nothing re-keys it), so the second op reaches the wire with a dead id and takes `AccountManagerQueue`'s message-not-found arm — which **deletes the `PendingOperation` row** ("Conflict: message not found, dropping") and, ⏳ **AS OF 2026-08-04 (limb 2, SUPERSEDED — see `IOS-GRAPH-003`, which REFUTES exactly this):** *"because a 404 satisfies `isConfirmedGoneError`, also runs `deleteConfirmedGoneHeader` on `(accountId, folderPath, oldId)`."* ⏳ **END OF SUPERSEDED TEXT (limb 2).** `IOS-GRAPH-003`'s status cell says so in its own words — `deleteConfirmedGoneHeader` fetches by **primary key**, and the post-move second-gesture path does not satisfy the folder segment baked into that key, so the header deletion reaches only the two paths that row enumerates and the second-gesture path is not one of them. ⏳ **AS OF 2026-08-04 (limb 3, SUPERSEDED as to the header half — the gesture half was real and is what became `IOS-GRAPH-002`):** *"So the outcome is not a no-op: the queued gesture is **discarded** and the header the user is looking at **disappears** until the next sync of the destination folder re-fetches the message under its new id."* ⏳ **END OF SUPERSEDED TEXT (limb 3).** The row's own recoverability claim still holds for the MESSAGE (sync restores it) and the ordering framing is unaffected — but the discarded gesture is a **never-drop question in its own right**: a 404 on an address the provider itself invalidated is evidence about the ADDRESS, not authority that the user's work is done (`MIS-IOS-004`). ⏳ **AS OF 2026-08-04 (limb 4, SUPERSEDED — the referral points at work that is now DONE):** *"⚠ **Not adjudicated here.** It is pre-existing rather than candidate-introduced, it is not what D11(b) reported, and judging it needs the Exchange move/queue interaction traced end to end. Whoever picks it up should start from `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage` + `AccountManagerQueue`'s `isConfirmedGoneError` arm, and should note that Graph exposes an immutable-id opt-in that would make the churn moot."* ⏳ **END OF SUPERSEDED TEXT (limb 4).** <br><br>📌 **CLOSURE OF THE SEVERITY NOTE (2026-08-05) — what is true at HEAD, limb by limb.** **(1) The discard is gone.** `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage` decodes Graph's `/move` response and **returns the destination `id`** (`GraphMessageRef`), landed in `cfe6720be` and carried through `moveProvingDestinations` → `ExecutedOperation.provenDestinations` → `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`. The adjacent never-drop question this note raised became **`IOS-GRAPH-002`, ✅ FIXED**. **(2) The header-deletion limb is REFUTED, not merely superseded** — by `IOS-GRAPH-003`, which is `🔓 OPEN by decision` and carries the primary-key argument in full. **(3) The forward referral is spent:** the work it hands off was picked up and adjudicated as `IOS-GRAPH-002` (FIXED) plus `IOS-GRAPH-003` (OPEN by decision); nothing here is still owed. **(4) `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` was NOT adopted** and is recorded in `IOS-GRAPH-002` as a follow-up ON TOP of the re-key, never instead of it — so the "immutable-id opt-in would make the churn moot" aside stays as a lead, not as a recommendation. 🚨 **THIS ROW'S OWN CLASSIFICATION IS UNCHANGED BY ALL OF THE ABOVE.** The lane-split finding, its registrable-and-recoverable verdict, and the do-not-revert-the-lane-key warning stand exactly as written — the note said from the start that it does not change this row's classification, and that remains true. |
| IOS-IMAP-008 | 🔓 **OPEN** — recoverable when the folder is fully covered by one fetch; the cc/bcc half is a deliberate decision already recorded in the routed topic | Sync; `\Deleted`; `isDeletedOnServer`; `SyncEngine.insertBackfillBatchGuardable`; `SyncEngine.selectStaleHeaders`; merge floor; `floorUID`; `ccBccBackfillDone`; one-shot latch; `Folder.backfillComplete`; insert-only filter | **The `\Deleted` filters are insert-prevention only, and an EXISTING row below the merge floor has no eviction path.** `selectStaleHeaders` removes a local row only when it is in `candidates` — and in `.uid` mode without `coverage.spansEntireFolder` that requires `uid >= floorUID`, the minimum UID the fetch materialised. A `\Deleted` row whose UID sits below that floor is never a candidate. The crawl cannot evict it either: in `insertBackfillBatchGuardable` the `if info.isDeletedOnServer { continue }` sits **above** `if existingIds.contains(info.messageId)`, so the row is skipped before the existing-row branch is reached. Deletion reconcile does not help — `UID SEARCH` finds the UID (it is still on the server) and `selectGhostUIDs` correctly refuses to call it absent, and IMAP `STATUS` still counts `\Deleted` messages so the count-mismatch trigger need not fire. The row therefore stays visible for as long as the server keeps the flag without expunging. **Two one-shot states are consumed on the way past:** the v10 cc/bcc update is skipped for that row while `ccBccBackfillDone` is still latched by the completing crawl, and `UIDWalkCursor.confirmRange` confirms the range regardless, so `Folder.backfillComplete` is set with that UID never materialised. **The cc/bcc half is already decided** — `Companion/Memory/Current/101-isdeletedonserver-has-four-materialisation-paths.md` records it as *"correct as is; moving it below would spend a write on a row the merge is about to delete"*. **This row adds the part that decision did not cover: the merge does NOT always reach it.** **Why REGISTRABLE.** Display-only. No message is deleted, mis-addressed or dropped; no wire operation depends on it; the row's content is the user's own real (soft-deleted) mail. **Recoverability, with the non-firing case named:** any sync pass where the server reports `coverage.spansEntireFolder` takes the floor-free branch and removes it — the ordinary case for a folder smaller than the fetch limit — and a server-side expunge removes it via `VANISHED` / count-mismatch reconcile. The case that does **not** self-heal is a large folder on a non-UIDPLUS server, where soft-deleted move sources accumulate below the floor indefinitely. **Do not close this by filtering `\Deleted` at the FETCH site**: the fetch's cardinality and UID floor measure COVERAGE and must keep counting `\Deleted` records, or the `ADR-IOS-042` / `MIS-IOS-002` mass-deletion shape returns. |
| IOS-NSE-004 | 🔓 **OPEN** — recoverable by the next top-window sync; pre-existing; the flag has never existed in the NSE subtree | NSE; `\Deleted`; `NSEIMAPConnection.performFetch`; `NSEIMAPConnection.mapInfoToMetadata`; `NSEMessageMetadata`; `StagedInboxRow.toMessageHeader`; `NSEDataBridge.insertNewHeaderFromStaging`; parallel materialiser | **The notification extension is a materialisation path the `isDeletedOnServer` census structurally could not see, and it drops the flag.** `rg -i deleted TabMailNotificationService/NSEIMAPConnection.swift TabMailNotificationService/NSEMessageMetadata.swift` returns **zero** matches, and `isDeletedOnServer` has zero occurrences anywhere under `TabMailNotificationService/`, in `StagedInboxRow` or in `NSEDataBridge`. `performFetch` receives a `MessageInfo` that carries `.deleted`; `mapInfoToMetadata` does not preserve it; the row is published to the ADR-IOS-049 in-memory read model before its durable write and can then be inserted by `insertNewHeaderFromStaging` as an ordinary Inbox header. The retracted basis is kept verbatim so a later reader can see what was believed and why it was wrong:** *"Bounded, and the bound is a verified fact rather than an estimate: the same merge pass reaps `populated = 1, aiCompleted = 0` rows older than `staleStagingWindowSeconds` (60 s), and `processedAt` is not on `stageHeader`'s `ON CONFLICT` SET list, so a re-headed row keeps its PREDECESSOR's staging instant and ages out on the original clock. A turnover, a push at the recycled UID and a merge must therefore all fall inside one 60-second window."* **What is actually true:** `mergeNSEStagingData`'s step-1 read is `SELECT * FROM nse_processed_message WHERE populated = 1` with **no `processedAt` predicate at all**; `abandonedCutoff` (`now − staleStagingWindowSeconds`) appears only at the **post-commit deletion** decision (`if msg.aiCompleted || msg.processedAt < abandonedCutoff { localMergedIds.append(msg.id) }`, and the analogous `.filter` before its `DELETE`), and the separate orphan reap targets `populated = 0` only. **`abandonedCutoff` is a DELETION predicate, not an ADMISSION predicate** — the row is merged into durable GRDB *first* and reaped *after*. It guarantees the row is not merged **twice**; it does not stop it being merged **once**. (`MIS-024` instance 3: a defense cited from a mechanism that is real and correct but whose call site is **downstream of the damage**.) The clause was doubly void: `aiCompleted` is not on the `ON CONFLICT` SET list either, so a row that reached terminal in run 1 and is then re-headed carries `aiCompleted = 1` and is deleted on **that** arm irrespective of age. **The three surviving defenses were then re-tested against HEAD and none of them closes the path.** (i) `isOldEpoch` needs BOTH epochs present — `guard let observed = msg.observedUidValidity, let stored = entry.lastKnownUidValidity, observed != stored` — so it is inert, not protective, on a nil-epoch folder. (ii) The reaction's step-4 `NSEDataBridge.purgeStagedStateForFolder` genuinely does precede the step-5 stamp and genuinely does abort the reaction on failure — but the reaction only runs once a turnover has been **detected**, and on a nil-epoch folder the sole detector is `verifyAndBootstrapPrePopulatedFolderEpoch`, whose `.unobservable` legs (no header carries an rfc822; the SELECT reports no UIDVALIDITY; the sample read or sample FETCH throws) leave the epoch nil — permanently, for the first two. (iii) INBOX-only staging kills the cross-folder UID collision and nothing else; it says nothing about a turnover recycling a UID inside INBOX. **THE PATH, END TO END (every step verified against HEAD, not inferred):** ① NSE run 1 completes for message X at INBOX UID 42 under epoch E1 — `persistProcessedMessage` (INSERT OR REPLACE) leaves `aiCompleted = 1`, X's body, X's summary/todos/reminder, X's `actionTag`. ② The app is backgrounded, so no merge consumes the row. ③ The server's UIDVALIDITY turns over E1→E2 and reissues UID 42 to a different message Y. ④ NSE run 2 for Y: `NotificationService.swift:549` calls `stageHeader`, whose `ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET` list overwrites `rfc822MessageId`, `observedUidValidity`, `subject`, `senderName`, `date`, … and **retains** `htmlContent`/`textContent`/`summaryBlurb`/`summaryTodos`/`actionTag`/`aiCompleted`/`processedAt`. ⑤ **`NotificationService.swift:652` — the aggravator, and it makes step ⑥'s precondition deterministic rather than accidental:** `NSEStagingDB.getCachedResult(db:accountId:messageId:)` selects `WHERE id = ? AND aiCompleted = 1` with **no RFC and no epoch term**, hits X's retained payload, logs *"STAGING cache HIT — skipping AI calls"*, fills the notification for Y with **X's `summaryBlurb`, `actionTag`, `reminderContent`, `reminderDate`, `reminderTime`**, delivers, and returns — so run 2 never reaches its own terminal `persistProcessedMessage` and the spliced row is left exactly as step ④ made it. The user is shown X's AI summary under Y's sender and subject **before any merge runs at all**, and this stage needs no nil epoch. ⑥ Next foreground: the merge. ARM 1 is inert (nil folder epoch), the folder is not quarantined, and there is no durable row at `acc:INBOX:42` (X was never merged and never synced), so `DurableIdentityLookup.find` returns nil and the **new-header arm** is taken — `insertNewHeaderFromStaging` (3457), which by direct grep does **not** call `nseMergeIdentityConfirmed` (its only two production call sites are 1639 and 1978, both existing-durable-row arms), and `persistRenderedBodyFromStaging` (2968), whose only guard is `guard htmlContent != nil || textContent != nil`. **THE END STATE — durable, and this is why it is not merely a display splice:** Y's `messageHeader` row is written with Y's correct identity and **X's `summaryBlurb`/`summaryTodos`/`reminderDate`/`reminderTime`/`reminderContent`/`actionTag`**; a `MessageBody` keyed `ContentKey(rawValue: headerId)` holds **X's body**; the post-commit `flushNSEBatchToFTS` indexes **X's text** under Y's header id and runs `UPDATE messageHeader SET bodyComplete = 1 WHERE id IN (…)`; a `messageAICache` row is upserted under `MessageIdentity.aiCacheKey(accountId:folderPath:rfc822MessageId:)` — i.e. keyed on **Y's RFC id**, so the poisoning survives any later UID re-key; and `queueSetTagPendingOp` inserts a `setTag` `PendingOperation` that will write **X's action tag as an IMAP keyword on Y**. **WHY IT IS NON-RECOVERABLE (the mantra's test, applied rather than asserted):** sync finds nothing to repair — Y's header genuinely IS UID 42 under E2; the body queue selects `headerComplete = 1 AND bodyComplete = 0` and this row is `bodyComplete = 1`, so it never re-fetches; and even if it did, `BodyFetchProcessor` inserts with `onConflict: .ignore`, which preserves the wrong body. No sync pass and no single ordinary user gesture restores it: the user opens what the list calls Y and reads X. That is C3 misattribution, which the mantra places in the non-recoverable set outright. **REACHABILITY, stated honestly:** the durable half needs an IMAP INBOX whose `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity` is nil at merge time — the `IOS-EPOCH-001` / `IOS-AI-003` population, which is narrow but **durable** rather than a window (a folder no row of which carries an rfc822, and a server that reports no UIDVALIDITY, never leave it), plus a genuine turnover that recycles a UID onto a pushed message. The notification half (step ⑤) needs no nil epoch and is reachable on **any** IMAP account. Frequency is not the test here: the mantra's rare-edge dispensation is available only for the recoverable set, and this is not in it. 📋 **IMPLEMENTATION BRIEF — do not implement from this row; hand it to a fresh implementation agent.** **File/symbol to change: `TabMailNotificationService/NSEStagingDB.swift`, `stageHeader` — and only it.** **The invariant to encode:** a staged row's retained body/AI payload may only survive a re-`stageHeader` when the incoming identity is **not positively different** from the identity already on the row; on positive disagreement the payload columns (`htmlContent`, `textContent`, `attachmentsJSON`, `icsText`, `hasUnresolvedCIDs`, `summaryBlurb`, `summaryTodos`, `actionTag`, `reminderDate`, `reminderTime`, `reminderContent`, `aiCompleted`, `notified`, `processedAt`) must be cleared in the SAME write transaction that rewrites the identity. Read-then-branch inside the existing `db.write` (delete the row, then plain INSERT) is acceptable and simpler than a `CASE WHEN` per column. **Positive disagreement means exactly what `NSEDataBridge.nseMergeIdentityConfirmed` already means by it, and MUST reuse that shape rather than invent a second one:** (a) both `rfc822MessageId`s present and unequal after the tree's identity-comparison normalizer, **or** (b) both `observedUidValidity`s present and unequal. Nil on either side of both doors ⇒ retain, unchanged. Door (a) is the load-bearing one **and it is available precisely where door (b) is not**: the IMAP NSE resolves its push by Message-ID (`fetchIMAPMessage(rfc822MessageId: headId)` → `searchMessageId` → UID), so both sides carry an RFC even on the nil-epoch folder that defeats the epoch door. If the NSE target does not already link the shared normalizer, thread it in via `project.yml` sources rather than writing a second normalizer in the extension. **Fail direction: clear the payload.** Staging is scratch — a cleared payload costs one NSE AI recompute and one main-app body fetch, both of which already have working paths; a retained one is a C3 misattribution. **This single change closes BOTH halves,** and the ordering that makes that true must be preserved: `stageHeader` runs at `NotificationService.swift:549`, strictly before the peer probe (~604) and before `getCachedResult` (652), so a cleared `aiCompleted` makes the step-5.5 cache lookup miss and run 2 does its own AI. Verify that ordering still holds before relying on it. 🚨 **MIRROR-IMAGE RISKS (`MIS-005`, ×4, and it CHAINS — name both in the fix's own commit body):** **(1) The merge-side "obvious" fix is a message-dropper.** Adding `processedAt >= abandonedCutoff` (or any age predicate) to `mergeNSEStagingData`'s step-1 `SELECT … WHERE populated = 1` would convert a misattribution into a **silently dropped staged message** — a legitimately slow but perfectly valid row (a long AI wake, a cold launch after a night of pushes) would be excluded from the merge and then deleted by the very reap that predicate borrowed its constant from. Do not touch the step-1 read. **(2) A clear that is too eager destroys the feature the retention exists for.** Retention is CORRECT for a re-push of the SAME message and for the `AIOwnershipLease` placeholder row, which `stageHeader`'s own doc names; clearing on *any* conflict, or on "not positively equal" rather than "positively different", would make a duplicate push discard the body and AI the previous run already paid for, and an RFC-less message could never accumulate its own payload across wakes. The gate must be positive evidence of a DIFFERENT message, never absence of evidence that it is the same one — this is `MIS-IOS-004`'s "could not determine ≠ authoritative" in its other direction. **(3) Do not reach for a generation counter, a lease, or a run token.** That is the compensating-mechanism shape `A3` and `MIS-003` forbid: the identity needed to decide this is already on the row. **RED-FIRST TEST (global Testing rule 12 — pin the INVARIANT, not the mechanism):** drive `stageHeader` twice against one staging DB with two different `rfc822MessageId`s at the same `accountId:messageId`, then assert the SYSTEM PROPERTY — after the second call the row carries **no payload belonging to the first message** (`htmlContent`, `summaryBlurb`, `actionTag`, `aiCompleted` all cleared) — and, at the merge level, that a merge of that row produces a `MessageHeader` and `MessageBody` whose content came from the second message or from nothing at all, never from the first. A test that merely asserts "the clear-arm ran" is a mechanism test and will stay green on a wrong spec. <br><br>✅ **IOS-NSE-005 CLOSURE (2026-08-04) — FIXED by `5813e44b1`, and the fix is exactly the implementation brief above with nothing added.** **The invariant that now holds:** *staged content produced for one message is never delivered, indexed, cached or put on the wire under a different message's identity.* **The change is `NSEStagingDB.stageHeader` and only it** — inside its existing write transaction it reads the stored `(rfc822MessageId, observedUidValidity)` and, on POSITIVE disagreement, DELETEs the row so its own statement lands as a plain INSERT and every column it does not write returns to its schema default (payload NULL, `aiCompleted`/`notified` 0, `processedAt` re-anchored, the AI-ownership lease cleared — correct, because a lease held for the predecessor says nothing about the successor). The doors are `NSEDataBridge.nseMergeIdentityConfirmed`'s, same precedence: **(a) RFC first and unconditional** (both `MessageIdentity.comparableRfc822Identity` present and unequal), **(b) epoch only when (a) could not adjudicate**. Nil on either side of both doors RETAINS. **`nseMergeIdentityConfirmed`'s `!folderQuarantined` term is deliberately NOT carried** (`MIS-018` — port the guard or say why it does not apply): there the comparison is a live NSE observation against the DURABLE `Folder.lastKnownUidValidity`, which lags mid-reset; here BOTH values are live SELECT observations the NSE made itself of the same folder, so no third party's staleness can manufacture a disagreement. **Ordering re-verified rather than trusted:** `stageHeader` is called at `NotificationService.process` strictly before the peer probe and before `getCachedResult`, so ONE write closes both halves. **THE THREE TRAPS WERE NOT TAKEN, and the register's brief was followed literally:** (1) `mergeNSEStagingData`'s step-1 `SELECT … WHERE populated = 1` is **byte-unchanged** — an age predicate there converts the misattribution into a dropped staged message; (2) the gate is POSITIVE evidence of a different message, never absence of evidence that it is the same one, so a duplicate push and the `AIOwnershipLease` placeholder both still retain (`MIS-IOS-004`); (3) no generation counter, shadow table, lease or run token (A3 / `MIS-003`). **A1 — AUTHORED, not restored:** `v2final` (`e28dd4edb`) has the identical `ON CONFLICT` list, the identical retention and the identical doc comment, so it **shares the defect**; shipped `07a4bb703` shares it and is **strictly weaker** (`observedUidValidity` does not exist at that tag, so door (b) is not even available, and its `getCachedResult` is byte-identical to v3's). There was no guard to port. **NO schema change and NO migration.** **Verification — red-first on BOTH halves, and two-sided:** four tests in `TabMailTests/NSE/NSEStagingIdentitySplicingTests.swift` drive the REAL `NSEStagingDB` writers and the REAL `NSEDataBridge.mergeNSEStagingData`. Two pin the defect (the durable merge carries no artifact of the predecessor — header fields, `messageBody`, `messageAICache`, `bodyComplete`, and NO `setTag` `pendingOperation`; and `getCachedResult` serves nothing of it); two are ANCHORS holding the opposite direction (a re-push of the SAME message keeps the work the previous run paid for; an unanswerable identity retains, and a live `AIOwnershipLease` claim survives). Inversion A (gate disabled = HEAD) turned the two defect tests RED with both anchors GREEN; inversion B (clear on ANY conflict = the mirror-image fix) turned both anchors RED with the defect tests GREEN. **Enabling change, recorded because it is durable:** `TabMailTests` could reach NO NSE-target symbol, so every pre-existing NSE staging test hand-mirrors the production SQL — and a mirrored row cannot red-prove a fix that lives INSIDE `stageHeader`. `project.yml` now compiles five NSE-only files into `TabMailTests` as well as the extension, with `#if TABMAIL_TESTS @testable import TabMail` (deliberately not `canImport(TabMail)`, whose value in an app-extension target depends on the module search paths). **What could NOT be proven, stated as the negative case (`MIS-IOS-008` / `MIS-019`):** the row's own analysis also names `stageBody` and `stageSummary` as bare `UPDATE … WHERE id = ?` with no compare-and-swap, so a ZOMBIE run 1 resuming from its AI await after run 2 has re-headed the row can still write its summary onto the successor. That is **outside this fix's scope by the brief's own instruction** (`stageHeader` and only it), it is NOT made worse here, and it is **not closed** — the zombie checkpoints (`OneShotFlag.hasFired()`) are the only thing standing in front of it. Detail routed to [`Companion/Memory/Current/107-a-staging-key-that-names-an-address-must-re-prove-identity-before-reusing-payload.md`](Companion/Memory/Current/107-a-staging-key-that-names-an-address-must-re-prove-identity-before-reusing-payload.md). |
| IOS-NSE-006 | ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04)** — the remainder `IOS-NSE-005`'s closure named as *"NOT closed"* and left explicitly out of scope. C3 misattribution, durable, repaired by no sync pass and no user gesture. Pre-existing | NSE; `NSEStagingDB.stageBody`; `NSEStagingDB.stageSummary`; `NSEStagingDB.persistProcessedMessage`; bare `UPDATE … WHERE id = ?`; `INSERT OR REPLACE`; no compare-and-swap; zombie run; `deliveredFlag`; `OneShotFlag.hasFired()`; `stagedIdentityPositivelyDiffers`; recycled UID; `id = "accountId:messageId"` | **`stageHeader` re-proves identity before REUSING payload already on the row; these are the writers that ADD payload to it, and they carried no identity term at all.** `IOS-NSE-005`'s brief was deliberately scoped to *"`stageHeader` and only it"*, and its closure recorded the negative case: *"a ZOMBIE run 1 resuming from its AI await after run 2 has re-headed the row can still write its summary onto the successor … the zombie checkpoints (`OneShotFlag.hasFired()`) are the only thing standing in front of it."* **Those checkpoints do not close it, and the reason is that they answer a different question.** `deliveredFlag` is per-RUN — it asks *"did MY run's watchdog already deliver"* — which says nothing about whether a LATER run has since re-headed the row; a predecessor whose watchdog never fired passes every checkpoint and writes. <br><br>**THE THREE WRITERS, enumerated by the PROPERTY rather than by the two the finding named (`MIS-006`: the class is *a writer keyed on an ADDRESS assuming it still names the same message*).** (1) `stageBody` — the predecessor's body becomes the successor's `MessageBody`, is indexed under the successor's header id, and sets `bodyComplete = 1`, which the body queue (`bodyComplete = 0`) never revisits and `BodyFetchProcessor`'s `onConflict: .ignore` would preserve anyway. (2) `stageSummary` — the worse half: the summary/todos/reminder are merged onto the successor's header AND cached in `messageAICache` under the **successor's RFC key**, so the poisoning survives every later UID re-key and is a permanent HIT, and the merge queues a `setTag` `PendingOperation` putting the predecessor's action tag **on the wire** against the successor. (3) `persistProcessedMessage` — the third writer, guarded here rather than left as the one exception; its damage DIFFERS and is stated rather than assumed: `INSERT OR REPLACE` rewrites the WHOLE row, so it does not splice mixed identities — it **destroys the successor's staged push outright** (a dropped message) and resurrects a predecessor the address no longer holds. <br><br>**THE FIX — one predicate, reused, no new machinery.** All three now call the SAME `stagedIdentityPositivelyDiffers` helper `stageHeader` already uses, inside the SAME write transaction as their statement (a check outside it is a TOCTOU seam a concurrent re-head slips through). Doors and precedence are `NSEDataBridge.nseMergeIdentityConfirmed`'s: **(a) RFC first and unconditional**, **(b) epoch only when (a) cannot adjudicate**. **NO schema change, NO migration, no generation counter, no run token, no lease** (A3 / `MIS-003` — the identity needed to decide this was already on the row). <br><br>🚨 **THE FAIL DIRECTION IS THE MIRROR OF `stageHeader`'s, and getting it backwards is the trap.** There the question is *may I KEEP payload already on the row*, so the safe answer to an unanswerable identity is RETAIN. Here it is *may I ADD payload to it*, so the safe answer is **WRITE**. Suppressing the write on absence of evidence would stop an rfc-less, epoch-less message ever accumulating a body or summary across wakes, and would make `persistProcessedMessage` drop whole legitimate runs — `MIS-IOS-004`'s "could not determine ≠ authoritative" in its other direction. Only POSITIVE evidence of a different message suppresses a write; an ABSENT row also writes (the ordinary first insert). **The refusal is not silent** (`IOS-NSE-005`'s own lesson about a dropped write): each writes an `NSELog.error` naming the id and the row — the NSE's durable file channel, not a detached console (topic 105). **The dropped payload is safe to drop precisely because the guard fired:** it was computed for a message provably no longer at that address, and whoever holds the address now re-fetches its body and computes its own AI through paths that already work. <br><br>**A1 — AUTHORED, not restored.** Shipped `07a4bb703` and the `v2final` sibling `e28dd4edb` both have `stageBody`/`stageSummary` as bare `UPDATE … WHERE id = ?` with no identity term, byte-identical to v3's; both **share the defect** and `07a4bb703` is strictly weaker (`observedUidValidity` does not exist at that tag, so door (b) is unavailable). There was no guard to port. <br><br>**Verification — red-first on both halves, two-sided.** Five tests in `TabMailTests/NSE/NSEStagingZombieWriterTests.swift` drive the REAL writers and the REAL `NSEDataBridge.mergeNSEStagingData`. Three pin the defect by sweeping durable state for a token unique to the predecessor (never by asserting "the refusal arm ran" — `MIS-015`); two are ANCHORS holding the opposite direction, and the mirror-image "suppress unless identity positively AGREES" fix turns `unanswerableIdentityWritesRatherThanRefuses` RED while leaving the defect tests green. <br><br>✅ **RE-RAISED AND REFUTED (2026-08-05, final audit train).** The codex half reported this row's three writers as a BLOCKING C3 misattribution — *"treats an unanswerable identity as permission to write"* — with a reachable UIDVALIDITY-turnover scenario and *"recovery: none"*. **The mechanism is real and the classification is wrong**: it is the residual on the CHOSEN side of the two-sided trade this row already states, and the proposed fix is verbatim the mirror-image trap named above. Refuted by three independent checks: this row; `stageBody`'s own doc comment carrying the same reasoning; and the anchor test `TabMailTests/NSE/NSEStagingZombieWriterTests.swift` `unanswerableIdentityWritesRatherThanRefuses()`, which exists precisely to go RED if the proposed fix is applied. The Claude half read the same code independently and agreed. **No change made.** Recorded as `MIS-026` — a blessing test and an anchor test are the same artifact seen from opposite sides of a decision, so the discriminator is never the assertion's text but *what breaks if it goes the other way*. <br><br>⚠️ **RETRACTED 2026-08-05 — the "deliberately NOT changed" carve-out for `getCachedResult` was WRONG, and it is now the FOURTH member of this class.** **What it said, verbatim, so the error stays searchable:** *"**Deliberately NOT changed, so the census is accountable.** `getCachedResult` keeps its `WHERE id = ? AND aiCompleted = 1` with no identity term: `stageHeader` runs strictly before it in `NotificationService.process`, so the row's identity is already this run's — adding a second term would be the redundant machinery A3 forbids, and the ordering is the guard (do not move that call later)."* **Why it is false: the ordering establishes only that `stageHeader` was CALLED, never that it LANDED.** `stageHeader` returns `Void` and wraps its whole write in `do { … } catch { NSELog.error("stageHeader failed: \(error)") }` — a thrown write is logged and **swallowed**, and no caller can observe it. The staging DB is a **non-WAL, cross-process App Group** database, so `SQLITE_BUSY` under contention is a live outcome. When that write throws, the predecessor's `aiCompleted = 1` row survives untouched at the address, `getCachedResult` hits on address alone, and `NotificationService` hands `EmailNotificationBuilder.fill` the successor's `senderName`/`senderEmail`/`subject` together with the **predecessor's** `summaryBlurb`, `actionTag` and reminder — including an `actionTag == "reply"` that escalates the banner to active + sound for a message it was never computed for. **Non-recoverable:** the notification has been delivered and seen, and no later sync un-shows it. `MIS-024` in its purest form — reading a mechanism proves it is CORRECT, never that it RAN, nor that it ran BEFORE the damage. **✅ FIXED (final audit train, 2026-08-05):** `getCachedResult` now takes the incoming `NSEMessageMetadata` and consults the same `stagedIdentityPositivelyDiffers` the three writers use, with the SAME fail direction as `stageHeader` — this is a *may I REUSE payload* question, so an unanswerable identity still SERVES and only POSITIVE evidence of a different message turns a hit into a miss. The mirror-image trap ("unknown ⇒ miss") is pinned RED by `unanswerableIdentityWritesRatherThanRefuses` and by `NSEStagingIdentitySplicingTests.unanswerableIdentityRetainsRatherThanClears`. Red-first evidence: `NSEStagingZombieWriterTests.cachedAiIsNeverCombinedWithADifferentMessagesHeader()` fails on the pre-fix code with `(delivered.contains(token) → true) == false` and `(active → true) == false`. The ordering remains load-bearing for the *write* half — still do not move the `stageHeader` call later in the run — it is simply not a substitute for the read's own identity term. `AIOwnershipLease`'s writers are left alone: their predicates are `WHERE id = ? AND aiOwner = ?`, they write lease bookkeeping rather than payload, and **the lease is not this guard** — `tryClaim` gates the right to COMPUTE, not the right to WRITE, and both generations use `.nse`, so it is generation-blind. Aiming the fix there would have left the real hole open. |
| IOS-LABEL-003 | 🔓 **OPEN** — inert; **registered rather than deleted, and the reason is `MIS-018`** | User labels; `MessageHeaderInfo.userLabelIdsAreAuthoritative`; `EmailProvider.swift`; `GmailProvider.parseGmailMessage`; `ExchangeProvider.parseGraphMessage`; anticipatory shim; dead code; rules 2/3/5; `remoteSetIsAuthoritative` | **Two production writers, zero production readers, four tests.** Declaration-grammar census at HEAD (`rg userLabelIdsAreAuthoritative TabMail/ Shared/ TabMailTests/ TabMailNotificationService/`, **9 hits total, all 9 enumerated**): one declaration (`EmailProvider.swift`, `var userLabelIdsAreAuthoritative = false`), **two** doc-comment references — `EmailProvider.swift`'s own *"⚑ CURRENTLY UNCONSUMED ON v3"* block and one in `StatefulGmailActionServerTests.swift` — **two** writes (`GmailProvider.swift`, `userLabelCatalog.isAuthoritative`, pre-existing; `ExchangeProvider.swift`, `true`, added by this candidate's `e265428ff`), and **four** test assertions (`ExchangeUserLabelCategoryTests` ×1, `StatefulGmailActionServerTests` ×3). ⚠️ **An earlier wording of this row said "one doc reference", so its enumeration summed to 8 against a stated total of 9** — the ninth is the test-file doc comment, and a row whose parts do not sum to its own total guarantees the corpus gets re-censused (`MIS-007`/`MIS-008`: read the hits, do not count them). **No production read of any shape.** `MIS-021` applied rather than assumed: the field lives on `MessageHeaderInfo`, which is `Sendable` and **not** `Codable`, so there is no serialisation path off it; the value is never written to a column, never passed to a provider call, and never reaches the wire. It is genuinely inert. **The declaration already says so** — *"⚑ CURRENTLY UNCONSUMED ON v3"* — and its stated dataflow premise checks out: every sync-side junction write is `MessageUserLabel(...).insert(db, onConflict: .ignore)` (four sites in `SyncEngineFullSync`, two in `SyncEngineDeltaSync`, one each in `SyncEngineBackfillDeep` and `NSEDataBridge`) and there is **no** sync-side junction delete, so nothing can erase membership and nothing needs the guard yet. **Why it is kept rather than deleted, stated as a trade and not a preference.** Deleting it satisfies rules 2/3/5 at the cost of `MIS-018`: the consumer it guards (`remoteSetIsAuthoritative:` in the reference's `SyncEngineFullSync`, gating a reconcile that REPLACES local membership) would then be ported later against an absent flag, which is the half-port shape — mechanism ported, guard left behind. Keeping it costs one inert field and cannot make a future reconcile *less* conservative, because the `false` default is the fail-closed direction. **The trip-wire, so keeping it is not free of obligation: `IMAPProvider` does NOT set it.** Any consumer that lands must set it `true` at the IMAP keyword FETCH in the same change, or IMAP labels fail closed forever. **The four tests are not blessing tests (`MIS-014`)** — they assert a real PRODUCER contract (that Gmail stamps it only once its catalog has loaded, and that Exchange stamps it `true`), which is live behaviour of live code, not an assertion that a defect is correct. **Reconsider only when a label reconcile is actually proposed**; at that point the choice is wire-it-up, never delete-and-re-add. |

## Exchange/Graph move-id churn — the `IOS-QUEUE-008` hand-off, adjudicated (2026-08-04)

`IOS-QUEUE-008`'s severity note surfaced this and explicitly declined to judge it ("⚠ **Not
adjudicated here** … judging it needs the Exchange move/queue interaction traced end to end"). It is
traced here. The finding was relayed as one claim; it is **two losses with different blast radii**
and they get different verdicts, so the two must not be discharged together.

⚠ **One premise of the relayed finding is REFUTED.** It stated that a second gesture after our own
Graph move takes the message-not-found arm and deletes *both* the `PendingOperation` **and** the local
header. The op deletion is real. **The header deletion is NOT reachable on that path**, because
`deleteConfirmedGoneHeader` is keyed by the header's full primary key — `MessageIdentity.headerId(accountId:folderPath:messageId:)` built from `currentOp.folderPath` — while
`AccountManagerActions.optimisticMoveToFolder`'s `updateAll` rewrites `folderId`, `folderPath`,
`isInInbox` and `observedUidValidity` and **never** rewrites `MessageHeader.id`. After a move the row's
PK still carries the *original* folder segment and the op carries the *destination* one, so the two
strings differ, `MessageHeader.fetchOne(db, key: headerId)` returns nil, `existed == false`, and the
function returns before touching anything. The header deletion is real on *other* paths, enumerated in
`IOS-GRAPH-003`.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Deferred policy |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-GRAPH-002 | ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-04, "Finish the Graph move locally using the `/move` response already in hand") — the closure is at the END of the Notes cell; the BLOCKING adjudication below is kept VERBATIM as history, because it is also the implementation brief and a later reader must be able to check the fix against what was asked for.** 🛑 **BLOCKING** — a durably-admitted `PendingOperation` is DELETED on evidence that satisfies none of the four exits, with **no user-visible signal**, on an address the app itself invalidated. Pre-existing (not candidate-introduced). **DO NOT fix ad hoc — the brief is at the end of this cell, and the obvious fix is a lane wedge** | Action queue; Exchange/Outlook; Microsoft Graph; `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage`; discarded `/move` response; `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"`; `EmailProvider.move` returns Void; `AccountManager.executeOperation` `.move` non-IMAP arm; `ExecutedOperation.allMembers`; `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`; `ProvenDestinationAddress`; `isMessageNotFoundError`; 404 as exit 2; `MIS-IOS-003`; `MIS-IOS-004`; THE ADDRESS PROBLEM | **THE ADDRESS PROBLEM in the Graph id space, with the IMAP half fixed and the Graph half never enumerated.** `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage` is `let _ = try await request(path: "/messages/\(encodedId)/move", method: "POST", body: jsonData)` — Graph's `/move` returns the moved message **with its new `id`**, and the response is bound to `_`. `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` appears **nowhere** in the tree (the only `Prefer:` header is `outlook.timezone` in `ExchangeCalendarProvider`), so the account runs on Graph's default mutable ids, which Microsoft documents as changing when an item moves between folders. Nothing re-learns the new id: `EmailProvider.move(ids:from:to:)` returns `Void`, `executeOperation`'s `.move` non-IMAP arm returns `ExecutedOperation.allMembers` (`provenDestinations: []`), and `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove` early-returns on `guard … !destinations.isEmpty`. So the local row keeps the dead id, and `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets`' non-IMAP arm — `messages.filter { !$0.messageId.isEmpty }`, `nil` epoch — happily admits it into the **next** operation. **This is `59423bb7d` ("Finish the IMAP move locally using the COPYUID already in hand") applied to one coordinate system only.** That commit states the invariant outright — *"A move changes a message's address, the server reports the new one … and the drain threw it away"* — and Graph is the second address space where that sentence is true verbatim. Recorded as `MIS-006` instance 5 and `MIS-IOS-003`. <br><br>**THE LOSS.** The next op reaches the wire naming the dead id, Graph answers `404 ErrorItemNotFound`, `AuthedHTTP` surfaces it as `ProviderError.networkError(HTTPError.networkError(statusCode: 404))`, `isMessageNotFoundError` matches, and the single-message arm executes `PendingOperation.deleteOne` under the log *"Conflict: `\(opType)` — message not found, dropping"*. **A 404 is a fact about the ADDRESS, not about the WORK.** Three different world states produce it — the message was permanently deleted (the op is genuinely moot, exit 2); the message MOVED, by us or by Outlook desktop or by a server rule (the op is neither done nor inapplicable — it needs re-addressing); or Graph is transiently inconsistent right after a change, which Microsoft documents. The arm collapses all three. That is `MIS-IOS-004` — *"could not determine" read as "provider says done"* — and **the file already contains the correct precedent one screen away**: `isPermanentlyInvalidError`'s doc says *"🚨 A BARE STATUS CODE IS NOT A CLASSIFICATION … 'the request was rejected and we do not know why' is an absence of evidence"*, and its predecessor was deliberately narrowed for exactly this reason. **Exit 4 does not cover it either** — exit 4 needs a **proven** reset disagreeing with the op's **durable** `observedUidValidity`, which on Graph is always `nil`, and it is scoped to an address *space*, not one item's id. <br><br>**WHY THIS IS BLOCKING AND `IOS-QUEUE-006` IS NOT — the distinction is the signal, and it is the whole verdict.** `IOS-QUEUE-006` is registrable because *"the local mutation and the durable op are the same transaction … a non-admitted id gets no local mutation either … the message therefore stays visible where the user left it"*, so the user sees the failure and re-swipes. **Here the local mutation LANDS and is never rolled back.** The user swipes to Trash, the row animates away and is written to Trash locally, and then the durable half is silently destroyed. The UI shows success; the server shows the message still in Archive; nothing anywhere tells the user, the AI tool or the notification router that the gesture died. The mantra's recovery test allows *"one ordinary user gesture"* — but a user who has been shown success has no reason to make one. <br><br>**AND THE STATE THAT RESTORES THE SIGNAL IS DATE-WINDOWED, so it is not guaranteed to fire.** The only thing that re-addresses the row is a sync that materialises the message under its new id and sweeps the remnant. `SyncEngineFullSync.canonicalizeLocalRows` cannot do it — it selects on `Column("messageId") == messageId && Column("folderId") == folderId` and the churned id is by construction absent, with the RFC discriminator *"deliberately SUBTRACTED"*. `selectStaleHeaders`' `.date` arm (Exchange's `staleWindowMode`) is `candidates.filter { $0.date >= floorDate && !remoteIds.contains($0.messageId) }` with `floorDate = fetched.map(\.date).min()`, and Graph orders by `receivedDateTime` — so a message **older than the destination folder's newest-N window is neither re-fetched under its new id nor swept**, unless the fetch reports `coverage.spansEntireFolder`. Exchange delta covers only ONE folder: `SyncEngineDeltaSync` mints the cursor with `getCurrentDeltaLink(folderId: inboxFolder.path)`, so `exchangeDeltaSync`'s `toDelete` sweep repairs remnants for **moves out of Inbox** and for nothing else — and even then it is a **DELETION predicate downstream of the drop** (`MIS-024` instance 5): it restores the *message*, never the *gesture*. For an Archive→Trash or folder→folder move of older mail, the remnant persists, every repeat of the gesture enqueues the same dead id, 404s and is dropped identically — the mantra's Q1 answers **NO**, which is the definition of the non-recoverable set. <br><br>**REACHABILITY — ordinary use, no unusual server.** Any Outlook/Exchange account, any two gestures on one message before the destination folder syncs: offline triage drained together on reconnect; a swipe in Inbox followed by a swipe in Archive; an undo of a drained move (undo is a reverse move, so it names the same dead id); and the `IOS-QUEUE-008` lane split makes the two ops **concurrent**, so one of the pair races the other's churn regardless of user timing. **Gmail does NOT share this shape** — `GmailProvider.move` is `messages.modify` add/remove label, the resource id never changes, and a Gmail 404 therefore really does mean gone. IMAP does not share it either, and for a reason worth stating because it is the trap: the *same* root cause exists on IMAP but is **caught by a second guard** — `admittedOrdinaryActionTargets`' IMAP arm refuses a row whose `observedUidValidity` the optimistic move nil'd, so the dead address never reaches the wire (fail-CLOSED). The non-IMAP arm has no such gate, so the identical defect is fail-OPEN. A guard that makes a bug benign in one arm is not a guard the other arm has. <br><br>📋 **IMPLEMENTATION BRIEF — do not implement from this row; hand it to a fresh implementation agent.** **The invariant to encode:** *a durable action leaves the queue only on one of the four exits; a `404` naming an address the app itself invalidated is not exit 2* — and the way to satisfy it is **not** to reclassify the error but to **stop discarding the address the wire already gave us** (`MIS-IOS-003`, `CLAUDE.md` § THE ADDRESS PROBLEM). **Files/symbols:** `TabMail/Providers/ExchangeProvider.swift` — `moveMessage(id:destinationId:)` (decode the response's `id`) and a `move(ids:from:to:)` overload returning an outcome, mirrored on the shape `IMAPProvider.move(ids:from:to:admittedUidValidity:) -> MoveOutcome` already has; `TabMail/Services/Account/AccountManagerQueue.swift` — `executeOperation`'s `.move` case, which already type-tests `provider as? IMAPProvider` and needs the sibling `as? ExchangeProvider` arm so `ExecutedOperation.provenDestinations` is populated; `TabMail/Services/MessageHeaderRekey.swift` — `ProvenDestinationAddress` is IMAP-shaped (`destinationUid: UInt32`, `destinationUidValidity: UInt32`) and a Graph destination is a `String` id with **no** epoch, so the type needs a provider-neutral id and a nil-able epoch. **ADR-IOS-068 is satisfied, not strained:** the new key is the provider's own native id from the response to the mutation this op issued — attempt-correlated, no RFC consulted, no `SEARCH` result promoted to a mutation target. **Fail direction: leave the epoch stamp NIL for Graph.** `finishMove`'s G2 is already written for this — a nil `observedUidValidity` is `.retainedForRetry` (recoverable), a *positive* disagreement is `.terminalStale` (the only terminal arm), so stamping anything invented would terminally drop the next gesture, which is the mirror image of the bug. Graph has no epoch space; nil is correct and is also the safe value. 🚨 **MIRROR-IMAGE RISKS (`MIS-005`, ×4, and it CHAINS — name both in the fix's commit body).** **(1) Reclassifying the 404 as retryable WITHOUT re-keying converts a silent drop into a lane wedge.** The op would name the dead id forever, fail identically forever, and — with `buildLanes` keying on `accountId:folderPath:messageId` (`IOS-QUEUE-008`) and `IOS-QUEUE-003` item 4's starvation concern — block every later op that shares its lane. The wedge corollary says a starved op has not been preserved, so that "fix" trades one never-drop violation for another. The re-key is what makes retry *terminate*. **(2) `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` is NOT a free win and must not be the first move.** It changes id format **account-wide**, so every value already minted under the mutable scheme becomes non-resolvable at once: `MessageHeader.messageId` and every `MessageIdentity.headerId` built from it, `PendingOperation.messageIds`, `MessageIdentity.aiCacheKey`, `nse_processed_message.id`, `MessageContentStore` content keys, and `Folder.path` (Graph mail-folder ids), plus the NSE's separate `Shared/API/GraphAPI` client, which would have to send the identical header or the two halves of the app would disagree about what a message id is. That is a one-way per-account migration plus a full re-sync, and Microsoft still documents the immutable id as changing on a move to an **archive mailbox** or an export/re-import — so it narrows the churn, it does not abolish it.
