Evaluate it as a follow-up on top of the re-key, never instead of it. **(3) Do not "fix" this by widening `isConfirmedGoneError` or by adding a Graph-specific retry cap** — a cap is a discard on a timer. **RED-FIRST TEST (global Testing rule 12 — pin the INVARIANT, not the mechanism).** The seam exists: `ExchangeProvider` takes an injectable `testSession` and `TabMailTests/Providers/ExchangeProviderMockTests.swift` drives it through the `FakeHTTP` `URLProtocol`. Script the fake Graph server to (i) answer `POST /messages/{old}/move` with `200` **and a body whose `id` is new**, and (ii) answer `404` to any later request naming `{old}`. Drive two queued gestures on one message (A→B, then B→C) through `drainPendingQueue`. **Assert the system property, not the re-key:** after both drains, either the wire received a move of that message to C, **or** a `PendingOperation` for it is still durably queued — never *"`pendingOperation` is empty and the server still has the message in B"*. Pre-fix that assertion is RED (zero rows, message in B). A test that asserts "the header was re-keyed to the new id" is a mechanism test and will stay green on a wrong spec (`MIS-015`). Add the symmetric non-vacuity leg: with the fake server returning the SAME id from `/move`, the same scenario must stay green (`feedback_non_vacuity_must_be_two_sided`). <br><br>✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-04 — the address is no longer discarded.** `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage` now decodes the `id` off Graph's `/move` response, and `moveProvingDestinations(ids:from:to:) -> MoveOutcome` carries it per member into `ExecutedOperation.provenDestinations` through the sibling `provider as? ExchangeProvider` arm in `executeOperation`'s `.move` case — **reusing `MessageHeaderRekey.finishMove`, not writing a second re-key.** `ProvenDestinationAddress` became provider-neutral (`sourceProviderId` / `destinationProviderId` as `String`, `destinationUidValidity` optional) and `MoveOutcome` moved out of `IMAPProvider` to sit beside it; the IMAP arm's guards are untouched and the values it writes are the same decimal UID strings as before. **The epoch is left NIL for Graph**, as this brief required — G2 gives a provider with no epoch space the same arm as an unknown epoch, because an invented stamp would be the *positive* disagreement that is `.terminalStale`. **When Graph's response names no id, nothing is substituted:** that member is returned in `provenIds` with no entry in `provenDestinations`, byte-for-byte IMAP's no-`COPYUID` shape, and the row keeps its old address rather than being re-keyed onto a guess. **A batch that fails partway returns its proven prefix**, routed through the existing `retirePartiallyCompletedOp` contract, rather than discarding an address the wire had already supplied — throwing would have re-created this same defect one mid-batch failure later; it rethrows only when nothing at all was proven, and rethrows `CancellationError` unconditionally. **Neither mirror-image trap was taken:** the 404 classification is untouched, so no lane wedge is introduced (the re-key is what makes retry *terminate*), and `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` was NOT adopted. `IOS-GRAPH-003` is deliberately left open. **RED PROOF** — `TabMailTests/Services/FinishTheMoveLocallyGraphTests.swift`, with `moveMessage`'s re-learned id re-discarded inside the build lock: *"✘ Test "A second gesture on a just-moved Outlook message still reaches the server" recorded an issue … Expectation failed: (landed → false) \|\| (stillQueued → false) ↳ the user's second gesture was DESTROYED: the server still has the message at ["graph-archive-folder"] and no PendingOperation survives to retry it"*, preceded on the wire by *"[Exchange] HTTP 404 POST …/messages/graph-1/move"*. The symmetric non-vacuity leg — *"Two gestures still work when the tenant's Graph ids survive a move"* — **passed in that same inverted run**, so the red is the churn and not the harness. ⚠ **What the tests deliberately do NOT exercise:** the destination `Folder` rows are omitted so `drainPendingQueue`'s post-drain sync is skipped, because that sync is a repair strictly downstream of the drop (`MIS-024`) and against a one-message fake server it would mask the defect entirely. The date-windowed reachability described above is therefore unchanged by this commit — the re-key is what makes that repair unnecessary, not a fix to it. <br><br>📌 **THE NO-ID ARM, ADJUDICATED (2026-08-04) — re-raised as a possible residual of this very fix, and the answer is that it must stay as written.** The observation: in `moveProvingDestinations`, `provenIds.append(id)` is UNCONDITIONAL while `provenDestinations.append` is conditional on a decodable `id`, so a 2xx whose body names none retires the op while contributing no destination address; the local row keeps the old provider id and a later gesture 404s. **The two appends answer two different questions, and only the second is unanswered here.** `provenIds` answers *"did the work happen?"* — settled by the 2xx alone, since `moveMessage` throws on any non-2xx, so reaching that line means Graph performed the move. `provenDestinations` answers *"where did it land?"* — settled only by a decodable id. Retiring the op is therefore **exit 1, provider success** — the first and most ordinary queue exit, not a discard. 🚨 **THIS IS NEVER A "WE COULD NOT DETERMINE" CASE, and that is the whole distinction from `MIS-IOS-004`: the SUCCESS is determined; only the new ADDRESS is unknown.** Conflating the two is what would make fail-closed look principled here. **Fail-closed is NOT available at the same cost; it is strictly worse, and it manufactures the very defect `cfe6720be` closed.** Withholding `provenIds` leaves a member queued whose work has ALREADY happened; its retry re-addresses the id Graph just invalidated and 404s into the `isMessageNotFoundError` arm, which deletes the `PendingOperation` — and here, unlike the second-gesture path `IOS-GRAPH-003` refutes, **the local header goes too**: a retry of the move op carries the op's own SOURCE `folderPath`, which is exactly the segment baked into the row's primary key, so `deleteConfirmedGoneHeader` matches and destroys the header, its `MessageBody` and its FTS row. That is `IOS-GRAPH-003` path (1) reached deliberately by a "fix". Changing the 404 classification as well does not rescue it — that is MIRROR-IMAGE TRAP 1's lane wedge, since the re-key is what makes a retry TERMINATE. **Attribution class: pre-existing-shape residual, NOT candidate-introduced, and a STRICT IMPROVEMENT** — at `07a4bb703` and `e28dd4edb` no destination was produced for ANY move, so this arm was 100% of moves; it is now the exception. **Reachability: not produced by Graph's documented contract at all** (`POST /messages/{id}/move` returns the moved message resource), so it needs a non-conforming gateway or proxy; and it is recoverable — the row keeps a valid old address, the destination folder's sync re-materialises the message, and one repeated gesture completes the work. **What DID change (`cfe6720be` follow-up):** the asymmetry is now stated at the append site instead of reading as an oversight, with the reason it must not be "fixed". **Do not build a receipt, a reconciliation pass or an outcome enum for it** — that is machinery for an arm the provider's contract does not produce, and the mantra declines it. |
| IOS-GRAPH-003 | 🔓 **OPEN** — REGISTRABLE; recoverable; pre-existing; ⚠️ **this row also REFUTES the relayed claim that the header deletion rides the second-gesture path** | Action queue; `AccountManagerQueue.isConfirmedGoneError`; `deleteConfirmedGoneHeader`; `MessageIdentity.headerId`; primary-key mismatch; `optimisticMoveToFolder`; `MessageContentStore.releaseUnowned`; `messageUserLabel` cascade; Exchange/Graph; externally moved message; `IOS-QUEUE-007` timeout retry | **A 404 also deletes the local header — but only where the op's `folderPath` matches the folder segment already baked into the row's primary key, which the post-move path does NOT satisfy.** `isConfirmedGoneError` matches `ProviderError.messageNotFound` and `HTTPError.networkError(statusCode: 404 \| 410)` structurally; `deleteConfirmedGoneHeader` then fetches `MessageHeader` **by primary key** and, if the row exists, deletes it and calls `MessageContentStore.releaseUnowned(… stores: [.searchIndex, .body] …)`, with the `messageUserLabel` and `messageReference` FK cascades taking the children. **The two paths where the key DOES match, enumerated rather than asserted:** (1) **a retry of the move op itself after its wire call already landed** — `IOS-QUEUE-007`'s abandoned-`withTimeout` task, a crash between the Graph `200` and the op's deletion, or the `"CRITICAL: Failed to delete completed PendingOperation"` arm; the retry carries the op's SOURCE `folderPath`, which is exactly the segment in the PK, so the header, its body and its FTS row are destroyed; (2) **a message whose Graph id churned because something ELSE moved it** — Outlook desktop, OWA, or a server-side rule — where the local row is still at its original address and the user's first gesture 404s. A third, longer path exists on our own churn: move S→D, then D→S (each dropped), then a gesture from S, at which point the op's `folderPath` and the PK segment coincide again. <br><br>**Why REGISTRABLE.** Nothing is destroyed on the server, no wire operation is issued, and no message is mis-addressed — C3 is not reached. Everything deleted is **re-derivable server state**: the header, the `MessageBody` (re-fetched by the ordinary body pipeline), the FTS row (re-indexed), and `messageUserLabel`, which for Exchange is a Graph **category** and is re-ingested by `SyncEngineFullSync` / `SyncEngineDeltaSync` / `SyncEngineBackfillDeep` from `info.userLabelIds` on insert. No user-authored content is touched — drafts, Outbox sends and attachments are outside this arm entirely. **The four-exit lattice does not govern this limb at all**: a header is cached server state, not a queued intention, so limb B is judged against the mantra and the Data Integrity rules, not against exits 1–4. Only the op deletion in `IOS-GRAPH-002` is a never-drop question. <br><br>**Recovery, with the mechanism, its caller, and its POSITION named (`MIS-024` instance 5).** *Mechanism:* the ordinary per-folder sync of the folder the message is actually in re-inserts it under its current Graph id — `SyncEngine.syncFolderMessages` → `runSyncMessages`, plus `exchangeDeltaSync`'s `messagesAdded` → `fetchMessageDetails` for the Inbox-scoped delta. *Caller:* `SyncScheduler`'s periodic pass, foreground refresh, pull-to-refresh, and the post-drain `foldersToSync` loop — note the 404 arm itself does **not** insert into `foldersToSync`, so no targeted sync is scheduled by the failure. *Position:* **strictly AFTER the delete.** It is a repair, not a guard; it does not prevent the loss, it only ends it. **The state where it does not fire is the same date window `IOS-GRAPH-002` names**: `selectStaleHeaders`' `.date` arm and Graph's `receivedDateTime` ordering mean a message older than the destination folder's newest-N window is not re-fetched, so a deleted header for old mail can stay absent until a crawl or a full re-sync reaches its date range. **If this is fixed later, fix `IOS-GRAPH-002` first** — a re-key removes the precondition for path (1) entirely. For path (2) the honest narrowing is that a `404` proves *"no message at this address"*, which on a folder-scoped-id provider is weaker than *"this message is gone"*; gating the header delete on the same evidence class the op retirement uses keeps the two consistent. **Do not close this by deleting the header more eagerly, and do not widen `isConfirmedGoneError` to the string-matching branch** — its own doc says why: *"IMAP error descriptions can be noisy and we never want a false positive to permanently delete user data."* |
| IOS-GRAPH-004 | 🔓 **OPEN** — REGISTRABLE; recoverable; pre-existing; ⚠️ **do NOT "fix" this by reclassifying the 400 — that is MIRROR-IMAGE TRAP 1 from `IOS-GRAPH-002`** | Action queue; Exchange/Outlook; Microsoft Graph; `AuthedHTTP.requestPreservingBadRequestBody`; `ExchangeProvider.patchMessage`; `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage`; `AccountManagerQueue.isPermanentlyInvalidError`; `GmailProvider.isAuthoritativeActionRejection`; `isGraphInvalidIdMalformed`; `ErrorInvalidIdMalformed`; `HTTPError.networkErrorWithBody`; bodyless 400; unclassifiable rejection; lane starvation; `buildLanes` | **A Graph `400` can never be classified, because the body that would classify it is dropped before it reaches the classifier — so an op naming a malformed id retries forever instead of retiring.** Four verified citations, each checked in-tree at HEAD rather than taken on report: (1) **the body-preserving transport exists and names Graph as an intended consumer** — `Shared/HTTP/AuthedHTTP.swift`, `requestPreservingBadRequestBody`, whose doc says it is *"a narrow opt-in for the handful of action-path call sites (Gmail label modify/lookup, **Graph move**) that must structurally classify a `400` error payload rather than guess from the status code alone"*; (2) **only Gmail actually consumes it** — `GmailProvider.swift:1256` (the private wrapper) and `:1462` (the label-lookup site); `ExchangeProvider.patchMessage` (`:1223`) and `ExchangeProvider.moveMessage` (`:1244`) both still call the body-dropping `request`, so every Graph mutation's 400 arrives as a bodyless `HTTPError.networkError(statusCode: 400)`; (3) **the classifier has no Graph arm at all** — `AccountManagerQueue.isPermanentlyInvalidError` is the single expression `GmailProvider.isAuthoritativeActionRejection(error)`, so a Graph 400 is `false` by construction; (4) **`isGraphInvalidIdMalformed` does not exist as code** — the only occurrences in the tree are a comment in `GmailProvider.swift:1333` naming it as *"the reference"* implementation and a line in `PLAN_IOS_REFACTOR_V3/PLAN_V3_S4_tier3.md`. **Consequence:** a Graph mutation rejected with a genuinely permanent `ErrorInvalidIdMalformed` falls through to the generic arm and is retried without a cap, forever, for as long as the address stays malformed. <br><br>**WHY REGISTRABLE AND NOT BLOCKING — the failure is a stall, not a discard, and it is scoped to one message.** Nothing leaves the queue, so no exit is taken and Never Drop User Intention is not violated; the `PendingOperation` stays durable and visible, and the op's own address is repaired by the very sync that re-materialises the message under its current Graph id. **The blast radius is bounded by the lane grouping, and that is the load-bearing fact:** `AccountManagerQueue.buildLanes` forms connected components of `accountId:folderPath:messageId` and `drainPendingQueue` launches **one Task per lane**, each draining its own lane **sequentially** while different lanes run concurrently (`AccountManagerQueue.swift:519`). A permanently-400ing op therefore halts the op chain **for its own message only** — it cannot starve the account, the folder, or any other message's gestures. That is the `IOS-QUEUE-006` shape (the user can see it and re-act), not the `IOS-GRAPH-002` shape (a silent destruction behind a UI that showed success). By the mantra's test, Q1 answers **YES** — a sync pass re-addresses the row and one ordinary gesture completes the work — so this fails closed and is registered rather than mechanised. <br><br>**WHY THE SHIPPED BEHAVIOUR WAS WORSE — HEAD is strictly safer on this axis and must not be reverted toward it.** `v1.6.38`'s `isPermanentlyInvalidError` (`AccountManagerQueue.swift:812` at that tag) returned `true` for `HTTPError.networkError(statusCode: 400)` and for `NSError(domain: "Gmail"\|"Exchange", code: 400)` — i.e. it retired the user's intention on **every** 400, including every transient and unexplained one. That is a never-drop violation and it is exactly the *"a bare status code is not a classification"* defect the current doc comment at `AccountManagerQueue.swift:1500-1524` was written to close. **The cost of the honest classification is a retrying row rather than a silently discarded gesture, and that trade was made deliberately** — `v2final` had a demote lane (`ProviderError.persistentActionFailure`) to park such a chain at the queue tail; v3 has none, so the retry is the price. **Do not restore the status-code arm, and do not add a Graph-specific retry cap — a cap is a discard on a timer.** <br><br>**SHAPE OF A FUTURE FIX, if one is ever wanted.** Route the Graph mutation call sites (`patchMessage`, `moveMessage`, and any sibling action-path request) through `AuthedHTTP.requestPreservingBadRequestBody` so the 400 body survives as `HTTPError.networkErrorWithBody`, then add a Graph arm to `isPermanentlyInvalidError` that mirrors `GmailProvider.isAuthoritativeActionRejection` **in its narrowness**: parse Graph's documented `{ "error": { "code": … } }` object and accept **only** a parsed `ErrorInvalidIdMalformed` (or a comparably deterministic documented rejection) as authoritative. **Everything else — an unparseable body, an empty body, an unrecognised code, any non-400 — stays retryable.** 🚨 **The trap, named because it has already been taken once in this subsystem:** reclassifying a Graph 400 as terminal **without** the body-parse is MIRROR-IMAGE TRAP 1 from `IOS-GRAPH-002` in reverse — there, converting a drop into a retry without re-keying makes a wedge; here, converting a retry into a drop without a parsed reason re-creates the `v1.6.38` never-drop violation. The parse is what makes the retirement *authoritative*, exactly as the re-key is what makes the retry *terminate*. **Attribution: pre-existing, not candidate-introduced** — the body-dropping call sites and the Gmail-only classifier both predate this candidate; what is new is only that the gap has now been enumerated. |

## Mandatory final review guard

For every Inbox or Drafts change, compare the exact candidate against `v1.6.38` (`07a4bb703`), the
v3 base. **Inbox display and sort timestamps must remain server arrival/received time; never
substitute the message-authored `Date` header WHEN THE SERVER SUPPLIED AN ARRIVAL TIME.** The
authored header is the last-resort fallback for a server that returns no `INTERNALDATE`, and for
that case only; it is registered as `IOS-DATE-001`. Every other substitution of the authored header
remains forbidden, and so does widening this exception to any provider that *does* report an
arrival time (Gmail `internalDate`, Graph `receivedDateTime`).

> **Correction (2026-08-04) — recorded rather than silently applied.** This clause previously read
> *"…never substitute the message-authored `Date` header"*, with no exception. **Taken literally,
> the shipped code violated it**: `IMAPProvider` falls back to `info.date` when `info.internalDate`
> is nil, byte-identically to `v1.6.38`. An absolute with an unstated exception is a recorded
> failure class in this repo (`feedback_absolutes_need_the_negative_case`, MIS-019), and this was
> that failure sitting live in the register's own final guard — a guard every Inbox and Drafts
> reviewer is told to check against. **The absolute is not weakened; it is stated with the one
> exception the code actually takes**, so the guard and the code stop contradicting each other and
> a future reviewer is not forced to choose which of the two to believe.

> **Retarget note (2026-08-02).** The reference's version of this guard also required comparing
> against pre-F1 `7b34f9476`. That commit is on the abandoned post-`v1.6.38` line and is not an
> ancestor of v3, so the clause is dropped as unresolvable here — **it is dropped, not weakened**.
> The timestamp half above is load-bearing and is carried verbatim. Where a v3 change ports
> reference behavior, the `REFERENCE:` anchor in its plan row names the exact `v2final` site to
> compare against; that is the replacement for the second comparison target, not an excuse to skip
> one.

## Two census counts, re-derived because the previous ones were scoped by a NOUN (2026-08-04)

Both numbers below replace a claim that was true *for the term someone grepped* and false for the
class (`MIS-007`). Neither is a new defect class; both are the count itself being wrong.

### A. `4a65117d5`'s "EVERY SITE OF THE CLASS" — retracted, TRUE COUNT 15

That commit's body claimed it had fixed **every** site where a `DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()`
gate sits inside a branch CONDITION rather than guarding a log body. **That holds only for the
`[InfiniteScroll]` log tag it enumerated.** Re-derived by STATE — *a debug-flag call appearing in an
`if`/`guard`/`else if` condition alongside at least one other term* — over `TabMail/`, `Shared/` and
`TabMailNotificationService/`: **18 raw hits, of which 3 are corpus contamination and 15 are live
sites.** The 3 excluded: `AIAction.swift` and `AISummary.swift` matched because the comma lives
*inside the print's string literal*, not in the condition, and `SyncEngine.swift:762` is a COMMENT
describing the arm `4a65117d5` already fixed — i.e. **the fix enlarged the corpus it would later be
counted in** (`MIS-008` ×11). The deliberately BROADER predicate — gate anywhere in a branch
condition, compound or not — returns **315**, which is the body-guard population and not this class;
it is recorded so the narrowing is visible rather than assumed.

`isLoggingEnabled()` is `DebugModeManager`'s only gate accessor, so the accessor set is not a further
source of undercount. Predicate: `rg -o 'DebugModeManager\.[a-zA-Z]+'` over every `.swift` returns
exactly three members — `isLoggingEnabled` 353, `shared` 15, `invalidateLoggingCache` 11 — and
neither of the other two gates anything. **353 is as of this commit and drifts as peers land gated
logs; the load-bearing claim is the member SET, not that integer.**

**Of the 15, ZERO are defects on the merits, and that is the finding.** The rule is not "a gate must
never appear in a condition" — it is *the gate must not decide CONTROL FLOW that must run in
production*. All 15 arms are log-only, and in the two cases where the enclosing block also returns
(`ThreadUtils.runFragmentMergeToFixpoint`, `Draft` Strategy-2 refusal) the `return` sits OUTSIDE the
gated `if`, so control flow is identical in both build configurations. `ThreadUtils.swift`'s
`if pass > 1, DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()` — added in range by `6d460aa99` and the specific
site this census was opened to adjudicate — is therefore **correct as written and was left alone**.
So the retraction is of the WORD "every", not of the work: the phrasing asserted a class-wide census
that had not been run, and running it found the class clean.

**Two sites are worth a reader's attention even though neither is a rule-12 violation**, recorded so
the census is accountable rather than merely reassuring:

- `AutoSizingHTMLView`'s `contentSizeObservation` KVO is created only when logging is unlocked. The
  arm ASSIGNS a stored property, so this one really is a gate controlling state — but the observation's
  entire body is a `print`, and the functional zoom KVO beside it is explicitly "always on", so not
  creating it in production is the intent rather than a lost behaviour.
- 🚨 **`InboxView`'s error banner is debug-gated** (`if let error = viewModel.error,
  DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()`), and that is its ONLY render site — `viewModel.error` is
  written at two places in `InboxViewModel` and read at exactly one, this one. **In a production build
  the inbox error banner therefore never appears.** Reported and NOT fixed here: `InboxView.swift` is
  outside this agent's edit scope, and whether user-facing raw error text is *wanted* is a product
  decision, not a mechanical one. Its consequence for the record is in the memory-topic correction
  noted under B.

### B. Ungated diagnostic logs added in `da57fde6f^..6391de9a5` — TRUE COUNT 3

Enumerated by DIFF (`git diff -U0 <range> -- '*.swift'`, `+` lines matching
`(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_.])(print|NSLog|os_log)\(`), then each candidate's gating read from the COMMITTED
blob rather than from the hunk — a `+print(` inside a pre-existing `isLoggingEnabled()` block is
gated and does not count. Range: 14 commits, 49 `.swift` files.

**3 ungated diagnostic logs were ADDED**, all in `AccountManagerActions.swift` (25 `print(` sites
before the range, 28 after): the pre-resolve C3 refusal, the execution-time C3 refusal, and the
per-member undo refusal (`[UndoStack]`). **All three sit on C3 REFUSAL paths**, where a refusal that
leaves no production trace is a genuine observability gap rather than noise — so the correct channel
is the ungated durable one, not a debug gate that would silence them (`Companion/Memory/Current/105`).

Two corrections to the scope this census was opened with, both in the direction of *fewer* sites:

- `AccountManagerActions`' **absence-probe** print is byte-identical before and after the range. It is
  a real pre-existing ungated `print`, but it was **not added here**.
- `ExchangeProvider`'s two sites appear as `+` lines **only because the enclosing block was
  re-indented** when `move` became `moveProvingDestinations`; the text is otherwise byte-identical.
  They were ungated before the range and after it, so **this range added no console noise there**.

  ⚠️ **"Two sites" is the brief's number and it is an UNDERCOUNT — do not reuse it.** It was taken
  from a 23-line window, not from the file. The two named above are merely the two that fell inside
  the window someone happened to be reading. What survives re-derivation is the *conclusion* — this
  commit range added no console noise to that file — not the count, which was never a count of the
  file. Same failure shape as `MIS-007`: a number whose corpus was someone's attention rather than
  the artifact.

  **State of `ExchangeProvider.swift` as of `be3db4785`, re-derived from the tree:** 21 `print(`
  total, **4 gated / 17 ungated**, and 4 `DebugModeManager` references. The 4 gated are the
  `[MoveTrace]` prints (lines 631, 684, 695, 1252), swept by the agent that owns the file. The 17
  ungated pre-existing `[Exchange]` prints are at lines 72, 84, 101, 204, 233, 283, 286, 356, 464,
  645, 859, 955, 982, 1063, 1092, 1121, 1273 — **out of scope, deliberately not swept**, recorded so
  the number survives.

  🚨 **The obvious predicate for this file returns a FALSE answer, so write the right one down.**
  `grep -n 'print(' | grep DebugModeManager` — a SAME-LINE test — reports **0 gated / 21 ungated**,
  because the gate here is a multi-line `if DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled() { print(…) }` block
  and therefore never shares a line with its `print`. The predicate that matches reality is
  block-aware: *for each `print(` line, does a `DebugModeManager` reference appear in the preceding
  ~8 lines?* That yields 4/17 and reproduces the owning agent's line numbers exactly. A same-line
  gate census is only valid for a codebase whose gates are all one-liners; this one's are not.

**Deliberately NOT swept:** the pre-existing ungated prints outside the range — `AccountManagerActions`
alone carries ~25 of them. That is a decision not to widen the fix, not a "pre-existing" excuse; the
count is recorded here so the number is not lost if someone later wants the sweep.

### C. Changed production Swift files in the release range — **110 / 102 / 93**, one number per command (2026-08-05, corrected 2026-08-05)

Three counts have circulated in this train: **110**, **102** and **93**. **All three are real and all
three reproduce exactly.** They are answers to three *different* commands over the same range, and the
only defect was ever quoting a number without the command that produced it (`MIS-007`: a census
inherits the shape of its instrument). Measured at candidate `339bd4ce2` against the release base
`07a4bb703`:

```
# 110 — WIDE. All three production trees; the filter is applied AFTER the diff, so no pathspec
#       globbing is involved at all.
git diff --name-only 07a4bb703..339bd4ce2 \
  | rg -c '^(TabMail|Shared|TabMailNotificationService)/.*\.swift$'
→ 110      # TabMail 93 + Shared 9 + TabMailNotificationService 8

# 102 — THREE-PATHSPEC. This is the form the round-4 brief actually specified.
git diff --name-only 07a4bb703..339bd4ce2 \
  -- 'TabMail/**/*.swift' 'Shared/**/*.swift' 'TabMailNotificationService/**/*.swift' | wc -l
→ 102      # TabMail 93 + Shared 9 + TabMailNotificationService 0   ← note the zero

# 93 — ONE-PATHSPEC. The `TabMail/` tree alone.
git diff --name-only 07a4bb703..339bd4ce2 -- 'TabMail/**/*.swift' | wc -l
→ 93
```

**Use 110 / 102, and 93 if you pass only the `TabMail` pathspec** — and always say which one you mean.

**Why 102 is short by exactly 8, and why the 8 are the NSE files.** The three-pathspec form does pass a
`TabMailNotificationService/` pathspec, so the loss is *not* "the pathspec only covers `TabMail/`". It
is a **directory-depth** artefact of the `**/` segment: `<tree>/**/*.swift` requires a `/` between the
`**` and the `*.swift`, i.e. at least one intervening directory level. Every changed `.swift` file
under `TabMail/` and `Shared/` sits at depth ≥ 2, so both pathspecs match in full (93 and 9). All eight
changed files under `TabMailNotificationService/` sit **directly in that directory** —
`NotificationService.swift`, `NSEStagingDB.swift`, `NSELog.swift`, `NSEIMAPConnection.swift`,
`NSEMessageMetadata.swift`, `GmailNSEClient.swift`, `OutlookNSEClient.swift`, `SharedNSEData.swift` —
so the `**/` never matches and the tree contributes **0**. Confirmed both ways: the single-star form
`-- 'TabMailNotificationService/*.swift'` returns **8**, and the plain directory-prefix pathspec
`-- TabMail Shared TabMailNotificationService` filtered on `\.swift$` returns **110**, agreeing with the
wide form.

⚠️ **Stated negatively:** the 17-file gap (`110 − 93`) is also real, but it belongs to the
*one*-pathspec form and to nothing else. `110 − 102 = 8` and `110 − 93 = 17` are both correct, of
different commands. Neither figure refutes the other.

#### ⚠️ RETRACTED: this section's own previous correction was wrong (recorded, not deleted)

The first version of this section (commit `603cf0da2`) asserted that **"the number 102 does not
reproduce at all"**, that the 102 form "is scoped to the `TabMail/` directory alone", and that **"the
drop is 17 files, not 8"**. **All three claims are withdrawn.** 102 reproduces exactly, under the
three-pathspec form quoted above; the disputed pathspec was not scoped to `TabMail/` alone; and the
drop under that form is 8.

**The diagnosis belongs in the text, because the mechanism is the durable part.** The refutation ran a
**differently shaped command** than the one it was disputing — one pathspec where the brief had
specified three — obtained a different number, and reported the disagreement as evidence that the
*other* number was unreproducible. That is `MIS-007` (a census inherits its search shape) arriving with
`MIS-008`'s consequence: a null-or-mismatched result read as a claim about the world rather than a
claim about the query. It is the **third** self-invalidating correction in this session, after the
`v71` "only whole-database FK gate" comment and the "fabricated quotation" accusation (both recorded in
`MIS-019`).

**The rule this earns: when refuting a count, run THEIR command first, verbatim, and only then your
own.** A correction is the single highest-risk sentence to write, because its author has just proved
someone else wrong and therefore feels least like checking themselves.

#### Retained and re-verified: the 109 → 110 bisect

**109 is a STALE count, correct through `43604ec81` and superseded at `afa5b9e33`.** Re-verified by
bisecting the range under the wide predicate: `07a4bb703..43604ec81` = **109**,
`07a4bb703..afa5b9e33` = **110**, and every commit after it (`9677d36f1`, `ae7dd105d`, `018677428`,
`339bd4ce2`) = 110. The +1 file is `TabMail/Services/MigrationTimingLedger.swift`, added by `afa5b9e33`
("Attribute foreign-key check time to the migration that pays it").

#### Retained and re-verified: the `ae7dd105d` attribution is wrong

⚠️ **The competing attribution of the +1 to `ae7dd105d` / `AutoSizingHTMLView.swift` is WRONG, and the
way it is wrong is worth keeping.** `ae7dd105d` touches exactly one Swift file,
`TabMail/Views/Shared/AutoSizingHTMLView.swift` — but that file was **already inside the range at
`43604ec81`** (re-verified), so modifying it again added nothing to a count of *distinct* changed files.
The instrument that produces this error is "find a commit near the boundary that touched a `.swift`
file"; the instrument that answers the question is "bisect the COUNT". A file's presence in the changed
set is idempotent — only the FIRST commit to touch it can move the number.

## The gate-in-a-BRANCH-CONDITION census over `TabMail/Views/` (2026-08-04)

**This is a different census from the ungated-`print` one above, and the distinction is the point.**
That one asks *"is this diagnostic silenced in production?"*. This one asks *"does a debug flag decide
CONTROL FLOW that must run in production?"* — i.e. whether the gate changes what the **user** sees. A
debug flag genuinely participating in a condition is not automatically wrong; it is wrong only when the
branch it selects is a user-visible surface.

**Predicate, stated beside the number (`MIS-007` instance 11).** A comment-stripped, brace-matching
walker over every `DebugModeManager` reference in `TabMail/Views/`, classifying each by whether it sits
in an `if`/`guard`/`while` **condition** or guards a **body**, and by what the controlled body does.
**Deliberately NOT a same-line predicate** — a multi-line `if DebugModeManager… { print(…) }` never puts
gate and subject on one line, so a same-line test reads every correctly-gated site as *ungated*
(`MIS-007` instance 12, and the `ExchangeProvider` section immediately above).

**Count: 95 code references** (99 raw string hits − 4 doc-comment mentions).

**Cross-check, two-way (`comm` in BOTH directions).** The walker's list and an independent `rg` list
disagreed by exactly 4, all in `AutoSizingHTMLView.swift`. The disagreement was run down rather than
averaged: the delta is precisely the `///`/`//` lines that *mention* `DebugModeManager` in prose, which
the walker strips and `rg` counts. Same corpus-contaminated-by-its-own-prose shape as `MIS-008`
instance 11 — read the hits, do not count them.

**Three shape blind spots were closed by hand, because a walker encodes assumptions invisibly:**

- `} else if DebugModeManager…` arms — 3 sites (`InboxView`, `ProviderSettingsView`, `ComposeView`),
  **all log-only**. Latent per topic 105 §3 (a non-logging statement added to such an arm would silently
  not run in production) but not defects today; deliberately left.
- `guard DebugModeManager… else { return "" }` — 2 sites, both `AutoSizingHTMLView` debug-JS builders
  (`htmlDebugReportJS`, `heightDiagnosticJS`). Correct.
- **A gate hoisted into a local boolean** (`let isDbg = DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled(); if isDbg { … }`)
  hides the branch condition from any walker keyed on the flag itself. Every such boolean in
  `TabMail/Views/` was enumerated with its uses; all gate only `print` statements. The
  `AutoSizingHTMLView` ones (`log`, `logEnabled`, `ll`, `dl`, `logBody`, `il`, `gl`, `logFn`, `debug`,
  `idStampJS`) have **no branch-condition uses at all** — they are passed into JS-builder closures.

**Result: exactly ONE site where a debug flag in a branch condition suppressed a surface the user must
see in production** — `InboxView`'s error banner, fixed in this commit. One further site changes what
the user sees and is **CORRECT**: `MailNavigationView`'s Debug-menu `NavigationLink` under
`DebugModeManager.shared.isUnlocked` — the debug menu is *defined* by debug mode. Everything else is
log-only. **Nothing here licenses a repo-wide gating sweep**; the count is recorded so it is not
re-derived, per the range-not-corpus rule settled by `af98d92c7`.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-SCROLL-003 | 🔓 **OPEN** — **ADJUDICATED 2026-08-04**, re-derived from the tree rather than folded on the reporting agent's word (`MIS-010`): pre-existing and byte-identical in shipped `07a4bb703` and in `v2final` `e28dd4edb`, so **A1 = NONEXISTENT**; **NOT candidate-introduced**; fails closed leaving **ZERO state residue**; recoverable by one ordinary gesture — but the frequency is **NOT rare**, and this row says so instead of calling it an edge | Infinite scroll; `InboxViewModel.loadMoreMessages` phase 2; `SyncEngine.isConnectionError`; `InboxViewModel.error`; `InboxErrorBanner`; `AccountManagerState.lastSyncFailed`; `SyncStatusSubtitle`; `BackgroundSyncLogger.logError` source `infiniteScroll`; connection error; airplane mode; offline pagination; silent no-op; `hasMoreMessages` untouched on throw; sentinel stays armed | Both `InboxViewModel.error` write sites are wrapped in `if !SyncEngine.isConnectionError(error)`. At the **`performSync`** catch that carve-out is covered by a second surface: `AccountManagerState.shared.lastSyncFailed = true` is set **unconditionally** in the same catch and drives the sync-status subtitle, so a connection failure is still reported. At the **infinite-scroll** catch there is **no such fallback** — no `lastSyncFailed`, no other surface — so a *connection* failure during pagination stays completely silent even now that the banner renders in production: the list stops growing and nothing says why. This is exactly the case companion topic 105's 2026-08-04 correction predicted (*"the guard suppresses that surface for connection errors, which is exactly when infinite scroll fails"*). **(b) Reachability and FREQUENCY — stated first, because the mantra requires the frequency question BEFORE the recoverability test and "an edge that is actually the common path is not an edge".** Phase 2 (`manager.fetchOlderMessages`) runs only once the phase-1 local GRDB page comes back empty — i.e. for any user who scrolls past their cached window, which is routine, not exotic. `SyncEngine.isConnectionError` then covers **every** `URLError` (airplane mode, DNS failure, "connection appears to be offline"), both raw NIO domains (`NIOCore.ChannelError`, `NIOCore.IOError`) and a substring list including `"timed out"` — so the suppressed case is ordinary offline / flaky-network paging. **This row therefore does NOT claim the case is rare; it claims it is recoverable and non-destructive, which is the operative test.** **(b2) No third surface — census with its predicate.** `grep -rn 'lastSyncFailed *=' TabMail/ Shared/` returns **SEVEN lines**, of which exactly **FOUR are assignment sites** — `InboxViewModel.performSync` (both polarities) plus `SyncScheduler`'s delta-sync completion and its foreground `poll()` (`result.failed`). ⚠️ **The other three are not writers and the earlier wording of this row wrongly implied the raw grep returned four** (`MIS-007`/`MIS-008` — a count is a claim about what the PATTERN can match, and `lastSyncFailed *=` matches the text, not the assignment): they are the property DECLARATION `var lastSyncFailed = false {` in `AccountManagerState.swift` and **two string-literal mentions** inside that property's `didSet` — a `print("[SyncPhaseMut] lastSyncFailed = …")` and a `BootProfiler.mark("'Last updated' subtitle: lastSyncFailed = …")`. The load-bearing claim is about **writers**, so re-derive it as *assignment sites outside the declaration*, not as a line count. **None of the four is on the pagination path.** The only durable channel that path has at all is `BackgroundSyncLogger.logError(…, source: "infiniteScroll")` inside `SyncEngine.fetchOlderMessages` — and it sits on the `isSelectFailedError` arm, which `continue`s to the next folder and never rethrows, so it is not reached by the errors that arrive at *this* catch; it is a developer channel (`error.log`, exported by `DebugLogView`'s "Error Logs") rather than a user surface in any case. **(c) Attribution — PRE-EXISTING; A1 verdict NONEXISTENT.** `git show 07a4bb703:TabMail/ViewModels/InboxViewModel.swift` and `git show e28dd4edb:…` both carry this catch verbatim — same `print`, same guard, same absent `lastSyncFailed` — beside a `performSync` that *does* have the unconditional write. **The shipped release never surfaced a pagination connection failure, so there is no behaviour to RESTORE and any fix would be authored work** (A1 corollary 3 — the release is a floor, not a ceiling; but it is also empirical evidence that this particular silence was tolerable to real users across the whole `v1.6.38` line). What `3573574ed` changed is the *other* half: before it, `InboxViewModel.error` had no production render site at all, so BOTH catches were silent for EVERY error; now non-connection failures at both sites render. The connection half is exactly the shipped baseline. **(d) Recovery — proved from the state, not asserted.** `hasMoreMessages = pull.mayHaveMore` is the last statement of the `do` block, and the appends to `loadedMessages` / `loadedIds` / `targetWindowSize` live inside the `pull.inserted > 0` branch, so a throw skips all of them; `isLoadingOlder` is cleared by `defer`. **The view-model state after a failed page is identical to the state before it — the failure leaves ZERO residue**, which is exactly what separates this row from `IOS-SCROLL-001` / `IOS-SCROLL-002`, where `hasMoreMessages` is written FALSE and the scroller genuinely stops. The sentinel therefore stays armed: `InboxView` renders `if viewModel.hasMoreMessages { … Color.clear.frame(height: 1).onAppear { viewModel.loadMoreMessages() } }`, so any scroll that brings that 1-pt row back into view re-attempts the page; independently, both list paths carry `.refreshable { await viewModel.refreshSync() }` → `performSync` → `reloadMessages()`, which recomputes `hasMoreMessages` from a fresh query. ⚠️ **Stated negatively (`MIS-019`):** this row does NOT assert that the `isLoadingOlder`→`false` rebuild *alone* re-fires `onAppear` — that is a SwiftUI structural-identity question nothing here proves, and the recovery claim deliberately rests only on the ordinary gesture, which is checkable. **(e) Accepted cost, and why the obvious fix is not obviously correct.** Recoverable ⇒ fail closed and let it be. The mantra's non-recoverable set is closed and exhaustive — C3 wrong-message mutation or misattribution, a dropped user intention including the wedge corollary, a brick, secret exposure — and this is a READ path that queues, mutates, sends and deletes nothing, so the not-rare frequency finding above does not promote it into that set. **Neither one-line fix is right.** Mirroring `performSync` (`lastSyncFailed = true` here) re-labels a *pagination* failure as a *sync* failure in `SyncStatusSubtitle` ("Last updated" vs "Updated") — a field whose three other writers are all whole-pass sync outcomes — and nothing on this path ever clears it, so it would stay set until the next successful sync or poll. Deleting the guard puts raw transport text (`NIOCore.ChannelError`, `"timed out"`) in front of the user on every connectivity blip, which is precisely what the carve-out exists to keep off-screen. A correct fix needs a THIRD signal, and authoring one at the close of the refactor is exactly the mechanism the mantra says not to build. **If it is ever revisited**, consider a scroll-local, non-sticky indicator owned by the sentinel row — which already knows a page was attempted and did not arrive — rather than a new write to a global sync flag. **No test blesses the current behaviour** (`MIS-014`): every `loadMoreMessages` test in `TabMailTests` exercises phase-1 local paging, dedup, `excludeIds` ordering or the diff rebuild, and none asserts the catch's suppression. |

## The ungated diagnostic-`print` corpus in `TabMail/Views/` — CLOSED DECISION (2026-08-04)

**This is a decision, not a backlog item.** It is recorded so the corpus is not re-censused every
round, and so the next reader can reproduce the numbers against a tree that has moved. It supersedes
the *sampled* count it was opened with. The range-not-corpus rule settled by `af98d92c7` still governs
what actually gets swept.

**PREDICATE — stated beside every number, because a count taken from a window is a count of the
window (`MIS-007` ×12).** Corpus: every `*.swift` under `TabMail/Views/` at the named rev (127 files
on the v3 line, 128 on `v2final`). A *print* is a match of `(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_.])print\(` in
**comment-stripped** source with string literals skipped. A print is **GATED** iff **(a)** any
**enclosing** brace-frame header contains `DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled`, **or (b)** an
`#if DEBUG` is open at that point, **or (c)** an enclosing frame header references a same-file hoisted
local declared `let X = DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()`. Frames are tracked by a character
scanner, so a multi-line header is matched whole and a `{` inside a string or a comment does not move
the depth.

**Two independently shaped implementations were run and agree EXACTLY — per file and per line.** The
second differs from the first only in handling Swift `"""` multi-line strings, which matters because
`AutoSizingHTMLView.swift` alone carries 44 `"""` delimiters. Agreement was then checked in **both**
directions against a differently-shaped heuristic (`MIS-007` instance 12: a one-way check accepts a
subset as agreement), and the residual was run down by hand rather than averaged — every ungated site
with a `DebugModeManager` reference in the 8 preceding lines (**10** of them) was read individually
and is genuinely ungated, because the nearby gate is a **sibling** block and not an enclosing frame;
every gated site with no `DebugModeManager` within 40 preceding lines (**5** of them, all
`EmailHTMLWrapper`) is genuinely gated via a hoisted local declared far above.

| Rev | GATED | UNGATED | prints total |
|---|---|---|---|
| shipped `07a4bb703` (`v1.6.38`) | 82 | **183** | 265 |
| `v2final` `e28dd4edb` (never shipped; 128 files) | 101 | 173 | 274 |
| `3573574ed` (the banner fix) | 94 | 171 | 265 |
| **HEAD `ad9d9047d`** | **96** | **169** (28 files of 127) | 265 |

**⚠️ Three numbers this census was opened with do NOT reproduce, and the delta is explained rather
than averaged (`MIS-007` instance 8 — when two predicates for the same question disagree, the
disagreement IS the finding, and you never take the bigger number because it feels safer).** The
opening numbers were `GATED=98 / UNGATED=167`, a 6-file sample of `55 → 51` ungated,
`ComposeView.swift 15 → 11`, and `SearchView.swift 2 → 2`.

- **`98 / 167` was measured one commit early.** At `3573574ed` this predicate gives `94 / 171`;
  `ad9d9047d` then gated the two `SearchView` remote-search prints, giving `96 / 169`. So
  `SearchView.swift` is `2 → 2` only *up to* `3573574ed` and is **0 at HEAD** — `ad9d9047d` is the
  commit that gated them.
- **The remaining 4-site gap is entirely `ComposeView.swift`, and it is an overcount of *gated*.** The
  opening numbers put ComposeView at `15 → 11` ungated; this predicate gives `19 → 15`, four higher at
  both endpoints — exactly `94 + 4 = 98` and `171 − 4 = 167`. **All 15 HEAD sites were read
  individually** and none has an enclosing gate: the `[Compose] Recompute…` trio, the `applyInlineEdit`
  phase logs that sit *beside* gated siblings, the `loadDraftOrPrepopulate` / `Found draft` /
  `Loaded draft` / `No draft found` group, and the three `[ComposeForward]` sites. Both censuses agree
  the corpus total is **265**, so this is a classification difference and never a detection one.
- **The sampled 6-file `55 → 51` is superseded by the whole-corpus `183 → 169`.** The direction and the
  conclusion are unchanged and now stronger; only the sample is retired.

**How many of the 169 carry user content — and the predicate for THAT number too.** The opening figure
was **41 of 167 by MANUAL classification** (`account.emailAddress`, `draft.subject`, reminder/task KB
statements, AIProbe keys). That is a judgement over the set and is **not mechanically reproducible**,
so it is recorded here as a manual number rather than restated as a measurement. A deliberately broad
mechanical proxy over the same set — a case-insensitive token match
(`emailAddress|\.subject|filename|statement|senderEmail|recipient|body|snippet|draft\.|account\.|title|query|AIProbe|prompt|kb|reminder|task`)
against each print's statement text — returns **44 of 169**, and **132 of 169 interpolate anything at
all**. The two corroborate each other's order of magnitude; neither is the other's proof.

**Three predicate faults, recorded because they are the reusable part.** Each was hit while building
this census, each is still reproducible in the tree, and each yields a *plausible, populated, WRONG*
answer rather than an empty one — which is exactly what invites belief (`MIS-008` instance 6).

1. **`print(` matches as a substring of an identifier.** `attachmentsFingerprint(` in
   `ComposeView.swift` scores as three ungated prints under a bare `print\(`. The word boundary is
   mandatory: `(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_.])print\(`.
2. **A hoisted gate local defeats any literal-text predicate.** `EmailHTMLWrapper.swift` writes its
   gate as `let isDbg = DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled()` and then `if isDbg { print(…) }` — **9**
   such one-liners today (`grep -c 'if isDbg { print('`), in a file this predicate scores **15 gated /
   0 ungated**. A predicate keyed on the flag's own spelling reads every one of them as *ungated* and
   would send someone to "fix" correctly-gated code.
3. **An immediately-enclosing-block walker is not chain-aware.** `SearchView.swift`'s
   `print("[Search]   noHeader sample FTS headerIds: …")` sits inside `if !noHeaderSamples.isEmpty {`,
   which is itself inside the `if DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled() {` opened two lines earlier.
   Only the *chain* answers the question; the innermost frame never does.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-LOG-001 | 🔓 **OPEN by decision — CLOSED as a question (2026-08-04)**; pre-existing and **REDUCED** by v3 (183 → 169 ungated over the same corpus); no data risk, no user-visible defect, no coupling to any other work; **this row is the record that the sweep is deliberately not opened**, not a to-do | Diagnostic logging; global `CLAUDE.md` rule 12; ungated `print`; `DebugModeManager.isLoggingEnabled`; `TabMail/Views/`; `stdout` discarded on device; Console.app; sysdiagnose; ADR-004 zero retention; `af98d92c7` range-not-corpus precedent; C3 refusal paths; minimal diffs; scope creep; `Companion/Memory/Current/105` | **(a) Defect.** **169 ungated diagnostic `print` sites across 28 of the 127 files in `TabMail/Views/`** at HEAD `ad9d9047d` — predicate and per-rev table immediately above — of which a manually classified **41** (broad mechanical proxy: 44) interpolate plausible USER CONTENT: `account.emailAddress`, `draft.subject`, reminder/task KB statements, AIProbe keys. They are compiled into Release builds and they execute there. **(b) Reachability.** A `print` on iOS writes to `stdout`, and on a device `stdout` is **discarded** unless a debugger or Console is attached — nothing in this tree redirects it (`Companion/Memory/Current/105` §1; ⚠️ re-verify that claim by READING the `freopen`/`dup2` hits, which are that topic's own explanatory comments and not code, per its own self-matching warning). So the whole exposure is: an attached Xcode / Console.app session on a connected device, or a `sysdiagnose` the user deliberately captures and hands to someone. **The app persists none of it** — no file, no server, no crash payload — so **ADR-004 zero-retention is NOT violated**: nothing leaves the device and nothing is written on it. **(c) Attribution — PRE-EXISTING, and this line IMPROVED it.** Same predicate, whole corpus: shipped `07a4bb703` = **183** ungated, `v2final` `e28dd4edb` = 173, HEAD = **169**. Not refactor-attributable and not candidate-attributable to any commit in this range; the range's own *added* diagnostics were censused separately and separately adjudicated (see `af98d92c7`, TRUE COUNT 3). **(d) Recovery.** No data risk, no user-visible defect, no migration, no schema, no protocol, no ordering constraint. The logs are ephemeral by construction (b), and any individual site can be gated later by a one-line change with zero coupling to anything else. **Nothing degrades while this stays open** — that is the property that makes deferring it free rather than merely cheap, and it is why this is a decision rather than a deferral. **(e) Accepted cost.** A 169-site sweep across 28 files at the close of the refactor is a large, entirely mechanical, wholly unreviewed diff that would need its own audit round — against the minimal-diffs rule and against the owner's explicit instruction not to let scope creep restart the cycle. The in-tree precedent already settled the scope: `af98d92c7` scoped this obligation to the **RANGE** (*"Ungated diagnostic logs added in `da57fde6f^..6391de9a5` — TRUE COUNT 3"*), and those 3 were adjudicated **correct as ungated** because they sit on **C3 refusal paths**, where a refusal that leaves no production trace is a genuine observability gap rather than noise. That precedent is the reason this row is a closed decision: a **new** ungated diagnostic inside a range is still a defect and is still swept — `ad9d9047d` gating the two `SearchView` prints that interpolated the account address is this same round doing exactly that — while the pre-existing corpus is not. ⚠️ **Stated negatively, because the absolute would otherwise be far too wide (`MIS-019`).** This row does **not** license adding ungated prints; does **not** cover any log that reaches a durable or network channel (`BackgroundSyncLogger`, `NSELogStore`, a crash breadcrumb, a worker `logger.*`); and does **not** cover a site whose text could carry a SECRET rather than user content — a secret in a log is the PRIME DIRECTIVE, not this row. It covers exactly the pre-existing, `stdout`-only, `TabMail/Views/` diagnostic corpus enumerated above. **If it is ever swept**, do it as its own commit against a **re-derived** count with the predicate restated beside it, and expect the number to have moved. |

## Final audit train — the fix round's own registrable remainder (2026-08-05)

Two items the final audit train adjudicated as **decisions**, recorded because neither had a durable
artifact before this round. `IOS-LOG-002` is the **counterpart** to `IOS-LOG-001`, which explicitly
disclaims this channel (*"does not cover any log that reaches a durable or network channel"*), so the
class it covers was invisible to anyone searching the register.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-TEST-010 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-05)** — harness-side residual; **NO production change**, and the reason production must NOT change is the substance of this row | Tests; `AccountManager.registerGestureIntent`; `enqueueWrite`; `drainWriteQueue()` FIFO barrier; unstructured `Task`; optimistic UI; gesture→durable-write window; `InboxGestureActionTests`; `SearchLocalResultIdentityTests`; `SearchRemoteResultIdentityTests`; `CoordinatedToolActionTests` | **`drainWriteQueue()` is a barrier for the QUEUE, not a precondition for a `registerGestureIntent` gesture, and its doc comment says the stronger thing.** `registerGestureIntent` is `nonisolated` and synchronous and ends by spawning `Task { await self.enqueueWrite { await self.executeIntentCycle(id: id) } }`; the test barrier is itself `Task { await AccountManager.shared.enqueueWrite { cont.resume() } }`. **Two unstructured tasks then race to the actor**, so the barrier can be appended FIRST and resume on an empty queue while the gesture's own write is still un-enqueued. The helper's doc — *"every write enqueued before this call has drained"* — is TRUE of the queue and FALSE of the precondition a caller reads it as. **Census, with the predicate:** `rg -n 'await drainWriteQueue\(\)' -g '*.swift' .` returns **39** call sites across four suites (`InboxGestureActionTests`, `SearchLocalResultIdentityTests`, `SearchRemoteResultIdentityTests`, `CoordinatedToolActionTests`); `rg -n 'drainWriteQueue'` returns 47, the difference being the **4** definitions and **4** prose mentions — count the `await` form, not the bare symbol. The EXPOSED subset is narrower still: only those asserting durable state immediately after a `registerGestureIntent`-shaped gesture. **Why production is NOT changed.** Making `registerGestureIntent` enqueue synchronously means either converting the actor-isolated `writeQueue` to a nonisolated `Mutex` or making the method `async` — but it is deliberately `nonisolated` and synchronous precisely so MainActor gesture paths need no suspension, which is what makes the swipe feel instant (ADR-IOS-001). **The gesture→durable-write window is inherent to optimistic UI, not a defect in it**, and closing it in the product to make a test helper's doc comment true is the tail wagging the dog. **Recoverability:** nothing user-facing is at stake at all — this is a test-timing residual, not a product path; the durable write still lands, just not necessarily before an unrelated barrier returns. **The sanctioned harness-side strengthening, if a suite ever proves flaky on this:** wait on a **monotone, gesture-owned** condition BEFORE the barrier — `AccountManager.shared.overlayOpRefCountForTesting()[id] == nil` or `pendingIntentCyclesForTesting()[id] == nil` — then take the FIFO barrier as today. Do NOT add a sleep, a retry, or a loosened assertion. **This was the next lever after the suite-trait fix that closed task #91** — `InboxViewModelInsertUndoneTests` was declared `.serialized` but not `.processGlobalState`, so it swapped `AppDatabase.shared` outside the cross-suite lock and a victim suite's `markRead` could `UPDATE` the wrong fixture; the trait was added in this same round. **This lever was deliberately NOT pulled pre-emptively**, because that flake was proven to be the missing trait and not this window. |
| IOS-LOG-002 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-05)** — the **persisted/exported** counterpart to `IOS-LOG-001`'s `stdout`-only scope; the message-content half WAS fixed and is named so this row is not read as covering it | Diagnostic logging; **always-on persisted channels**; `NSELog.step`/`NSELog.error`; `os_log(privacy: .public)`; unified log; sysdiagnose; `BackgroundSyncLogger`; `error.log`; `DebugLogView` share buttons; account email address; user-authored folder names; global `CLAUDE.md` rule 12; `Companion/Memory/Current/105`; `c44789559` | **(a) Defect.** Two always-on channels persist diagnostics that interpolate user-derived strings, and neither is covered by `IOS-LOG-001` (whose scope is expressly *"the pre-existing, `stdout`-only, `TabMail/Views/` diagnostic corpus"*). **Channel 1 — `NSELog`:** `step`/`error` both fire `os_log(.error, privacy: .public)`, which iOS PERSISTS in the unified log and hands verbatim to a `sysdiagnose`. **Channel 2 — `BackgroundSyncLogger`:** ungated at the write, appended to a file in the app container, and **exported by `DebugLogView`'s share buttons**, i.e. it leaves the device when the user shares it. **(b) The census, by CHANNEL and then by dataflow rather than by keyword** (`MIS-007` instance 18 — a keyword predicate at a SINK is blind to content arriving through an ALIAS assigned in another file, which is exactly how the title sites were missed the first time). **Class A — message content, ✅ FIXED, not deferred:** `c44789559` shaped the step4 sender/subject and step6a AI-blurb lines; this round shaped the remaining **three**, all of which logged `UNMutableNotificationContent.title`, which `EmailNotificationBuilder.fill` sets to `"New email - <senderName>"` — `deliverOnce`, `serviceExtensionTimeWillExpire` and `deliverPassive`. **Class A is now EXHAUSTIVE for message content:** `fill` also sets `c.subtitle = s.subject` and `c.body = reminderContent ?? summaryBlurb ?? ""`, and **no site logs `.subtitle` or `.body`**. A correspondent's name is a THIRD PARTY's PII — someone who never opted into anything — which is why that half was fixed rather than registered. **Class B — the account holder's OWN address, DEFERRED (this row):** three unified-log sites (`NotificationService.swift`, `NSE process: … email=`, `NSE FAIL: no accountId for`, `NSE imap_reconnect: no accountId for`) plus the `BackgroundSyncLogger` corpus — `rg -o 'BackgroundSyncLogger\.[a-zA-Z]+\(' TabMail/ Shared/ TabMailNotificationService/` = **412** call sites, of which **51** interpolate `emailAddress`/`accountEmail` on the call line (a LINE-oriented proxy; a block-aware count will differ, per `MIS-008` instance 12 — restate the predicate if you re-derive it). **Class C — user-authored folder names, DEFERRED (this row):** `NSEIMAPConnection`'s `NSE IMAP SELECT \(folderPath) failed` plus **12** `logBackfill`/`BackgroundSyncLogger` lines interpolating `folder.name`/`folderName`, same proxy caveat. **(c) Why classes B and C are NOT swept — a genuinely two-sided trade, which is the whole reason this is a decision and not a to-do.** `NSELog`'s own documentation states that firing at `.error` is the established field-visibility trick because the NSE is otherwise invisible in the field. Redacting `accountEmail` from `NSE process:` removes **the only account attribution available BEFORE `accountId` resolves** — and the very next failure line is literally `no accountId for …`, i.e. the state in which nothing else identifies the account. Redacting it from `BackgroundSyncLogger` makes multi-account sync / backfill / push triage impossible on the primary field-debug artifact. Concrete legitimate work would be lost, so this is the chosen side of a two-sided trade, not an unclosed hole. Class A had no such counterweight — a length still tells you a title reached the banner, which is the entire field question — which is why the two halves are dispositioned differently. **(d) Exposure, stated honestly.** The account holder's own address on their own device's logs is a materially weaker exposure than a correspondent's name: the unified log requires an attached Console session or a `sysdiagnose` the user deliberately captures, and `error.log` requires the user to press Share. **ADR-004 zero-retention is not violated** — nothing is transmitted, and the file channel is the user's own container under their own control. **(e) Recoverability / cost of leaving it.** Nothing degrades while this is open, and any individual site is a one-line change with zero coupling. ⚠️ **Stated negatively (`MIS-019`):** this row does **not** license adding NEW user-content interpolations to either channel — a new one inside a range is still a defect and is still swept; it does **not** cover message content (class A is fixed, and the three title sites are named above so this row is not misread as deferring them); it does **not** cover anything that could carry a SECRET, which is the PRIME DIRECTIVE and not this row; and it does **not** license gating `NSELog.step` itself, which is deliberately always-on and load-bearing. |

## Fix round 3 — refusals recorded, and the two remainders the round's own fixes left (2026-08-05)

Four rows. **Two are refutations** of P0 findings filed by the external reviewer: they are recorded
here rather than argued again because nothing in the tree recorded the earlier refusal, and one of
them (`IOS-DRAFT-013`) was consequently **re-filed verbatim in a later round**. A refutation with no
durable artifact is a finding that will be paid for once per round, forever. **Two are the
registrable remainders** of this round's own fixes, stated with their reachability rather than
claimed closed.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-NSE-007 | ✅ **NOT A DEFECT (2026-08-05)** — raised at **P0** by the external reviewer (round 3, `A1-R3-002`); the mechanism it names **does not exist in this tree**; re-verified against source before this row was written | NSE; notifications; RFC 822 `SEARCH`; `EmailNotificationBuilder.fill`; `c.userInfo["messageId"]`; `NotificationService.swift`; `NotificationActionRouter.execute(actionId:messageId:accountId:)`; `resolveAfterMerge`; `ExpectedMessageIdentity`; C3; notification mutation authority | **The claim:** *"the NSE converts an RFC 822 `SEARCH` result into notification mutation authority"*, evidenced by `EmailNotificationBuilder.fill` "still exporting the chosen UID" via `info["messageId"] = messageId`. **Why it is not a defect: the cited symbol does not exist.** There is no `EmailNotificationBuilder.swift` anywhere in `TabMailNotificationService/`; the symbol is shipped-era. What is actually there, verified by census: (1) the **only** write to `c.userInfo` in the current NSE is in `NotificationService.swift`, and it sets `info["reconnect_state"] = "ok"` — nothing else; (2) the delivered notification's `messageId` is passed through **unchanged from the push payload** (`request.content.userInfo["messageId"]`, the worker-supplied id), never the NSE's `SEARCH` result, which feeds `fetchMessageInfo` for **content staging** only; (3) the consumer, `NotificationActionRouter.execute(actionId:messageId:accountId:)`, resolves through `resolveAfterMerge(messageId:accountId:)` rather than an RFC `SEARCH`, and its archive/delete arms carry `expectedIdentities: ExpectedMessageIdentity.map([header])` as a C3 content witness. **Do not re-raise without first greping the symbol** — this is the `MIS-024` family one step further along (*zero callers* → *zero existence*), recorded there as instance 8. |
| IOS-DRAFT-013 | ✅ **NOT A DEFECT (2026-08-05)** — raised at **P0** by the external reviewer in round 3 (`A2-01`) as a **verbatim repeat** of round 2's `F3`, which had already been adjudicated and refused; it was re-filed only because nothing in the tree recorded the refusal | IMAP drafts; `IMAPProvider.saveDraft`; `deleteDraftStrong`; `UID EXPUNGE`; `expungeScopedToTargets`; UIDVALIDITY assertion; `withActionConnectionSelection`; `selectMailboxTracked`; mailbox generation; RFC 3501 §2.3.1.1; `requireUidValidity`; `fetchMessageInfosBulk`; A5 move-path assertion | **The claim:** `saveDraft` / `deleteDraftStrong` can issue a `UID EXPUNGE` after their last UIDVALIDITY assertion, so a turnover in that window would destroy the wrong draft. **Four independent grounds it is refused on, all re-verified against source:** (1) both draft-destruction blocks run inside `withActionConnectionSelection`, which performs exactly **one** `selectMailboxTracked` before `body`, and the call-site census shows no re-`SELECT` inside either block — there is no point at which a new mailbox generation could be observed; (2) **RFC 3501 §2.3.1.1 forbids UIDVALIDITY changing during a session** — a turnover is observable only across a `SELECT`, so within one selected session the UID→message mapping is stable by protocol; (3) pre-body and post-body `generation == acquiredGeneration` guards bracket `body`, so a reconnect throws a retryable `ProviderError.notConnected` rather than continuing on a new socket; (4) both guards fail **safe** — a withheld live epoch of `0` against a recorded `> 0` makes `requireUidValidity` false, and **the branch that is skipped is the destructive one** — and independently `fetchMessageInfosBulk` re-proves the target exists immediately before `STORE \Deleted`. **The move path's A5 assertion is not a precedent for adding one here:** it exists precisely because the move path **does** re-`SELECT` (source → destination → source). The draft paths do not. The absence is structural, not an omission. |
| IOS-DRAFT-014 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-05)** — a **pre-upgrade population**, deliberately not backfilled; recoverability stated per `MIS-IOS-008` including the case that does **not** recover | Drafts; compose; `Draft.instanceEpoch`; `v76_addDraftGenerationAndTypedIdentity`; no backfill; `LocallyAuthoredDraftOpenAuthority.resolve`; generation CAS; `DraftStore.admitSave`; single-writer column; upgrade from `v1.6.38` | **What the user observes:** after upgrading from `v1.6.38`, a draft that already existed before the upgrade cannot be reopened by tapping it in the Drafts folder — the card says *"No editable copy of this draft was found on this device."* **Why:** `v76_addDraftGenerationAndTypedIdentity` adds `Draft.instanceEpoch` with **no backfill**, and every reopen path's first guard is `guard let instanceEpoch = draft.instanceEpoch, !instanceEpoch.isEmpty`. **Why it is not backfilled:** `instanceEpoch` is a **single-writer CAS guard** (`DraftStore.admitSave`'s observed-predecessor compare-and-swap). Fabricating a value adds a second writer to the exact column whose safety property is that only the admission path writes it — the `feedback_single_writer_column_is_a_guard` failure this repo has already been bitten by. Admitting these rows any other way would mean weakening the CAS, which is a strictly worse trade than the refusal. **Recoverability, both directions stated:** a **reply or forward** draft recovers in ONE ordinary gesture — reopen the parent message and tap Reply/Forward, because `Draft.draftKey(replyTo:isForward:newId:)` is deterministic and the compose admission re-stamps the generation on its first save. A **new-compose** draft (`new:<UUID>`) does **NOT** recover: the UUID has no other handle in the UI, and `Draft` rows have zero sync-engine construction sites, so no sync pass rebuilds it. The population is bounded (it is exactly the drafts open at upgrade time) and shrinks to zero as each one is re-saved. |
| IOS-DRAFT-012 | 🔓 **OPEN** — accepted irreducible remainder of the round-3 draft-eviction fix; recoverable only in the sense that it is a **narrower** window than the one it replaces, so it is stated rather than claimed closed | Drafts; compose; background maintenance; `DraftStore.evictImpl`; `DraftSessionRegistry.register`; `registrationGeneration()`; rollback; `ComposeView` register-before-load; authored bytes; attachment directory | **What can still be lost:** a `DraftSessionRegistry.register` that lands **between** `evictImpl`'s final generation read (the last statement inside its write transaction) and the transaction's own commit is still invisible to the sweep, so that compose's draft row, its authored compose chat turns and its attachment directory are deleted. **Why it is not closed:** closing it requires holding a lock across a DB write, which the resilience rules forbid, or a durable table/column, which the owner's standing cheap-migrations requirement forbids. **Reachability, stated honestly:** it requires (a) more drafts than the eviction cap, (b) the user opening a compose for a draft that is beyond the cap, and (c) that open landing inside the commit instant itself — microseconds of straight-line code, not the whole sweep. **What the fix did change:** the exposure was `[pre-transaction snapshot .. commit]` before round 2, `[this row's own live check .. commit]` after round 2, and is now `[final generation read .. commit]`. The justification previously written at the site — *"the compose holds the draft in memory and its next save re-inserts the row"* — was **FALSE and is retracted**: `ComposeView` calls `register(draftId)` **before** its asynchronous `loadDraftOrPrepopulate()`, so the compose that loses the race is one that is still OPENING and holds nothing to re-save. |

## Migration-performance pass — the free-space requirement, and the drop-and-re-derive lever REFUSED (2026-08-05)

Two rows from the measurement pass that motivated running 14 of the 16 `v68…v83` migrations with
`foreignKeyChecks: .immediate`. The first is a **device precondition** nothing in the tree recorded.
The second is the **owner's own proposed lever, measured and refused** — recorded here because a
lever that is refused without a durable artifact gets re-proposed every time migrations are slow.

| ID | Status | Subsystem / keywords | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| IOS-MIGRATION-003 | 🔓 **OPEN by decision (2026-08-05)** — a **transient headroom precondition**, not a defect in either migration; recorded because a device that cannot satisfy it hits the documented "Updating…" boot-hang shape and nothing said so | Migrations; `v81_addActionTagSetAt`; `v82_accountScopedUserLabelIdentity`; WAL; free disk space; single transaction; `AppDatabase.swift`; profile H; boot hang; `StorageEstimator`; `runPruneIfOverBudget` | ⚠️ **RENUMBERED 2026-08-05 from `IOS-MIGRATION-001`, which was in use for TWO unrelated subjects** — the other `IOS-MIGRATION-001` row (the `registerMigration` → `registerTimedMigration` adjudication, ✅ NOT A DEFECT) keeps the original id; this headroom row takes `IOS-MIGRATION-003`. `AppDatabase.swift`'s two in-code pointers were updated in the same commit. ~~**The requirement:** `v81` and `v82` each need roughly **300 MB of free space** at profile H (500k headers / 1M label associations / 3.4 GB database) — WAL pages held until their single transaction commits.~~ ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-05 — `v81` NO LONGER CARRIES THIS REQUIREMENT AND `v82` IS NOW THE ONLY BODY THAT DOES.** `v81`'s `UPDATE messageHeader SET actionTagSetAt = … WHERE actionTag IS NOT NULL` was removed the same day (harness-measured at profile H: the `UPDATE` alone took **1,320.8 ms**, and removing it dropped the migration's peak file+WAL+shm from **3,697.7 MB to 3,388.1 MB** — i.e. it WAS the whole **309.6 MB** requirement for this body; the legacy-row fail-safe in `sweepStaleActionTags` already handles an unstamped tag); `v81` is now `ADD COLUMN` only, with negligible WAL. **The row stays OPEN because `v82`'s half is unavoidable** — it is a genuine table rebuild, not an incidental full-table pass — so the ~300 MB precondition, its boot-hang reachability, and its one-gesture recovery all still stand exactly as described below; only the number of bodies subject to it changed, from two to one. **Neither leaves the file larger:** `v82` measured 3,385.4 MB before → 3,385.4 MB after, and the pages return to the freelist, so this is transient headroom and **not** permanent growth. That distinction matters: permanent growth would feed `StorageEstimator.totalSizeMB()` → `isOverBudget()` → `SyncEngine.runPruneIfOverBudget`, which deletes `MessageHeader` **rows** — a schema migration causing message pruning. It does not. **Reachability:** a device with under ~300 MB free cannot complete the migration, `AppDatabase` init throws, and the app sits at the "Updating…" splash — this app's documented boot-hang shape (see the `PLAN_HANG_FIX` note in `AppDatabase.swift`). **Recovery is one ordinary user gesture:** free space and relaunch. Nothing is corrupted; GRDB rolls the failed migration back and the chain resumes from the last committed identifier. **Why it is not "fixed":** both bodies are already single-pass; chunking either into committed batches would break the atomicity `v82`'s id rewrite depends on and would leave a database half-rewritten between batches, which is strictly worse. ⚠️ **Stated negatively (`MIS-019`):** this row does **not** say every migration needs 300 MB — the other 14 are `ALTER TABLE`/`CREATE INDEX` shapes with negligible WAL — and it does **not** license widening either body into a second full-table pass without re-measuring the peak. |
| IOS-MIGRATION-002 | ✅ **CLOSED AS A DECISION (2026-08-05)** — the owner's *"drop things instead and rely on delta sync"* lever, **measured and REFUSED**; recorded so it is not re-proposed the next time a migration is slow | Migrations; drop-and-re-derive; fail-closed; delta sync; `messageBody`; `v70_dropMessageBodyHeaderFK`; `messageUserLabel`; `MessageUserLabel`; `MessageHeaderRekey.apply`; `UserLabelMenuView`; `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`; `foreignKeyChecks: .immediate` | **The lever, verbatim:** *"if migrations take long, we should even consider just dropping things instead and rely on delta sync, ie, the fail closed strategy."* **It does not apply, on three independent grounds.** **(1) The only re-derivable mass is already dropped.** `messageBody` is a re-fetchable cache of rendered HTML, and `v70_dropMessageBodyHeaderFK` drops the whole table outright — the lever's single valid target is already pulled. **(2) `messageUserLabel` is NOT re-derivable, and the census is broader than a sync-path search finds.** Predicate: `rg -n 'MessageUserLabel\(' TabMail` → **14** construction sites. Seven are sync-side header-INSERT paths (`SyncEngineFullSync` ×4, `SyncEngineDeltaSync` ×2, `SyncEngineBackfillDeep` ×1); five are `PreviewMocks` (not production data). **The two that settle it are neither:** `UserLabelMenuView` writes the junction row for a label the **user applied by hand** (optimistic local write paired with a queued `PendingOperation`), and `NSEDataBridge` re-inserts it on the NSE re-key path. `MessageHeaderRekey.apply` states the property outright and is built around it — `messageReference` is **REBUILT** from the header's own `References`/`In-Reply-To`, while `messageUserLabel` has **NO rebuild source** and is therefore **CAPTURED before the delete and re-inserted under the new id**. Dropping the table loses user-visible labels with no recovery path, which is a dropped user intention, not a fail-closed edge. **(3) The cost was never in CARRYING rows — it was in VALIDATING them.** Measured over the whole `v68…v83` chain at profile H: 61.8–87.4 s total, of which combined migration BODY time is 19.2–22.2 s and **42.5–65.0 s (69–74%) is `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`**, run over the entire database once inside *each* of the 16 migrations by GRDB's `.deferred` default. `v68_addFolderUidValidityResetPending` is one `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` with a **0.3 ms** body and an **8,750–10,661 ms** FK check. Declaring `foreignKeyChecks: .immediate` on the 14 migrations whose bodies cannot create a violation removes that cost **without dropping anything** — **independently re-measured by the implementing agent on a quiet machine, shipped chain vs patched chain back to back: profile H 56.0–84.6 s → 28.0–28.3 s, profile L 12.7–13.0 s → 3.7–4.8 s, profile M75 (75k headers / 628 MB) 2.45–2.47 s → 1.30–1.31 s, profile M25 (25k / 209 MB) 0.78–0.80 s → 0.40 s, profile S (5k / 28 MB) 0.147–0.149 s → 0.078–0.080 s.** `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` returned zero violations after every run. (The earlier measurement pass reported 87.3–89.4 s → 31.3–31.4 s at H; the re-run's absolute numbers move with machine load, the ratio does not.) ⚠️ **What the fix does NOT buy, because past ~200k headers the residual is BODY work, not validation:** at 200k the patched chain is still 3.7–4.8 s on a Mac (7–19 s device-adjusted at the ×2–4 correction) and at 500k it is 28 s (56 s–1.9 min device-adjusted), dominated by `v82`'s label rewrite, `v70`'s super-linear `DROP TABLE` and `v83`'s index build. **The "a few seconds on device" boundary lands at roughly 70k headers at ×4 and 140k at ×2.** No further foreign-key-mode tuning moves that line; only deferral or laziness would, and neither was implemented. **`v71` deliberately keeps `.deferred`** as the single whole-database gate, so a PRE-EXISTING orphan is still caught; `.immediate` cannot see one. ⚠️ **Stated negatively (`MIS-019`):** this row does **not** say dropping-and-re-deriving is always wrong — it says the lever had exactly one valid target here, that target is already taken, and the remaining mass is not re-derivable. If a FUTURE migration is slow, re-measure the split between body time and validation time **before** reaching for it; the answer here was validation, and it may not be next time. |
